Format per entry: **date · phase · question · options considered · PROVISIONAL call made (if any) · status (open / answered → where recorded)**

Doctrine: Variorum is the journal's home/truth; Fragments is a co-writer projection surface. Recorded in AppSpec §14 (2026-07-07 delta), master plan row 9b (slotted after Phase 9, her call the same day), `notes/plans/phase-09b-fragments-bridge-brief.md`, and `notes/fragments-bridge-contract.md`. One PROVISIONAL inside the brief awaits her: the graduated-entry policy. Original framing kept below for the record.)*

1. **2026-07-07 · cross-app · The Fragments journal bridge — one-way, two-way, or skip? [ANSWERED — see note above]** The author's Fragments app (iOS, `~/dev/fragments`) ships a deliberately minimal "journal satellite" *explicitly designed for a co-writer journal app*: Supabase table `journal_entries` keyed `(user_id, date)`, plain-text `body`, deterministic UUIDv5 id `journal|<user>|<dayKey>`, row-LWW, RLS — the table is its only external ingress, by design (Fragments SyncSpec §8). Connecting Variorum costs the model **nothing**: the contract wants one plain-text body per day, which is exactly `renderedText` of a journal post — a one-way read-side projection push; blocks/fans/commits stay Variorum's own. Options: **(a) one-way push** Variorum→Fragments (display in Fragments' Day screen; needs a second transport + auth to Fragments' project, dayKey/UUIDv5 derivation, change detection, and an edit-war rule for days Fragments' own editor also wrote); **(b) two-way** (Fragments edits flow back via ordinary `autosave` — S5 covers conflicts — but that makes Fragments a second capture surface right as Phase 8 gives Variorum its own); **(c) skip** (accept two disjoint journals — the bifurcation is the real cost of skipping). **Decide after Phase 8** with a few weeks of real usage evidence about where she actually journals; the build is small once decided. **Status: open — author call, deliberately postponed for evidence.**

## Phase 8 (2026-07-07) — implementer PROVISIONALs + author actions for the checkpoint

2. **2026-07-07 · Phase 8 · The capture editing control.** Options per the phase plan's flagged interpretation: (a) SwiftUI `TextEditor` — simplest true-to-scale choice for entry-scale text; the AppSpec §4 TK2 mandate targets the *Mac editor core*, not a phone capture field; (b) a `UITextView` wrapper — more control, more code for no current requirement; (c) plain text fields — too little (multi-paragraph entries are normal). **PROVISIONAL: (a)**, implemented as one live `TextEditor` for the focused block with read-cheap tap-to-focus siblings (P3). Phase 15 (full iOS surface) revisits the control when block identity must live on the phone — *including the whole-buffer edit-cost diff (`EditCost.utf16Cost`, O(buffer) per keystroke; fine at capture scale, inherent to a whole-string `TextEditor` binding, noteworthy once large Mac-authored blocks are edited from the All-posts scope — review round 1 NIT).* **Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 8 checkpoint (2026-07-07): (a) RATIFIED as shipped — recorded in the phase plan's checkpoint ratification round + AppSpec §14's Phase 8 checkpoint delta; Phase 15 owns the revisit.**

3. **2026-07-07 · Phase 8 · Device credential mechanism (plan-flagged PROVISIONAL).** Shipped as flagged: a paste-in form (project URL + API key + optional bearer token, blank = the key) storing to the Keychain (`AfterFirstUnlock`), on **both** platforms — the Mac app needs runtime credentials too (it is sandboxed and cannot read `.env.supabase.local`), and one mechanism beats two. Re-paste after her key rotation. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-07, mid-Phase-9 QUESTIONS walk with the coordinator): RATIFIED as shipped — Keychain paste-in on both platforms stands; recorded here and ticked in the Phase 8 handoff checklist.** **SUPERSEDED later the same day (item 9 below): the paste-in demotes to interim scaffolding — it retires into a real sign-in at Phase 9c (the pulled-forward auth core); the iCloud-Keychain-sync tweak is declined (no in-between mechanisms). It remains the shipped mechanism until 9c lands.**

4. **2026-07-07 · Phase 8 · A4 daily-drive mechanism.** The fence shipped: debug builds default to the separate dev store (`Variorum-dev/`); the real store from a debug build needs `-VariorumRealStore` / `VARIORUM_REAL_STORE=1`; release opens the real store. **The sync seam is fenced too (review round 1, BLOCKER — so her ratification is informed):** debug and release builds share a Keychain, and there is exactly ONE production mirror — so an ungated dev-store session would have auto-adopted her pasted credentials, pulled her corpus into the dev store, and pushed dev-session writes to the mirror (which replicates to her real devices). Now a dev-store session **never syncs at all** (no credential auto-load, no setup door — the status row says "Sync off (dev store)"); the one mirror pairs with the real store only. Consequence to ratify with the mechanism: a blessed-debug daily-drive via the opt-in gets real store + sync, exactly like Release; plain debug/dev work is fully offline. **The iOS consequence (review round 2 — read before the device round):** the opt-in exists only when Xcode launches the process; a DEBUG build installed on the phone and launched from the home screen always resolves the dev store, where sync is fenced OFF (no setup door) — so the phone must be installed as a **Release** build for the checkpoint's credential paste and device rounds (Xcode: Product → Scheme → Edit Scheme → Run → Build Configuration → Release, then run to device once). Alternating an Xcode debug launch (args present) with home-screen launches would also silently split the phone's journal across two stores. If Release-install feels wrong for the phone, the alternative on the table is **iOS defaults to the real store** (the simulator's container never touches her corpus; the fence's iOS value only guards hypothetical future on-device dev builds) — her call. **Her call (plan A4): daily-drive a Release build, or a blessed debug run via the opt-in — and the iOS install mode above.** **Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 8 checkpoint (2026-07-07): Release builds on BOTH devices; the fence stands exactly as shipped (the opt-in stays a dev tool; "iOS defaults to real" declined) — recorded in the phase plan's checkpoint ratification round + AppSpec §14's Phase 8 checkpoint delta.**

5. **2026-07-07 · Phase 8 · `newBlock` never stamps `last_touched_at`/churn — spec alignment?** Found while building the idle sweep: `newBlock` (and the mint helpers under it) writes no touch stamp and feeds no churn, so a mint-only post (one-burst entry; a teardown-minted tail; drag-drop bulk) never moves in the touched sort and — without the sweep's added `created_at` branch — would be invisible to idle insurance forever. AppSpec §10 says *text-changing ops* update `last_touched_at` and churn, and the 2026-07-06 delta stamps even structural ops ("restructuring is working on the post") — typing brand-new text arguably must stamp *a fortiori*. Options: (a) `newBlock` calls `registerTextChange` (stamp + churn = the minted text's length) — spec-truest, but changes Phase 2-tested op behavior and every mint call site's expectations; (b) stamp only, no churn; (c) leave as-is — **with the honest coverage statement (corrected per review round 1, which caught the original overclaim):** the sweep's `created_at` branch covers *never-touched* posts only (a fresh one-burst entry). A mint into a **previously-touched post whose stamp is already behind the sweep mark** — an idle-/teardown-minted tail on an old post, left alone afterwards — is invisible to BOTH branches and gets **no idle insurance until the post's next real edit**. *Same family (round 2): a never-touched post CREATED before the mark and filled later — e.g. a "Today" page opened blockless one day, swept past, then given its one block days later — is equally invisible (the created_at branch windows on creation, not the mint).* Those residual gaps are pinned/documented (`aMintIntoAPreviouslyTouchedAndSweptPostIsMissed_documentedGap`) and are exactly what (a)/(b) would close. The sort gap is cosmetic and rare since real typing autosaves immediately after. **PROVISIONAL: (c) for this phase** — no op-behavior change mid-phase. Suggested owner if (a)/(b): Phase 9, where crystallization mints live in the editor. **Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 8 checkpoint (2026-07-07): option (a) — `newBlock` stamps `last_touched_at` AND feeds churn. Implementation OWNED BY PHASE 9 (folded into its Step 1 op wiring; the documented-gap test flips into the red-on-old pin) — recorded in the phase plan's checkpoint ratification round, AppSpec §14's Phase 8 checkpoint delta, and the Phase 9 plan's reconciliation note.**

6. **2026-07-07 · Phase 8 · A5 mirror migration awaits her paste (author action, not a question).** `Server/supabase/03_visibility_horizon.sql` is written, parsed by the in-repo DDL walk, and pinned by the fake-server tests — but the permission system (correctly) refused headless credentialed access to her production Supabase, so **she runs the file in the SQL editor** (same flow as Phase 5's gate), then optionally the env-gated integration test. Until it runs, the deployed changefeed still has the visibility-skew window; clients need no change either way. **Status: RESOLVED (2026-07-07, mid-Phase-9): the author ran `03_visibility_horizon.sql` in the Supabase SQL editor — the deployed changefeed now serves behind the visibility horizon. The env-gated integration test remains optional at the device round; ticked in the Phase 8 handoff checklist.**

7. **2026-07-07 · Phase 8 · Re-dating an entry onto an occupied day (review round 1, NIT).** The entry editor's DatePicker (`setEntryDate` — user-set day, past/future allowed) can move an entry onto a day that already has a live journal entry, producing two same-day entries in one tap, locally and silently. Coherent with the hand-merge doctrine for the *offline* double-create (two visible entries on the day's page, no DB uniqueness by design), but this local one-tap door was previously unrecorded. Options: (a) allow silently (the current behavior — same convergence story as the offline race); (b) warn-and-proceed in the UI; (c) refuse and point at the existing entry. **PROVISIONAL: (a)** — the day's page shows both, nothing is lost, and inventing a refusal is product behavior; a UI hint is a Phase 13-adjacent nicety. **Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 8 checkpoint (2026-07-07): (a) RATIFIED — allowed silently, as shipped — recorded in the phase plan's checkpoint ratification round + AppSpec §14's Phase 8 checkpoint delta.**

## Phase 9 (2026-07-07) — implementer PROVISIONALs

8. **2026-07-07 · Phase 9 · Structural rewrites are full re-renders (the plan's pre-decided reading, logged per its ground rule).** Every structural gesture rewrites the editor surface through ONE path: a fresh `renderPost` fetch → Phase 7's full re-render (content-anchored scroll restore + text-undo clearing ride along) → caret re-placed by identity (blockID + intra-block offset mapped through the op's known seams: divide → successor's start; extract → extracted block's start; merge → the join). Structural ops always change the slot sequence, so Phase 7's differential path doesn't apply; a gesture is a deliberate act, not keystroke-rate, so a full re-render is within budget. Options considered (in the pre-written plan): (a) full re-render — shipped; (b) extend the differential path to sequence-changing results — the recorded fallback if Step 8's measurement or the author's feel pass finds jank on long posts. **PROVISIONAL: (a)** — Step 8 measured the full-path latency at ~16.6 ms on a 10k-word windowed post. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-07, mid-phase — author directive at the QUESTIONS walk): build option (b) NOW, this phase — the differential structural rewrite is the gold standard for the everyday gestures (untouched text never moves; no restore step at all beats a correct restore step; full re-render is O(document) per gesture while differential conforms to P1 as written), and this phase uniquely holds the verification net (the soak) and the models. Scope she accepted: differential apply for divide/extract/makeBlock/merge derived from the OPERATION RESULT's known seams (never from diffing — categorically distinct from the rejected diff-recovered-blob model, OverallModel §8), behind the SAME one door (an internal strategy of applyStructuralResult); the W-ceremony confirm and structural undo STAY full re-render permanently; text-undo clearing stays on every structural rewrite; a DEBUG oracle asserts surface == fresh store projection after every differential apply, exercised by the soak; Step 8 re-measured both paths at 10k and 50k words. The full-re-render v1 reading and its 16.6 ms number stand above as recorded history (it remains the W/undo path's cost). Recorded in the phase plan's banner note + closeout.**

## Mid-Phase-9 author decisions (2026-07-07 — the sync/key conversation with the coordinator)

9. **2026-07-07 · Phase 9c (new row) · The auth core pulled forward; one account with Fragments.** Context: Fragments hardcodes a genuinely publishable key because real user auth + RLS stand behind it (the key grants nothing); Variorum's mirror is deliberately auth-less for the personal phase (SyncSpec §2's single-user token; SECURITY DEFINER RPCs with no per-user check), so **any key the app holds is a full bearer token to the journal corpus** — the Phase 8 paste-in faithfully reflected that, and the two apps can only converge by Variorum gaining auth, never by a mechanism swap. **Decision: pull Phase 14's auth core forward as Phase 9c** (immediately after the 9b bridge): Supabase Auth sign-in, `owner_id` population + stamping through Operations, RLS + RPC rework on the mirror (SyncSpec §12 lockstep, author-paste), transport token refresh (sync-engine surgery — Fable per the standing rider), the paste-in retiring into the sign-in screen, the key demoting to truly publishable. **Identity goal ratified: ONE account — the author's Fragments account IS her Variorum account** ("I don't see the point" of two — and one identity is the cleaner story for the shared journal, though the bridge would function either way). **Open sub-question (the 9c plan's, not blocking):** the trust mechanism — (i) Variorum's project trusts Fragments' issuer (third-party JWT/JWKS trust; verify current Supabase support at plan time via a Sonnet lookup), (ii) shared JWT secret across the two projects (works everywhere; couples the projects' compromise blast radius), (iii) one merged Supabase project (rejected-leaning: couples deployments/migrations and strains the hands-off-Fragments boundary). **What does NOT move:** multi-tenant/commercial hardening, non-author provisioning, the E2EE decision (plaintext stays ratified meanwhile), and the variorum-edition export stay at 14. Supersedes #3's same-day ratification (see its status). Recorded: master plan row 9c + Phase 14 row updated, KnownIssues auth row split (9c core / 14 commercial), AppSpec §14 delta, SyncSpec §2 note, `notes/plans/phase-09c-auth-brief.md`. **Status: DECIDED (sequencing + the one-account goal); the mechanism is the 9c plan's to resolve.**

## 2026-07-09 — the author's first hands-on session (decisions in AppSpec §14's 2026-07-09 delta; these two are the items she deliberately left OPEN — record them faithfully, without adding certainty she doesn't have)

10. **2026-07-09 · Phase 11 · Must the W always be a full ceremony?** The author is conflicted, explicitly and on the record. Her argument *against* ceremony-always: structural changes undo easily (session undo, cheap pointer inverses) — "if structural changes can be undone easily, I think it becomes hard to think of why we wouldn't have an easy W that could be undone if needed." The strongest concrete sub-case: **cross-boundary/auto Make Block when the span matches uniquely in EVERY take** (CutPropagation's unique-match condition) — the identical middle mints as a clean single-variant block and the differing outer pieces keep per-take fans, W-style, with no marking decision left for a human to make. The counterweight: "the W is the one deliberate ceremony" is a ratified design identity (OverallModel §7), and auto-marking all takes IS a semantics change to it. She notes the answer "depends a lot on difficulty" and suggests open items like this may need **both-ways testing via distinct commands** during Phase 11 daily use rather than a paper decision. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-13, asked live at the Phase 11a Step A9 gate via the coordinator, the auto-proceed demo in hand): AUTO-PROCEED ON UNIQUE MATCH** — when the span's boundaries land at exactly one spot in EVERY take, the split proceeds with no ceremony sheet (the identical middle mints single-variant; differing outer pieces keep per-take fans; one undo compound reverses); the W sheet appears ONLY when some take doesn't match uniquely — the ceremony survives exactly where a genuine per-take decision exists. Recorded in the Phase 11a plan's A9 sitting record; AppSpec §14 delta lands at the closeout ritual.

11. **2026-07-09 · Phase 11 · Cross-block edit semantics on FANNED blocks.** Ratified the same day for single-variant×single-variant: cross-block edits become **merge-then-edit compounds** (a cross-seam delete just works). For fanned blocks she floated the further step — **per-variant deletion without merging**: "arguably, even for blocks that have variants it should still delete what you chose to delete on each block without merging" — i.e. the selected span is removed from *each take* of each touched block (where it appears), block structure untouched. She is explicitly unsure ("not sure exactly on that right now"). Her governing principle, recorded verbatim in the §14 delta: power-user machinery must not show up as daily friction — "there is no other writing editor where you can select a bunch of text, hit delete, and it doesn't delete... if your text looks like basic text with no variants, it should behave like basic text." Open sub-questions for 11's design round: what happens to takes that don't contain the span (skip? refuse?); is this a lineage event; how does it interact with the OPEN W question above. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-13, same sitting, refined across two rounds of her questions): three parts.** (1) The PLAIN delete gesture on a fanned cross-block span = **VISIBLE-TAKE DELETE** — remove exactly the selected text from the take being read plus the neighbor's piece; fan, seam, and block structure untouched; never guesses ("delete deletes"; the two-paragraphs-instead-of-joined deviation from basic text is accepted on a badged block — the fan hangs on the seam). (2) **"Delete in every take"** ships as a deliberate named command — unique-match per take (the #10 discipline), APPLIES to matching takes and REPORTS the takes it couldn't match (visible, one undo; partiality is honest for a take-scoped command; her call: apply + report over refuse-unless-all-match). (3) **Merge stays the explicit gesture** for when the seam should go, speaking about what it does to the fan. Sub-questions resolved en route: empty takes confirmed legal (`newBlankVariant` + the ratified blank-takes-skip rendering); "read without this block" = choose (or lens) a blank take — per-thread, non-destructive; a "mute block" one-click sugar noted as cheap-if-wanted, deliberately NOT built this phase. Recorded in the Phase 11a plan's A9 sitting record.

## 2026-07-09, evening — the on-device pass (recorded by the 9b coordinator at kickoff; decisions in AppSpec §14's evening delta)

*(Amendment to #10/#11, her evening instruction: cross-fan bracketing is **COMMITTED Phase 11 build scope**, not just a design round — and the open items above are settled by **asking her LIVE at implementation time**, never by a paper decision beforehand. Their status stays OPEN; the settling method is now recorded. The Phase 11 brief item 7 carries the upgrade.)*

12. **2026-07-09 · Phase 10 · Paste spacing preservation — the mechanism.** *(NARROWED 2026-09-07 by U-19 (3), the use-report round's 18c — RATIFIED, not provisional: TYPED newline runs no longer reach the tail (the Return key is structural in Write with the chip on), so the k−1 rule's typed half is superseded — a blank line inside a paragraph is ⇧⏎; PASTED runs keep the rule unchanged; the chip OFF restores Return-as-content for the whole note. The k−1 rule itself stands at every divide seam, including the "make every newline a block" repair — QUESTIONS #93(b).)* Decided (direction level): pasted items keep the spacing and newlines between them — today bulk entry segments a paste into blocks and the blank lines between the pieces vanish from the editor surface (single-`"\n"` join). Open (the how): **(a)** the inter-item whitespace becomes block **content** (e.g. trailing newlines on the preceding block — I4-clean, syncs for free, but changes what `renderedText`/export emit for pasted posts and how the `"\n\n"` join composes); **(b)** a render-time rule (the surface re-creates the pasted spacing without storing it — but "stored-nothing separators" was ratified as exactly `"\n\n"`/single-`"\n"`, so a variable-width separator needs a stored home somewhere, which smells like denormalization). **No PROVISIONAL call permitted — the author asked to be asked live at build time. Status: OPEN — Phase 10 asks her at implementation.** **Author follow-up (same evening): ask-live CONFIRMED, and the conversation broadens** — cover the various spacing use cases *including how newline ENTRY is handled*, not just paste; her note, verbatim: "the editor is so ugly as is, and we'll have to fix it." The functional spacing/newline treatment is this item's Phase 10 scope; pure visual polish stays Phase 13. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-12, asked live at Step 9 via the coordinator, options demoed first): option (a) — inter-piece whitespace becomes block CONTENT (k newlines between pieces → k−1 trailing on the preceding block; the editor separator re-creates the last), the SAME k−1 rule at crystallization (typed blank lines persist; a paragraph now completes when the NEXT one begins), and the export join follows the merge-join precedent (`"\n\n"` unless the left block already ends in a newline — spaced pastes round-trip exactly; unspaced posts byte-identical to before). PLUS the presentation half, added now: a uniform presentation-only per-PARAGRAPH gap (stored nowhere; per-paragraph not per-seam because AppSpec §12 keeps boundaries invisible at rest). Both consequences acknowledged by the author (the bridge re-projects spaced days with their spacing; the iOS trailing-blank-lines defect must later distinguish accidental accrual from stored spacing). Recorded in AppSpec §14's 2026-07-12 delta + the Phase 10 plan's Step 9 closeout.**

## Phase 9b (2026-07-09) — implementer PROVISIONALs (walk at the checkpoint; full detail in the phase plan's closeout)

13. **2026-07-09 · Phase 9b · The contract gap: unpinned id-derivation and wire facts.** `notes/fragments-bridge-contract.md` pins the deterministic-id RECIPE but not Fragments' namespace UUID, no worked examples (the plan's Step 1 tests presumed they existed), not the canonical-user string's exact form, and no RPC wire shapes. Options: (a) stop the phase and report (disproportionate — the missing facts are consumable only at the real-project pass, which is already author-deferred post-9c); (b) guess values into code (forbidden class); (c) **make the facts injectable configuration + pin the RFC 4122 algorithm and recipe composition by test + ship PROVISIONAL wire DTOs pinned by no-network tests + file precise asks in the contract file (its designed purpose)**. **PROVISIONAL: (c)** — asks 8–13 appended to the contract for the author to carry to her Fragments agents; the (already deferred) real pass is the reconciliation point and is now additionally gated on asks 8–12 being answered. **Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 9b checkpoint (2026-07-09): handling (c) RATIFIED as shipped — and the asks came back the same evening** (the author carried them; the Fragments agents' answers are recorded in `notes/fragments-bridge-contract.md` and the phase plan's reconciliation round): namespace + derivation parity CONFIRMED with real worked vectors; the canonical-user two-value design CONFIRMED (plus a fail-closed rule when `user_settings` has no row); THREE wire-shape corrections (aggregate push response, `p_`-prefixed params + the `date` column, keyset cursor on raw `server_updated_at`); `created_at` follows the winning push (the fake was wrong); clamp = 10 min with the `clock_ahead:` prefix (ask 14). The real-project pass is now gated on 9c's sign-in only. **Ask 13's replaced-body improvement: confirmed feasible, NOT built — the author's own Fragments-side call, still open with her.** **Update 2026-07-09/10: she decided YES — Fragments builds it as their Phase 15.7 (shape drafted, NOT final); their FINAL reply arrived 2026-07-10 and was re-reconciled at the 9c kickoff — emission re-pinned genuine-OVERWRITE-only with consumer-side loss classification (three discrimination rules), the fourth `conflicts[]` producer added, leave-inline confirmed (contract asks 11/13); the only remaining pending event is their deploy ping (9c Step 6(iv) carries the conditional consume item).**

14. **2026-07-09 · Phase 9b · Landing/projection readings, bundled (each mechanism traced in the phase plan's closeout).** **(a) Variorum-side journal deletion does not propagate to Fragments** — `sync_delete` is implemented in the transport (the dialect is complete) but the engine never calls it: a deleted day just stops pushing (the only reading that can never destroy Fragments-side text she didn't choose to lose); consequence: the day's body stays visible in Fragments until she edits it there, whereupon it re-lands in Variorum as a fresh entry — nothing typed is ever lost. Alternatives: push a tombstone via sync_delete; push an empty body. **(b) A Fragments deletion racing a Variorum edit loses to the edit** (the SyncSpec §6 tombstone-rescue shape: a projection-side deletion of stale text must not destroy newer truth-side text; truth re-projects, un-tombstoning under their strictly-newer rule); the clean-deletion case ships exactly as the plan sketched (insure via the fifth-occasion auto-commit, then empty). **(c) Sealed/locked entries land incoming bodies as `'fragments'` variants despite the seal** (the ratified S5-outranks-the-seal posture extended to the second writer; the sealed chosen text is never overwritten). *The sealed-EMPTY corner — CORRECTED at round 3 (the round's MAJOR): the earlier "the row is consumed with a note and the body stays safe in Fragments" claim was FALSE — safe only while sealed; consuming the row left the body behind the cursor with no state, and the first post-unseal push (no agreed hash, no known stamp → wall-clock fallback) LWW-destroyed it everywhere. Since "nothing typed in either app can be permanently lost" is ratified doctrine, a fix was owed; the SHAPE is yours to ratify with this item. Shipped (PROVISIONAL, shape (a)): the landing DEFERS — the whole feed holds, visibly stalled with a note naming the sealed entry, until you unseal, whereupon the held row re-serves and lands normally (full recovery, no Fragments-side action needed; pinned red-on-old). Alternative shape (b), not taken: a day-scoped push hold marker — other days keep flowing, but the consumed body can never re-serve, so it stays stranded in Fragments until you edit that day there again, and it needs new marker state with a subtle interaction with the deletion-race bookkeeping. (a) reuses the tested straggler machinery and is the SyncSpec-§12 posture (stall over guess); its cost — the feed pauses on a corner case you created and can end with one click — seemed right, but the trade is genuinely yours.* **(d) Edit-war convergence: truth re-projects** — after a both-changed conflict landing, the next push projects Variorum's rendered text back over the Fragments row (the brief's "momentarily behind" read as convergence-to-truth; the war terminates because their clobber guard never re-pushes unchanged text; both texts survive in Variorum — a guarantee the round-2 review made TRUE for steady state by re-stamping pushes minimally above the last SEEN remote stamp (an unseen same-round Fragments save now goes `.stale`-then-rescued instead of being silently overwritten; residues: first contact, 1 s stamp granularity — contract ask 13 closes them). *Consequence to know (round-1 review, NIT 7): the conflict card fans the block, which GRADUATES the day — after one edit war, subsequent Fragments edits land as further rescue cards (push-only regime), not text updates, until the `'fragments'` variant is pruned or archived. Ratified policy applied as written; flagged so it doesn't surprise.* **(e) Archived entries stay push-eligible** (archive is kept-out-of-view, not gone — freezing the projection on archive would be silent divergence). *Companion consequence (round-1 review, NIT 6): archived entries also COUNT toward the duplicate-day pause — archiving one half of a converged double-create does NOT release the pause; delete or re-date does (the status row copy says so). Conservative on purpose: an archived sibling is still the day's text, and pushing the survivor would silently drop it from the projection.* **Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 9b checkpoint (2026-07-09): (a)–(e) RATIFIED as shipped, both recorded consequences acknowledged; the sealed-empty SHAPE ratified as the shipped defer-at-cursor (a).**

15. **2026-07-09 · Phase 9b (round-1 review, MAJOR 2) · The push sweep's P1 relaxation — author ratification required (CLAUDE.md: relaxing a performance invariant is never an autonomous call).** The bridge's change detection renders + hashes EVERY live journal day per round — cost ∝ journal-corpus size, P1's letter. Why it ships anyway (mechanism traced in the closeout): no container stamp covers everything that changes rendered text (`chooseVariant`/`deleteBlock`/`deleteVariant` stamp only their own rows), so every cheap stamp filter has silent-miss classes, and a silently stale projection is the bridge's worst failure shape — correctness rides the hash. Mitigation shipped with the fix round: bridge CADENCE triggers are throttled to a minimum round interval (default 60 s, an engineering default of the `settleDebounce` class — adjust freely), so edit-settle bursts cost one sweep per interval instead of one per settle; manual "Bridge now" bypasses. The real remedy (a bridge-local dirty-day set maintained at the operations layer, or a verified stamp filter with per-op coverage tests) is KnownIssues-owned by Phase 12. Options: (a) ratify hash-sweep + throttle as shipped (12 owns the remedy); (b) demand the dirty-day set now (pulls Phase 12 work forward); (c) accept a stamp filter with known silent-miss classes (rejected by the tracing — silent divergence). **PROVISIONAL: (a). Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 9b checkpoint (2026-07-09): (a) RATIFIED — the hash sweep + 60 s throttle stand as shipped; the dirty-day remedy stays owned by Phase 12.** (Ratified after the author took the plain-terms tradeoff walk; her "why not before 9c?" question answered on the record: the bridge never runs in production before 9c's sign-in exists, so pulling the cross-cutting remedy forward buys nothing in the interim — it belongs with hardening's corpus-scale benchmarks.)

## Phase 9c (2026-07-10) — implementer PROVISIONALs

16. **2026-07-10 · Phase 9c · The bridge's Fragments-session mechanism (Step 5).** Step 0 proved the delegated sign-in delivers `provider_token` + `provider_refresh_token` (a real Fragments-project user JWT) — but Supabase never persists or refreshes provider tokens (~1 h life), and the bridge needs an indefinite Fragments session. Options (full weighing in the 9c plan's "Step 5 design note"): (a1) refresh the provider pair as the registered confidential client — puts the client SECRET on-device and stacks on unverified OAuth-server token semantics; (a2) refresh as a second public client — recorded as almost certainly unworkable (refresh tokens are client-bound); (b) the app registers as its own public client and runs a second delegated flow — no secrets, but stacks on the same unverified PostgREST-acceptance semantics; (c) a direct Fragments-project sign-in for the bridge (her Fragments email+password entered once; only the session persists, never the password) — the exact session class Fragments' own app uses, acceptance guaranteed by construction, zero secrets, and the shape the 9b contract already promises ("a user-auth session for the author's Fragments account, stored untracked on her machines"). **PROVISIONAL: (c)** — the only shape with zero unverified semantics; the captured provider pair stays persisted-but-unconsumed as the upgrade evidence if she prefers strict one-touch later. The identity stays ONE account either way — (c) is a second *session*, never a second account. **Status: ANSWERED early — RATIFIED by the author mid-phase (2026-07-10, in-session with the coordinator: "direct fragments sign in is fine"); Step 5 builds (c) as ratified, not provisional; the provider pair stays persisted-but-unconsumed as upgrade evidence; recorded in the phase plan's closeout at phase end.**

## Phase 11a (2026-07-13) — implementer PROVISIONALs

18. **2026-07-13 · Phase 11a (Step A1) · What does the SEAL refuse among the new metadata surfaces?** The lockstep event adds three author-facing metadata surfaces — block names (v1_6), canvas positions (v1_5), lens edits (v1_7) — and the ratified seal doctrine has two lines that pull differently: *"the seal refuses everything UNDER the post"* (2026-07-05, everything-under letter) vs *"the seal freezes AUTHORING"* + the 9d "Show Takes under seal — viewing isn't authoring" ratification (and the post itself may still be retitled/tagged/archived under seal). Options per surface: (a) refuse (everything-under letter); (b) allow (viewing/identity-metadata isn't authoring). **PROVISIONAL calls shipped: block names REFUSE under seal** (a block is under the post — unlike the post's own title, which lives ON the container row; the retitle parallel argues (b) and is recorded); **canvas positions ALLOW** (a card's x/y is arrangement of a VIEW — no content, order, chosen, or commit is touched); **lens CRUD ALLOWS** (a lens is "a saved way of looking"; the composition stays safe because the lens→chosen doors — ✓/adopt — and take-minting — diverge/branch-all — refuse under seal through their own ops' existing guards). Each is one guard line to flip if she rules otherwise. **Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 11a checkpoint (2026-07-13, via the coordinator): ALL THREE RATIFIED AS SHIPPED — block names refuse under seal; canvas positions allow; lens edits allow (with ✓/adopt/diverge-here still refusing under seal through their own ops' guards). Recorded in the phase plan's checkpoint ratification round.**

19. **2026-07-13 · Phase 11a (Step A2) · The post-level version link's ANCHOR.** I8 says posts are edges-only across versions; the edge table is block-to-block (AppSpec §7's letter — `source_block_id`/`target_block_id` FK-reference blocks), so "Mark as Versions" on two posts must ride a block-level edge. Options: (a) ONE `version_of` edge between the two posts' FIRST live blocks (shipped) — coarse, cheap, one row; the peer traversal (`Queries.versionPeerPosts`) matches ANY live version_of edge between the posts' placed blocks, so the post relationship tolerates restructuring of everything except the anchor pair itself (dividing/releasing the anchor block moves the edge out of the placed set and the posts read unlinked — re-mark to relink; coarse, not forensic); (b) version_of edges on every corresponding block pair — rejected (O(n×m) rows for arbitrary posts, no defined correspondence, forensic-smelling); (c) a post-level edge table/column — a schema-level product call, hers. `duplicateAsVersion`/`promoteLens` use the same anchor shape (first slot pair) and ADDITIONALLY mint per-block `derived_from` edges, so version copies stay peer-linked through anchor restructuring in practice (the aligned view's row kinship rides derived_from, not the anchor). **PROVISIONAL: (a). Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 11a checkpoint (2026-07-13, via the coordinator, after the label-vs-map tradeoff walk — the label is one coarse findability pointer; the per-block MAP rides derived_from and exists exactly where real history exists, minted automatically by copies/promotes; inventing per-block edges between independent posts would be fake history): option (a) RATIFIED — the first-block anchor stands as shipped; the narrow re-mark-after-anchor-restructuring cost accepted; a post-level link table revisited only if re-marking annoys in real use. Recorded in the phase plan's checkpoint ratification round.**

## Phase 11b (2026-07-16) — implementer PROVISIONALs (the plan's pre-answered open interpretations, implemented as written; walk at the checkpoint)

20. **2026-07-16 · Phase 11b (Step B3) · Reference drag-out lineage: gesture-style copy, NO edge.** AppSpec §7 says a selection dragged from a reference "lands as a pool-drag-style copy" — but a reference is not a block, so a `derived_from` edge is *impossible*: the edge table is block-to-block, and a reference-to-block provenance link would be a new edge/table class (a schema-level product call — yours, never invented; also SyncSpec §12 territory, since a schema change is a lockstep event). Implemented per the plan's answered interpretation 1: the *gesture* is pool-drag-style (PDFKit's built-in selection drag out of the panel's PDF pane; the editor's existing drop path lands it — bulk entry at the tail, content edit in a block, exactly like any dragged text), the result is ordinary text with no provenance row. Options if you want provenance later: (a) live with none (shipped); (b) a `reference_id` origin column on `edge` or a new edge type — schema change, Phase 12+ lockstep event. **PROVISIONAL: (a) as the plan directs. Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 11b checkpoint (2026-07-16, via the coordinator): RATIFIED AS SHIPPED — gesture-style copy, no edge; recorded in the phase plan's checkpoint ratification round.**

21. **2026-07-16 · Phase 11b (Step B1) · The PDF path: attributed-string → CTFramesetter pagination.** The plan's answered interpretation 5 ("the attributed-string print/PDF path over renderedText semantics; typographic polish is explicitly Phase 13's") implemented as a pure function: the plain post/reading projection (the same bytes `.md` exports) set in the system font, paginated onto US-Letter pages by CTFramesetter into a CGPDFContext — deterministic, headless-testable, no print panel. A bold title line rides post PDFs (readings already open with their `# name` heading). Alternatives considered: an offscreen NSTextView + NSPrintOperation (needs a live view + print machinery in tests) — rejected for testability at equal fidelity. **PROVISIONAL: as shipped. Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 11b checkpoint (2026-07-16, via the coordinator): RATIFIED AS SHIPPED — the CTFramesetter path stands; Phase 13 owns typography; recorded in the phase plan's checkpoint ratification round.**

22. **2026-07-16 · Phase 11b (Step B2/B1) · Two small interpretation calls bundled for the walk.** **(a) A drop on the POOL lands as scraps in that pool** — the KnownIssues/plan letter says "drop .md/plain text on the library or a pool → new post", but a pool drop minting a *post* would make the pool door meaningless; read as: the library drop mints a post, the pool drop appends blocks to that pool (`importBlocks`, the pool's own effective segmentation). **(b) Bulk export includes ARCHIVED posts, under `Archived/`** — "every live post lands exactly once" + the no-lock-in totality principle; archive is kept-out-of-view, not gone (the Phase 9b archived-entries-stay-push-eligible doctrine); tombstoned posts do not export (the SQLite file stays the lossless archive). **(c) POOL SCRAPS are absent from bulk export entirely, and no export door exports a pool at all (round-1 review, MINOR — the reviewer's catch of this bundle's own asymmetry: (b) surfaced archived posts under the no-lock-in principle but not pool scraps, which are equally your work).** The shipped manifest matches the ratified letter ("every live post lands exactly once" — `kind = 'post'`), so a bulk download today silently misses every scrap; the Export context menu hangs off library/journal rows and readings only, so a pool's scraps are reachable one caret-block at a time at best. The two shapes, verbatim from the finding: "`Pools/<title>.md` in the manifest, or an explicit recorded exclusion" — one manifest addition + one test either way. Deliberately NOT defaulted (a product call); the manifest's behavior was unchanged pending her answer. **Status: ANSWERED at the Phase 11b checkpoint (2026-07-16, via the coordinator): (a)/(b) RATIFIED AS SHIPPED, and (c) ANSWERED — INCLUDE POOLS: bulk export includes pool scraps under a `Pools/` folder (her pick of the reviewer's first shape), BUILT at the checkpoint per the 11a A9 precedent (`bulkExportManifest` gains one `Pools/<title>.md` per live pool, scraps in pool order through the same join semantics; totality + shape pinned). The build-time calls ratified with the bundle: includeReadings ALWAYS-ON (the checkbox offered and declined), read-only bookmark scope, the handle-derived block filename. Recorded in the phase plan's checkpoint ratification round.**

## Phase 13, Stage A (2026-07-18) — implementer PROVISIONALs (R55: noted-and-continued; walk at the END taste checkpoint)

23. **2026-07-18 · Phase 13 (A1/A2) · Block comments — four bundled calls.** **(a) Seal posture: REFUSE all comment writes under a sealed post** (add/edit/delete/restore), per the everything-UNDER-the-post letter (2026-07-05) and the v1_6 block-name precedent; the alternative (allow — a comment is a note ABOUT the text, not the text; the canvas/lens viewing-isn't-authoring class) is one guard line to flip. Reading stays free: both comment surfaces list under seal. **(b) Travel copies PRESERVE the source note's `created_at`** (updated_at = the op clock; fresh IDs, owner-stamped): a note's date is part of the note, and it keeps the copied list in display order — the alternative (now-stamps, the poolDragCopy variant precedent) would scramble merge's chronology. **(c) Comment text is METADATA under sync (server-version-wins CAS), deliberately NOT S5:** conflict-as-variant is scoped to `variant.working_text` (the one mutable prose buffer); a comment edit race converges to either text — acceptable for single-author working notes; the race is pinned. **(d) DELETED comments do not travel** — the recovery surface stays with the original block. **Status: ANSWERED 2026-07-22 (13b Stage 4 kickoff sitting, S4-26 — all four blessed).**

24. **2026-07-18 · Phase 13 (A1/A2) · Presentation/callouts — four bundled calls.** **(a) Seal posture: REFUSE under seal** (a callout frame changes what the page shows — closer to authoring than a lens). **(b) Split travel: structural successors INHERIT the source's frame** (extract/divide/divide-W — the span was the callout's content). **(c) Merge keeps the frame only when BOTH sources agree** (both-nil included); a callout merged with plain prose mints a PLAIN block — no side silently wins. The alternative (first-source-wins) is recorded. **(d) `color` stores a THEME-TOKEN NAME, never a literal value** — the R1 two-voices rule; the voice layer resolves tokens at render time; unknown kinds/colors round-trip undamaged. **(e — added at the Stage A round-1 review, finding 3: the poolDragCopy NAME asymmetry.)** `poolDragCopy` now carries presentation and comments but has NEVER carried `block.name` (a pre-13 behavior the new travel made visible): a named, framed, commented scrap dragged into a draft keeps its frame and notes but silently loses its name. `duplicateAsVersion`/`promoteLens` carry all three. Options: (i) leave as-is (a scrap's name is pool-local shorthand; the draft copy earns its own); (ii) poolDragCopy copies the name too (one line). Behavior UNCHANGED pending your call. **Status: ANSWERED 2026-07-22 (S4-27 — (a)–(d) blessed; (e) RULED: poolDragCopy copies the name too; Stage 4 builds).**

25. **2026-07-18 · Phase 13 (A1) · Sections & extents — four bundled calls.** **(a) Chapters are CURATED-only:** smart readings refuse `createSection` (they have no `reading_post` rows to assign; their sectioning story stays `grouping_tag_id`); if a smart book saved to the shelf should someday show chapters, that is a promote-to-curated design, not a section table change. **(b) A LOCKED reading freezes chapter STRUCTURE (create/delete/reorder/assign) but RENAME stays free** — the `renameReading` parallel (a finished book's chapter retitles without un-finishing). **(c) `word_target` is a display/progress TARGET, never an add-time refusal** (word counts change with every edit; the shelf shows progress, the boundary offers extend-or-start-new); both extent setters refuse under a locked reading (extending a finished notebook = un-finish first). **(d) `setReadingMaxPosts` added** (the v1 column's first setter — B2's extent face needs it); lowering below current membership is legal, further adds refuse at the standing capacity gate. **(e — added at the Stage A round-1 review, finding 2: the chapter delete/restore shape.)** `deleteSection` tombstones without touching memberships (the dangle rule), which made an accidental chapter delete silently unrecoverable (re-creating mints a fresh id the surviving pointers can never reattach to). Fixed with **`restoreSection`** — one tombstone flip, assignments reattach for free — plus `Queries.deletedReadingSections` as the recovery surface; refuses when the section is live, when the reading is locked, and when the section died WITH its reading (readings themselves have no restore door — a standing pre-13 boundary, flagged here in case you want one someday). No undo entry (the tags/locks class — the door is the recovery, the comments parallel). **Status: ANSWERED 2026-07-22 (S4-28 — blessed as shipped; four spawned directions recorded in the 13b plan: per-post word target N36, restoreReading N37, the GC hard-delete note, smart→smart+curated N38).**

26. **2026-07-18 · Phase 13 (Stage B) · The structure stage's PROVISIONAL bundle (R55 noted-and-continued; walk at the END taste checkpoint).** **(a) The daily journal book's designation:** "Today's Entry"/⌘J creates-or-opens the day's entry (the untouchable rule) AND ensures a designated daily book — created as curated "Journal" (profile journal, entry-date sort), PINNED to the shelf, and recorded under the `daily_journal_book` setting on first use; membership is ordinary (R50's letter). A finished/full daily book never wedges the door (the entry opens, the note speaks, "Use as Daily Journal" re-designates — round 2 MAJOR 2). **(a′ — CORRECTED at round 2 (BLOCKER) and RATIFIED by sitting 8/R57:** the original claim "the one-per-day rule's scope stays exactly the daily door" was FALSE as first shipped — the rule's real scope is the container `profile='journal'` + `entry_date` SHAPE, which `canonicalJournalEntry` and the Fragments bridge sweep key on, so `.journal`-profile dream entries collided with the daily journal and the bridge. Non-daily journal-like books are NORMAL NOTEBOOKS (her ruling): their "+ new entry" mints a **`.generic`** dated post with one-block segmentation set explicitly; `entry_date` is a generic column with no bridge/daily coupling; only the daily journal wears the journal shape. Several entries on one day stay legal in those books; the daily rule holds exactly where the shape lives.)** **(b) Unfiled posts TRAIL the chapters** in a chaptered book (a chapter is organization; stragglers at the end); the alternative (unfiled first, as filing pressure) is one line. **(c) The Blocks scrap rows STAY in the sidebar** under the renamed section (R44 gives the four words; the ratified Phase 10 drag-into-draft reason — scrap and draft visible together — survives the rename; detail-only Blocks would break the drag). **(d) Recovery + Tutorial homes:** off the final sidebar; reachable via ⌘K and the Library's toolbar menu. **(e) Quick-entry posts are untitled** (the typed text becomes segmented content; a title is a later rename — Enter-to-post wants zero friction). **(f) The shelf's tags column derives**: smart books show their query tags; curated books show member posts' most-applied paths (top 3). **(g) Book word counts are ONE-SHOT loads** (appearance/refresh), never held observations — the corpus-scale aggregate must not re-run at autosave rate (P1); the count can lag a keystroke until the next shelf visit. **(h — added at round 2, MAJOR 3: derived membership IS membership on the drainage surfaces.)** The Pool ("posts in no book") and the R35 membership chip now count SMART-book matches as being in a book — R29/R30's stated purpose is drainage via tags, and promote-tags-to-book must actually drain. The recorded consequence for your walk: **derived membership FLAPS** — tagging a post pulls it out of the Pool and onto the chip; untagging returns it; an EMPTY-spec smart book collects everything (the Pool reads empty); a malformed spec collects nothing (fail closed). **Status: ANSWERED 2026-07-22 (13b S4-29 — all eight blessed; (h)'s flapping confirmed as her own reading: membership comes and goes with tags).**

## Phase 13, Stage C (2026-07-18) — implementer PROVISIONALs (R55: noted-and-continued; walk at the END taste checkpoint; #27 = C1, #28 = C2/C3)

27. **2026-07-18 · Phase 13 (C1) · The writing surface's bundle.** **(a) INLINE comments' mechanism (R7's offered setting): the uniform per-paragraph gap widens by one strip height (22pt) when the setting is ON, and the tinted strips render in that gap.** Reasoning: an overlay cannot reserve vertical space in a TK2 flow, per-block paragraph-style attributes would be clobbered by ordinary typing and smear comment layout into every write path (the fragile class §3 warns about), and a UNIFORM gap (intra-block newlines and seams alike) keeps §12's boundaries-invisible-at-rest exactly as the 2026-07-12 ruling reasoned. Consequence you'll see: inline comments ON = a uniformly airier layout everywhere, not just under commented blocks. The margin-card default has no such effect. **(b) Drag handles (R19) ride the seams-visible mode** — the boundaries-shown state is the working-with-blocks state; at rest the surface stays calm (§12). The alternative (hover-revealed handles, always available) is one condition. **(c) A narrow right gutter degrades margin comment cards to a "#N" count badge** (cards need ≥150px; the badge is still a door to the panel). **(d) A callout's emoji renders in the LEFT gutter beside the frame**, not inside the text run (the run is the author's prose; an in-flow glyph would be a text edit). **(e) The ⌘⇧B seam toggle is a menu BUTTON ("Toggle Block Seams"), not a checked Toggle** — the menu can't observe the synced setting without a standing bridge; the Settings pane shows the checked state. **(f) A spaced callout block's export loses stored-spacing byte-exactness through the blockquote mapping** (the "> " prefixes change the conditional join's newline-suffix check) — deliberate: the mapping is a presentation transform; `renderedText`/the bridge stay byte-exact (pinned). **(g) Handle drops target VISIBLE frames only** (no auto-scroll mid-drag; long-distance moves go through the Blocks TOC's reorder). **(h) The status line shows "blk 2/5 · 12w · 1,204w post" but NOT the anchor's "commits N"** — a per-caret-move commit count is a DB hit per caret step; deferred until wanted. **Status: ANSWERED 2026-07-22 (13b S4-30 — (c)/(d)/(f)/(g)/(h) blessed; (a)/(e) explained in-sitting and standing; (b) superseded by S4-19's hover-setting ruling; (g) spawned N39 — positional move doors).**

28. **2026-07-18 · Phase 13 (C2/C3) · The takes-presentations + canvas bundle.** **(a) The marginGrid "Seamless" toggle FOLDED into the universal "Mark changing block" toggle (default ON — R13/R22's letter):** v2's own toggle removes the WASH only (the cards stay); the old Seamless hid the take grid too, which would have fought follow-the-scroll (a mode that exists to keep cards visible). Unchecking now = wash off, cards stay; closing cards = the ⑂ toggle / Close All / scrolling away under follow. The alternative (a separate read-plainly mode that hides cards entirely) is one condition if you miss it. **(b) R24's short/long rule = a 200-character threshold** (~under three lines at the 68ch measure), pure and pinned (`TakesViewModel.usesHorizontalStrip`); recorded cost: in strip form a LONG take on a short block clips at the one-card height (the one-card-high letter) — whole in every other presentation. **(c) Follow-the-scroll outranks the per-block ⑂ close:** while the option is on, every near-viewport fanned block is bloomed BY THE MODE — clicking ⑂ closed does nothing visible (no third "explicitly closed" state was invented); scrolling away or turning the option off closes. **(d) Follow-the-scroll's reach = wherever blooming exists** (the single-column article in all four styles + the locked columns' in-cell blooms); UNLOCKED columns have never had a bloom surface (each column is its own complete read) and keep that pre-13 shape. **(e) The canvas takes switcher writes CHOSEN (`chooseVariant`, one step at a time, ends clamped)** — the PROVISIONAL reading of R40's "switch that block to another one of its own takes": it is an explicit, deliberate per-block gesture, so I3's deliberate-choice doctrine holds, and what you arrange spatially is what the post IS. The alternative — a view-local display pick that never touches chosen (the lens-flavored reading) — is recorded; it would need per-canvas display state and a "this differs from chosen" marker. Sealed posts refuse and speak. **→ (e) ANSWERED 2026-07-18 (taste checkpoint, Sitting 9 batch 4 — R88): the ALTERNATIVE wins.** The canvas switcher becomes VIEW-LOCAL ("it's only chosen if chosen very carefully"): ‹ › changes only what the card shows, a differs-from-chosen marker appears, chosen moves only through an explicit choose gesture (⌘⏎ / the choose door), and canvas picks are savable as a lens. Built in the checkpoint fix round. **(f) The margin grid reserves a FIXED 340pt rail on every row** (empty until a row blooms) so the article column stays one straight measure (R9's calm); the alternative (rail only on bloomed rows — the pre-13 shape) made the article width wobble per row. *(Sitting 9/R74 additionally rules the grid gains vertical room + a density option, up to ~3 high × 2 wide — built in the fix round; the fixed-rail question itself stays open for the walk.)* **Status: fully ANSWERED 2026-07-22 (13b S4-31 — (a) clarified (seams setting untouched) and standing; (b) RULED: fixed-height strip + in-strip scroll (N40); (c)/(f) blessed; (d) uncommented, treated blessed; (e) was R88).**

Offered for promotion into 13c beside A39; **deliberately not taken** (moving her own scope is hers). **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-28, the 13d sitting): A21 stays at 13e.**
