33. **2026-07-25/26 · Phase 13c (batch 1B) · A7/A8/A10 — five bundled calls.** *(13c Stage 1 ladder, R2-4: this entry and a duplicate filed as #35 were the SAME five calls, on a list you walk top to bottom — answering one would have left an identical entry marked OPEN. Merged here on #35's fuller wording; #35 is now a pointer.)* All adjudicated DEFENSIBLE against the spec by the architect and all still yours to ratify. **(a) The new "Delete Block" door is NOT restricted to empty blocks.** Your A8 was written about empty blocks, but `deleteBlock` has never been restricted (it is the Phase 2 operation), the block-TOC row menu has always offered it unrestricted, and the block is undoable, now redoable, and tray-recoverable — so a restriction nobody ruled looked like invented product behavior. The alternative (offer it only when the block is empty, so the door is honest about the safe case) is one availability line. **(b) Deleting the post's LAST block is ALLOWED**, leaving a blockless post. Decided against the spec: no invariant makes a post's block count load-bearing (I10 governs a block's *variants*, not a post's blocks), and the blockless post is an explicitly-modelled state — it is what a fresh post is, and typing rebuilds from it (pinned end to end). The alternative (refuse the last block, "a post keeps at least one block") would be a NEW rule. **(c) The "archive" half of A8 is deliberately NOT built.** `block.archived_at` has sat in schema v1 since Phase 1 with NO operation, NO query and NO face anywhere in the app — building one means deciding what an archived block *means* (does it leave the post's render? where is it seen? what restores it?), which is product design, not a door. The architect judged this the right restraint. Delete + the tray + undo/redo cover the case you named; say the word if you want block archive as its own thing. **(d) When BOTH blocks either side of the deleted seam are empty, the one BEFORE it wins.** Backspace means "remove what is behind me"; preferring the block after would delete the very block your caret sits in and would never converge under repeated presses. Deterministic, not derived from which key you pressed. **(e) Structural redo is ⇧⌥⌘Z** (⌥⌘Z's mirror), deliberately distinct from ⌘⇧Z, which stays NSTextView's ordinary text redo — the two stacks never interleave, so their doors never share a key. **Also recorded, not actioned:** redo's insurance asymmetry (a source block edited between undo and redo is re-released with its edit intact but uncommitted) — **the same asymmetry undo already has**, deliberately not "fixed" by inventing a sixth quiet auto-commit occasion. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-28, the 13d sitting): all five RATIFIED as shipped — her words, "these seem fine".**

34. **2026-07-26 · Phase 13c (batch 1B, architect finding F3) · Backspace at a seam between two TEXTED blocks does nothing — should it merge?** Surfaced when the batch's own pin failed and proved the *pin* wrong rather than the code: the ratified "a cross-seam delete just works" (2026-07-09) covers a **selection spanning text**; a bare separator intersects neither neighbour, so both cross-block plans have always returned nil and that keystroke has always refused. A7's carve deliberately covers only the EMPTY-block case. **Why this is worth your eye rather than a quiet fix:** it invokes your own stated principle verbatim — *"there is no other writing editor where you can select a bunch of text, hit delete, and it doesn't delete… if your text looks like basic text with no variants, it should behave like basic text."* In every ordinary editor, backspace at the start of a paragraph joins it to the one above. **Options:** (i) leave it — merge stays an explicit named gesture, and a structural merge fired silently by a keystroke is a large implicit act; (ii) backspace at a seam **merges when NEITHER block has takes** (basic text behaves like basic text) and refuses-and-speaks when either does — merge already exists as the operation it would call; (iii) as (ii) but always, with the fan consequence spoken. **NOT built — this is product behavior and yours. Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-27, the ratify walk with the architect): OPTION (ii) — backspace at a seam MERGES when NEITHER block has takes, refuses-and-speaks when either does. Owned by 13d (it gates there); the §14 delta lands when it ships.** *[BUILT 13d batch 2, 2026-07-27 — the merge is the ordinary `merge` operation through the standing gesture path, and "has takes" is the merge ceremony's OWN instant-vs-ceremony threshold, read from the store rather than the editor's stale projection; the refusal names the fanned side and points at ⌘⇧M. AppSpec §14 carries the delta.]*

35. **MERGED INTO #33** *(13c Stage 1 ladder, R2-4 — this was a duplicate of #33's five bundled 1B calls, filed twice; the number is kept so nothing above or below renumbers, and #33 carries the fuller wording.)*

36. **2026-07-25 · Phase 13c (Batch 1C) · A46 — the "dead click" was INVESTIGATED and there is NO fourth bug; and option E is MEASURED.** Two results, both changing what she should decide. **(a) The dead click does not exist.** Sent to trace it, the batch measured the real thing — actual mouse events through the window's own hit-testing and delivery — and found that the phantom space below a post's last line IS the editor's own text view, the click reaches it, and the caret moves: to the **end of the document**. It cannot land *inside* the phantom because there is no text there — which is her own sentence, *"nothing was actually there."* Both halves of her report describe correct text-view behaviour over space that is not text. Five candidate mechanisms were tested and refuted (an overlay eating the hit; a shrinking document leaving the scroll parked past its end; a huge last block; a gap in the middle of the document; the gutters/insets) — all recorded in the batch record so nobody re-walks them. **Nothing was fixed**, per the hallucination guard's own rule that a finding whose mechanism does not trace to the symptom is recorded and dismissed. One genuinely un-routed **6 pt** strip at the bottom of every scroll view was found, proven to be **AppKit's** (a vanilla `NSTextView` does the same), left unfixed, and pinned at size so the class can't grow unnoticed. **(b) Option E measured well and is now the recommendation on evidence:** ~**1 ms per 1,000 words** (1.27 / 2.90 / 11.52 / 20.06 ms at 1k / 3k / 12k / 25k words), flat-to-improving per word, removes exactly the over-estimate, and — the open risk, resolved favourably — **survives scrolling away and back** (TK2 does not discard the layout). Against Phase 6's whole-document rate of 2.1 ms per 1k, one block is ~2× cheaper per word and structurally bounded. Caveats stated: the scroll extent corrects on the next display pass rather than instantly, and the **trigger policy** (which block, on what event) is an undecided design question. **E was deliberately NOT built** — the promise was that it would be measured, and it was. **Consequence for her:** removing the phantom is now the ONLY remedy for what she experienced, and if **A22** (typewriter room) is ever built, that space must be REAL room the caret can enter or it will feel exactly as dead. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-28, the 13d sitting): RATIFIED — no fourth bug, nothing fixed, the 6 pt strip stays pinned at size; Q3's posture was #39's KEEP. (A22 was subsequently BUILT at 13d batch 5 per her sitting ruling — the room is deliberate allowance with the click landing the caret at the text end.)**

37. **2026-07-26 · Phase 13c (batch 1D) · With NO daily journal book designated, a Fragments-landed day lands in the Pool and the round SAYS WHY — rather than the bridge creating one.** Your A1 is fixed: a day the bridge lands from Fragments now joins the designated daily journal book, exactly as ⌘J does (and it repairs entries that landed bookless before the fix, on the next landing that writes to them — your 2026-07-11 and 2026-07-16 entries are the two in your store). The one genuine open call is what to do when **no daily book is designated at all** (or the designation dangles at a deleted book). The landing is never blocked either way — that is doctrine, not a choice. Options: (i) land and skip silently; **(ii) land, skip, and surface it in the bridge status row (SHIPPED)**; (iii) land and CREATE the book, as the Mac door does on first use. **Why (ii):** the Mac door creates and pins a "Journal" book *because you pressed a button* — a headless overnight round minting and pinning a book on your shelf is a materially larger act, and it would be the first thing the bridge ever created besides journal entries. (i) leaves you exactly where you were — an entry in the Pool with no explanation, which is what you reported. Pinned that no reading is minted in this state. **Also bundled (recorded, one-line answers):** the shelving runs on every landing that WRITES to a day, not only when it mints one — that parity with ⌘J's *ensure* is what makes it repair your orphans — and it **never re-shelves an entry you removed** (any membership row, live or tombstoned, means the pair is already decided). **Status: ANSWERED 2026-07-28 (the 13d sitting) — OVERTURNED to option (iii).** Her words: *"the fragments entries should arrive as part of Journal"*. [BUILT 13d batch 5: the ensure half — create-and-pin included — moved INTO `shelveInDailyJournalBook`, so the bridge landing, the phone's capture and ⌘J share one rule with no creation residue at any door; ⌘J's own first-press behaviour is byte-unchanged. The `noDailyBook` case retired with the ruling. A finished / full / smart designated book still keeps its own status — creation never replaces a book she chose. AppSpec §9's entry carries the delta.]

38. **2026-07-26 · Phase 13c (batch 1D) · The bridge has NO cadence of its own, even signed in — should it get a timer?** Your A2 ("doesn't seem to refresh often enough") traced to the sign-out: bridge rounds ride *sync* rounds, and a signed-out Variorum session completes none, so from 2026-07-20 to 07-25 the bridge ran **exactly once per launch** — five days, with your Fragments session perfectly valid. That hole is FIXED (a lifecycle event now reaches the bridge when sync can't deliver a round, and the status row no longer shows a five-day-old round as "Bridged 11:04 PM"). **What is left is a design question, deliberately not taken:** even signed in, nothing pulls Fragments while you sit in Variorum without typing or switching apps — there is no periodic timer anywhere in the app, for sync or the bridge. A timer would fix that, and the reason it is yours is that **every bridge round hash-sweeps every live journal day** (the P1 relaxation you ratified at QUESTIONS #15), so putting that on a clock forever is a posture change on top of it. Options: (a) leave it — lifecycle + typing are the triggers, as designed; (b) a slow bridge poll (say every 5–10 min) with the sweep as-is; (c) a poll only after the Phase 12 dirty-day remedy makes the sweep cheap. **NOT built. Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-28, the 13d sitting): (a) — leave it, no timer; lifecycle + typing stay the triggers; revisit with Phase 12's dirty-day remedy.**

39. **2026-07-26 · Phase 13c (Batch 1E) · The PHANTOM FIX is BUILT — memo option E, PROVISIONAL in full, and it exists so you can rule on Q3 having FELT it.** Batch 1C measured E and deliberately did not build it (the promise was that it would not be built on arithmetic). Batch 1E built it: **when you scroll near a very long block whose height TextKit 2 has only GUESSED, Variorum asks for that one block's layout, once — and the empty space underneath it goes.** Measured in a real window this batch: a 3,000-word block's guess of 7,837 pt collapses to 7,300 pt, and the document gives back the same 537 pt in the same instant. Your A3/A4/A41 report is what this addresses, and 1C established that removing the phantom is the only remedy — there was never a click bug behind it. **What guards it, because the danger was never the cost but how OFTEN it could fire:** a hard **cap** (64,000 characters in one block ≈ 11,600 words — above it the block is skipped and recorded, so nothing can ever cost you a visible hitch; derived from 1C's own numbers at half a frame's budget, leaving about 4× of room); a **floor** (blocks under ~350 words are never touched, so an ordinary post costs literally nothing, ever); one block per pass; the work happens after scrolling STOPS, never during it; and it is scoped to the main Write editor only. **THE KILL SWITCH is in Settings → Appearance → Editor: "Lay out very long blocks as you reach them", default ON.** Turn it off and the old behaviour returns exactly, with no relaunch — which is the position I wanted you in before you rule.

    **On resizing — the one thing that changed between the build and the fix pass, so you know what you'll actually feel.** The brief said "at most once per block per session". Building it, I measured something nobody knew: a **window resize / sidebar toggle / split-divider drag makes TextKit 2 throw the layout away** and go back to guessing (a laid-out 3,000-word block returns to an 11,492 pt guess at a narrower column). Under the literal rule the phantom would have come back on your first sidebar toggle and never been corrected again — a fix that silently stops working, and you reporting the same bug twice. So the rule is now **once per block per COLUMN WIDTH**: after a resize the block is measured properly again. It stays cheap because it only ever runs when TextKit really did throw the layout away, and because a drag produces **one pass after you let go**, never one per pixel of dragging (14 width samples ⇒ 1 layout; pinned, and 16 passes with that guard removed).

    **Options on Q3, all live:** (i) keep it as shipped; (ii) turn it off (posture A — live with the phantom); (iii) keep it but change the cap or the floor — say what felt wrong and the numbers move. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-27, the ratify walk, after real use): KEEP AS SHIPPED — default ON, cap/floor as derived, the kill switch stays.**

40. **2026-07-26 · Phase 13c (Stage 1 ladder, R2-1) · Backspace on an empty block that is NAMED or wears a CALLOUT — should it still delete?** The round-2 review found a real hole and it is FIXED: an empty block carrying a **block comment** no longer vanishes to a seam backspace. The carve was authorized only on *"there is no text to lose"*, and a comment is authored text — worse, once the block is only in the tray its notes have no recovery surface at all, because `deletedBlockComments` is keyed by the block (AppSpec §7's *"never skimpy"* ratification quietly stopped holding on that path). Archived takes were the same class, guarded a round earlier. **What is genuinely yours** is the weaker pair the reviewer raised beside it: a block that is EMPTY but carries a **name** or a **callout frame**. **SHIPPED READING (PROVISIONAL): it still deletes.** The reasoning, so you can overturn it cheaply: a name and a frame are columns ON the block row, so the tombstone keeps them, ⌥⌘Z and the tray's restore bring them back with the block, and the tray even lists the block BY its name — nothing becomes unrecoverable or unreachable, which is exactly the line comments and archived takes cross. The other consideration pushing the same way: block TAGS cascade too, so a guard on "anything attached to the block" would narrow the carve back out of existence and undo the A7 fix you asked for. **The alternative** (a named or framed empty block keeps the refusal, on the ground that you deliberately placed something you can SEE) costs two more projection signals plus one guard line. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-27, the ratify walk): KEEP AS SHIPPED — a named/callout empty block still deletes; undo and the tray restore it whole.**

41. **2026-07-26 · Phase 13c (Stage 1 ladder, R2-2) · A9 — "i desperately need divide in takes mode" — DEFERRED to 13d, with the reason, because it is not what its ledger row said it was.** Your A13(a) (callouts everywhere + a shortcut) shipped at this fix pass exactly as the sitting traced it — one registry declaration plus wiring. **A9 is not that shape, and the record said it was**, which is why this is written down rather than quietly slipped: divide from Takes today DOES work — the door is on the Takes bottom bar, and invoking it carries you to Write with your caret placed and runs the divide there (the fire-and-DO choreography, which is the machinery 13b built for this class). What you are asking for is that it act **without leaving Takes**, and that is a different build: the structural planner is written over the full-document editor's block index, and a divide on a FANNED block — which in Takes is the ordinary case — opens the take ceremony, whose whole state machine currently belongs to the Write editor's controller. Doing it natively means giving Takes its own planning path and making the ceremony mountable outside Write. **Deliberately not attempted at 4 a.m. inside a review fix pass;** owner named: **13d**, beside the rest of the refusal/ergonomics floor. **What you can decide in one line:** whether the Takes divide door's current "carries you to Write" behaviour is acceptable until then (it is honest and it works), or whether 13d should lift it to the top of its list. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-27, the ratify walk): the carry-to-Write door is acceptable until 13d; native divide-in-Takes lands in 13d — her words: "it should be done in 13d but wherever in 13d is fine" (no promotion to the top).** *[BUILT 13d batch 3, 2026-07-27 — divide acts natively on BOTH boards (the shared-model precedent) and the take ceremony mounts in place, seeded from the take the caret is in rather than the chosen one. The verdict is the ONE planner's, pinned by a parity test against Write's own door; the fanned/unfanned fork is read from the STORE at run time, not the frozen projection. Extract, make-block and merge keep the carry — the scope was divide only.]*

42. **2026-07-26 · Phase 13c (Stage 1 ladder, R3-1 — the CAP round's post-cap fix) · Undo an empty-block deletion, write a NOTE on the block that came back, then press redo: it now REFUSES rather than deleting the block again. Is that what you want?** The round-3 review found the last and worst instance of a class two earlier rounds had each patched: the empty-block backspace carve was checking "is there anything to lose here?" against the editor's *projection*, and the editor deliberately freezes that projection while you are typing (it has to — your buffer is the truth until it is written). So a note arriving mid-burst — from your phone, from sync, or from your own comments sheet — was invisible to the check, the block went, and the note went with it, into a place the "deleted comments are always recoverable" surface cannot reach. **Fixed properly rather than patched again:** the question "is this block still empty of everything?" is now asked *inside the same database transaction that deletes it*, where the answer cannot be stale. That was worth doing at the level it was done, because three patches in three rounds is the shape of a wrong level. **What is yours:** a REDO also deletes the block, so it now asks the same question — and writing a comment is not a structural act, so it never clears the redo future. The consequence: undo the deletion, write a note on the block, hit redo, and Variorum says the block isn't empty any more and does nothing. **I judged that right** (the alternative re-opens exactly the hole just closed, one door over), but it is the one visible behaviour this fix invented, and every other structural redo re-applies unconditionally. **Options:** (i) keep it — a redo that would destroy a note you just wrote refuses and says so; (ii) let redo re-apply unconditionally like every other one, on the ground that it is undoable and tray-recoverable and consistency matters more. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-28, the 13d sitting): (i) — KEEP the refusal.**

43. **2026-07-27 · Phase 13c (Batch 2A) · Outline now opens with your posts OPEN — three bundled calls about the surface A39 asked for.** Your A39 is built: Outline is the book's writing surface — Write's own prose measure, chapter headings that read as headings, and the same one live editor that migrates everywhere else. Three calls inside it are yours, and each is one line to overturn.

    **(a) A post reads OPEN by default; COLLAPSE is the deliberate act. (SHIPPED — the one that genuinely reverses a ruling of yours.)** *[RULED 2026-07-27 at the walk: OVERTURNED — option (i), Outline opens COLLAPSED. BUILT in 13d batch 1 (the model now tracks the EXPANDED set); the paragraph below is kept as the record of what was shipped and argued.]* At Sitting 9's batch 12 you ruled the other way — *"you shouldn't see the content within a post on the book screen unless you click something"* (R106) — and that is why posts have been collapsed cards until today. I flipped it for **Outline only**, on three grounds, and you should weigh them rather than take them: your own A39 word is *"collapsible"*, which is what you say about things that start open; *"literally looks like write mode"* is not what a page of cards looks like; and the batch brief's instruction was explicit — *"build it as the place she writes a book, not as a decorated list."* **What protects you if I read it wrong:** your card design is untouched and one click away — "collapse all" restores exactly the old view, and a post you collapse stays collapsed. **The alternatives:** (i) revert to cards-by-default and let Outline be reached open only via "expand all"; (ii) remember it — the surface opens however you last left it (needs somewhere to persist, see (b)). Book and Read are unaffected either way.

    **(b) Collapse state lives for as long as the book is open, and is not persisted.** Close the book and reopen it and everything is open again. This matches every other view-local state in the app (fan expansion, canvas picks, the Compare toggles) and needs no schema. The alternative — persisting it per book — is a `lens`-flavored idea and a real design question (is a folded book a *saved way of looking*, i.e. exactly what a lens is?); deliberately not invented.

    **(c) A collapsed post shows batch 12's CARD — title, date, excerpt, word count, "open ↗" — unchanged.** The alternative considered and not taken: a one-line folded heading (title only, or the `tocHandle` first-sentence rule that already exists for unnamed blocks), which is tighter and more outline-like but throws away a card you reacted well to. On short posts the card is nearly as tall as the open post, so collapsing buys little there — a taste observation, routed to the Design & UI review row rather than acted on.

    **Status: ANSWERED — (a) OVERTURNED at the 13c walk (Outline opens COLLAPSED; built 13d batch 1); (b) and (c) RATIFIED as shipped at the 13d sitting (2026-07-28).**

44. **2026-07-27 · Phase 13c (Batch 2B) · BOOK is now the two-up spread you asked for — four bundled calls about how it flips and how a page presents itself.** Your A43/A44 are built: two whole posts side by side, each its own vertical scroll, ◀ ▶ turning the leaf two posts at a time with ◁ ▷ nudging by one so you can re-pair, and the active page editable as either the takes stack or normal Write. Four calls inside it are yours, each one line to overturn.

    **(a) ONE Write | Takes control for the whole spread, in the bottom bar, governing the page you're writing in. (SHIPPED — PROVISIONAL.)** Your instruction was *"stack view + the normal write view should be the editing forms offered in the … view"*, and "offered in the view" is what I built to: one control, one meaning, no new persistence, and it lives with the flip controls because that is the spread's own chrome. **The alternatives:** (i) **per page** — a small control in each page's header, so the left page could sit in Takes while the right sits in Write; costs a second control and raises "what does Takes mean on the page that isn't active" (see (b)); (ii) **per post**, reusing the per-post mode memory a post already has in its full editor — attractive because a post would present the same way wherever you meet it, but it makes a click in the book quietly rewrite how the post opens in its own editor, which you didn't ask for; (iii) **global**, one setting for every book. Like collapse state (#43(b)), the choice is not persisted — reopen the book and it is back to Write.

    **(b) Only the page you're IN shows its editing form; the other page stays read-cheap prose.** This is P3's one-live-editor rule, and here it is architecture rather than tidiness: a takes board owns its own liveness authority and registers its own command handler, so two mounted boards could wear two live rings and ⌘S would reach whichever mounted last rather than the one under your hands. Clicking the other page migrates the ring, exactly as everywhere else. **What you'd feel:** switching to Takes shows the stack on the page you're in; the facing page keeps its prose (with its fan badges) until you click into it. **The alternative** — both pages showing takes at once — is a genuinely bigger build (one shared authority across two boards, plus routing that knows which board you mean), so it is worth saying out loud rather than assuming you don't want it.

    **(c) The jump SLIDER is gone from Book's bottom bar.** *[RULED 2026-07-27: option (ii) — scrubbers on BOTH horizontal surfaces; the slider is BACK on Book's spread, BUILT in 13d batch 1.]* The batch brief said *"no horizontal scrollbar — that was her complaint about the current surface"*, and I built to it. **Flagged because it sits against your own earlier words:** the 13b paged view had that slider because you asked for it — *"flip through the pages and scroll on the bottom horizontal bar"* (S4-9(2)). Your later complaint was aimed at OUTLINE, where a horizontal control on a vertical page made no sense; on a horizontally-flipping spread it arguably does. **Nothing was lost:** the navigator lists every post and clicking one lands its page, which is the same model-driven jump the slider performed. **Options:** (i) keep it out; (ii) bring the slider back on Book only (it is ~15 lines and its brain — `scrubIndex` — is still here and still pinned).

    **(d) Arrow keys turn the leaf, but only while no page is live.** ← → turn the leaf, ⌥← ⌥→ nudge — and the moment a page holds the live editor those key equivalents are withdrawn entirely, so your arrows type instead of flipping the book. That is deliberate (a key equivalent is checked before the text view ever sees the key, so a permanent one would steal your arrows), but it means the same key does two things depending on whether you have clicked into a page. **The alternative:** put the flips on modified keys always (⌘← / ⌘→, say) so they never change meaning — at the cost of arrows not turning pages in the reading posture, which is what makes it feel like a book. **Status: ANSWERED — (c) RULED at the 13c walk (scrubber on BOTH; Book's returned in 13d batch 1); (a), (b), (d) RATIFIED as shipped at the 13d sitting (2026-07-28).**

45. **2026-07-27 · Phase 13c (Batch 2C) · READ now has real book PAGES — the five calls the uniform-page brief left open, all answered provisionally so you could see them rather than read about them.** Read is the pair you asked for: **SCROLL** (the flow you already had) and **PAGES** — a two-page spread of identical leaves with the text flowing across them. It is not editable, per your A43 ruling. Five things inside it were the brief's open questions; each is one line to overturn.

    **(a) PAGES is a second FACE of Read, not a fourth tab. (Brief Q1.)** The picker stays `Outline · Book · Read`, and a small `scroll | pages` control appears under it when you are in Read. Your own words framed Read as "the Apple-Books/Kindle **pair** of modes", and a fourth segment would have said the pair was two unrelated views. **The alternatives:** (i) a fourth top-level segment (four modes, and the picker stops fitting the rail); (ii) put the sub-toggle in the paginated bottom bar instead — but the SCROLL face has no bottom bar at all (your A6 argument), so the door would vanish exactly where you need it to come back. **The name is yours** — the brief's candidates were "Print", "Book pages", "Spread"; I used **Pages**, which is also the word the old post-per-page view wore before 2A renamed it.

    **(b) EVERY POST STARTS A NEW PAGE; text flows across pages WITHIN a post. (Brief Q2 — and this one deliberately goes AGAINST the brief's stated lean, so it is the first thing to look at.)** The brief leaned "chapters break to a new page, posts within a chapter flow together". I built the stricter rule, and the reason is not taste: with posts flowing, a page's content is determined by every page before it, so the moment an estimate somewhere earlier is corrected, **every page after it reflows — including the one under your eyes.** Computing it honestly instead means measuring the whole chapter before showing you page one, which is the cost shape this app exists to refuse (P1). Anchoring at posts makes a correction change the page NUMBER and nothing else. **What you will see and may not like:** a short journal entry occupies a page with white space below it, the way an entry does in a printed book of entries. **The alternative** (posts flow) is real work, not a flag: it needs either a full-book measurement pass at open, or accepting that pages re-flow under you as you read.

    **(c) The paginated bar KEEPS a scrubber — which is the opposite of what Book does.** *[RULED 2026-07-27: option (i) — scrubbers on both; Read's Pages keeps its own and Book regained one, BUILT in 13d batch 1.]* The brief §4 designed the slider for this view over the estimated page domain, and I built to it; 2B's brief said the opposite for Book and I built to that too (QUESTIONS #44(c)). So the two surfaces now show you both answers, which was worth more than guessing. **Your Outline complaint was about a horizontal control on a vertical page** — neither of these is that. Rule once, for both: (i) scrubbers on both horizontal surfaces; (ii) scrubbers on neither; (iii) as shipped.

    **(d) A window resize SCALES the paper; it never repaginates. (Brief Q4.)** The page is a fixed logical rectangle, so a wider window gives you bigger pages, not more text per page — and the page numbers never change under you. **The alternative** (repaginate to fill the window) makes "page 12 of 240" depend on how wide the window happens to be, which is exactly the thing a page number is supposed to be immune to. There is no font-size setting for the reading page today, so the "a font change repaginates" half of the brief's question has nothing to bite on yet; if that setting ever arrives it repaginates, which is honest.

    **(e) Read still OPENS in SCROLL, and PAGES is one click away.** A finished book opens into Read (your R2 ruling) and lands on the flow it has always landed on; the sub-mode is session state, not remembered. **The alternatives:** (i) open into PAGES — a bigger change than it sounds, because it silently changes what every finished book has opened into for two phases; (ii) remember the last face per book (needs somewhere to persist it — the same `lens`-flavoured question as #43(b)).

    **Also worth one look, though nobody asked:** a CALLOUT block renders on a page as an **indented passage** rather than a coloured frame. A page has no overlay layer by design (it is drawn by the same engine that computed the page break, which is what keeps the seams honest), and an indent is a presentation transform that changes no character. If you want the colour on the page, say so — it is a real piece of work, not a flag. **Status: ANSWERED — (b) RATIFIED and (c) RULED scrubbers-on-both at the 13c walk; (a), (d), (e) and the callout-as-indent note RATIFIED as shipped at the 13d sitting (2026-07-28).**

46. **2026-07-27 · Phase 13c (Batch 2D) · SMART BOOKS CONVERT NOW, and a book can be READ in an order it isn't saved in — the N38 memo's five rulings, answered provisionally so you can see them, plus two A31 calls.** Your S4-28.4 sentence is built: *"adding a chapter and curating a smart book a little should be allowed, but then you should be able to make a new 'smart book' off the same tag."* The 4F memo laid out three shapes and recommended **A (materialize-and-convert)**; I built A, whole, and each of its five rulings is one line to overturn. **Nothing here needed a schema change**, which is the main reason A was the right one to build in your absence — the other two shapes are not one-line reversals.

    **(1) THE CENTRAL CALL — a converted book STOPS collecting. (SHIPPED — shape A.)** When you convert, the posts it has collected become real membership rows, in the order you were looking at them, and the book is an ordinary hand-shaped book from that instant: chapters, drag order, a post cap, finish, restore — all of it, already built and already tested. New tag matches stop arriving. **The alternative you might have meant** (memo shape B, the true hybrid: it keeps collecting AND holds chapters) is genuinely bigger, and the memo named the knot honestly — every consumer would have to learn a union of derived matches and pinned rows (how do they interleave in the order? what does the capacity gate count? and the sharp one: a post you chaptered whose tag you then remove — does it stay because you chaptered it, or go because it no longer matches?). That last question has no answer that doesn't surprise someone, and it would need your adjudication case by case. If you want B, say so; it is a phase's worth of design, not a flag.

    **(2) The fresh collector is OFFERED, checked ON by default, and named "<book> (collecting)". (SHIPPED.)** The second half of your sentence. It carries the same filter, the same sectioning tag, and the same order the book had — and deliberately NOT its post cap, word target, or finished state (a collector born capped or born finished cannot collect, which is the one thing it exists to do). **Alternatives:** always mint it (no choice), or never (you make one by hand). Its name is editable in the offer.

    **(3) "+ new chapter" ON a smart book opens the offer, one confirm from converting. (SHIPPED — the memo's lean.)** Before this, chapters on a smart book were a dead end with an explanation. Now the door is rendered, and pressing it converts and then opens the chapter prompt — the act you asked for, finished. **The alternative:** leave "+ new chapter" absent there and put conversion only in the shelf menu (which also exists, as the deliberate door). **Worth knowing:** the memo expected THREE refusals to become offers, and only this one turned out to be a real dead end — adding a post to a smart book already had a better answer than converting (it tags the post so the filter collects it — your R98), and a smart book's order is now controllable like any book's (below). So the drag-a-post-onto-a-smart-book gesture is untouched.

    **(4) The order you were looking at is FROZEN into hand order. (SHIPPED — the memo's lean.)** At the moment of conversion nothing moves on screen: the book's sort flips to manual and the positions are written in the order displayed. **The alternative** (keep the stamp sort live after converting) is defensible but works against the point — you convert in order to shape by hand. Two smaller decisions ride with it: the post CAP is honored (a capped smart book materializes exactly the posts it was showing, so it is never born over its own cap), and your FILTER and grouping tag stay on the row — the filter as provenance, the grouping tag because it keeps working: your tag-derived sections survive the conversion and hand over to real chapters the moment you make one.

    **(5) There is NO curated → smart door. (SHIPPED — the memo's lean.)** Converting back would delete your hand order and your chapters into a derived list. If you ever want it, the honest road is a fresh smart book off the same tags — which is exactly what the offer in (2) makes. **But conversion IS undoable**: ⌥⌘Z puts the smart book back exactly (the same rows, the same sort), and ⇧⌥⌘Z re-applies it.

    **A31 — "see a book by its saved custom order or by an attribute like time… more like lenses where the order can be saved."** Two halves, and you should know which was which. **(a) The attribute half already existed and had no door.** A book has carried a sort field (manual / entry date / created / last touched / major revision, each way) since Phase 2 — and its only button lived in the Phase 10 sidebar, which Phase 13's Shelf·Library·Pool·Blocks rework retired. So the machinery has been sitting there unreachable ever since, which is a large part of why this reads as a missing feature. It now lives in the book's own navigator, as an `order ·` menu. **(b) The "saved like lenses" half is SHIPPED as the lens grammar, PROVISIONAL:** picking an order there is a way of LOOKING — view-local, writes nothing, and every other reader (export, the Fragments projection, another device) still sees the book's own order — and "Save as This Book's Order" is the adopt door. Pinned: browsing an order returns exactly what saving it would show, so the preview never lies. **The alternative, and it is the one I could not take:** SEVERAL named saved orders per book, genuinely like several lenses. That needs somewhere to store them — a new table or column — which is a SyncSpec §12 lockstep event and never a batch's call. Say the word and it gets designed properly. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-28, the 13d sitting): all seven RATIFIED as shipped — shape A stands; the named-saved-orders schema idea stays unbuilt.**

47. **2026-07-27 · Phase 13c (Batch 2E) · COMPARE CAN NOW SHOW ONLY THE BLOCKS THAT RELATE — four calls, and one of them retires a question you were already owed.** Your sentence (2026-07-22) was *"we should have a setting to only show related blocks - that would make the pane feel more usable."* It is built: a third chip beside "follow this post's order" and "lineage colors", and turning it on collapses every column to the blocks lineage actually connects across the posts — the unrelated stretches simply go. Nothing is moved and nothing is deleted; it is a way of LOOKING, one click back, and it lives only as long as the view does (like the other two).

    **(a) "Related" means the SAME lineage the rails already draw — transitive, not just one step. (SHIPPED — PROVISIONAL.)** A block counts as related if the app can walk a lineage path between it and a block in the first post, however many divides and merges deep (the same computation that decides where the coloured rails go and where the columns align). **The reason it is not the narrower "one step only":** the filter would then hide blocks that are visibly wearing a rail, and the surface would contradict itself in a single glance. **The alternative** is real if you want it: direct relationships only, so a copy-of-a-copy stops counting — but the rails would have to narrow with it, or the two would disagree.

    **(b) The post-level VERSION LINK does not make blocks related. (SHIPPED.)** Marking two posts "versions of each other" says *these posts are versions*; it never says which BLOCK corresponds to which. So a version-linked pair that shares no lineage filters down to nothing, and the column tells you so in as many words ("related blocks only — nothing from this post") rather than sitting blank under a header that says "versions". **The alternative:** treat a version link as making the whole post related — which makes the filter do nothing in exactly the case it exists for.

    **(c) It is OFF by default.** *[RULED 2026-07-27: default ON — BUILT in 13d batch 1; the filter's semantics, copy and OFF placement are untouched.]* A filter that HIDES material should be reached for, not arrived in: with it on by default, opening Compare against an unrelated post would land you on nothing but an explanation, and against a version twin it changes nothing at all (every block is kin). So on-by-default buys nothing where it helps and confuses where it doesn't. **One line to flip** if you'd rather it always started on.

    **(d) Turning it on while you are TYPING in a block it hides: the block goes, your text is saved, and it says so.** The live editing ring moves off (there is no pane left to show it), the text you typed is written to the store before anything disappears, and a line appears: *"Related blocks only" hid the block you were writing in — your text is saved. Turn it off to go back to it.* **The alternative I deliberately did not take:** refuse the toggle while you're mid-edit ("close the block you're writing in first") — a view toggle that stops working because you happen to be typing is exactly the friction this phase exists to remove, and it would refuse most often when you're deepest in a comparison.

    **AND — this retires a question you were owed (13b Batch 4C, judgment call 0).** You had an open call about where an UNRELATED column's material should sit when other columns are related: as shipped, it trails below every aligned row, so on a long post fully matched to another column that third column reads blank for a very long stretch. **The setting answers it** — with the filter on, an unrelated column contributes nothing at all and the question of where its run lands does not arise. So I changed NOTHING about the filter-OFF placement (it is byte-for-byte what shipped), on the reading that you asked for the setting rather than for a different placement. **If the OFF behaviour still bothers you in use**, the two alternatives from 4C are still one arm away each: flush the unrelated column at the first aligned row, or flush it once its remaining kin count reaches zero. **Status: ANSWERED — (c) RULED default-ON at the 13c walk (built 13d batch 1); (a), (d) and the filter-OFF placement RATIFIED at the 13d sitting (2026-07-28); (b) RATIFIED there too, after her clarifying question (the mark is post-level, one anchor edge, no block correspondences — answered on the record in the 13d plan's sitting section).**

## Phase 13d (2026-07-27/28) — the build batches' one-line calls, bundled for the sitting

48. **2026-07-27 · Phase 13d (batches 2–4) · Seven build-time calls, each one line to change, bundled so the walk reads them in one place.** Every one is either standing-behavior parity or an endorsed judgment call — nothing here blocks anything; the BINDING build records are the 13d plan's batch records + acceptances. **(a) The seam-merge speaks:** "Blocks joined — ⌥⌘Z undoes it." on every backspace-merge (batch 2). Endorsed as load-bearing (⌘Z cannot reverse a structural act; the line names the door that can) — but it fires on EVERY join; make it transient or drop it if it reads as noise. **(b) The board divide speaks the same way:** "Block divided — ⌥⌘Z undoes it." (batch 3, the native Takes/Lenses door). Same question. **(c) Divide with NO caret cuts near the block's start** (batch 3): snap-and-proceed settles offset 0 onto the nearest interior word edge — byte-parity with what the carry-to-Write door always did, surfaced now that the door is native. A refusal instead ("click first") is one line. **(d) ⌘⇧⏎ SUPERSEDED ⌥⌘N** as insert-block-below's shortcut (batch 4 — your A14 key was free, but the act already had a key, and the registry's one-shortcut-per-act honesty meant replace, not join; both-keys wants a registry alias concept — say the word if you want ⌥⌘N back). **(e) Spellcheck's two found-not-confirmed defaults** (batch 4): text completion is now forced OFF whenever checking is on (the TEXTKIT2 §3 crash pairing — the pins found completion defaulted ON everywhere), and take panes now follow the spellcheck setting where they previously inherited AppKit's autocorrect-ON silently — Write and the boards now agree, which CHANGES take-pane behavior at the default level. **(f) The paragraph-tall gap is ADDITIVE** (a full line on top of the standing gap, batch 4) — because an absolute one-line gap would be SMALLER than the shipped gap when inline comments are on. **(g) ⇧⏎'s early mint** (batch 4, A15-B2, ruled at the gate): pressing ⇧⏎ while typing fresh text at a post's end mints the block right then — the ratified idle-mint fired ~2 s early; you will see the block exist sooner than the clock would have made it. **Status: ANSWERED (2026-07-28, the 13d sitting): "Keep all seven" — everything stands as built.**

## Phase 13g (2026-08-01) — batch 3's build-time calls, bundled for the sitting

49. **2026-08-01 · Phase 13g (batch 3) · THE FORMATTING ACTS ARE BUILT — nine build-time calls, each one line to change, bundled so the walk reads them in one place.** Your rulings F-1…F-3 said what the vocabulary is (bold · italic · strikethrough · inline code · `#`/`##`/`###` · `- ` bullets · `- [ ]` checkboxes) and that storage is markdown-as-content. They did not say what ⌘B does to a selection that spans three paragraphs, or what happens when you bullet a line that is already a heading — those are the calls below. **Nothing here needed a schema change or an operation**: a formatting act is an ordinary edit through the ordinary typing path, which is why the whole thing is reversible with ⌘Z and refuses under a seal without a line of new refusal code. The BINDING build record is the 13g plan's batch-3 record.

    **(a) ⌘B on a selection that crosses lines wraps EACH LINE separately.** `**one**\n**two**`, not `**one\ntwo**`. **Why:** the tokenizer is line-scoped by law (batch 2's ruling — it is also what keeps markdown from ever disagreeing with block identity), so a single pair of markers around a multi-line selection would be *literal stars on screen* — visible junk, silently. **The alternative:** REFUSE a multi-line wrap and say why. That is more conservative and more annoying; per-line is what the format can actually express. One rule to flip.

    **(b) Setting a line lead REPLACES whatever lead the line had.** Bullet on `# Title` gives `- Title`, not `- # Title`. **Why:** the three line kinds are mutually exclusive to the tokenizer, so stacking them would make the `#` read as literal text inside a bullet. **The alternative:** refuse to change a heading into a list item.

    **(c) The toggle CLEARS only when EVERY affected line/piece already carries the act.** Select one bulleted line and one bare one, press Bullet List: both end up bulleted. Press it again: both clear. **The alternative:** flip each line independently (which never converges to a tidy list).

    **(d) A blank line inside a multi-line selection is left alone — but a LONE blank line is bulleted.** Bulleting across a paragraph gap doesn't put a `- ` in the gap; putting the caret on an empty line and pressing Bullet List starts the list, which is the everyday gesture.

    **(e) ⌘B with no selection inserts `****` and puts the caret between the markers** (the "turn bold on and type" gesture) rather than doing nothing. Note the consequence you will see: until you type something between them, `****` is literal text on screen — the tokenizer treats empty content as literal, which is batch 2's pinned decision and not something this batch changed.

    **(f) ⌘B and ⌘I are the only chords; the other five ship MENU-FIRST (Format ▸ …).** The collision sweep found ⌘B and ⌘I free, and they are the platform's own emphasis keys (their shifted neighbours ⌘⇧B block seams and ⌘⇧I context bar read as the heavier gesture on the same letter). For strikethrough, inline code, headings and the two list acts, no platform chord is standard enough to be obvious, and inventing one would owe a sweep no ruling asked for. **Say the word and any of them gets a key** — the helper (⌘/) already lists all seven, the keyless ones by their menu path.

    **(g) Clicking a checkbox's box toggles it, and the hit target is the box's middle two characters.** A click yields a boundary position, so accepting the box's outer edges would also catch clicks half a character outside it — i.e. it would steal a caret you aimed at the text. The trade is that the target is about two characters wide. **The alternative:** widen it to the whole `- [ ] ` lead (bigger target, and clicking just before the box no longer places a caret there).

    **(h) Format ▸ Checklist always makes an UNCHECKED box.** Checking is the click. (And a CHECKED line is still a checkbox to the act, so pressing Checklist on `- [x] milk` removes the box rather than unchecking it.)

    **(i) The Format menu is enabled in every note mode — Write, Takes, Lenses, Compare, Blocks and Canvas — and in the book/spread/pool editors.** Every one of them hosts a live editable surface, so greying the door there would be a lie. What it does NOT have is a ⌘K palette row: seven formatting rows in the command palette read as clutter beside "New Note" and "Merge Blocks". One line if you want them there.

    **Status: ANSWERED 2026-08-02 (the housekeeping-session walk): "49 all looks good" — all nine RATIFIED AS SHIPPED. Record: the 13e plan's walk section.**

50. **2026-08-01 · Phase 13g (batch 4) · LINKS AND READ'S SECOND FACE — six build-time calls, each one line to change.** Your ruling F-5 said links are in, that a writing surface opens them on ⌘-click, and that READ renders markdown fully formatted with markers hidden and a plain click opening a link. It did not say which ADDRESSES the app will follow, what a rendered bullet looks like, or what "Add Link" does to a selection that spans three paragraphs — those are the calls below. **Nothing here needed a schema change or an operation**, and nothing here touches `renderedText`, export, or the Fragments bridge: the stored text is untouched and Read simply draws it differently. The BINDING build record is the 13g plan's batch-4 record.

    **(a) A click follows `http` and `https` addresses ONLY; anything else is INERT.** It still LOOKS like a link (the syntax is what it is) and clicking it simply does nothing. **Why:** a link's target is ordinary text you typed, and the app must not hand an arbitrary scheme to the system on a click — `file:` reaches the disk and the custom-scheme space is every other app on the machine. **The alternative:** open anything the system can open, or open anything after a confirm. One line, and the allowlist is a set you can add to.

    **(b) A rendered bullet is `•`, and a rendered checkbox is `☐` / `☑`.** Read SUBSTITUTES the lead rather than merely hiding it — a bullet list that rendered without bullets would read as loose lines, which is not what "fully formatted" means. **The alternative:** hide the lead entirely, or keep the hyphen. And a checked box in Read is only a box: **Read never toggles one**, because Read has no editing gesture at all (your A43 ruling). Ticking happens where you write.

    **(c) "Add Link" acts on ONE line — the line the selection STARTS on.** The emphasis acts wrap each line of a multi-line selection (that was #49(a), and `**one**` / `**two**` are both real), but three EMPTY links from one gesture are junk and only one of them could hold the cursor the act exists to place. **The alternative:** one link per line, or refuse a multi-line selection and say why.

    **(d) "Add Link" on text that is ALREADY a link REMOVES the link, address and all.** It is the same toggle rule as every other formatting act (#49(c)) — and ⌘Z puts it back, exactly like unbolding. **The alternative:** refuse, or jump the cursor into the existing address to edit it. Worth knowing because the act is named "Add".

    **(e) Add Link ships MENU-FIRST, with no chord.** The platform's own link chord is ⌘K, which this app has bound to Find Anything since Phase 13 (R15) — the one key a reader would guess is the one key that is taken, and re-pointing ⌘K at a text act would break the most-used door in the app. No second candidate is standard enough to be obvious. **Say the word and it gets one.**

    **(f) ⌘-click opens a link on EVERY text surface, including a read pane and a sealed note.** Opening a link is not an edit, so editability is beside the point; what mattered was that a plain click is already spoken for (it places the cursor, and in the read tier it seeds the cursor for the editor). **A consequence worth seeing on the page:** a link's `(address)` is part of the link to a ⌘-click, not just its words — the dimmed address is visibly part of the thing, and unlike the checkbox click this gesture carries a modifier and competes with nothing.

    **Status: ANSWERED 2026-08-02: "i think 50 also all seems fine for now" — all six RATIFIED AS SHIPPED (her note on (a): copy-paste generally carries the https anyway). Record: the 13e plan's walk section.**

51. **2026-08-01 · Phase 13g (batch 6) · YOUR MORNING RULING SET IS BUILT — five build-time calls, each one line to change.** Your round-5 rulings said WHAT: comments on the left with the right-hand "+" gone, "block 1/16" instead of the raw ID, an Apple-Notes-shaped Library list, inline title editing, a takes projection for the un-live pane, and a modes switcher in the note-only window. They did not say what a note-card row does with a note that is in three books, or what happens to a title you were typing when you click away — those are the calls below. **Nothing here needed a schema change or an operation.** The BINDING build record is the 13g plan's batch-6 record.

    **(a) The retired "+" is not replaced.** Your words were *"stop showing the comment + to the right completely"*, so the margin's add-a-comment chip (13b 4D's N35) is gone rather than moved to the left with the cards. Leaving a comment is still ⌘⇧' , the bottom bar's "comments" button, or Block ▸ Comments — the three doors N35 was built beside. **The alternative:** the same chip, in the LEFT margin now that the take icon no longer contends for the space. One line.

    **(b) A comment card's rule stays on the card's LEFT edge.** In the right margin the rule sat between the card and the page; on the left it now sits on the card's outer edge instead of the one facing your text. Classic marginalia points AT the line it annotates, so the other edge is arguably righter — but that is a look call and you have not made it. **The alternative:** flip the rule to the card's inner edge.

    **(c) A note card names its CURATED books only.** A smart book's membership is derived by walking its filter, which is what the membership chip does for ONE note and must not be done for forty rows at once (P1). So a note that only matches a smart book shows no book on its row. **The alternative:** show smart membership too, and pay for it in the list.

    **(d) A note card's snippet is the first block that HAS text, looking at most three blocks in.** A note that opens with a blank line shows the next block's words; a note whose first words are four blocks down shows nothing rather than being scanned for them (a snippet must cost the same on a 10,000-block note as on a three-block one). **The alternative:** a bigger window, or scan until text is found.

    **(e) Clicking away from a title you are editing SAVES it; ⎋ cancels.** The little rename window is gone from that door, so there is no "Rename" button to press — ⏎ and losing focus both commit, because an inline field with a hidden commit key would eat what you typed. **The alternative:** clicking away cancels (safer against stray edits, worse against lost ones).

    **Status: ANSWERED — (a)(b)(d)(e) ratified as shipped 2026-08-02 ("51 seems fine to me"); (c) RULED at the 13k kickoff, same session: SHOW smart books on cards — "the number of specifically defined smart books you have is probably small, no? you still have to choose to deliberately create a smart book." Bounded evaluation, measurement-first (P1). BUILD: Phase 13k row K4. Record: the 13e plan's walk section + the 13k plan's ledger.**

52. **2026-08-01 · Phase 13g (batch 7) · YOUR ROUND-6 NOD SET IS BUILT — seven build-time calls, each one line to change.** Your rulings R6-1…R6-4 said tag cards on the top bar, the bottom bar's two slide-out groups, the untitled-date display rule *"preserved on export"*, and quicknotes auto-tagged into a smart book. They did not say what happens to a note you deliberately NAMED "Untitled", or which day an undated note shows, or whether a quick note still sits in the Pool — those are the calls below. **Nothing here needed a schema change**, and nothing touches `renderedText`, the export BODY projections or the bridge's join brain: a title has never been body content. The BINDING build record is the 13g plan's batch-7 record.

    **(a) The literal word "Untitled" counts as NO TITLE — which is what fixes the notes already in your library.** Until this batch ⌘N and File ▸ New Note actually STORED the string "Untitled" as a real title, so your "500 untitled things" are titled, not nameless: a rule keyed only on blank titles would have fixed none of them. The mint now stores nothing at all, and the display rule treats the literal as absent so the existing ones show their dates too. **The consequence:** a note you deliberately named "Untitled" renders its date instead. **The alternative:** only blank titles count, plus a one-time sweep that REWRITES those rows — which writes to every one of your notes to fix a display problem.

    **(b) An undated note shows the day it was WRITTEN, in your calendar.** The chain is: your title, else the note's `entry_date` if it has one, else the local calendar day of `created_at`, else "Untitled" (still reachable, just rare). Local, not UTC, because `entry_date` beside it has always been a local calendar day — a UTC slice would read a day LATER for anything written after early evening. **The alternative:** a prettier format ("August 1, 2026") instead of `2026-08-01`. It is one line; `2026-08-01` was chosen because it matches what the journal, the export filenames and the date picker already say.

    **(c) EXPORT says the same thing — filenames already did.** Your refinement is built: a reading export heads an untitled note with its date, not with "Untitled". Worth knowing, because it means the ratified 11b convention already agreed with you: a bulk export has named untitled notes by their date alone since 11b (`2026-07-11.md`), and the only thing that changed is that notes carrying the literal "Untitled" now join them instead of exporting as `2026-07-11 Untitled.md`. **A POOL with no name is still "Untitled"** — a pool has no date, and your ruling was about notes.

    **(d) The tag cards FOLD with the rest of the top bar.** Collapsed (which is the default since your WT-2 ruling) the cards become one `＃` door with the count — the bar's own grammar, where every half keeps a glyph so folding is never a one-way door. **The alternative:** the cards stay visible even when the bar is folded, since they are the thing you asked to see. One line.

    **(e) The chips show the FULL PATH (`life/goals/2024`), truncated if long.** Hierarchy stays surfaced (R45), and the colour is keyed on the whole path — so `life/goals` and `work/goals` are different cards in different colours. **The alternative:** show the leaf only (`2024`) and put the path in the tooltip; shorter cards, less obvious nesting.

    **(f) The block ▾ items no longer DIM by the planner's verdict.** On the flat row, Divide/Extract/Make Block/Merge faded when they could not act; a menu row cannot carry that, so they are plain rows that FIRE and speak their refusal — which is exactly what the Takes bar's own block menu has done since 13b. Nothing lost an act; every door is still declared and the registry walk proves it. **The alternative:** disable them instead, which trades a spoken reason for a grey word.

    **(g) A quick note is now in QUICKNOTES and NOT in the Pool.** Your R6-4 gave every quick note the `quicknote` tag and gave that tag a smart book — and the Pool has excluded smart-collected notes since the 13-B review (promoting tags to a book must DRAIN the pool). So the pad's note lands on the shelf instead of in the Pool, and its status line says so. It is still bookless in the *curated* sense — no membership row exists — so removing the tag puts it straight back in the Pool. **The alternative:** exempt the Quicknotes book from the Pool's drain, so quick notes sit in both. One line.

    **Status: ANSWERED IN FULL (2026-08-02, completed at the 13k kickoff): (a) OVERTURNED — the literal-"Untitled" clause retires, only a BLANK title counts, NO sweep ("i can delete them or rename them myself"), the ⌘N nil-mint stays — 13k K1; (b) RATIFIED ("i think 2026-08-01 is fine, easier to scan than august 1, 2026"); (c) RULED — nameless pools SHOW their created day ("i think that would be helpful") — 13k K2; (d) FLIPPED — tag cards DEFAULT VISIBLE, folding a deliberate choice ("tag cards should fold into the bar if you deliberately choose that but it should default to visible") — 13k K12, reconciled with the bar redesign K11; (e) FLIPPED — chips show LEAF ONLY — 13k K3; (f) RATIFIED ("block menu rows fire and speak is good i think"); (g) RATIFIED ("a quicknote should be in quicknotes, it goes to the pool if you delete that tag"). Record: the 13e plan's walk section + the 13k plan's ledger.**

## Phase 13k (2026-08-02) — batch 1's build-time calls, bundled for the sitting

53. **2026-08-02 · Phase 13k (batch 1) · THE FULLY-RULED SET IS BUILT — twelve build-time calls, each one line to change, bundled the way #49–#52 were.** Your rulings K1–K6 said WHAT: the literal "Untitled" is a title again, nameless pools show their created day, chips show the leaf, ⏎ continues a list, and the right-click gets a Callout door. They did not say what ⏎ does in the MIDDLE of a list item, or whether a take card's right-click should carry the callout too — those are the calls below. **Nothing here needed a schema change or an operation**, and nothing touches `renderedText`, the export BODY projections or the bridge's join brain. The BINDING build record is the 13k plan's batch-1 record.

    **(a) ⏎ continues a list ONLY when the caret is at the line's END.** Your ruling's letter ("⏎ at the end of a texted list line"). So a ⏎ in the *middle* of `- milk and bread` splits the item and the second half gets no bullet. **The alternative:** continue wherever the caret is inside the item, which is what Notes and Bear do — one comparison to widen.

    **(b) ⇧⏎ never continues a list.** It is A15's own gesture (a newline that always lands inside a real block), it is the one key AppKit cannot tell apart on its own, and a soft break inside an item is exactly where a second bullet would be wrong. **The alternative:** treat it like ⏎.

    **(c) HEADINGS do not continue.** Your ruling names the two list leads (`- ` and `- [ ]`); a second `# ` under every heading is nobody's intent. **The alternative:** continue headings too.

    **(d) A continued checklist item is born UNCHECKED**, even from a checked one — the same rule Format ▸ Checklist follows (#49(h): checking is the click).

    **(e) The exit deletes exactly the LEAD.** ⏎ on `- ` empties the line; ⏎ on `- [ ]   ` (spaces you typed past the box) leaves those spaces behind, because they are text and only the lead is the list. **The alternative:** clear the whole line.

    **(f) ⏎ continues lists in a TAKE PANE as well as in Write.** A list should read the same wherever you are typing (R118 — Write is never privileged over Takes). A pane has no structural semantics at all, so this adds nothing but the lead. **The alternative:** Write only.

    **(g) The right-click Callout door rides BOTH the Write editor and a take card.** Your K6 said "the editor context menu"; the frame is the BLOCK's and takes share it (R16/R27), so the card's menu is a legitimate place for it and 13c R2-2's whole lesson was that Write was privileged only by where a door was first hung. **Compare's panes are untouched** — they carry no Variorum context menu at all today, only Format. **The alternative:** Write's right-click only.

    **(h) A card's callout acts on the block you RIGHT-CLICKED**, not on the block that last held the keyboard. A right-click does not move the keyboard, so the routed door's "the block you're in" would have named the wrong card. Worth knowing because the menu-bar door still means the focused block, and on a board those can now differ.

    **(i) The `＋` popover's suggestions still show FULL paths.** Chips narrowed to the leaf (your ruling), but the adder is a chooser over the taxonomy — picking between `life/goals` and `work/goals` needs both words. Hierarchy also stays surfaced in the chip's tooltip and in Settings' manager. **The alternative:** leaves there too.

    **(j) A nameless pool's EXPORT filename follows the same rule** — `Pools/2026-07-21.md` rather than `Pools/Untitled.md`. The display brain lives in core precisely so a downloaded folder says what the app said (your 13g refinement, applied to the surface #52(c) had left out).

    **(k) K1 changes ONE export-facing thing, stated rather than smuggled:** a note whose stored title IS the literal "Untitled" exports as `<date> Untitled.md` again, because that string is a name now. Nameless notes are unaffected — they have been named by their date alone since 11b. **And no sweep runs anywhere**, per your ruling.

    **(l) A note card lists CURATED books first, then the smart ones that collect it — and a name in both halves shows once.** Curated first because a membership row is a decision you made and a smart match is one the filter made; each half is alphabetical within itself. Book names have no uniqueness, so "Ideas" curated + "Ideas" smart is reachable — a row reading "Ideas, Ideas" seemed worse than the rare ambiguity. **The alternative:** one merged alphabetical sort, or show both.

    **Status: ANSWERED 2026-08-02 (the closeout sitting): "the rest of 53 i approve" — (a)–(k) RATIFIED AS SHIPPED; (l) RATIFIED "fine for now," with her pointer honored: the deeper what-is-a-book questions (the hybrid modified-smart-book identity her message described) are PHASE 13i's — confirmed as the phase she was thinking of. Her list-feel note recorded: "i'll have to play with how the lists feel but i like the idea of leaving it easily and it's markdown anyway so not as annoying to add to as apple notes." Record: the 13k plan's round 5.**

54. **2026-08-02 · Phase 13k (batch 2) · THE FORMATTING SET IS BUILT — seven build-time calls, each one line to change, bundled the way #49–#53 were.** Your rulings K7–K10 said WHAT: the formatting toolbar goes in the bottom bar, callout frames render in Take mode, inline code gets its own tint, and Tab indents a list. They did not say whether a take card's frame should wrap each card or the whole fan (you ruled that one at the sitting), nor what ⏎ does on an empty item that is three levels deep — those are the calls below. **Nothing here needed a schema change or an operation**, and nothing touches `renderedText`, the export BODY projections or the bridge's join brain. The BINDING build record is the 13k plan's batch-2 record.

    *(One thing that is NOT a call, stated here because you will SEE it: the nested-list widening means a line in an EXISTING note that already begins with spaces and a hyphen now renders as a list item where it used to render as prose. Zero stored bytes changed anywhere — it is what the app draws, not what it saved — and it is your ruling's intent. Your handoff says it too.)* **— SEEN AND RATIFIED by the author the same night (2026-08-02, back at the machine): "i'm kind of confused by what you're talking about with spaces before a - ? like a single indented list item? I think that's fine realistically." Her reading is exactly right, and the consequence is ratified; it no longer needs the handoff's flag.**

    **(a) The toolbar carries EIGHT acts, not seven.** The ledger said "the seven formatting acts"; that count predates Add Link, which shipped in 13g batch 4. The bar renders the same eight the Format menu does, read from the one table both use — so rendering seven would have meant a second list that could drift. **The alternative:** drop any of them from the bar (it stays in the menu). One line.
    
    **(b) The take-mode frame renders in TAKE MODE only.** Lenses and Compare are untouched: their columns have no single "takes region" (Compare spans several notes at once), so a frame there would need a geometry nobody has ruled. Their callout DOORS are unchanged and still work — setting a frame from Lenses shows up in Write, Takes and Read. **The alternative:** decide what a frame means in a multi-column view, which is a design question rather than a line.

    **(c) The code chip covers the BACKTICKS too, not just the words between them.** They are part of what you typed, and a tint that stopped short of them would leave two dimmed ticks floating outside their own chip. In READ the markers are hidden, so there the chip is exactly the words. **The alternative:** tint only the content on the writing surfaces, and live with the ticks outside it.

    **(d) The reading page has its OWN code tint, not the voice's.** Read's paper and serif are deliberately voice-independent (only the room around the page follows the voice), so its chip is the paper's own warm neutral rather than terminal's or study's. **The alternative:** let the page's chip follow the voice, which would make Read's page slightly less its own thing.

    **(e) ⏎ on an empty NESTED list item OUTDENTS it; only at the outermost level does it leave the list.** Your K5 ruling — "⏎ on an empty list item removes the lead and exits" — was made before nesting existed, and at the outermost level it is byte-identical to what you ruled. One level in, pressing ⏎ on the empty item moves it out one level and keeps the bullet, so "finish this sub-list and carry on with the parent" is two presses. It is what Notes, Bear, Obsidian and Notion all do. **The alternative:** the literal reading — one ⏎ leaves the list from any depth, dropping straight to plain text at the margin.

    **(f) Read draws six levels of nesting at most, with the same bullet at every level.** The stored text is never clamped — nest twenty deep and the text says twenty — but a page is a fixed width, so what Read DRAWS stops stepping in at six. And a nested bullet is still `•`, not a different glyph per level: the editing surface shows `- ` at every level, and giving Read a second hierarchy language would make the two disagree. **The alternative:** alternating glyphs (`•` then `◦` then `▪`), the way an HTML list renders.

    **(g) Turning a nested bullet into a nested checklist KEEPS its nesting; clearing the list lead removes it.** So Format ▸ Checklist on an indented bullet gives an indented checkbox, while pressing Bullet List again on that same line gives you plain text at the margin — because the line stops being a list at all, and a paragraph left indented by two orphan spaces is not what "clear" means. **The alternative:** keep the indent through the clear too.

    **Status: ANSWERED 2026-08-02 (the closeout sitting) EXCEPT (d): "the rest of 54 seems fine" — (a)(b)(c)(e)(f) RATIFIED AS SHIPPED; (g) RATIFIED in her words ("i think it should keep the nesting") — the gesture is caret-on-the-indented-line + Format ▸ Checklist (or the bottom-bar button), which does exactly that. (d) HELD on her question ("what does this mean… Read's page has its own warm-neutral chip?") — explained: Read's paper page is deliberately voice-independent, so its code chip is the paper's own warm gray rather than the terminal/study tint; her flick pending. Record: the 13k plan's round 5.**

55. **2026-08-02 · Phase 13k (batch 3) · THE BAR IS REBUILT — ten build-time calls, each one line to change, bundled the way #49–#54 were.** Your rulings K11 and K12 said WHAT: the context bar is always the compact icon row (← · book · ＃ · ›), the › is a real forward door, "in xyz book" becomes the book icon, and the tag cards show by default with ＃ as their deliberate fold. They did not say what ⌘⇧I should do once the bar has no fold left, or where the cards sit relative to the four icons — those are the calls below. **Nothing here needed a schema change or an operation**, nothing touches `renderedText`, the export BODY projections or the bridge's join brain, and the NAVIGATION under the bar is 13f's, byte-untouched (the history arithmetic, ⌘[ / ⌘], the W21 ascent). The BINDING build record is the 13k plan's batch-3 record.

    **(a) ⌘⇧I now folds the TAG CARDS.** It used to fold the whole bar, and your ruling retired that fold — so rather than leave a chord with nothing to do, it became the ＃ icon's keyboard twin (same key, same always-available rule, renamed "Toggle Tag Cards" in the menu and ⌘K). **The alternative:** retire ⌘⇧I entirely, which is one fewer chord and one fewer thing to remember.

    **(b) The four-door row is a NOTE's row; a place wears two.** At the shelf, the library, inside a book or on the recovery surface there is no note, so there is no book icon and no ＃ — just ← and ›. Greying them there instead would render doors that could never act in that context (the standing P-2 rule). Inside a note the row never changes: a note in no books still HAS a book icon, greyed, exactly as you ruled. **The alternative:** four doors everywhere, two of them permanently dead at a place.

    **(c) The cards ride AFTER the four icons, not between ＃ and ›.** Chips vary in number and width, so putting them inside the cluster would move the › every time you add a tag — the same growing-and-shrinking the ruling is about, one layer down. This way the icons are a fixed cluster you can aim at. **The alternative:** cards between ＃ and ›, next to their own door.

    **(d) ⌘-click on a note in ONE book opens the list anyway** (a one-row dropdown), rather than behaving like a plain click. Your words gave ⌘-click the list; with one book the list has one row. **The alternative:** ⌘-click does nothing special when there is only one book.

    **(e) The book dropdown is navigation only.** It lists the books and opens one at this note. Add-to-book, remove, and diverge stay on the masthead's membership chip, which is where they have always lived. **The alternative:** make the bar's dropdown the full chip, and have two of them.

    **(f) The fold is a THIS-MAC preference, so it is shared and it sticks.** Every window's bar and every note read one stored choice, and it survives quitting — a deliberate choice that forgets itself is not a choice. It is stored where the hover handles and the paragraph gap are (local `UserDefaults`), never in the schema and never synced, so it does not travel to the phone. **The alternatives:** per-note, per-window, or a synced setting that follows you to every device.

    **(g) A door with nowhere to go is visible and greyed, with a tooltip that says why** — "Nothing to go back to — this is where you started", in the menu's own words. **The alternative:** hide it, which is what the bar used to do and what made the row change shape.

    **(h) The glyphs are your screenshot's own:** "←" and "›" as characters, the system book and ＃ symbols, all four plain — no capsule outlines (the old back chip and book chip both wore one). A look call, and captures of every state are in the batch record for your flick. **The alternative:** any of it, restyled.

    **(i) The BOOK ASCENT is still not a declared capability.** Back, forward and the tag fold are now declared doors on the bar (so the registry walk covers them); opening a book at a note has never been declared — not here and not on the membership chip that has carried the same act since Phase 13. Declaring it would give it a formal availability rule and a written refusal. **The alternative:** declare it, which is a small addition and a slightly larger register.

    **(j) The ＃ is never grey**, even on a note with no tags — folding an empty card row is where the `＋` lives, so greying it would hide the only way to add a first tag. **The alternative:** grey it until the note has a tag, and reach the `＋` some other way.

    **Status: ANSWERED 2026-08-02 (the closeout sitting): "55: i approve all of these" — (a)–(j) RATIFIED AS SHIPPED, including (i)'s undeclared ascent (the declare-it alternative stays one small line, 13e's if ever wanted). Her two clarifying questions answered on the record: the "book ascent not registry-declared" line means the open-the-book-at-this-note act was never entered in the internal capability catalog (it works; it just lacks a formal availability rule + refusal entry and the registry walk's coverage); the "dropdown's two doors" hand-check = plain click and ⌘-click on the book icon, whose modifier is read at the AppKit last inch tests cannot pin. Record: the 13k plan's round 5.**

## Phase 13e (2026-08-17) — batch 1's build-time calls, bundled for the sitting

56. **2026-08-17 · Phase 13e (batch 1 — the door set) · FIFTEEN build-time calls, each one line to change, bundled the way #49–#55 were.** Your kickoff-sitting rulings said WHAT: duplicate book names REFUSE at the door (round 3), callouts get colour swatches and a real emoji picker (E1/A13(b)), and the book ascent enters the capability registry (E11). They did not say whether a FINISHED book's name counts as taken, or what the emoji picker should be, or whether the colour rows keep their words — those are the calls below. **Nothing here needed a schema change, an operation-level invariant or a SyncSpec §7 repair**, and nothing touches `renderedText`, the export BODY projections or the bridge's join brain. The BINDING build record is the 13e plan's batch-1 record.

    **(a) A FINISHED (locked) book's name IS taken.** Your ruling's words were *"archived/deleted books don't count"* — and books have no archive, only a finished flag, which is not deletion: a finished book sits on the shelf, in ⌘K and on every note card, so a second book wearing its name is exactly the confusion the ruling is about. **The alternative:** exempt finished books, so a name is free once the book is done. One line.

    **(b) A DELETED book frees its name immediately** — the other half of the same words. Consequence, stated rather than smuggled: RESTORING that book (`restoreReading`) can re-create a duplicate pair, because nothing checks names at restore time. That is the offline-race tolerance your ruling already accepts, and adding a name check to a RECOVERY door would mean refusing to give you your book back. **The alternative:** count deleted books too (a name is never released).

    **(c) A second UNNAMED book refuses.** The shelf's New Book alert substitutes "Untitled" (or "Untitled Journal") for a blank field, and since 13k K1 that string is a real name — so the second blank-field create now says *"A book named 'Untitled' already exists"*. **The alternatives:** exempt the placeholder names so unnamed books stack, or make the Create buttons refuse an empty field outright (which is a different door change).

    **(d) A book may always be renamed to its own name, casing included** — "Orbit" → "ORBIT" goes through, because a book cannot collide with itself and re-capitalising is a rename you are entitled to make.

    **(e) The name match is ends-trimmed and case-folded, and NOTHING else.** `"my  book"` (two spaces) and `"my book"` are two names, and so are "Élan" and "Elan". Your ruling said case-insensitive; every wider fold would refuse a name you are entitled to use. **The alternative:** collapse internal spacing and/or fold diacritics too.
