    **(f) The conversion offer's TYPED fresh-collector name is guarded; the DERIVED one is not.** Type "Ideas" into "keep a fresh smart book collecting this filter" and it refuses; leave it blank and the op's own "<book> (collecting)" default goes through even if that name is somehow taken — a name the app computed is not *purposefully* naming two books the same thing (your ruling's own words). **The alternative:** guard the derived name too, and have the conversion refuse over a name you never typed.

    **(g) A refused collector name refuses the WHOLE conversion** — the smart book stays smart, nothing is minted, nothing half-done. **The alternative:** convert anyway and skip the fresh collector (which would silently drop the thing you asked for).

    **(h) The guard lives at the DOORS, never in the operations.** `createReading` and `renameReading` still accept duplicate names by design: the tutorial/mobile seeders, the daily journal book's own create-and-pin (which AppSpec §9 promises *never throws*) and the sync apply path all mint through them, and a name refusal in any of those would break something that must not break. Your "not a schema rule" reading, taken literally and one layer up.

    **(i) The five Mac doors are guarded this batch; the PHONE's two are not.** Guarded: the shelf's New Book (all three buttons) and Rename, the book navigator's Rename, Settings ▸ Promote to Book, and the conversion offer's collector field. The phone's create-book and rename-book doors call the same core rule but were left alone — 13e is a Mac phase, and the shared brain sits in core precisely so the phone's two call sites are one line each. It rides the mobile parity ledger. **The alternative:** wire the phone now (two lines, out of this batch's scope).

    **(j) A colour row keeps its WORD beside the swatch** — "🟦 Blue", never a bare chip. A swatch-only row would be the one control in the app that cannot be read aloud (the R93 legibility mandate), and the words are what the K6 drift pin compares across the three doors. **The alternative:** chips alone, or chips with the word only in the tooltip.

    **(k) "Remove Callout" carries no swatch** — it takes a frame away rather than choosing a colour. Toggle and Emoji likewise. **The alternative:** give Remove an empty/hollow chip.

    **(l) The swatch renders per APPEARANCE (light/dark), not per voice.** The callout palette has never had a voice dimension — one wash per token per appearance, shared by Write's frame and Take mode's — so a swatch shows exactly the colour the block will wear. **The alternative:** nothing to change here unless the palette itself gains voice-specific washes, which is a design question.

    **(m) The emoji picker is the SYSTEM one** — the accessory row now reads `[ field ] [ Emoji & Symbols… ]`, and the button opens macOS's own character palette pointed at the field; typing or pasting a glyph still works exactly as before. It is a button in the accessory rather than a third alert button because an alert button would dismiss the prompt the picked glyph is meant to land in. **A HAND-CHECK, not a call:** whether a glyph clicked in that system panel lands in the prompt's field is the one inch no test can drive — if it does not, the field still accepts typing and paste (today's behaviour), and the fix is to move the prompt out of the modal alert. **The alternative:** build our own grid of curated emoji, which is a design fork I did not take.

    **(n) The menu-bar (Block ▸ Callout) swatches ride SwiftUI, so they are a hand-check too.** Write's right-click and a take card's right-click are real `NSMenu`s and their chips are pinned by test; the menu bar's rows are SwiftUI labels, where an image can be tinted by the framework rather than drawn as-is. It is asked for correctly (a non-template image, `.renderingMode(.original)`); if the menu bar's chips look grey while the right-click's look coloured, that is this, and the row's word is unaffected either way.

    **(o) The ascent is declared BOOK-scoped, keyless, and exempt from the Takes-parity table.** It joins `.openPeerPost` as a NAVIGATION act rather than an editing one — nothing is edited, so no surface can be "behind Write" — and literally so: its two doors are the context bar (above the pane) and the membership chip (in the masthead), both of which render over every mode. No chord, because your ruling named none and an invented one would owe a collision sweep nobody asked for. **Nothing you can see changed** (#55(i)'s letter): the bar's book icon is still grey-with-a-tooltip on a bookless note, and it now reads that greyness FROM the registry instead of deciding it locally.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13e (2026-08-17) — batch 2's build-time calls, bundled for the sitting

57. **2026-08-17 · Phase 13e (batch 2 — the fan's hands) · FIFTEEN build-time calls, each one line to change.** Your A19 said *"Reorder takes"* and your A18 said *"⌘-click in Takes to select blocks to merge; merge several when they have no takes"* — they did not say what happens at the ends of a fan, whether a locked take may be moved, or what a fanned selection should do. Those are the calls below. **Nothing here needed a schema change or new merge semantics**: `fan_position` was already a jittered fractional-index key (SyncSpec §8) and merge is AppSpec §9's, untouched. The BINDING build record is the 13e plan's batch-2 record.

    **(a) A LOCKED take and an ARCHIVED take can both be reordered.** The lock freezes the BUFFER, not identity metadata — the ratified `setTakeLabel` precedent — and an archived take's place in the ordering is where it comes back to. **The alternative:** refuse either (one guard line each).

    **(b) Stepping happens within the ACTIVE fan — the takes the cards show.** An archived take sitting between two active ones is neither stepped onto nor stepped over: it keeps its own key, so the move lands the take beside the card you were looking at. **The alternative:** step over the whole live fan, archived takes included, which would make one press appear to do nothing.

    **(c) Reordering the fan does NOT touch the note or accumulate churn.** Block `reorder` stamps neither, and AppSpec §9's touch-list names divide-W, merge and poolDragCopy — not reordering. Rearranging takes is not working on the prose, and it must never reshuffle the library's meaningful sort. **The alternative:** stamp `last_touched_at` (the divide-W/merge posture).

    **(d) The arrows are KEYLESS.** Your ruling named no chord, and an invented one would owe a collision sweep nobody asked for (the `.toggleStar` precedent). They are `.surface` doors — the target is the CARD you point at. **The alternative:** name a chord and I'll declare it in the key table.

    **(e) The glyphs follow the strip's direction, and the end arrow greys rather than vanishing** — ◀ ▶ in carousel / slot split / the rail, ▲ ▼ in the stack, and the first card's "earlier" is disabled (the pager's disabled-◀ grammar). A one-take fan draws no arrows at all (P-2). **The alternative:** hide the unusable arrow, which makes the card's furniture jump as takes move.

    **(f) Moving a take relative to ITSELF writes nothing** — no row, no sync row, no undo entry (the extent-setter no-op convention).

    **(g) E15 — the board defers on a DIRTY BUFFER only; a structural flight is deliberately not folded in.** Write's editor also defers during a flight because its controller has a flight-exit settle hook; the board has none, and its native divide relies on ordinary adoption to show the successors. Adding the flight arm without the hook would leave the successors unrendered until the next delivery. **The alternative:** build the board a flight-exit hook (a real change, not a line).

    **(h) E15 — the echo check is STRICT: any live buffer means "refetch".** With no buffer the board's display IS the projection, so a byte-identical delivery adopts on the spot; with a buffer in hand nothing cheap can tell a stale photograph from a newer one, so the fresh-read path decides. Cost: at most one extra read per delivery inside the settle window (exactly what both editors pay at the same moment). A LAX check would freeze the display; a strict one only ever costs a read.

    **(i) ⌘-click on a LINK still follows the link.** 13g's F-5/L10 gave ⌘-click that meaning on every pane, and a shipped ruling outranks a new gesture on the same chord; off a link — which is the whole of an ordinary block — the chord was unclaimed. **The alternative:** make ⌘-click select blocks even over a link, and reach links another way.

    **(j) ⌘-click CONSUMES the click: the live ring does not move to the block you pick.** Picking blocks is not writing in one, and a claimed chord that also moved the caret would be two gestures on one press. **The alternative:** focus the pane as well.

    **(k) A fanned PAIR carries into Write, where the merge ceremony lives.** Your ruled threshold (S4-23(4) — any fan on either side opens the ceremony) is untouched by this door, and the ceremony is mounted on Write's surface; so the pair travels there, visibly, exactly as the Takes merge door has always done. The quick-vs-ceremony DEFAULT flip is 13j's row 4b, deliberately not this batch's. **The alternative:** make the ceremony mountable on the board (a real extraction, 13j's orbit anyway).

    **(l) A fanned run of THREE OR MORE merges right here and SPEAKS about the folded-away takes.** Write's own multi-block merge has never consulted fan arity — it chains and says what happened to the other takes — so refusing in Takes what Write allows would put Takes behind Write (R118). Nothing vanishes silently: the status line names the count and the road back. **The alternative:** refuse fanned multi-selections and say "one pair at a time".

    **(m) Adjacency and fan arity are read from the STORE, and a gapped selection refuses BY NAME before anything is minted.** The takes projection is frozen while a buffer is dirty, so a projection-read order could carry the wrong pair into Write. The refusal keeps your picks (nothing to re-click) and mints nothing.

    **(n) The selection is session-only and prunes itself.** It dies with the board (like fan expansion), drops blocks the note no longer holds (a merge's own sources, a delete, a sync arrival), and the banner renders only while something is picked — at rest the page is exactly as calm as before. "clear" is inline chrome rather than a declared act (the pager's ◀ ▶ precedent).

    **(o) ⌘-click picks blocks on the TAKES board's article cells only.** Not on Lenses (its columns are a different question), and not on a slot-split bloom, where the fan cards REPLACE the article cell — close the fan and the block is pickable again. **The alternative:** put the gesture on the row's whole width, which would mean claiming ⌘-click on the fan cards too.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13e (2026-08-17) — batch 3's build-time calls, bundled for the sitting

58. **2026-08-17 · Phase 13e (batch 3 — take ↔ block structure) · TEN build-time calls, each one line to change.** Your A20 said *"shortcut for moving a take into being a new block beneath the current block"* and your A21 said *"…or turning all takes into their own blocks"* — which the A45 finding reframed as the repair for *"my current takes should've been blocks."* Both are built. The one architectural fork they hid — whether the take's ROW moves to the new block or the block gets a fresh copy — was traced and ruled by the architect (ruling **AR-1**, in the 13e plan): **the new block gets a copy, and the original take is ARCHIVED on its own block.** The rejected road (moving the row) would have needed a brand-new sync repair rule to be safe, and would have made a block she could never fix if two devices moved different takes out of it offline. Everything below is a call inside that ruling; the BINDING build record is the 13e plan's batch-3 record.

    **(a) The original take is ARCHIVED, not deleted** (AR-1). Nothing is lost: it keeps its commits, its label and its lock, and "archived · n" under the block's takes brings it back. **The alternative:** delete it (its commits become unreachable), or leave it in the fan (the text would then read twice).

    **(b) A LOCKED take may be moved out.** That is exactly what archiving a locked take does today, and this door archives — so it follows that door rather than inventing a third behaviour. The lock freezes the BUFFER and nothing here edits it; the archived original keeps its lock, and the new block's copy is NOT born locked (the standing copy doctrine). **The alternative:** refuse a locked take (one guard line).

    **(c) "Turn all takes into blocks" keeps the take the block is SHOWING, and the others become blocks below it in fan order.** So the note still reads exactly as it read — the first block's text never changes — and the run below is the same order the clipboard door writes (shown take, then alternates). **The alternative:** keep the FIRST take in the fan instead, which puts the blocks in strict fan order but silently changes what the block shows.

    **(d) BLANK takes are skipped, and the receipt says so** ("1 blank take was left alone"). Minting an empty block serves nobody. Moving a single blank take refuses by name instead. **The alternative:** convert blanks too (empty blocks below).

    **(e) The chords are ⌥⌘⏎ (this take) and ⇧⌥⌘⏎ (all takes)** — your words asked for shortcuts, and the sweep found the Return family's only bound chords to be ⌘⏎ (choose take) and ⌘⇧⏎ (new block below), so the pair reads as that family's other two members. Both are menu items and both are in the helper (⌘/). **The alternative:** re-pick either key, or drop the chords and keep the menu doors.

    **(f) ARCHIVED takes ride nowhere** — they stay on the source block, exactly as an unmarked take stays on A in the divide ceremony. **The alternative:** convert them too, which would resurrect set-aside text as blocks in the note.

    **(g) The new block INHERITS the callout frame and gets COPIES of the block's comments; the source keeps its own.** The standing structural-successor travel rules (a divide's halves behave the same way). **The alternative:** mint plain / carry no comments.

    **(h) The take's LABEL rides onto the new block's take; its COMMITS do not.** A whole-take ride keeps its name (the merge-carry precedent), and commits are never copied anywhere in the system — they stay with the archived original, which is exactly where the road back is. **The alternative:** drop the label too.

    **(i) The lineage edge is stamped `manual`.** The new block records that it descends from the block it came out of; `manual` is the existing vocabulary and a new value would be a schema change. **The alternative:** none without a schema event — recorded so the wording is yours to know, not to fix.

    **(j) Moving the take a block is SHOWING is allowed** — the block then shows its default take instead (the standing rule for archiving a shown take), and the note keeps reading. Refused only when the block has just ONE take: a take moves out when there is another left behind. **The alternative:** refuse to move the shown take at all.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13e (2026-08-17) — batch 4's build-time calls, bundled for the sitting

59. **2026-08-17 · Phase 13e (batch 4 — E2, block multi-select and multi-delete) · TWELVE build-time calls, each one line to change.** Your A17, verbatim: *"delete several blocks at the same time from a post - highlighting all of the blocks perhaps? block highlight instead of text highlight across blocks?"* Built. The BINDING build record is the 13e plan's batch-4 record.

    **(a) It lives on BOTH post surfaces — Write's editor AND the Takes board.** Your words name the surface where "text highlight across blocks" is the alternative, which is Write; R118 then forbids leaving the board behind, and the board already had the ⌘-click selection batch 2 built for merge. So it is ONE selection with TWO acts (merge, delete), not two selections. **The alternative:** Write only.

    **(b) NOT on the Blocks list (⌘6) or Canvas.** Your ask contrasts block highlight with TEXT highlight, which is a writing-surface contrast; a list already deletes a row at a time. **The alternative:** add multi-select to the Blocks list too (its own selection host).

    **(c) ⌘-click toggles a block; ⌘⇧-click extends the run from the last one you clicked.** ⇧ ALONE is untouched — it stays the text selection's, which is the very gesture your ask contrasts with. **The alternative:** re-pick either chord.

    **(d) NO keyboard shortcut.** The obvious key is ⌫, and ⌫ belongs to the caret in the very surface this act lives in — a delete key meaning two things depending on invisible state is exactly the class the app retired. **The alternative:** grant a chord (it would need a collision sweep and a decision about ⌫).

    **(e) The highlight is a soft wash plus an accent border, edge to edge across the block** — the same band the editing indicator draws, so a selected block reads as "the whole block" and still tells itself apart from the caret block. **The alternative:** any other look; it is one line of colour.

    **(f) An ORDINARY click puts the selection down.** She is writing again, not picking. The banner's "clear" is the deliberate door; this is what keeps a forgotten highlight from following her around the note. TYPING does not clear it (the highlight is visible while she types). **The alternative:** keep the selection until cleared explicitly, or clear on typing too.

    **(g) The delete is ONE undo entry over every picked block** (⌥⌘Z brings them all back at once, ⇧⌥⌘Z takes them again). A multi-delete is one mistake to reverse, which is what makes deleting a dozen blocks safe at all. **The alternative:** one entry per block.

    **(h) It is the standing UNRESTRICTED delete** (your #33(a) ruling): the block goes, whatever it holds — takes, comments, a callout frame. Every block still lands in the note's deleted-blocks tray. **The alternative:** refuse blocks that carry takes or comments, or warn first.

    **(i) NO confirmation prompt.** Your A12 destructive-undo warning shipped at 13d for the case where text becomes unreachable; a delete here is undoable, redoable AND tray-recoverable, so a prompt would be friction over three safety nets. **The alternative:** a confirm above some count.

    **(j) A refusal rolls the WHOLE gesture back — no partial acts.** A sealed note refuses at the first block through the operation itself; a picked block that vanished under her (another pane, the other half of a split, a sync arrival) unwinds everything that already landed and says *"One of the blocks you picked is already gone… Nothing was deleted."* Those words are new: that refusal used to print its raw internal name. **The alternative:** delete what it can and report the rest.

    **(k) A refusal KEEPS your picks** (nothing to re-click), and a successful delete clears them. **The alternative:** clear on refusal too.

    **(l) The selection is session-only and prunes itself** — it dies with the surface, and drops blocks the note no longer holds, so a block that comes BACK (an undo, a tray restore, a sync arrival) never arrives already highlighted. The banner renders only while something is picked; at rest the page is exactly as calm as before. **The alternative:** none proposed — this is batch 2's rule, applied.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13e (2026-08-18) — the ladder round A fix session's calls, bundled for the sitting

60. **2026-08-18 · Phase 13e (ladder round A — the cold review's fixes) · FIVE calls, each one line to change.** The phase's first cold review found no blockers and no majors; these are the calls made while fixing the small things it did find. The BINDING record is the 13e plan's round-A section.

    **(a) The Takes board's multi-delete now behaves EXACTLY like Write's when a picked block has vanished under you** (deleted in another pane, moved, or arrived-deleted from your phone): the whole gesture rolls back, nothing is deleted, and it says *"One of the blocks you picked is already gone… Nothing was deleted."* Before this fix the board quietly dropped the missing one and deleted the rest — one act with two behaviours, depending which surface you were on. **The alternative:** none proposed; #59(j) already ruled the rollback for this act, and this is the board honouring it.

    **(b) That refusal now KEEPS your picks on the board too** (#59(k)'s rule, which the board's own vanished-pick path did not follow — it cleared them). **The alternative:** clear on this refusal.

    **(c) Recorded, not changed: a picked block that MOVED to another note is deleted where it now lives** — on both surfaces. Both hosts name the blocks you picked and let the operation act on them; a block that left the note is still a real block, and "delete the blocks I picked" is what happens. It is rare (blocks move between notes only by a deliberate act elsewhere) and it is undoable. **The alternative:** refuse a pick the note no longer holds, which would make the board refuse where Write acts unless both were changed together.

    **(d) The merge door now says a picked block is GONE instead of saying the blocks "aren't next to each other."** Same race, one door over: the adjacency test fails for a missing block, so the sentence blamed the wrong thing and sent you looking for a neighbour that was standing right there. A genuine gap still says gap. **The alternative:** one sentence for both cases.

    **(e) The take card's right-click rows now take their key hints from the ONE place the keys are declared** (so the ⌥⌘⏎ / ⇧⌥⌘⏎ pair #58(e) leaves yours to re-pick moves in one edit, not three). Nothing you see changed — the words are pinned byte-identical. **The alternative:** none; this is housekeeping the review asked for.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13e (2026-08-18) — batch 5's calls (the canvas: the drag fix + the bank), bundled for the sitting

61. **2026-08-18 · Phase 13e batch 5 (canvas — E12's drag fix and E7's bank) · ELEVEN calls, each one line to change.** The BINDING record is the 13e plan's batch-5 section.

    **THE DRAG FIX, first — because it explains itself:** your *"they jitter while moving and seemingly resist moving"* was ONE mechanism, measured rather than guessed. Every canvas drag was measured against the card it was moving, so each mouse-move reported "how far you've come, minus how far I've already moved you" — which halves the travel and stalls every other event. Dragged 100pt, the card went 40 and stuttered the whole way. It now measures against the canvas itself, and a card follows the pointer exactly.

    **(a) The bank starts CLOSED, and remembers per canvas** (on this Mac, like card sizes and take picks). A canvas you have never arranged still opens to its cards in the spread grid — the shipped face — and the bank is something you open when the board gets crowded, which is the case you named ("when there are many"). **The alternative:** open it by default whenever cards are unplaced (the board would then start empty with a full strip).

    **(b) The bank lists cards in the NOTE's order, and has no order of its own.** Giving the strip a hand-arrangeable order would need somewhere to store it — a schema change — and this batch's rule was no schema. **The alternative:** a persisted bank order, at the cost of a lockstep event.

    **(c) A card dropped out of the bank lands with its TOP-LEFT corner where you let go.** **The alternative:** centre the card under the cursor.

    **(d) The card's right-click row "Reset position" now reads "Return to Bank".** Same act, same op — dropping a card on the strip does exactly what that row does, so they say the same thing. **The alternative:** keep the old words.

    **(e) Returning a card that is ALREADY in the bank does nothing, silently** (rather than refusing with the store's "no position row" complaint — a true sentence about rows and a false one about what you did). **The alternative:** speak a refusal.

    **(f) While the bank is OPEN, a banked card is in the STRIP and not on the board** — never in both places at once — and the board's extent, fit-all and the overview count only placed cards. Closing the bank puts them back on the board exactly as before. **The alternative:** show them in both places.

    **(g) Click-in-order reaches banked cards: clicking a chip numbers it** just like clicking a card, so parking a card never puts it outside the one spatial→structural door. **The alternative:** numbers only on the board.

    **(h) The bank's door sits in the canvas top strip ("Bank · 4") and appears only when something is parked or the strip is open** — a fully-arranged board with a closed bank has nothing for it to say. **The alternative:** always show it.

    **(i) A chip shows the card's first ~90 characters at a fixed 150×52.** **The alternative:** any other size/preview length.

    **(j) All three canvas drags — moving a card, resizing one, dragging a chip out of the bank — measure in ONE named canvas space** (rather than the screen). It is what lets a drop be asked "board or bank?" and answered without a second geometry brain. **The alternative:** none proposed; this is the fix's shape.

    **(k) The RESIZE grip was fixed with the same rule**, though your report named moving only: the grip sits at the card's corner, so growing the card moved the grip, and a 60×40 pull grew the card by 36×24. It now grows exactly as far as you pull. **The alternative:** none proposed.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13e (2026-08-18) — batch 6's calls (comments in the margin · the tags face), bundled for the sitting

62. **2026-08-18 · Phase 13e batch 6 (E7-comments + E8 — the margin-hover comment door, and the tags face on the listings) · SIXTEEN calls, each one line to change.** The BINDING record is the 13e plan's batch-6 section.

    **YOUR SITTING QUESTION, ANSWERED FIRST — *"comments need to be on the left instead of the right - is that true in this phase?"*** It was already true on the Mac before this batch, and the sweep says so with evidence rather than from memory: every comment face the Mac draws sits on the LEFT — the margin card and its narrow-gutter count badge (moved by your own R5-3 ruling at 13g), the Takes board's margin note, and now the new hover door. Nothing was found lurking on the right. The ONE thing that is not on the left is on your PHONE: a commented block wears a small ●n marker at the end of its last line, which is the phone's own ruled grammar (it has no margin to put a card in) — untouched here, and yours to move if you want it moved.

    **(a) The margin's "+ //" door is back — but only under the POINTER.** 13b built it always-on at the caret block, on the right; you retired it there ("it conflicts with the take icon"). It now appears in the LEFT margin on whatever block you are pointing at, exactly where that block's comment card would read, and vanishes when you move away. **The alternative:** also show it permanently on the caret block.

    **(b) Only UNCOMMENTED blocks offer it.** A block that already carries a comment shows its card there, and the card is the same door. **The alternative:** offer "+" beside an existing card too (two doors in one margin).

    **(c) Inline-comment mode and narrow windows keep their own grammar** — the strip and the count badge respectively; the invitation appears only where a margin card would fit. **The alternative:** an invitation in those modes too, wherever it can be squeezed.

    **(d) It says "+ //" in the terminal voice and "+" in the study voice** (which has no comment glyph). **The alternative:** one wording for both.

    **(e) It is NOT tied to the "reveal drag handles on hover" setting** — that setting names the handles. Pointing at a block offers the comment door whether or not handles reveal. **The alternative:** fold it under that setting, or give it a setting of its own.

    **(f) A real bug went with it, and it was yours to have hit: on a plain page, hovering revealed NOTHING — not even the drag handle.** The surface skips its decoration pass entirely when nothing is decorated (no seams, no callouts, no comments, no highlight), and the hover faces need that pass to know where the blocks are. So the handle reveal has been invisible on ordinary notes since it shipped. The page now takes its measurements while the pointer is inside it and stops again the moment it leaves. **The alternative:** none proposed — this is the fix's shape.

    **(g) The reading surface offers no comment door** (it never had one — reading stays quiet). **The alternative:** a read-side add door.

    **THE TAGS FACE — your 13f round-11 ask, whose second half ("does it exist within the post?") shipped at 13g/13k as the context bar's cards. This is the FIRST half: the high-level area could only ADD, and only tags that already existed.**

    **(h) The Library's rows and the Pool's rows now wear the SAME cards your note wears** — the tag's colour, its leaf, `✕` to take it off, `＋` to put one on. Not a new tag mechanism: literally the same component. **The alternative:** leave the listings' plain `#path` text and add a remove button to it.

    **(i) `＋` on a listing row TYPES a tag on, creating it if it is new** (the same door the context bar's ＋ has always used). So you can invent a tag while looking at the library, not only while inside a note. **The alternative:** keep the listing's old menu of existing tags only.

    **(j) In the Notes-mode card list the tag row stays invisible until you point at the card** (and holds its room, so nothing jumps); in the List mode's column it always shows. **The alternative:** always show on both, or never on the cards.

    **(k) A selection of several notes now offers "Untag N notes" beside "Tag N notes"**, listing only the tags the selection actually wears. **The alternative:** no bulk remove, or one menu listing the whole taxonomy.

    **(l) Bulk acts are forgiving: a note that already has the tag (or already lacks it) is quietly skipped, never an error.** **The alternative:** report the skips.

    **(m) The row shows ALL of a note's tags** — no "+2 more" cap. Long runs compress rather than overflowing. **The alternative:** cap at N with an overflow chip.

    **(n) The taxonomy-wide checklist sheet ("Tags…") stays on the Library row's right-click menu**, where it was; the inline `＋` no longer duplicates it as a list of every tag. **The alternative:** keep the long menu inline as well.

    **(o) Tagging a note is now a declared act in the capability catalog** (it never was), naming its three renderers — the context bar's cards, the Library's rows, the Pool's rows. Nothing you see changed. **The alternative:** leave it undeclared.

    **(p) The Pool's rows were included, though your ask named the Library.** They are the same surface family (both are "notes at a high level"), and leaving them behind would have meant two different tag grammars in one app. **The alternative:** Library only.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13e (2026-08-18) — batch 7's calls (find & replace across takes · the copy doors' flush), bundled for the sitting

63. **2026-08-18 · Phase 13e batch 7 (E9 + E10 + the routed drag rider) · TWELVE calls, each one line to change.** The BINDING record is the 13e plan's batch-7 section.

    **WHAT E9 IS, IN ONE SENTENCE — the takes half of find & replace you asked us to record at the QoL sitting** (*"a future phase will for sure need to handle takes find and replace and whole library search"*): the find bar is unchanged, and a new menu row beside it — **Edit ▸ Find ▸ Replace All in Every Take** — carries the SAME search and replacement into every other live take of the blocks the matches touch, then tells you what it did and what it skipped. (The whole-LIBRARY search half is untouched; it has its own roadmap row, at your "after the existing 13 phases" slotting.)

    **(a) It is an ACT beside the bar, not a mode or a checkbox.** There is no scope switch to leave on: the ordinary ⌘F family behaves exactly as it did, and takes are reached only by choosing this row deliberately. **The alternative:** a sticky "in every take" toggle in the Find menu.

    **(b) What it does inside an unseen take, precisely.** If the gesture replaced EVERY occurrence of the phrase in the take you can see (an ordinary Replace All), it replaces every occurrence in the other takes too. If it replaced only SOME of them, it reaches an unseen take only where the phrase occurs exactly ONCE — otherwise which occurrence would be a guess, so it is skipped and named. (This is the same "unique match or nothing" rule Delete in Every Take has used since Phase 11.) **The alternative:** always all, or always unique-only.

    **(c) It lands as ONE act, undone with ⌥⌘Z (structural undo), not ⌘Z.** The other takes are on no surface, so they can only be written by an operation — and putting the visible take through the same transaction is what makes one undo return everything it touched. An ordinary Replace All is unchanged (⌘Z, as always). **The alternative:** two undos (text undo for what you see, structural for the rest).

    **(d) Skipped takes are always NAMED with their reason** — "(locked)", "(archived)", "(no match)", "(appears more than once)" — in one line with the count. Locked takes are never edited around their lock; archived takes are out of the fan and out of the act. **The alternative:** refuse the whole gesture unless every take matches.

    **(e) A match in the paragraph you are still typing refuses the WHOLE gesture** (with the existing sentence: it becomes a block once typing pauses). That text is not a block yet, so it has no take to carry anything into. The ordinary Replace All still handles it normally. **The alternative:** replace what it can and report the rest.

    **(f) The row has NO keyboard shortcut.** An act that edits takes you cannot see felt like one to choose from a menu rather than to hit by muscle memory. **The alternative:** give it a chord (the Find family's own keys are taken).

    **(g) The Takes tab does not offer it.** The whole find family is Write's, by the QoL round's own ruling ("a find across the post has no meaning on a per-take pane"), and this act reads the find bar's two fields — so it exists exactly where the bar does. **The alternative:** offer it in Takes, moving you to Write and firing against whatever the bar's fields hold (an empty replacement field would delete every match — which is why it was not built).

    **(h) Fired with nothing to find, it says so** ("Nothing to replace — open Find and Replace (⌥⌘F) and type what to find and what to replace it with"). **The alternative:** open the bar for you.

    **THE COPY DOORS (E10) — a tech-debt row from the 13f review, closed.**

    **(i) Duplicate as Version, Make this a post (from a lens or an arranged column), and the membership chip's Duplicate now all flush your typing before copying.** Before this, a copy taken within half a second of a keystroke was minted from the previous text — the original was always fine, but the copy silently lacked the sentence you had just finished. **The alternative:** none proposed; this is the fix.

    **(j) The sweep found one more of the same kind and fixed it too: dragging a scrap into a draft** copied the scrap's stored text, so a scrap dragged in immediately after typing in it arrived stale. **The alternative:** leave it, as out of the row's letter.

    **THE DRAG RIDER — the sibling of your canvas report.**

    **(k) The takes board's "drag to shorten this block's takes" handle had the SAME defect your canvas cards had** (*"they jitter while moving and seemingly resist moving"*): it followed a little over half your pointer and landed short. It was found by batch 5's sweep, not by a report, and is now fixed with the same one-line rule. Measured: a 200 pt pull moved the strip 120 pt and committed 76. **The alternative:** none proposed.

    **(l) Nothing else on the Mac carries that class** — the sweep table in the plan file lists every drag gesture in the app with a verdict, and this was the last unfixed one. **The alternative:** none.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13e (2026-08-18) — batch 8's calls (the system-wide quick-note hotkey), bundled for the sitting

64. **2026-08-18 · Phase 13e batch 8 (E14 / W26 + the two window-lifecycle riders) · ELEVEN entries — nine build-time calls, each one line to change, plus the two riders' dispositions as (j)/(k).** *(Count wording reconciled at ladder round B, B-6b — the header said NINE while the batch record said eleven; both were describing the same (a)–(k) bundle.)* The BINDING record is the 13e plan's batch-8 section.

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE — the thing you asked us to make sure you landed on again:** **⌃⌥⌘N now opens the quick-note pad from ANY app**, with Variorum in the background and nothing clicked. It was proved on your machine in the sitting with an unsandboxed probe ("i heard the glass chime!"); this batch built it inside the real, SANDBOXED app and drove it with Finder and then Terminal in front — it registered clean (status 0, no permission prompt) and fired both times.

    **(a) It is a FOURTH DOOR on the same act, not a second act.** Quick Note has one entry in the capability catalog wearing four doors — the menu-bar ✏︎, ⌘⇧N, File ▸ Quick Note, and now the system chord — so the pad, the mint and the wording are literally one implementation. **The alternative:** a separate "Quick Note from Anywhere" act with its own row everywhere.

    **(b) The act wears TWO keys, and both stay live: ⌘⇧N inside Variorum, ⌃⌥⌘N from anywhere.** They are different mechanisms (one is the app's menu, one is the system's), so a chord that shadowed the other would have cost you the menu item. **The alternative:** retire ⌘⇧N and keep only the system chord.

    **(c) Summoning now BRINGS VARIORUM FORWARD first — and that includes the menu-bar ✏︎, which did not before.** Both of those doors are pressed while another app is in front, and the floating pad could open behind it. **The alternative:** open the pad without activating (the pre-13e menu-bar behaviour).

    **(d) The chord is asked for ONCE for the whole app, however many windows are open** — and it keeps answering after you close every window (the menu-bar extra's own promise). **The alternative:** one registration per window.

    **(e) It is installed when the main window first appears.** Consequence, stated rather than hidden: a launch that never shows a window has no hot key until one opens. (That state is the separate windowless-launch bug on the deferred list; it did NOT occur in either of this batch's launches.) **The alternative:** install at app launch through a different road — possible, and it would need its own way to reach the pad.

    **(f) A registration the system refuses is RECORDED, not announced.** The status goes to the log (`RegisterEventHotKey … status=`) and the door simply does not answer; nothing pops up. **The alternative:** a visible message when the chord could not be taken (e.g. another app already holds it).

    **(g) The chord is FIXED — there is no recorder UI**, exactly as you ruled at the sitting ("a recorder UI is a later at-her-word add"). The machinery is shaped for one: the label in the catalog is the single source the system registration is derived from, so changing the chord is one string. **The alternative:** build the picker now.

    **(h) The helper (⌘/) gained its OWN row for it**, separate from ⌘⇧N's, because it is a different promise — this one works while Variorum is not in front. **The alternative:** one combined row.

    **(i) The quick-note pad opens EMPTY on a system summon, like every other door** — one summon, one note; an abandoned pad keeps nothing. Unchanged from 13f, restated because the chord makes it much easier to summon. **The alternative:** re-open the last pad's text.

    **THE TWO WINDOW RIDERS — both dispositioned, nothing invented.**

    **(j) The note-window CLIPPED TOP you reported at 13g ("the top of it i couldn't scroll to … it was cut off") is FIXED, and has been since 13k.** The mechanism was found this batch and proved by reverting it: the context bar used to appear only once a note's books had loaded — which happens a moment AFTER the window is drawn — so on a note in a book the bar was inserted above the title after the fact, and the title's top was clipped until you resized. 13k's rebuilt always-visible bar removed the trigger without anyone noticing. There is now a test holding that property. **The alternative:** none proposed; the deferred-work row is removed.

    **(k) The windowless-launch bug did not occur** in either of this batch's app launches (both came up with a window), so nothing was captured and nothing changed. Its row stands with its capture-on-occurrence protocol. **The alternative:** none.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13e (2026-08-18) — batch 9's calls (the promised Keychain fix), bundled for the sitting

65. **2026-08-18 · Phase 13e batch 9 (ladder round B's B-1 — the lazy/off-main Keychain fix the deferred-work row had promised) · TEN entries: eight build-time calls and two things deliberately NOT changed.** The BINDING record is the 13e plan's batch-9 section.

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** the app no longer asks the system Keychain for anything on the main thread — not while it is starting up, and not while the Encryption settings sheet is open. **Why it matters to you:** the beachball-at-launch state (the app running with NO window, which you have hit, and which also froze test runs) was CAUGHT IN THE ACT back in August — the main thread was sitting inside a Keychain read, waiting on the system's security daemon, before any window existed. That read now happens later and elsewhere. **Nothing about your sign-in, your keys, or your encryption changes** — same reads, same writes, same order, same wording; only which thread does the waiting.

    **(a) The persisted sign-in is read at FIRST USE, not while the app is being built.** It is still read exactly once, still adopted exactly as before, and a sign-out before anything read it still logs the saved session out properly. **The alternative:** keep reading it during construction and try to make that read faster (it is not slow — it is occasionally BLOCKED, which no speed-up fixes).

    **(b) Signing in supersedes the saved session outright** — once you sign in (or out), the older saved item is never read again, so it can never come back over the top of what you just did. **The alternative:** read it anyway and compare.

    **(c) The custody hops (the Encryption sheet's) run on ONE shared queue, in order.** Every Keychain call the sheet makes still lands in the exact order it always did — which matters for the one place that reads-then-writes (the biometric toggle), and that pair now travels together in a single hop. **The alternative:** fire each hop independently (simpler, but it would give up the ordering for nothing).

    **(d) The "require Face ID / Touch ID to unlock sync" toggle now flips only once the change has actually landed in the Keychain.** Before, the flip and the write were the same instant because both happened on the main thread. On a healthy machine you will see no difference; on a stalled one, the sheet stays responsive and the toggle catches up. **The alternative:** flip the toggle immediately and write behind it (it would lie if the write were refused).

    **(e) The re-key door's mid-run custody adoption is now awaited through the same road** — the contract it exists for (this device holds the new keys BEFORE the server truncate) is unchanged and, if anything, tighter: the wait now covers the Keychain write itself. **The alternative:** none proposed.

    **(f) Two things were deliberately NOT moved, and both are stated rather than left silent.** (i) The check for *whether* this Mac has Face ID / Touch ID at all: it is not a keys read (it asks the biometrics system, not the Keychain), and it is read while the sheet is drawing — making it async would mean the sheet's first frame claiming "no biometrics on this device" until a refresh returned. (ii) A one-line cleanup of the retired Phase 8 paste-in item, which still runs on the main thread at sign-in: it is neither of the two families the deferred-work row named, and a delete of a long-gone item does not go near the mechanism that was captured stalling. **The alternative for either:** move it too, in a batch that owns it.

    **(g) The engine's own key reads were not touched at all** — they already happen off the main thread, on their own rounds, and the batch's fence named them untouchable. **The alternative:** none.

    **(h) Five existing test lines gained the word `await`** (nothing else changed in them) because two acts became asynchronous; each edit is called out in the test itself. **The alternative:** none — this is what a signature change costs.

    **(i) The fix is held by nine new tests** that record which thread every Keychain call landed on, and they were proved to FAIL on the old code (both halves, separately). **The alternative:** none.

    **(j) The windowless-launch row itself stays open** — the fix its capture pointed at is now built, but "it never happens again" is a claim only real use can make. Its capture-on-occurrence protocol stands, and the row needs a new owner now that this phase is closing. **The alternative:** close the row on the strength of the fix.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13j (2026-08-18) — batch 1's calls (the mini-round: your ⌘Z hint, copy truth, and deleting a paragraph)

66. **2026-08-18 · Phase 13j batch 1 (J7 + J8 + J9 — the three things your first morning back turned up) · SEVEN entries.** The BINDING record is the 13j plan's batch-1 section, including the one precise trace you were owed before anything was built.

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** selecting a paragraph and pressing Delete now deletes the paragraph; the refusals stopped guessing; and ⌘Z with nothing left to undo now points you at ⌥⌘Z instead of doing nothing.

    **THE TRACE, first — because you asked what was actually wrong.** When you select a paragraph, you also select the newline at the end of it (that is what a triple-click and a drag-to-the-next-paragraph both do). That newline belongs to NO block — it is the seam between two of them — so the editor read your selection as "an edit that reaches across a boundary" and handed it to the two roads that handle those. Both of those roads require the selection to touch **at least two blocks' text**, and yours touched one block plus a seam. So both declined, and the last line in the chain spoke. **The open tail was NOT the cause** — that was the plausible guess in the write-up, and the trace ruled it out: a note with no unfinished paragraph refuses in exactly the same way. Both facts are held by tests.

    **(a) A selection covering WHOLE paragraphs plus Delete now runs the same delete you get from ⌘-clicking blocks** — one ⌥⌘Z undoes the whole thing however many paragraphs went, and ⇧⌥⌘Z puts them back. **The alternative:** empty the paragraph's text and leave an empty paragraph sitting there (what happens today if you select the text WITHOUT the trailing newline).

    **(b) It deletes a block WHATEVER it holds — takes included.** This follows your own unrestricted-delete ruling (#33(a): "the block goes, whatever it holds") and matches exactly what ⌘-click + Delete already does. **The alternative, and it is a real one:** refuse when a selected paragraph has takes, the way backspace-at-a-seam refuses ("a keystroke won't fold a fan away", your #34 ruling). The two rulings point different ways here, and the difference is that a MERGE folds takes into another block's text while a DELETE sets the block aside whole — undoable now, and in the tray once batch 2 builds it. **This one is genuinely yours to settle; it is one line of code either way.**

    **(c) Selecting TWO whole paragraphs and pressing Delete now removes both.** It used to join them and leave one empty paragraph behind. Better, and it is what your ruling says — but it does change something that was ratified back in Phase 11, so it is named here rather than buried. **The alternative:** keep the join for two-or-more and let the new road handle only single paragraphs.

    **(d) PARTIAL selections are untouched** — half of one paragraph through half of the next still joins the two, exactly as before. **The alternative:** none proposed; you ruled this half explicitly.

    **(e) Every delete receipt stopped promising a tray that does not exist.** They now say only what is true — ⌥⌘Z puts it back while this session is open. **Batch 2 builds the tray and these are the lines that graduate**, so you will see them change again once the promise is real. **The alternative:** leave the copy and build the tray first.

    **(f) The refusal that confused you now names ONE cause, and gives a way out of it.** Instead of "a locked take, unsynced text, or text that isn't a block yet", it says which of those it actually is — a locked take, a block that hasn't synced, the unfinished paragraph, a fan at the seam, an archived take, a comment, or a partly-covered block — each with its own road. **The alternative:** keep one line and make it vaguer still.

    **(g) ⌘Z with nothing left in the text says so, and names the key that CAN help.** It never takes the key — ⌘Z that can undo text still does, every time. ⌘⇧Z got the same treatment for redo. It stays silent when there is nothing structural to point at, so a fresh note never lectures. **The alternative:** make ⌘Z itself do the structural undo when the text stack is empty (this would join two stacks your 2026-07-04 decision deliberately keeps apart).

    **ONE THING LEFT ALONE, named rather than hidden:** the iPhone's two delete messages still promise the tray. They were out of this Mac batch's scope and the phone has no tray either; the parity row carries it. *(The phone's receipts were made true at batch 2's rider.)*

    **Status: (b) and (c) RULED at the 13j round-6 sitting, 2026-08-18 — both RATIFIED as shipped (the 13j plan's run record is the binding text). The rest: walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13j (2026-08-18) — batch 2's calls (the tray, the graveyard, the receipts' graduation)

67. **2026-08-18 · Phase 13j batch 2 (J2 + J6 + J8(a) + the phone-receipts rider) · SEVEN entries.** The BINDING record is the 13j plan's batch-2 section, including two VERIFY-FIRST findings the brief required before anything was built.

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** every note now carries its own tray — a quiet **⌫ N** in the note's bar and a named item in the `note ▾` menu — listing what that note has deleted and what a divide or merge kept whole, with one **Put back** on every row; the Library's "Unplaced blocks" table is gone; and the delete receipts name the tray again, because now it exists.

    **FINDING 1 — there is no such thing as an archived BLOCK.** The sitting called the first section "Deleted & archived", so the brief asked me to check before labelling it. The `block.archived_at` column exists in the schema and in the model, and the sync layer carries it — but **no operation anywhere writes it**, and there is no "archive block" act in the app at all. Archiving is something TAKES and NOTES can be; blocks can only be deleted. **So the section is called "Deleted."** Naming a state the app cannot produce is the same defect as naming a door that isn't there. **The alternative:** build a block-level archive (a real feature, its own decision).

    **FINDING 2 — Put back does NOT return a deleted block to its old spot.** The sitting's design note said a put-back block returns to where it was, because its old placement row still holds its position. The shipped `restoreBlock` does not do that: it leaves the old row tombstoned and puts the block at the **END** of the note. (The exact-position machinery exists, but only on ⌥⌘Z's path, which flips the original row.) **I did not change the operation** — an ops-layer change needs its own state-transition tests and a deliberate record, so the brief said report first. The tray therefore says "Put back at the end of this note", which is true. **The alternative, and it is a real one: make `restoreBlock` flip the old placement row when it survives, so Put back and ⌥⌘Z land in the same place.** That is the behaviour the sitting described, and it would make the tray better. It is a small change to a shipped operation and it is yours to authorize.

    **(a) The badge counts DELETED blocks only, and hides at zero** — your round-1 "calm at rest" plus round-5's "originals must not inflate an urgent-looking count". Consequence, named: a note with preserved originals but nothing deleted shows **no badge**, and the `note ▾` item is how you reach those. **The alternative:** count both.

    **(b) The badge sits AFTER the four constant icons, before the tag cards.** Your K11 ruling says the icon row never changes; your J2 ruling says this door appears only when there is something in the tray. Both are kept: the four aiming targets are untouched, and the badge appears beside them — so the only thing that ever moves is the tag cards, and only when you have just deleted something. **The alternative:** park it at the far right where nothing moves at all.

    **(c) It is called "This Note's Tray"** — your own word, and the word the receipts have always used. **The alternative:** anything you like; it is one constant (`TrayPath.menuLabel`) and every receipt, ceremony explainer and menu item follows it.

    **(d) A preserved original's Put back lands it after its last surviving successor**, not at the note's end — so putting back a divide's original drops it right beside the two blocks it became. With no successors left, it goes to the end. This is composed at the face over `placeBlock`; no operation changed. **The alternative:** always the end (simpler, and wrong-feeling where the successors are still there).

    **(e) "Show Original Block" now opens THIS note's tray** instead of navigating to the Library table you called "that cursed table of lost hopes and dreams". It had to land somewhere real the moment the table went. **Its final form is batch 3's** — a read-only peek of the original with its takes — so this is a bridge, not the destination. **The alternative:** remove the door until batch 3.

    **(f) The delete receipts graduated.** They now say the blocks wait in This Note's Tray and name the ⌫ door, alongside ⌥⌘Z — two different promises, because ⌥⌘Z dies with the session and the tray does not. **The alternative:** name only one road.

    **(g) The iPhone's two delete messages stopped promising a tray.** The phone has no tray face and no undo door, so they now say what is true there: *"The block leaves this note, with its takes. There's no undo here."* and *"Block deleted."* No mention of the Mac (the no-redirect rule). **The phone's own tray is a parity-ledger row.** **The alternative:** leave the sentence and build the phone tray sooner.

    **Status: RULED at the 13j round-6/7 sitting, 2026-08-18 — FINDING 1 answered by her BLOCK-ARCHIVE ruling (build it, riding batch 6 — the section takes back "Deleted & archived"); FINDING 2 RATIFIED (Put back returns to the old spot — built at batch 3); (a) ratified deleted-only; (b) ratified after-the-icons; (c) ratified "This Note's Tray". The binding text is the 13j plan's run record. The rest: walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13j (2026-08-18) — batch 3's calls (the recovery doors: the original's peek, the lens notice, deleted takes)

68. **2026-08-18 · Phase 13j batch 3 (J3 + J5 + deleted-take recovery + the F2 placement rider) · SIX entries.** The BINDING record is the 13j plan's batch-3 section.

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** a block that came out of a divide or a merge now offers "See original from divide…" — on its right-click menu, in ⌘K, and as a quiet line in its History — opening a read-only look at the original with **Put back in note** and **Open this note's tray**; a saved lens whose pick was archived or deleted now SAYS so in the column with a one-click Restore; a deleted TAKE can be put back at last; and Put back returns a deleted block to its own place, as you were told it would.

    **(a) F2 is BUILT — your call from batch 2, as the architect ruled it.** `restoreBlock` now flips the placement row the deletion took down, so a block comes back **where it was**, and Put back and ⌥⌘Z land in the same spot. Fresh-at-end survives as the fallback for the one case that genuinely has no old place: a divide's original that was released to lineage and deleted later. The receipt reads the operation's own answer, so it never promises the wrong one. **It is still yours to ratify** — this entry is the "built as ruled" note, not a second asking. **The alternative:** the shipped-before behaviour (always the end).

    **(b) Deleted takes can be put back — and the door has no undo entry.** `restoreVariant` is a tombstone flip: the take comes back with its label, its place in the fan, its parent and its savepoints, exactly as it was. It sits in the recovery-door family (`restoreBlock`, `restoreSection`, restore-comment), which deliberately register NO undo entry, because the door IS the recovery and an entry whose inverse is "delete it again" is a second way to lose the thing. **The alternative:** make it undoable like a structural act.

    **(c) Put back does NOT move the block's default take pointer.** If the take you are restoring used to be the block's default, the delete moved that pointer to another take and the restore leaves it there. Reason: ⌥⌘Z remembers what the pointer WAS at the moment of the delete; a recovery door opened days later does not, and guessing would move a pointer you may have set by hand since. **The alternative:** restore the pointer too when the take was the default (this needs the fact stored somewhere, which is a schema question).

    **(d) The lens notice is worded to the ACTUAL state, and two of its four states carry no button.** "This pick was archived…" and "This pick was deleted…" each get a Restore that opens the right door. A pick whose take does not exist on this device at all, or points at another block (both only reachable through a sync race), get the notice WITHOUT a button — there is nothing to restore, and a button that could only fail is the door P-2 forbids. **The alternative:** one hedged sentence for all four.

    **(e) The notice appears on SAVED lens columns only.** An unnamed/scratch column's picks live in the view, not the store, so they cannot come back dangling after a restart — there is nothing to notice. **The alternative:** show it on scratch columns too (it would only ever fire mid-session).

    **(f) The lineage row shows a DATE, not a time.** "From a divide on Aug 18, 2026 — the original is preserved". The capture caught the time-of-day version pushing the sentence's point off the end of the line. The tray's rows keep their time, because "when did I delete this" is often a same-day question. **The alternative:** time everywhere, or nowhere.

    **ONE THING NAMED RATHER THAN HIDDEN:** the new take-restore act is declared in the capability registry (`Put Back Take`), while the tray's own two Put backs — shipped in batch 2 — are not. Both are inline rows on a recovery panel; the registry grows as work touches it, so the asymmetry is a tidy-up for a later pass, not a defect. It is recorded for the closeout's propose-only list.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13j (2026-08-18) — batch 4's calls (the defaults pair: divide duplicates, merge carries all)

69. **2026-08-18 · Phase 13j batch 4 (J4/J4b — the fanned defaults flip + the two ceremony doors) · FIVE entries.** The BINDING record is the 13j plan's batch-4 section.

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** your two rulings are now the Mac's behaviour — dividing a block that has takes cuts the take you're LOOKING at and copies every other take whole onto both halves, and merging blocks that have takes joins the two you're looking at and brings every other take along — with both ceremonies kept as deliberate doors you summon: **Divide with Ceremony… ⌥⌘⇧D** and **Merge with Ceremony… ⌥⌘⇧M**, in the Block menu, the block's right-click menu and ⌘K, exactly as you asked ("never only a buried menu item").

    **(a) The N-way MERGE CHAIN carries all too, and the consequence compounds.** Selecting across three or more blocks merges them in one go, as always — but now each link brings its takes along instead of leaving them behind. The consequence, said plainly because it is yours to accept or refuse: a carried take rides WHOLE and never receives the other blocks' text, so after merging three blocks the chosen take is all three joined, while each block's alternates stand alone beside it. That is the same thing that happens on a two-block merge, twice over — "quick to delete", your own words for it. **The alternative:** leave chains as they were (a fan in a chain folds to lineage and the app says so), which would mean two merge gestures with two different answers.

    **(b) The ceremony doors REFUSE where there is no ceremony, rather than quietly doing the plain thing.** ⌥⌘⇧D on a block with one take says *"This block has one take — ⌘⇧D divides it in place. The ceremony is for placing a cut in each take of a fan."* ⌥⌘⇧M on an unfanned pair, and on a run of three or more (the pairing sheet has two columns by construction), say their own versions. **The alternative:** have them just perform the plain act — the act happens, but the menu item promised a sheet and gave you something else.

    **(c) Both ceremony rows show in the block's right-click menu ALWAYS, not only on a block that has takes.** That menu has rendered every structural row unconditionally since your 2026-07-09 ruling (it already shows "Extract Selection" with no selection, "Merge Blocks" with no seam) and a refused row speaks. The alternative — show them only where they can act — is one condition away for divide, but merge's condition needs real work to compute at menu-build time, and one row conditional beside one row not is two grammars for one pair. **The alternative:** conditional rendering for both, at the cost of that work.

    **(d) Dividing a one-word take names the ceremony as the road.** A single word has no interior cut, so ⌘⇧D refuses there as it always has — and the refusal now adds *"Divide with Ceremony (⌥⌘⇧D) can send a whole take to one side"*, because parking a whole take at one end is exactly the ceremony's vocabulary. **The alternative:** the bare refusal.

    **(e) The receipts say what happened, counted from the operation's own answer.** *"Block divided — the take you were in split across both blocks, and 2 other takes rode whole onto each. Extra copies are quick to delete; ⌥⌘Z undoes."* and *"Blocks merged — the takes you were looking at joined, and 1 other take came along whole…"*. They deliberately do NOT claim "nothing was left behind": ARCHIVED takes do stay with the released blocks, as they always have. **The alternative:** shorter receipts, or ones that mention the archived case.

    **ALSO SWEPT, because copy naming a behaviour the app no longer has is the defect you reported in 13e:** the backspace-at-a-seam refusal used to say "Use Merge Blocks (⌘⇧M) to pair the takes yourself" — ⌘⇧M does not pair any more, so it now names both roads; the Takes bar's Divide and Merge tooltips; the field guide's "divide / the take ceremony" and "merge" entries; and three tutorial lessons (which also stopped claiming divide carries you to Write — untrue since 13d). *(CORRECTED at ladder A round 1, finding A2 — this paragraph overclaimed: the sweep fixed only the TEXTED-seam refusal, and the same sentence lived on in a second home — the empty-looking seam's diagnosis, `CrossBlockRefusal.roadClause(.seamBesideFan)`, inside J8(b)'s own file — still teaching "⌘⇧M pairs" until the reviewer's fix pass. Both speakers now end in ONE shared roads constant, `FannedDefaultsCopy.fanFoldRoads`, pinned so they cannot drift apart again.)*

    **Status: (a), (b), (c) RULED at the 13j round-9 sitting, 2026-08-19 — all three RATIFIED as shipped (the 13j plan's run record is the binding text). The rest: walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13j (2026-08-19) — batch 5's calls (the savepoint cadence: your history stops being thin)

70. **2026-08-19 · Phase 13j batch 5 (J11 — the savepoint cadence) · SIX entries.** The BINDING record is the 13j plan's batch-5 section.

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** your ruling is now the app's behaviour — every time you leave a note (switching away, closing its window, quitting, filing a quick note) Variorum leaves one quiet savepoint for each take you changed; a note you leave open gets one after about two hours instead of twenty-four; Settings ▸ Writing has the one setting with three choices; and a run of those quiet savepoints shows in a take's history as one line you can open, so ⌘S savepoints still stand out.

    **(a) WHERE "leaving" is drawn, and the three things that deliberately are NOT it.** A note is left when no pane of a window is showing it any more. So: navigating away, closing the split, a note being pushed out by "Open Beside", closing the window, and ⌘Q all leave a savepoint. What does NOT: **closing an Open-strip row with its ✕** (the pane goes on showing the note — its own rule is that ✕ closes a row and nothing else), **moving focus between two panes** (both notes are still on screen), and **switching modes inside a note** (Write ▸ Takes ▸ Canvas — the note is still open, only its surface changed). **The alternative** for the ✕ specifically: treat closing the row as leaving, which would savepoint a note you are still looking at.

    **(b) A LONE quiet savepoint is not collapsed.** Runs fold from two upward; a single `auto` row renders as itself, because "1 quiet savepoint — show" would be a line hiding one line behind a click. **The alternative:** fold from one, for perfect uniformity.

    **(c) The setting's help NAMES the four things it does not govern.** Sealing a note, locking a take with unsaved changes, undoing a structural change over edits, and a journal entry arriving from Fragments all still save a take first, in every mode including "Only when I press ⌘S" — they insure text about to become unreachable, which is not a schedule. The help says so in as many words, because a setting that reads as "nothing else writes savepoints" while four occasions still do is exactly the copy problem you reported in 13e. **The alternative:** shorter help that leaves it unsaid.

    **(d) The two-hour backstop moves only if it was still the shipped default.** No screen has ever exposed `idle_commit_hours`, so in practice your store reads 24 and moves to 2 — but if a value had ever been set by hand it stays. **The alternative:** move it unconditionally.

    **(e) A quick note gets a savepoint the moment it is filed.** The note it mints is open in no pane, so no other road would ever reach it, and a captured thought with no savepoint at all is the thin history you reported. **The alternative:** leave quick notes without one until they are opened and left.

    **(f) With the same note open in TWO windows, leaving it in one savepoints it while the other still shows it.** The shown-notes signal is per window. The cost is one extra quiet savepoint of text that genuinely changed — which the run collapse then folds away — and the alternative is a cross-window check on every navigation to prevent it. **Recorded, not hidden**, in case you want it prevented.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

71. **2026-08-19 · Phase 13j batch 6 (J1 — the Trash, block archive, the purge memo) · FIVE entries.** The BINDING record is the 13j plan's batch-6 section, and the design that returns to you is `notes/plans/phase-13j-purge-memo.md`.

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** everything you delete now rides one 30-day story — the Library's deleted listings became a **Trash** you can filter by kind and sort by date, every row says how many days are left and offers one **Put back**, the same countdown appears in a note's own tray, blocks can now be **archived** (kept forever, no clock) as well as deleted, and a preserved original whose blocks all go to the Trash visibly moves onto their clock instead of quietly promising permanence. **Delete Now and Empty Trash are NOT built yet** — they need the purge, which is the memo waiting for your go.

    **(a) The ⌫ badge still counts DELETED blocks only, so archiving alone shows no badge.** That is your ratified #67(a) applied to the new state: the badge stays an urgent-looking count of things on a clock. Archived blocks appear in the tray's first section, and the archive receipt therefore names the `note ▾` menu item — a door that is always there — rather than a badge that might not be. **The alternative:** count archived blocks too, and the badge appears whenever a note has anything set aside.

    **(b) The Library's door still reads "Archived & Deleted" while the section inside says "Trash".** The door names both halves it holds (the calm Archived section, and the Trash), and every piece of copy elsewhere in the app that points at that surface reads the same constant. **The alternative:** rename the door "Trash & Archived" — one edit, and every pointing sentence follows.

    **(c) An ARCHIVED block is deliberately not a "preserved original" and not offered by the lineage door.** Archive is a set-aside you chose; a preserved original is a leftover from a divide or a merge. Keeping them apart is what lets the lineage door's Put back stay a simple placement — placing an archived block without clearing its stamp would leave a block that is both in the note and marked archived. **The alternative:** let the lineage door offer archived blocks too, and have it clear the stamp on the way.

    **(d) The Trash's filter carries "Originals" as its own row.** A stranded preserved original is neither a deleted block nor a note, so it filters on its own and says "preserved original — goes with its deleted blocks" in the row. **The alternative:** fold it under "Blocks", where it is easier to miss.

    **(e) A stranded original shows in BOTH its note's tray and the global Trash.** That is deliberate — the tray is where you see it MOVE (which is what your round-8b concern was about), and the Trash is where you would go looking for everything that is counting down. It is one row in each place, never two in the same place. **The alternative:** the Trash only.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

72. **2026-08-19 · Phase 13j batch 6b (J1 — THE PURGE BUILD) · FIVE entries.** The BINDING record is the 13j plan's batch-6b section; the design you already ruled and pre-authorized is `notes/plans/phase-13j-purge-memo.md` (its §6b addendum records what the build corrected mechanically).

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** "really deleted" is real now — **Delete Now and Empty Trash exist in the Trash** (Delete Now confirms every time per your round-10 ruling; Empty Trash names the full count by kind per round 8), rows leave every surface the instant you confirm, and the invisible **janitor** then removes the bytes locally and from your account's mirror once every synced device has seen the deletion (the horizon; a device unseen 60 days drops out) — with the content-free ledger refusing any stale device's attempt to push a purged thing back, quiet savepoints pruning at your ruled 90-days/keep-10 with a "Never" setting beside the cadence one, and ⌘S savepoints never touched. **Nothing is deployed:** the mirror artifact is finalized and waits for the architect's one-window under your round-8 pre-authorization; until then the marker-7 build refuses to sync by design.

    **(a) A row the server says is PURGED is dropped locally too, not just from the push queue.** The memo's sentence said the client "drops that outbox entry"; the build also removes the row's local family, because a purged row's mirror copies no longer exist — keeping a local live orphan would diverge forever with no feed able to correct it, and the convergence tests you get this batch demand both devices end identical. **The alternative:** drop only the queue entry and let the ledger scan catch the row later — same end state, a window of divergence in between.

    **(b) A block you deleted EARLIER on its own clock does NOT ride its note's later purge.** It is its own Trash row with its own countdown, so it purges on its own schedule; what does go with the note is its old placement row, so if you later put that block back it lands at the note's end rather than its old spot — and its "from ‹note›" context goes blank when the note's bytes go. **The alternative:** purging the note takes every block that ever lived there, clocks be damned.

    **(c) Delete Now never appears on a preserved original's row.** An original rides its family (your round-8b ruling) — when you Delete Now the last of its blocks, the confirm's receipt already covers it, and it goes in the same sweep. **The alternative:** a per-original door, which would make "preserved" a lie twice over.

    **(d) Archived blocks and archived notes are UNTOUCHED by every purge road.** Archive is kept-forever; no intent can name a live row (the op refuses: "Only things in the Trash can be deleted for good"), and a note's purge takes only the blocks that died WITH it. Nothing you set aside can leave by any door but your own delete first.

    **(e) The savepoint pruning's janitor half consults the setting before minting, but honors intents that already traveled.** If you switch to "Never" after a pass minted intents, those intents complete (the instruction already left the device); nothing NEW mints from then on. **The alternative:** un-minting standing intents on a setting change — racy across devices and against the intent table's own idempotence rule.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint; the deploy window (mirror → Mac → phone, bundled) is the architect's act under your standing pre-authorization.**

73. **2026-08-19 · Phase 13j batch 7 (J10 — the tutorial's deletion chapter + the note-history brief) · SEVEN entries.** The BINDING record is the 13j plan's batch-7 section.

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** the field guide has a **deleting & getting it back** chapter — four lessons that make you actually delete things (a whole paragraph, several at once, a block archived, a block moved out to scraps, a whole lesson sent to the Trash and put back) and then show you every road back: the note's own tray, the Trash and its clock, the block's History face with its preserved original and its deleted takes — with every act in it executed by the walk, so a lesson that promises a door the app doesn't have is now a red test.

    **(a) The chapter sits after "structure" and before "ways of looking", and it is called "deleting & getting it back".** That is where its prerequisites have just landed — you have made blocks, fanned them, divided them, and read about the two undos — and the honest next question is how to throw a piece away. **The alternative:** last in the book, as a closing "everything is safe" chapter.

    **(b) One lesson asks you to delete THAT LESSON from the library and Put it back from the Trash.** It is the most reproducible round trip the app has (the note comes back into this book, in this chapter, in its place — the walk asserts exactly that), and it makes the Trash a place you have been before you need it. It also names Help ▸ Restore Tutorial… in the same breath, in case it feels vertiginous. **The alternative:** teach the Trash with a note you made yourself, which nobody has at that moment.

    **(c) The chapter teaches the difference between emptying a paragraph and deleting it, out loud.** A triple-click takes the paragraph AND its line break, and that is what makes it a delete; selecting only the words inside and pressing ⌫ gives you an empty block, still there. Both are shipped behaviour (your ratified #66(b)/(c) sit on the first road), and the lesson says which gesture does which rather than letting the difference be a surprise. **The alternative:** teach only the delete and leave the other case to be discovered.

    **(d) A FIX rode the chapter: the multi-delete receipts now name a preserved original they just stranded.** Writing the lesson traced a gap in batch 6's work — the stranding sentence ("The original preserved from this block's divide rides the Trash with it") was wired into the two single-block delete doors but NOT into the ⌘-click multi-delete or the whole-paragraph-selection road, which are the likeliest doors to strand one: picking a divide's two successors together and deleting them is exactly how a family's last live descendant goes. Both compound doors (Write's and the board's) now say it, pinned red-on-old. **The alternative:** leave it, and have the sentence appear only when blocks are deleted one at a time.

    **(e) Three older lessons were refreshed, bounded, because they had drifted.** "the workspace" now names the ⌫ tray badge that joins the context bar's four constant doors when a note has deleted blocks; "keeping takes safe" now teaches the savepoint cadence you ruled at round 1 (leaving a note leaves quiet savepoints; the ~2 h backstop; the one setting; the collapsed run line) — it had been describing a history with nothing in it but ⌘S; and "the two undos" now names **⇧⌥⌘Z redo** (shipped at 13c and never taught) and the ⌘Z sign at the junction. Every other lesson was checked and left alone; the full site-by-site table is in the batch record.
