Giving it the shared receipt is a two-line change; it is recorded rather than done because a receipt appearing in a new place is a taste call, not a bug fix.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

74. **2026-08-24 · Phase 13l Act 4 (backups, the drill & the deletion-aware restore matrix) · FIVE entries, all shape rather than substance — every RULING you made was built as ruled.** The BINDING record is the 13l plan's "ACT 4 — RECORD".

    **(a) Where the runbook lives, and that it rides in the backup folders.** Your ruling settled the CONTENT (the warning pair, the time-travel sentence, the both-sided deletion truth, the mirror hierarchy); the plan left the HOME to the architect, PROVISIONAL. Built: `notes/DataSafetyRunbook.md` is the authored home, the same text ships inside the app, a test pins the two byte-equal — **and every backup run drops a copy as `RESTORE.md` into both backup folders.** The reason for that last part is the whole point of a runbook: the moment you need a restore recipe is the moment the app may not open, so a copy that lives only inside the app is not one. **No Help-menu opener was built** — a menu item pointing at a repo file is a door that cannot act for anyone but you on this Mac (P-2), and a second copy of the text in code is exactly the drift the docs rules forbid. Alternatives if you want them: a Help ▸ Data Safety window rendering the shipped text (cheap, one more surface), or the repo file alone with no in-folder copy (loses the under-stress case).

    **(b) The Data Safety pane is also the RESTORE FACE.** Your post-sitting ruling asks for the warning pair "in runbook AND restore face" — and there is no restore *face* in the app, because restoring is a Finder operation, not a button (quit, swap the file, open). So the warnings live in Settings ▸ Data Safety, which is where restoring is discussed. **Both warnings always show**, the one that applies to this Mac leading: you may sign in later, and a warning that appears only afterwards is not a warning. If you would rather restoring had a real door in the app — pick a backup, it does the swap for you — that is a genuinely different (and bigger) thing, and it is the natural home of the "browse a backup, copy something out as a new note" candidate you already parked.

    **(c) The second folder's layout, and the freshness colour.** The picked folder receives `<your folder>/Variorum/Backups/` with `RESTORE.md` one level up at `<your folder>/Variorum/` — one tidy thing on the Desktop rather than a drift of `.sqlite` files, and an obvious place for Act 5's `.md` exports to sit beside. The face turns a destination's line ORANGE when its newest copy is more than **two days** old; two is a guess (the job runs daily while the app is open, so two days means either the app was closed or something stopped). Both are one-line changes.

    **(d) The registry gained its FIRST Settings-pane acts, and that is a precedent.** Back Up Now, Choose Folder and Stop Copying There are declared in the capability registry and walked like every other door — they touch your data, which is what a registry of acts is for. But the Appearance and Tags panes have never declared theirs (create/rename/delete tag among them), so this makes Settings half-declared. Options: leave it (declare acts that touch data, not acts that touch preferences — the line drawn here), finish the sweep in a later phase, or drop these three back out. **Nothing is broken either way** — the walk passes because it names its renderers explicitly.

    **(e) The integrity check now leaves the copy in a different journal mode, deliberately.** A backup copy inherits WAL mode from the store, and a read-only open of a WAL file needs a `-shm` it cannot create — so the check that shipped an hour earlier in this act would have failed on *every real copy*. It now opens the copy read-write, moves it to `journal_mode = DELETE`, checks it, and removes any stray sidecars. That makes the artifact genuinely self-contained (the property the runbook promises), at the cost of the check writing one header byte to the snapshot it is checking. The alternative — never touch the copy, and report "could not be checked" honestly — was rejected as useless.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

Original entry: **2026-08-24 · Phase 13l Act 5 (the faces — plain-text insurance, the sync-status line, the secret sanity check) · SIX entries.** The BINDING record is the 13l plan's "ACT 5 — RECORD". Every RULING you made was built as ruled; these are the shapes the brief left to the architect.

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** Settings ▸ Data Safety gained the two lines your kickoff ruling 3 named — **every note as plain Markdown, written into your picked folder once a week** and kept 4 deep, and **an honest sync line** ("everything has reached your account" / "3 changes are still waiting"), which reads as perfectly normal when you are signed out — and Settings ▸ Sync ▸ Account ▸ **Encryption** gained the **check** you asked for in August: type your passphrase or the 12 words, and Variorum tries the real unlock and tells you yes or no, changing nothing.

    **(a) The plain-text export's cadence and retention: WEEKLY, keep 4 — PROVISIONAL, both.** The brief left these unruled and named a lean; this is that lean. Weekly rather than daily because a full library export is bulky and the picked folder may be cloud-synced (a daily one would re-upload every note every day); keep 4 rather than 14 because their job is "the most recent prose exists in plain text", and REACH into the past is what the `.sqlite` snapshots beside them are for. Both are one number each. If you would rather have daily-and-keep-7, or monthly-and-keep-12, say which.

    **(b) No picked folder means NO plain-text copies at all.** The exports go to `<your folder>/Variorum/md-exports/` and nowhere else, so until you pick a folder this does nothing and the pane says what turns it on. The reason is that the alternative — writing them inside the app's own sandbox container — puts plain text on the same disk as the library, in a folder you have no reason to open, dying with the container it is insuring against. **The alternative if you want it:** write them into the container too, so the insurance exists from the first launch.

    **(c) The sync line has NO doors, deliberately.** It says what is true and stops; signing in and forcing a round live on the sync row at the bottom of the sidebar, which is where they have always been. Adding a "Sign in…" button here would be a second road to one act, and the pane's job is evidence. **The alternative:** give it the sign-in door so the answer and the fix sit together.

    **(d) The "still waiting" line is the only one that wears a colour.** Signed out, dev build, and everything-sent all read quietly — a local-only Variorum is a complete Variorum, and a line that looked like a warning would be training you to ignore the colour. A real backlog turns orange. **And a fact worth knowing:** a never-synced store's outbox is never actually empty (the settings seeds sit in it), so a signed-out Mac honestly reports a few changes it would send.

    **(e) The check is NOT declared in the capability registry, and the reason is structural rather than an omission.** `CapabilityID` lives in the Mac target; the whole Encryption surface lives in `Shared/` so the phone renders the same flows — so no door on that surface *can* consult the registry, and none ever has (Unlock, Regenerate, Change Passphrase, Replace Keys, Forget are all undeclared for this reason). Declaring only the new check there would have put an entry in a registry that the code rendering it cannot read. This widens #74(d)'s recorded gap with a mechanism: Settings is half-declared because half of it is Mac-only. **The alternative:** a later phase moves the whole Encryption surface's doors into the registry, which means giving `Shared/` a registry of its own.

    **(f) The check's home is the ENROLLED state only.** In every other state — not enrolled, locked, keys replaced — a real Unlock door is already on screen, and using it verifies the secret by doing the thing. The check exists for the state where neither secret is ever exercised: this device unwrapped once, months ago, and the Keychain has carried it since. **The alternative:** offer it everywhere, and accept that it duplicates the door beside it.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

76. **RATIFIED 2026-08-24 (author, at the polish-round sitting via the question tool): "All three as shipped"** — silent travel stands; the re-key resurrection corner stays recorded-not-closed; graduated notes keep freezing their Fragments day. Original entry: **2026-08-24 · Phase 13l Act 6 (journal-day deletions travel to Fragments) · FIVE entries.** The BINDING record is the 13l plan's "ACT 6 — RECORD". Your RULING was built as ruled; these are the shapes the brief left to the architect, plus one thing the work found.

    **WHAT SHIPPED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** deleting a journal day in Variorum now deletes it in Fragments too — at delete time, once, and **never** when you have written that day in Fragments since (in which case your Fragments words come back to you as a fresh note instead, exactly as they always did).

    **(a) A travelled deletion is SILENT — a count on the round, not a note.** The bridge's notes surface is the "needs attention" line, and a deletion that did what you asked is not attention. So a round reports how many deletions it sent, and says nothing on screen. **The alternative:** say it out loud the first time it happens ("3 days removed from Fragments"), which would be honest but would also mean your status row lights up the first time you run this after deleting anything.

    **(b) THE FIRST ROUND AFTER THIS SHIPS CATCHES UP ON EVERY DAY YOU EVER DELETED — EXCEPT ONE POPULATION, AND FOR YOUR STORE TODAY THE WHOLE QUESTION IS MOOT. *(CORRECTED 2026-08-24 by the end-of-phase ladder — LR-MAJOR-1; the original sentence read "…WILL CATCH UP ON EVERY DAY YOU EVER DELETED" with no exception, which was over-broad.)*** Any journal day the bridge ever agreed on, whose note is gone, travels its deletion on the next round — including ones you deleted months ago, because the bridge kept its record of them. **The exception: a day you deleted BEFORE a re-push staging** (a re-key, or the 14d migration act of 2026-08-16) **never travels at all, and never will** — see (d), which is corrected in the same breath. **What your store actually holds, measured against your newest backup rather than guessed** (`variorum-backup-2026-08-24-013712.sqlite`, read-only, on a copy): **11 bridged days, every one of them carrying a real agreement, none staged, and every one still claimed by a live note.** So there are **zero stranded days and zero deletions waiting to travel** — the catch-up round this entry warns about will be a quiet one, because you have not deleted a bridged journal day. Nothing is at risk either way: each travelled deletion is guarded exactly like a fresh one. **The alternative:** only travel deletions made from today forward, and leave the backlog stale.

    **(c) A CORNER THE WORK FOUND, in the RE-KEY machinery, and did NOT fix.** When you re-key your encryption (or when the 14d migration act ran), the bridge marks every day for a re-push from truth. If a day is **deleted inside that window** while its Fragments row is still ahead of the bridge's read position, that row lands again — and because the marker matches no body, the landing does what it always does with an unrecognised body: it makes a **fresh note** for that day, carrying the day's own last projected text. Content you deleted comes back — from Variorum's own projection, not from anything you typed. It is narrow (only between a re-key and the next round), nothing is lost, and the note is deletable. **I left it alone because fixing it means changing how landings are classified, which is not this act's, and because the alternative — a landing that refuses to re-create a day you deleted — is a product call about whether Fragments can ever resurrect a day at all.** Your call whether a later phase closes it.

    **(d) A deletion caught by a re-push staging does NOT travel — and that refusal is PERMANENT, not a one-round window. *(CORRECTED 2026-08-24 by the end-of-phase ladder — LR-MAJOR-1; the original sentence said "a day deleted in that one-round window", which was wrong about the duration and about who is affected.)*** A marked day has no comparable agreement (its Fragments body is sealed under the old key), and the guard's whole letter is "only when the Fragments side still matches what we last pushed" — so the leg refuses rather than guesses. For a day whose note is still LIVE the mark really is momentary: the next round re-pushes its text and the mark is replaced. **But a day with no note left has nothing to re-push**, so its mark is never replaced by anything, ever — the deletion is refused on every round for the life of the store, and the day keeps its stale Fragments row forever. The affected population is every day deleted **before** a staging as well as during one. **What now happens instead of silence** (the ladder's fix, built): such days are collected and **named on the bridge's status line every round** — "N deleted journal days can no longer be removed from Fragments by the bridge (2026-07-09, …) … Remove them in Fragments by hand if you want them gone." Nothing guesses; the divergence is simply no longer invisible. **The road out is yours — QUESTIONS #78.** (Your store has none of these today; see (b)'s measurement.)

    **(e) "The day still has a note" means ANY note, not any JOURNAL note.** If a journal entry graduates into an ordinary note but keeps its date, its Fragments day is **not** blanked — it still exists, so it was not deleted. What it does instead is what it already did before this act: the Fragments day quietly freezes at its last body, because the push half only follows journal-profile notes. **The alternative** (and it is a real question, just not this act's): make the projection follow a graduated note too, so the day keeps updating.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

## Phase 13l (2026-08-24) — the end-of-phase ladder's call (LR-MAJOR-1's road out)

78. **CONFIRMED 2026-08-24 at the polish-round sitting (author, the full situation re-explained via the question tool): "Keep the disposition" — prevention rides 13p as recorded below.** **DISPOSITIONED 2026-08-24, the morning after (author): *"I think we don't have to solve this right now? … whatever you think is best could probably ride along then [13p]."* The architect's recommended shape, recorded at her ask: **(b) — restaging stops stamping the sentinel onto days whose note is already gone**, so their old agreement survives and deletions still travel VERIFIED. Structural prevention; the guard never weakens; no unverified deletion is ever pushed; and it needs NO Fragments-side change (pure Variorum restage logic). Rides 13p (restaging is re-key machinery — a rider sits in that plan). Her backlog is zero, so nothing waits on it. The original entry:**
78. **May a journal-day deletion travel to Fragments when the bridge can no longer say what Fragments holds?** *(Raised by the end-of-phase ladder, not by a build session; the mechanism is recorded at `BridgeEngine.DeletionScan.stranded` and pinned by `aDayDeletedBeforeARestageIsStrandedForeverAndSaysSo`. **Nothing was built for this** — the engine now says out loud that it cannot act, and stops there, because "delete her writing somewhere else on a guess" is exactly the class that is never decided autonomously.)*

    **THE SITUATION IN ONE PARAGRAPH.** When you re-key your encryption — or when the 14d migration act ran on 2026-08-16 — the bridge marks every journal day it has ever agreed on, so that Variorum's truth re-pushes under the new key. For a day whose note is still live that mark lasts one round. **For a day whose note is already deleted, the mark lasts forever**, because the thing that clears a mark is a successful push and the push follows live notes. That day's deletion is then refused on every round for the life of the store, and its Fragments entry stays exactly as it was.

    **Your store today has none of these** (measured against your newest backup: 11 bridged days, all agreed, all still claimed by a live note). This is a question about the machinery, not a backlog you are living with.

    **(a) Leave it as it now is — refuse, and SAY SO.** The bridge names the stranded days on its status line every round, with the honest sentence "remove them in Fragments by hand if you want them gone". You keep the guarantee that the bridge never blanks a Fragments entry it cannot vouch for; the cost is a line that stays until you act by hand. *This is what ships today.*

    **(b) Let the deletion travel on the PRE-STAGING agreement — i.e. stop staging days that have no note.** The narrow, mechanical version: `stageRepushFromTruth` marks a day so its text re-pushes; a day with no text has nothing to re-push, so marking it accomplishes nothing except destroying the record of what Fragments holds. Skip those rows and their real agreement survives, so the deletion travels normally on the next round. **The risk this trades against:** that agreement is a hash of the plaintext body, and after a re-key the Fragments side holds it sealed under a dead key — so "what we last pushed" is true about the words, and stale about the bytes. The guard is a little weaker than it looks, and only you can say whether that matters.

    **(c) Travel it with no agreement at all.** The bluntest: a bridged day with no note left is a deletion, full stop, and it blanks. This is the one shape I would argue against — it is the exact posture that made the `sync_delete` road unsafe (see #76's record): a push that cannot compare cannot detect a Fragments save it never saw, and the whole guard exists so that a deletion here can never take words you typed there.

    **(d) A different door entirely: a "clean up Fragments" act you press.** The engine keeps refusing automatically, and the stranded list gets a button that travels those deletions on your explicit say-so, one confirm, naming the days. Deletion-by-guess never happens; deletion-by-decision becomes one click instead of a trip to another app. *(This is the most work of the four, and the only one that turns a permanent status line into something you can finish.)*

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

79. *(SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-08-31 — by her OWN pre-13u Act 10 item 10 ruling, built at wave 1: a plain click on a note row's BODY now SELECTS the row and shows the library's info panel (the pick becomes exactly that row — it still has its exit); the TITLE click and a double-click open. The "plain click drops the pick" half below is history; the ⇧/⌘⇧ and List-mode-only halves stand.)* **RATIFIED 2026-08-24 (author, at the polish-round sitting via the question tool): "Ratify all three"** — plain click drops the pick; ⇧-click ranges with ⌘⇧ as toggle; picks live in List mode only (the Notes-cards-learn-picks alternative stays unbuilt, at her word if ever). Her location question answered at the sitting: the gesture lives in the Library's LIST view mode. Original entry: **⌘-click multi-select now works — and three small shapes inside it are yours.** *(P-0, the ladder's fix wave. The door you ruled at 13j's close — "allow for command clicking to select multiple items and deleting all of them" — was DEAD in the shipped build: a tap gesture on the row swallowed the click before the list's own selection saw it, so ⌘-click just opened the note and the bulk bar could never appear. That is fixed and **verified by ⌘-clicking the running app**, not only by tests: two rows highlight, "Tag 2 notes" and "Delete 2 notes…" appear, plain click still opens. The same swallow is why the bulk TAG menu had been unreachable since long before 13l — it works now too.)*

    **(a) A plain click DROPS the pick. PROVISIONAL.** Pick three notes, then click a fourth to read it, and the first three are let go. Every Mac list behaves this way, and without it a pick has no exit but deleting it — the bar would follow you around until you ⌘-clicked each note off again. **The alternative:** leave the pick alone, so glancing at one note does not cost you the other three; the exit then has to be something else (Escape, or a click on empty space).

    **(b) ⇧-click takes the run between two rows, and ⌘⇧-click is treated as ⌘.** The range runs from the last row you ⌘/⇧-clicked to the one you shift-click, in the order the list is showing — and a ⇧-click with nothing picked yet simply picks that row rather than doing nothing. ⌘⇧ together resolves as "toggle this one", because that is the gesture you were told about. None of this was in your ruling; it is what a Mac list does, offered rather than assumed.

    **(c) Leaving the List mode drops the pick too.** Only the List mode draws the selection — the Notes cards and the Timeline do not — so carrying a pick into them would leave "Delete 4 notes…" standing over notes nothing on screen shows as picked. **The alternative** (and it is the bigger version of the same question): teach the Notes cards to show a pick, so multi-select works in the Apple-Notes-shaped list too. That is real design work, not a fix, which is why it is here rather than done.

    **Status: open — walk at the checkpoint.**

80. **2026-08-25 · THE POST-13L POLISH ROUND — your ratify bundle · SIX entries.** The BINDING record is `notes/plans/post-13l-polish-round.md` (the ledger, the ladder's three rounds, the batch records).     **(c) Promote is the Versions pane's only permanent one-way act with NO confirm** (ladder LR1-7). Restore confirms, Delete confirms; Promote fires instantly and version-hood is unrecoverable by pinned derivation. One mis-click beside Restore permanently removes a moment from the listing. The alternatives: a confirm (the confirmation-consistency principle reads either way — it is container-scale but also non-destructive), or leave it fast. **RATIFIED 2026-08-25 (the launch-strategy sitting, her words: "promote having no confirm") — Promote stays fast, no confirm.**

Built to your rulings' letter; the alternative — routing restore's outgoing blocks through the ordinary Trash — is the "Trash noise" your eighth-sitting ruling declined. Her own escape confirmed TRUE at the sitting: **promote-instead-of-restore is the zero-loss road** — promoting the old version mints it as a first-class note, deletes nothing, ancestors survive; the confirm may point at it.

82. **2026-08-30 · PHASE 13p-fix — ONE DECISION, yours: should the store key be BOUND to this Mac?** *(The binding record is `notes/plans/phase-13p-fix-dp-keychain.md`; the mechanism is in `KnownIssuesAndTechDebt.md` at owner 13x.)*

    **What we found while fixing the keychain bug.** The code asks for the store key to be stored `WhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly` — a class whose name promises it never leaves this machine. On macOS that class only applies if the item also sets `kSecUseDataProtectionKeychain` or is synchronizable, and the plain items set neither **deliberately** (moving them would strand real data — including the key to your library). So the class is not in force, and two production comments said otherwise until today. They are corrected.

    **What it means concretely — AND THE SCOPE IS NARROWER THAN THIS ENTRY FIRST SAID (corrected 2026-08-30; the first draft overstated it).** This is **an AUTO-MODE property, not a property of your setup.** Under Auto the plain key item lives in `~/Library/Keychains/login.keychain-db`, which your login password unlocks at login and which **Migration Assistant and Time Machine carry to other hardware** — so a restored backup on a second Mac plus your account password opens the library. Under the DEMANDED modes the plain item is **deleted** by the custody law ("a gate whose key still releases silently is theater"), so that road does not exist: the key lives only in the ACL'd data-protection item behind Touch ID, plus the passphrase/recovery wraps in the envelope sidecar. **You are in Biometric mode and your plain item is verifiably gone**, so nothing of yours is riding the login keychain today. What this decides is what AUTO buys — for the default every future user gets, and for you if you ever switch back.

    **What binding would actually change (stated properly):** the data-protection keychain **does not migrate to new hardware**; the login keychain does. So (b)/(c) do NOT put a prompt in front of Auto — Auto stays silent on this Mac. They mean an Auto key stops *travelling*: a Time Machine restore onto a new Mac would ask for your passphrase (which you have) instead of opening silently, and 13x's adoption road would use the passphrase leg.

    **This may be exactly what you want** — it is also what will make 13x's "adopt the library on a new Mac" pleasant, and it is a *convenience/hardness* trade, not a bug. But it was never decided.

    **(a) Leave it — the key travels with your account.** Convenient across your own machines; the threat it doesn't cover is someone who has both a backup and your account password. *This is what ships today.*

    **(b) Bind it to the device** — migrate the plain items into the data-protection keychain (read without the flag → re-add with it → delete the old), per family, with a round-trip pin. Then a restored backup on new hardware needs your passphrase or recovery phrase, which you have. Costs: a migration touching the key to your library, and 13x's adoption story gets a ceremony.

    **(c) Bind only the STORE key, leave E2EE custody as is.** The store key is the one that opens everything, so it is the one worth surgery. The E2EE device keys are **not a device-unique secret**: they are a local copy of class keys that live wrapped in the `key_vault` table on the account, and your encryption passphrase (or the recovery phrase) unwraps them — that IS what "enrolling a device" does, and it is exactly what you did tonight after the drill clobbered that item. So losing the local E2EE copy costs one passphrase entry; losing the store key with no envelope would cost the library. (c) binds the one that matters and leaves the cheap one alone, which also halves what the migration has to touch.

    **Status: RULED 2026-08-30 (the pre-13u kickoff sitting) — option (c), BIND THE STORE KEY ONLY.** Her words: *"I agree with binding the store key only - it closes the last gap where only a computer password is enough to get your text, which is consistent."* Builds in the pre-13u round's Fable custody batch (Act 9), TN3137 migration round-trip pinned; the iCloud-escrow verification check is pulled into the same round at her word (*"I'm fine with verifying the escrow round trip this phase by the way"*) — escrow is the load-bearing Auto safety net post-binding. Her follow-on (is the nudge enough for Auto users without iCloud Keychain?) is the sitting's R-pre13u-4, pending. The binding record is `notes/plans/pre-13u-round.md`'s RULINGS block.

85. **2026-08-31 · pre-13u Act 0, the Fable `Sync/` half — the two convergence repairs' judgment
    calls, PROVISIONAL under the overnight away-run rules (built to best judgment, bundled here
    for ratify; the binding record is the round plan's "ACT 0 FABLE-HALF RECORD").**

    **(a) The Journal keeper's "same book" predicate: curated + `profile='journal'`, never the
    name.** The one named function is `Queries.dailyJournalBookCandidates`; the mint stamps the
    same constant, so mint and repair cannot drift. Chosen over the mint guard's folded-name leg
    because `BookNaming`'s E13 doctrine keeps deliberate same-name books legal (a name-keyed
    repair "would delete a book she made on her phone" — that doctrine's own words), and a
    renamed real Journal must not shield a duplicate from healing. Pinned from both sides.
    **The one behavioral corner worth your eyes:** restoring a SUPERSEDED old Journal from the
    Trash while a new designated one lives makes two live journal-profile books again, and the
    repair re-converges them at the next sync touch — the restored book's entries merge into the
    keeper (smaller id) rather than standing as a separate book. Defensible under
    one-daily-Journal (nothing is lost; every entry lands in THE Journal), but it means "restore
    my old Journal as its own book" is not a state the system will hold. Ratify or redirect.

    **(a) AMENDED at the ladder's F6 (2026-08-31, BLOCKER — fixed): the predicate above was
    OVER-BROAD and is superseded.** Profile alone is NOT the daily mint's signature: the product
    deliberately mints additional journal-profile notebooks through two of your own doors (the
    Shelf's "Create Journal Book" — §14 item 10's separate notebook — and the phone's `.journal`
    kind), and the profile-only repair MERGED them into the Journal on the next batch touching a
    reading (the larger id retired, its entries moved — possibly into a finished keeper; your own
    device's changefeed echo fired it single-device too). **The predicate is now the mint's whole
    signature** — curated + the journal profile + the default name "Journal" under `BookNaming`'s
    key (ends-trimmed, case-folded; the E13 rule reused) — as ONE pure function,
    `Queries.isDailyJournalMintShape`; the mint stamps exactly that triple, and the three app
    doors share the one profile constant. Two deliberate notebooks now survive their own echo
    (pinned red-on-old). **The corner this creates, for your eyes:** a daily Journal you RENAME
    ("My Journal") no longer converges with a fresh phone mint — the two stand as separate
    journal-profile books until you merge them by hand (pinned as documented behaviour,
    `aRenamedDailyJournalStandsBesideAFreshMint`). The earlier restore-an-old-Journal corner
    above now holds ONLY while the restored book still wears the default name. And the reverse
    edge: a journal-profile book you name "Journal" yourself through Create Journal Book (the
    door allows it only while no daily Journal is live) wears the mint's signature and WILL
    converge with a later fresh mint, as the same book. Ratify the narrowed predicate, or
    redirect — the alternatives are a row-level "born designated" mark (a §12 lockstep event,
    `reading` gains a column) or the profile-only rule with the notebook doors changed to a
    different profile (a product change to what "journal book" means).

    **(b) The pristine rule's engagement extension (R-pre13u-8).** Your ruled rule is per-note
    rendered-text hashes. As built, a copy also reads EDITED on signals the render cannot see —
    a fanned or archived take, a savepoint, a comment, a non-reserved tag, a membership in
    another book, a seal/archive/entry-date, floating notes, a reference, book-level
    extents/finish. Reasoning: a take fanned on shipped text leaves the hash pristine, and
    retiring that copy would silently discard her take — exactly what "an edited copy is NEVER
    silently retired" forbids. Every signal errs only toward keeping copies; the both-pristine
    common case (two untouched seeds) is unaffected.

    **(c) The tag.path loss class, inherited knowingly:** the Journal loser's row metadata (a
    custom name, extents, a finish) and its shelf-pin entry do not travel to the keeper — the
    "worst case a lost rename, never corruption" class the tag.path repair already accepted;
    determinism outranks. The pin dangles harmlessly (liveness-filtered read).

    **(d) The ≥3-copies generalization (sample library):** your ruling covers pairs; with three+
    copies, every EDITED copy survives and PRISTINE copies retire beside ANY survivor (keeper =
    smallest-id pristine only when nothing is edited). The uniform extension of both pair rules;
    vanishingly rare (needs 3+ devices seeding offline).

    **(e) The retirement's Trash shape, and Put back (the ladder's F4, 2026-08-31 — recorded,
    not built).** When the dedupe retires a copy it tombstones the BOOK and its thirteen member
    notes under ONE stamp — a family shape no ordinary op of yours produces. Two consequences:
    **(i)** a dedupe you never asked for deposits fourteen rows in your Trash (deliberate —
    ordinary tombstones, every one recoverable, never purge intents); **(ii)** `restoreReading`
    revives only stamp-matched MEMBERSHIPS, never containers, so Put back on the retired BOOK
    yields a book whose members are still in the Trash — the honest road is fourteen put-backs
    (the book, then each note; a note's own Put back revives its membership by the stamp). The
    Act 0 record's "Put back works" is corrected to this. Your calls: should `restoreReading`
    widen to revive stamp-matched member CONTAINERS (an op-semantics change to a recovery door —
    it would also change what putting back any book whose notes died WITH it means), and is the
    fourteen-row deposit acceptable as is, or should a retired copy row in the Trash as one
    grouped entry (a Trash-listing question, the Versions-under-their-note precedent)? Nothing
    here is built; the retirement stays ordinary tombstones.

    **(f) The engagement rule's narrowing to MANUAL savepoints (the ladder's F5, MAJOR — fixed).**
    The (b) extension counted every `commit_point` as her hand, but the system mints AUTO
    savepoints on untouched seeded text (leaving a story you merely opened; the idle backstop),
    on every device — so in any real store both copies read EDITED and the ruled dedupe never
    fired (two Sherlock books forever, silently). Engagement now counts `kind = 'manual'` only:
    your deliberate ⌘S protects a copy; the insurance occasions do not. Ratify.
