#91 — **KEEP-ON-TOP DIVIDE: what does the TOP block show when the take you divided was not the take the block was showing? (2026-09-07, the use-report round's 18c batch 1 — U-2's one unstated edge; built PROVISIONAL, one line to change.)** U-2 settles where the OTHER takes go (they stay on the top block) and says nothing about which take each successor RENDERS. On Write it cannot come up — the surface shows the chosen take, so the take you divided IS the one the block was showing. On the **Takes board** the caret sits in any take's pane, and on the **phone** a PEEKED take is the displayed one; there, the two are different. **(a) BUILT — each successor shows the take you divided** (`Operations.divideW` gained `displayedVariantID:`, defaulting to nil = its old rule; the ceremony passes nil and is byte-unchanged). Reason: it is what BOTH platforms' shipped default (`divideDuplicating`) has always done, U-2 changes only where the other takes go, and it keeps the receipt's promise literal — *"the take you were in split across both blocks"*. So after dividing a spare take on the board, the page reads the spare's two halves and the take that was showing rides whole onto the top block behind them. **(b) The op's older rule — the top block keeps rendering the take it was rendering** (whole), the bottom block showing the divided take's tail. It is AppSpec §9's written divideW sentence and it changes nothing on screen above the cut; its cost is that the note then reads the paragraph whole and then its tail again, which looks like a duplication bug. **(c) Refuse the gesture from a non-displayed pane** and make her choose the take first — honest, and one more step on a board whose whole point (A9) is dividing the take you are in. **Nothing depends on this outside those two surfaces; the fix either way is one argument.**


#92 — **THE TYPED RETURN'S THREE UNRULED EDGES (2026-09-07, the use-report round's 18c batch 2 — U-1's build; all three built, all PROVISIONAL, each one line to change.)** U-1 says Return makes a block wherever the caret is, and §5.1 spells out the four caret positions; these three cases it does not name, and the pins that hold them are named in the batch record (R-2).

**(a) A non-empty SELECTION + Return.** **BUILT: not this gesture** — the keystroke stays exactly what it was (inside a block, the selection is replaced by a newline; across blocks, the standing cross-block road answers). Reason: it is the precedent 13k K5 set in the same file for the same question (`MarkdownActs.listReturn` treats a selection as ordinary, because "the ruling is about a caret"), and replacing a span is a different gesture from breaking a paragraph. **Alternatives:** (i) delete the selection, then act structurally where the caret lands — what most editors do, and two acts in one keystroke here (the deletion is an ordinary edit, the block act a structural flight); (ii) refuse and say so.

**(b) A block with NO legal interior cut** (a one-word block, caret inside it). **BUILT: the divide door's own refusal, spoken** — S4-22's word-edge floor forbids the mid-word cut, so the planner answers `nothingToDo` and the door says it. Reason: typing a content newline instead would silently re-open the k−1 spacing class the ruling closes, and the refusal already names the doors that CAN act. **Alternatives:** (i) treat it as the block's end (an empty block below — acts, but not what she asked for); (ii) let it be content, one narrow exception.

**(c) An EMPTY open tail** — §5.1's letter says *"Return inside an open tail → the tail's own road, unchanged"*, and its pin (9) (her S1 scenario) requires a block. **BUILT: the two are reconciled by the tail's own mintability** — a tail with text in it still crystallizes (that road MAKES the block, untouched), and an EMPTY tail, which plans nothing at all, is the trap §4.1 traced as her *"several new lines"*: there the key closes that render artifact (the shipped `.closeTail` verdict, no store write) and mints the block. Without this the batch would have left her reported scenario exactly as she reported it. **Alternative:** keep §5.1's letter literally and accept that a note whose tail was left open by the idle mint answers Return with a newline on the previous paragraph — which is the bug this letter exists to fix.


#93 — **"MAKE EVERY NEWLINE A BLOCK": THE LIST EXCEPTION AND TWO NEIGHBOURS (2026-09-07, the use-report round's 18c batch 3 — U-6's build; all three built, all PROVISIONAL, each one line to change.)** U-6 asks for the button and says what happens to the takes (*"any take at the top paragraph of its block"*); U-19 (4) settles that lists stay whole. What §5.1 leaves to the implementer is the exact shape of "whole", the fate of a blank line, and what a bulk door does when ONE of its blocks refuses.

**(a) THE LIST EXCEPTION's exact predicate.** **BUILT: a break sits between two consecutive content lines EXCEPT when they are adjacent — exactly ONE newline between them — and BOTH read as list leads** (`MarkdownSyntax.isListLead`, the K8 law's own predicate: an optional indent run, then `"- "`; checkboxes are the bullet shape and match for free). Consequences, each pinned: a nested item stays with its parent; a **blank line inside a list IS a break** (the exception is "exactly one newline", and a blank line is how a paragraph ends everywhere else in the app); a list's prose neighbours break normally, so an intro line and a closing line each become their own block while the list stays one. **Alternatives:** (i) a blank line inside a list is NOT a break either — a list stays whole across its own blank lines, which reads well and means one gesture cannot separate two lists she deliberately spaced apart; (ii) no exception at all — every newline is a break, and a five-item list becomes five blocks (what she ruled against).

**(b) A BLANK LINE between two paragraphs.** §5.1's parenthetical says *"blank lines vanish into separators"*; **BUILT: they do NOT — the ONE cut brain's k−1 rule stands** (`BoundarySnapping.seamTrimmedHalves`, your A11 ruling of 2026-07-28, whose whole subject was dividing on an empty line putting the blank line NOWHERE). A run of k newlines leaves k−1 on the preceding block as content, the rendering join re-creates the last, and the page reads byte-identically after the repair. Reason, beyond A11 being yours: U-19 (2) — *"the eager deleting of whitespace has already been an annoyance"* — and a repair that disagreed with dividing by hand at the same seam would give one gesture two answers, the class S4-22 closed. **Alternative:** §5.1's letter — the blank line goes and the paragraph gap between blocks stands in for it. Tighter on screen; it costs a byte you typed, and it makes ⌘⇧D at that seam and this button disagree.

**(c) A BULK door where one block refuses.** **BUILT: all-or-nothing, the shipped bulk grammar** — the note-wide door and both selection doors run every block inside ONE `withCompound`, so a refusal (a locked take, a block that moved) rolls the whole repair back and speaks the operation's own reason. It is exactly what `deleteSelectedBlocks` and the merge chain do. **The cost, stated:** a note carrying ONE locked take cannot be repaired at all until you unlock it. **Alternative:** skip the blocks that refuse, repair the rest, and name what stayed behind in the receipt (the `shelveInDailyJournalBook` posture — "it never throws, it returns what happened"). Softer, and it breaks the bulk grammar's promise that a selection acts as one thing.

**A documented corner, recorded rather than fixed:** a break that falls immediately BEFORE an INDENTED list line loses that line's indent, because the one cut brain trims the seam's leading whitespace (`seamTrimmedHalves`'s standing behaviour, which plain ⌘⇧D shares). It needs a list whose very FIRST line is nested — a degenerate shape — and changing it would change every divide door's trim, so it is left as it is.


#94 — **THE LIST BLOCK UNDER U-1's KEY: TWO EDGES THE RULING DID NOT NAME (2026-09-07, the 18c ladder's round 1 — findings R1-3 and R1-4; both BUILT as option (a), PROVISIONAL under the orchestrator's judgment J10; each one line and its pins to flip.)** U-1 says Return makes a block wherever the caret is; U-19 (4) and §5.1 say lists stay whole and the list door "keeps its behavior". Two positions on a list line fall between those sentences, and in both the choice is between **"as it was before U-1"** and **"as Notes does it"** — the implementer's reading, adopted: option (a) in each is a RESTORATION of the shipped pre-U-1 shape, not a new behaviour.

**(a) ⏎⏎ at a list's end — what the list block ends with.** The first ⏎ continues the list (`"\n- "`); the second, on the empty item, retracts the lead (13k K5) and then makes the block (U-1). As batch 2 shipped, the continuation's newline stayed behind as the block's content (`"- one\n"`) — a permanent blank last line on every list exited by the key, so ⏎⏎ after a list read one line taller than ⏎⏎ after prose. Under K5 alone that line was never orphaned: the caret STAYED on it and prose continued there in the same block; U-1 moves the caret into a new block, which is what turned the line into a leftover. **BUILT — (a): the exit retracts the continuation's OWN newline with the lead when the item was the block's last line.** Only that newline: the line before it must itself be a list line (a continuation always follows one), so a blank line you typed above the item stays (U-19 (2)), and an item exited mid-block keeps the seam's k−1 spacing (A11). The block ends at `"- one"` and ⏎⏎ after a list reads exactly like ⏎⏎ after prose. **Chip ON only** (the 18c ladder's round 2, R2-2): under the chip OFF (`one_block` — every journal note by seed) the key is content and never moves the caret, so nothing is orphaned and nothing is retracted — the exit is 13k K5's unchanged: the lead goes and you stay on the empty line (pin `theChipOffKeepsTheListExitAsK5Shipped`). **Alternative (b):** keep the blank line — it is what a Markdown export wants before a following paragraph — and say so in the field guide. Pins: `aListItemsReturnContinuesTheListAndTheEmptyItemEndsItAndMakesTheBlock` · `anEmptyListItemExitsTheListAndThenTheKeyMakesTheBlock` · `returnAtDepthContinuesThenOutdentsThenExits`, with the two guards `anEmptyItemExitedMidBlockKeepsTheSeamsSpacing` · `theExitNeverRetractsABlankLineSheTyped`.

**(b) ⏎ in the MIDDLE of a list item.** The list door claims a list line only at its END (continue / exit / outdent), so a mid-item ⏎ reached U-1's act and DIVIDED the list block — `"- one two"` with the caret after `one` became two blocks, `"- one"` and a bulletless `"two"`. Before U-1 the same key typed a content newline (`"- one\n two"` — the bullet was already lost mid-item, but the list stayed ONE block). **BUILT — (a): a caret anywhere on a list line, short of its end, is the list door's: content, the block stays whole** (the exact pre-U-1 shape; the line's end stays the door's own, and the block's very start still pushes the paragraph down). **Alternatives: (b)** split the ITEM into two bullets in the same block (`"- one\n- two"` — Notes, Bear and Obsidian's gesture; a behaviour you have never had here); **(c)** the batch-2 shape (divide the block). Pins: `aCaretInsideAListLineIsTheListDoorsAndStaysContent` · `aCaretInsideAListItemKeepsTheKeyAsContentAndTheListWhole`.


#95 — **THE BULK BARS' "Merge choosing takes…": TWO OPTIONS OR THREE? (2026-09-07, the use-report round's 18f batch 1 — U-4's button + U-16's two choices; BUILT as option (a), PROVISIONAL, a menu row and a road to change.)** U-4 says merge's summoned door *"should be a button, not just a shortcut"*; U-16 says a merge over SEVERAL blocks offers exactly two choices — bring every take · only the shown ones — *"but i don't want a super complex ceremony for merging multiple blocks together."* Neither says what the door offers when the picked run happens to be exactly TWO blocks on a surface that has no pairing sheet: the Takes board's ⌘-click banner and the Blocks workbench's bulk bar act on a PICKED RUN and have never mounted the pairing columns (which live in Write, where the sheet is).

**(a) BUILT — the two bulk bars offer the TWO run options at every pick size** (a `▾` beside Merge Selected Blocks: *Bring every take* · *Only the shown ones*), and the pairing surface stays on the doors that have always carried it — ⌥⌘⇧M, the menu bar, the block context menu, the ⌘K palette, and (new this batch) Write's bottom-bar `block ▾` row. Reason: the bars' act IS the picked-run act (`mergeSelectedBlocks` / `mergePicked`), U-16 rules that a run gets two choices and never the pairing surface, and a door that offered a third option only when the pick happened to be two would make one bar answer one gesture two ways. Nothing is lectured — both rows act.

**(b) A pair picked on a bar offers three**, with *Pair by hand…* performing the visible carry into Write (the shipped `moveToWriteAndRun` choreography for `.mergeWithCeremony`, which lands at the second block's start). Truer to mockup B's three-option row; it costs the workbench a road to Write it does not have today, and it means the bar's offer changes shape with the pick's size.

**(c) The bars host the pairing sheet themselves** — extract the merge ceremony's state machine behind a host protocol the way `DivideWCeremony` was at 13d Batch 3, so all three surfaces mount one sheet. The architecturally clean answer, and a batch of its own: it is ~250 lines moved plus a conformance per surface, and 18f batch 2 is already rebuilding this sheet's face.

#96 — **THE CEREMONY SHEET'S CARET LANDING: ONE RULE FOR BOTH DIVIDE SHAPES (2026-09-08, the use-report round's 18f ladder — round 1's R1-13, built on the orchestrator's steer, PROVISIONAL; one line to change.)** The instant divide (⌘⇧D, both the plain and the keep-on-top roads) lands the caret at the BOTTOM block's start ("the paragraph I just started" — the divide door's own rule since 13d); the summoned sheet ("Divide choosing takes…", the W ceremony) landed it at the FIRST successor's start (13d's ceremony rule). **(a) BUILT — ONE caret rule: the sheet's confirm lands where ⌘⇧D lands (the bottom block's start); on the Takes board the ring parks on the second successor's RENDERED take** (round 2's R2-4 made that literal: the take the successor actually shows, read off the op's own placement, never `variants[0]`). Reason: the sheet is now the same gesture with a preset row in front of it — "confirming the sheet unchanged reproduces the instant default byte-for-byte" is the letter's own pin, and a different landing for the same rows was the one thing that still differed. **(b) The sheet keeps the first successor's start (13d's rule) and only the presets follow ⌘⇧D** — defensible if you think of "Choose per take…" as a different act whose result you want to review from the top. Pins: `DivideArrangementTests.confirmLandsTheCaretWhereTheInstantDivideDoes` (Write) · `TakesDivideTests.theRingLandsOnTheSecondSuccessorsChosenTakeNotItsFirstContributor` (the board).

#97 — **A PANE GOING LIVE WITH NOTHING REMEMBERED: THE TAKE'S START OR ITS END? (2026-09-08, the use-report round's 17o batch b ladder — round 1's R1-3; the START BUILT, PROVISIONAL, one default to change.)** U-20 says every door into Write lands at the remembered caret offset, and the batch's rule says a surface with no caret "lands at the block's start as today". The ladder found the PANE side of that rule was an accident: when the Takes board (or Read's `edit` face) rang a pane for a block whose caret was never reported — Blocks' row, the Library's Open, the outline's card, a note opened fresh in Takes — the caret sat wherever AppKit's `string =` had left it, the END of the take, and the live pane then REPORTED that end as "where she was", so ⌘1 landed Write at the end of the paragraph (a regression the batch introduced; before it, Write landed at the start). The reported-END half is a bug and is fixed either way. What remains is the product edge neither ruling names: **where a programmatic ring landing puts the caret when nothing is remembered.** **(a) BUILT — the take's START:** Write's own no-memory landing is the block's start (the Act 10 rows 39/49), the batch promised "the block's start as today", and one rule for every surface is the pane's coordinator (`PaneSurface.swift`, `noteLiveness`, applying the `PaneCaretLanding` a host passes as `PaneSurfaceView.caretOnGoingLive` — an `Int?` until the sanity pass's RAISE-2 retyped it; the entry rule that arms `.start` when nothing is remembered is the pure `PaneCaretLanding.entering`, ONE copy for both models since the ladder's round 2, R2-4 — the line a ruling for (b) flips); a CLICK always keeps the click's own place, and the typewriter room still asks for the END explicitly (`.end`). **(b) The take's END** — the N9 "just start typing" reading: an arriving editing surface is for continuing, and appending is what continuing means. It was never ruled; it was AppKit's leftover. If (b) is preferred, the one default flips (`PaneCaretLanding.start` → `.end` where the models arm the note's entry; Write's own nil landing would then disagree with the panes', which is the reason (a) was built). **THE SCOPE, made exact at the ladder's sanity pass (RAISE-2 — the orchestrator's J20, the narrower change built):** the START governs the note's ENTRY landings only — the Takes/Lenses N9 landing on the current block (and its no-fact fall-through), the fan panel's bloom, Read's `edit` face entering on the current block, the Blocks workbench's focused row — the panes that REPORT their caret. **Everything else keeps the shipped END (`.leave` — the selection stays where AppKit's `string =` left it):** the six hosts that pass no landing (the fan and stack cards, the collapsible card, Compare's two panes, Canvas, the Write panel's embedded board, the Book Index's clicked-block-goes-live landing — the Act 10 record's row 22, unchanged), and every POST-STRUCTURAL ring landing on the boards (the survivor after a delete or archive, the merged block, the divide successors, the moved take — "continue at the end of what you just made"), and a `Text`-tap into a NON-current block on Read's `edit` face and the workbench panel. Whether those post-structural landings should ALSO follow (a) is part of this same question — today they are (b) by inheritance, never by ruling. Pins: `PaneSurfaceTests.aProgrammaticTransitionWithNothingRememberedLandsAtTheTakesStart` · `…aHostThatPassesNoLandingKeepsTheShippedEndOnAProgrammaticTransition` · `CurrentBlockContinuityTests.aRingLandingWithNothingRememberedLandsAtTheTakesStartAndWriteFollowsIt` · `TakesLandingTests.theFallThroughLandingAsksForNoScrollAtAll` (the fall-through's `.start`).
*(Dated pointer, 18l-b U4, 2026-09-27: the Book Index's landing named in the `.leave` list above now parks at (a), the block's START, on the warrant of your 18l U0 report — its END was the blank line under the text in your notes. The call is **#153**, PROVISIONAL; every other host in the list is unchanged.)*

#98 — **A MODE ROUND TRIP WHOSE CARET IS OFF THE RESTORED VIEW: TOP THE CARET'S LINE, OR REVEAL MINIMALLY? (2026-09-08, the use-report round's 17o batch b ladder — round 2's RAISE-M1, the orchestrator's J21; the TOP landing BUILT, PROVISIONAL, one call site to change.)** The ladder found and fixed the page's half of her row 7 ("switching from takes to write doesn't leave you where your cursor was"): the session scroll anchor a Write editor saves on leaving was restored against the PRE-FILL frame on every cold mount (a mode switch mints one), clamped at a couple of dozen points, and so never restored — every ⌘2 → ⌘1 in a long note re-topped the page on the caret's block instead of returning to the view she left. Now the cold mount parks the restore for the same layout-settle hop the reveal waits for; the anchor lands first, then the reveal's guard finds the caret on screen and moves nothing — the round trip returns to the view she left. What remains is the product edge neither ruling names: **when the remembered caret is NOT inside the restored view** (she scrolled away from it before switching modes, or the note reflowed while she was away). **(a) BUILT — the caret's line lands at the viewport TOP:** the reveal is `JumpPrimitive.jump`, the Phase 6 hybrid road, whose deliberate shape is "the line at the top" — right for row 20's Blocks → Write jump ("doesn't move you there"), which she asked for and which stays; one reveal, one shape, every door. **(b) Reveal MINIMALLY on a round trip** — scroll only as far as brings the caret's line into view (the bottom edge when it was below, the top when above), the way a caret reveal while typing behaves; the Blocks jump keeps its top landing (a jump is a different act from a return). Costs a second reveal shape keyed on the door. **(c) Do not move on a round trip** — the view she left is restored and the caret stays off screen until she types (AppKit's own caret reveal then scrolls minimally). Honest to "leave the page alone", but "⌘1 then type" would land a keystroke off screen. The one call site: `EditorViewModel.consumePendingReveal` (`JumpPrimitive.jump` at its tail — (b) is a `scrollRangeToVisible`-shaped branch keyed on the bare arrival; (c) is a `return`). Pins: `LandingOffsetTests.aColdMountRestoresTheSessionScrollAnchorAtTheSettleHop` · `…aModeRoundTripReturnsToTheViewSheLeftWhenTheCaretIsInsideIt` (the restored half, RULED by mechanism) · `…aSeededCaretFarDownALongNoteArrivesInsideTheVisibleRect` (row 20's top landing, unchanged whatever she rules here).

#99 — **THE SIDEBAR NOTE BOX: THE DOOR'S ⏎, AND WHAT THE BOX SHOWS WHILE THE INDEX IS LIVE (2026-09-08, the use-report round's 18d batch 1 — U-10's build; both BUILT, both PROVISIONAL, each one line to change.)** U-10 and mockup figure C settle the box's shape; these two edges fall outside both. **(a) THE DOOR'S KEY — built WITHOUT the ⏎.** The mockup labels state 2's one door *"edit here ⏎"* and §5.4 repeats the glyph. A SwiftUI `keyboardShortcut` is a KEY EQUIVALENT, checked *before* the responder chain, so a window-wide Return on a sidebar button steals Return from the masthead's title field, the masthead's `#` tag field and the book Index column's filter — the exact class `SpreadBookView`'s flip shortcut already guards against by refusing to exist while an editor is live. A glyph that promises a key nothing binds is worse than no glyph, so the door ships as **"edit here"**, a button, click-only. Options: **(i) as built** — no key; **(ii) declare a chord in the registry** (⌘⏎ is free and conventional for "go"), which makes the glyph honest and gives the act a real door in the capability walk; **(iii) bind plain Return, scoped to the box** — possible only with focus machinery the sidebar does not have today, and it would still fight a focused text field. `NoteBoxCopy.editDoor` is the one line. **(b) THE INDEX'S LIVE STATE HAS NO BAR, deliberately.** The box's third state is *"the bar moves here"*, and the bar is the full editor's (`CommitBar` over an `EditorViewModel`). Outline and the paged Book mode each mount that editor, so they get the bar. **The book's Index mounts no full editor by design** — it is a projection tier that upgrades ONE BLOCK to a live pane (`BookIndexEditModel`, P-3's one-authority rule), and it has never had a block menu of any kind. So in Index the box's live state shows the note's identity, the *"editing"* mark, and the FACTS (state 2's body) — strictly more than Index has ever shown, and no door is lost because none existed. Options: **(i) as built**; **(ii) give the Index's live block a bar of its own** — a second bar over a different authority, i.e. a new surface and its own acceptance unit, not a line; **(iii) do not show the box in Index at all**, which would take back the identity and facts it now gives her there. The line: `SidebarNoteBox`'s `.live` branch, which falls through to the facts when it is handed no bar.

#100 — **THE TRAY'S ARCHIVED VIEW: "ARCHIVING A BLOCK", OR "A BLOCK OR A TAKE"? (2026-09-08, the use-report round's 18d batch 2 — U-9's build; (a) BUILT, PROVISIONAL, one string to change.)** U-9 and mockup figure D settle the tray's shape; figure D's own caption words the archived view's explainer *"Kept, no clock. Archiving a block or a take keeps it here with no countdown."* The tray's archived population is `Queries.archivedBlocks` — archived BLOCKS of this note. An archived TAKE has never been the tray's to list: it lives among its block's archived takes, in Block History, which is exactly what the shipped `RecoveryCopy.takeRestoredArchived` tells her when a restored take lands there. So the mockup's sentence names a population this view cannot hold, and the batch's brief fences what the tray holds ("no change to what the tray HOLDS").

**(a) BUILT — the sentence says BLOCK**: *"Kept out of this note, with no clock. Archiving a block keeps it here with no countdown."* — and the archived · takes cell, which is empty by construction, says where they DO live (*"Takes aren't archived here — an archived take stays with its block, under Archived takes in Block History"*), so nothing is merely denied. Reason: naming a state the surface cannot produce is the exact defect `NoteTrayTests.theTraysSectionsNameOnlyStatesTheProductCanProduce` has existed to catch since 13j batch 6, and that pin gained an arm for it here. The shipped `trayNothingArchived` had said "a block" all along.

**(b) The ARCHIVED view grows archived takes** — a per-note listing of `variant.status='archived'` beside the blocks, with `unarchiveVariant` (it exists, `Operations+Deletion.swift:55`) as its Put back — and figure D's caption becomes true. This is a change to what the tray HOLDS: a new query, a new put-back road and its receipts, a filter cell that is no longer empty, and the question of whether Block History's archived region then shows the same rows twice. Its own acceptance unit, not a string.

**(c) Drop the `takes` chip from the Archived view** — no empty cell, no sentence to get wrong. Figure D draws all three chips in BOTH views, and the architect's shape calls the filter "one row shared by both views"; a control that changes shape when the view changes is what (a) keeps it whole to avoid.

#101 — **THE DRAGGED COLUMNS' MEMORIES: HOW MANY, AND WHOSE? (2026-09-08, the use-report round's 18d batch 3 — U-13's build; both parts BUILT, PROVISIONAL, one line each to change.)** U-13 says *"the panel with the chapter names should also be draggable to be a bit bigger"* and *"table of contents in read mode for a note is horizontally limited and not changable, should be adjustable."* It settles that the columns drag; it does not settle what REMEMBERS the width. Two edges fall outside it.

**(a) THE BOOK HAS THAT PANEL TWICE, AND THEY KEEP SEPARATE MEMORIES — as built.** By wave 2c's own rule the minimal navigator STEPS ASIDE in Index, where *"the index column IS the outline pane grown up"* — so the panel with the chapter names is the navigator in Outline · Book · Read, and `BookIndexView`'s column in Index. Both became the shared leading `SideColumn`, and each remembers its own width (`bookNavigatorWidth`, 190…360, opening at 190; `bookIndexColumnWidth`, 240…460, opening at 300). **Reason:** they are not the same panel — the index column carries a filter field and a per-row count, which is why it shipped 110 pt wider — and one memory would mean dragging the navigator narrow silently squeezed the index's filter field. **Alternative (b): ONE memory for "the book's left panel"**, so the boundary never moves when she changes tab; it costs the two panels their different natural measures (the shared width would have to satisfy the wider one's floor). **(c) Per BOOK**, so a 13-chapter book and a three-note book can differ; that is a new key shape (`bookNavigatorWidth.<readingID>`) and the first thing it does is forget when she makes a new book. The lines: `BookNavigatorLayout.widthKey` and `BookIndexColumn.widthKey`.

**(b) ONE POSTURE FOR EVERY BOOK AND EVERY NOTE — as built.** All three widths (the two above and Read's `readModeIndexColumnWidth`, 240…640, opening at 340) are single global keys, the `readModeOutlineShown` / `shellSidebarWidth` class: how wide she wants a contents list is how she reads, not a fact about one note. **Alternative:** per-note / per-book keys, the `postMode.<id>` / `readFace.<id>` class — right if she wants a long-titled book wide and a journal narrow, and the same forgetting cost as (c) above. Local-only either way (UserDefaults, never synced — the `CurrentBlockMemory` class). Pins: `OutlineRoomTests.theNavigatorIsTheWidthSheLeftItAtAcrossARemount` · `…theIndexsChapterPanelIsTheWidthSheLeftItAtAndKeepsItsOwnMemory` (which asserts the two memories are separate) · `…theReadContentsColumnIsTheWidthSheLeftItAtAcrossARemount`.

#102 — **THE DECORATION LANE ON EVERY READ PAGE: MAY A NOTE'S MEASURE SHIFT 64 PT BECAUSE IT CARRIES A COMMENT? (2026-09-12, the use-report round's 17p batch b — R-Q4's build; raised at the batch-b ladder, R1-6; the shipped arm stands PROVISIONAL, one line to change.)** Her R-Q4 rules the BOOK OUTLINE: *"the book Outline's inline editor reserves ~40 pt on the left whenever the note has a comment or a callout emoji; the badge sits in the lane; the emoji moves inside the callout frame's leading inset; nothing covers text; comments stay on the left."* The batch's walk found that the Outline shows an unfocused note through the READ TIER (`ReadPostView` → `PaneTextView.makeReadSurface`), so the lane was opted in at that FACTORY — and the read tier is also the workspace's read-cheap pane (`RootView.readCheapProse`), the note window's read face (`NoteWindowView`) and the spread/paged book (`SpreadBookView`). On every one of those, a note with a comment or a callout glyph now starts its prose 72 pt from the surface's edge where an uncommented note starts at 8 — the page's left edge moves per note. The overlap defect existed on those pages too (prose at 8 pt, decorations clamped at 2 — the badge on the words), so the shipped outcome is the spec-defensible one (*"nothing covers text"*); but whether a Read page's MEASURE may vary by note is a face decision no ruling makes, and the implementer recorded it as an accident of scope, not a choice.

**→ RATIFIED (a) 2026-09-13, her morning sitting (R-Q16): "Keep it everywhere" — nothing covers text on any page.**

**→ SUPERSEDED IN DIRECTION 2026-09-14 (her 13u1 kickoff message — #108 / R-13u-9): comments and callouts move into the whitespace GUTTER and never move the text — the lane-reserves-measure approach (this question's whole option space) is out. "Nothing covers text" survives as a constraint; the mechanism is 13u3 item 3's rework.**

**(a) BUILT — the lane rides every read surface, reserved only while the note carries a comment or a callout glyph** (`PaneTextView.makeReadSurface` sets `reservesDecorLane`; the trigger is `BlockDecor.needsLane`, the brain `DecorLaneReservation`). Reason: it is R-Q4's letter applied to every surface that had the defect, and an uncommented note keeps its whole measure. Cost: a commented note's page sits 64 pt right of its neighbours' in the workspace pane, the note window and the paged book; in a book Outline, commented and uncommented notes have different left edges.

**(b) The read tier reserves the lane ALWAYS** — one line (`needsLane` answering true on a read surface, or the factory reserving at construction) — so every Read page has ONE stable left edge and a comment arriving never moves the text. Cost: every uncommented note on every read surface gives up 64 pt of measure for a lane it never uses — R9's own concern from the other side (the margins are sacred, and so is the measure; 68ch was the mockups' number).

**(c) The lane is the BOOK OUTLINE's alone** — `BookView` opts its read tiers in (a parameter on `ReadPostView` down to the factory) and the workspace pane, the note window and the paged book keep the shipped 8 pt prose, their comment count drawn by the COMPOSITION in the SwiftUI pad beside the surface rather than by the overlay inside it. More machinery, and the pages R-Q4 did not name would need their own rule for where a comment shows.

The line: `App/Variorum/Variorum/Surface/PaneSurface.swift`, `makeReadSurface` (`view.reservesDecorLane = true`); the sweep pin `BlockDecorLaneTests.everyDecoratedSurfaceHasItsLaneAnsweredOneWayOrTheOther` names the read tier's cell and changes with the answer.

#103 — **THE NOTES CARD'S ONE DATE SLOT: THE SORT'S STAMP, THE STATED DAY, OR BOTH? (2026-09-12, the use-report round's 18h batch 1 — R-Q7/R-Q8's build; (a) BUILT, PROVISIONAL, one line to change.)** Her R-Q7 settles the Library's List row (*"a muted trailing stamp"*) and the note masthead's; the brief's rule for what a row's stamp SAYS is *"the sort's own — edited under edited/title, created under created, the entry date under date."* The **Notes mode's card** (13g R5-6, her Apple-Notes-shaped list) has only ONE date slot, beside the title, and it has always shown `hit.detail` — the note's **entry date**, the day she stated. Making it read the one brain (which the brief also asks for) therefore REPLACES a fact rather than adding one: under the edited sort the card now says *"edited today 1:22 PM"* where it used to say *"2026-09-09"*.

*(18h ladder round 3, R3-2 — for 17q's build: the List row still prints the raw stored `entry_date` beside the title while the Notes card speaks the one brain's words, so the two modes word one fact in two grammars and under the `date` sort the row shows the same day twice — the row's slot goes through `TimeStampFace` in the same act.)* **→ RESOLVED at 17q**: the slot is a `CatalogRowStamp` like every other time-word in the Library, and the caller hands it `RowStamps.listDay`, which is nothing under the `date` sort.)

**→ RULED 2026-09-13 (R-Q17), her words: "in the library / pool there's enough room to show both. if the area is small, the same UI i mentioned earlier of clicking on the time should switch to show you the date created or the date associated with the note" — the card shows the stated day AND the sort's stamp where there is room, the click-flip where small; the build is 17q's (the successor session).**

**→ BUILT 2026-09-12 at 17q**, to her letter: ONE rule (`LibrarySortFace.rowStamps`) serves BOTH note renderers. The Notes card offers two arrangements to `ViewThatFits(in: .horizontal)` — **both facts beside the title** where the row has room, and **ONE click-flipping stamp** where it does not (the masthead's own grammar: it opens on the SORT's own stamp and a click shows the other, a click again returns; a note with only one fact gets no flip door at all — a door that could not act is not rendered). **The List row's beside-the-title slot now speaks the same brain** (the 18h ladder's R3-2, resolved: it printed the raw stored `2026-01-15` beside worded stamps) and **stands down under the `date` sort**, where the trailing stamp is already saying that day. The List row's TRAILING stamp and the masthead's stamp are byte-unchanged. Pins: `LibraryCardStampsTests` (5).

**Three calls 17q made inside her ruling, all PROVISIONAL and each one line to change:**
**(i) WHERE THE PAIR SITS — reading (i) of the R1-17 clause below**: both beside the title, the shape she pointed at. (ii)/(iii) are one `Spacer` away.
**(ii) WHAT THE SECOND FACT IS UNDER THE `date` SORT**: there the sort's own stamp IS the stated day, so showing "both" would be showing one fact twice. The honest second is the **EDIT stamp** — so under that sort the card reads *dated Jan 15 · edited Jul 1*. (The alternative is to show the day alone there, which makes the date sort the one sort whose cards carry less.)
**(iii) THE NO-STATED-DAY EDGE, answered as a CONSEQUENCE rather than as a special case** — see "The related edge" below: under the `date` sort a note with no `entry_date` used to show no stamp at all; the one rule now gives it the EDIT stamp, because "the other fact" is what fills the second slot and the first is silent. That is the direction this question floated ("show `created …` for those rows") arrived at from the other end. If she wants the silence back it is the same one line.** *(The 17q ladder's R1-6, the other half of this edge: the LIST ROW stays SILENT for the same note under the same sort — its trailing stamp is `rowStamp(.entryDate)`, nil-stamped when she stated no day, and its beside-the-title slot is nil under the `date` sort by R3-2's letter — so the card says "edited …" where the row says nothing. The row cannot be given the edit stamp from the `listDay` slot without printing one day twice on every DATED row (R3-2 again), and its trailing stamp is the 18h face 17q was fenced from; making the row match the card is therefore a change to that fenced slot — hers, beside (iii), never a one-line tidy. The words stay one brain either way; only which FACT each renderer states differs.)*

**(a) BUILT AT 18h, SUPERSEDED BY HER RULING ABOVE — the card's one slot was the SORT's stamp**, worded by `TimeStampFace` (`CatalogRowStamp` with `LibrarySortFace.rowStamp`). Reason: it is what she asked for in her own comparison (*"I like how apple notes does it where you can see the time it was last edited"*), it is the same words the List row's trailing stamp shows so the two modes cannot disagree, and the stated day is still one click away in the note (and is what the `date` sort shows). Cost: a journal-shaped note whose NAME is already its day now shows that day twice under the date sort and not at all under the others.

**(b) The card keeps the entry date and GAINS the stamp** — two dates on the card's line (`2026-09-09 · edited today 1:22 PM`), or the stamp at the card's trailing edge beside the book names. Nothing is lost; the card gets busier, which is the opposite of the Apple-Notes shape she pointed at.

**(c) The card's slot is always the EDIT stamp**, whatever the sort — one fact, always the same fact, and the sort's own stamp lives only on the List row. Simplest to read; the cost is that sorting the Notes mode by created shows an order the cards cannot explain.

**The related edge, same line:** under the **date** sort a note with NO `entry_date` shows no stamp at all (the sort orders it by `COALESCE(entry_date, created_at)` but the row will not print a day she never stated — "dated Sep 8" would be the app claiming a fact it does not hold). If that silence reads as a bug rather than as honesty, the answer is to show `created …` for those rows, which is one branch in `LibrarySortFace.rowStamp`.

The line: `LibraryCatalogView.noteCard` (the `CatalogRowStamp` in the title's `HStack`) and `LibrarySortFace.rowStamp` in `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Library/LibraryTimeFace.swift`.

**One clause this question never carried, added at the 18h ladder (R1-17) for whoever builds R-Q17:** her R-Q7 says *"the Library rows get a muted **trailing** stamp too"*, and on the **List row** it is trailing — past the `Spacer()`, at the row's right edge. **On the Notes card it is not:** it sits immediately after the title, before the `Spacer()`, where the book names hold the trailing end. That was deliberate (the Apple-Notes card she compared it to puts the date beside the title) and this question was framed as *which fact* only — so her word "trailing" is unanswered on the one surface that diverges from it, and R-Q17's *"there's enough room to show both"* settles the FACTS without settling where the pair sits. The three readings: **(i)** both beside the title, as the card does today — the shape she pointed at; **(ii)** the stated day beside the title, the stamp past the `Spacer()` — "trailing" becomes true on both surfaces, at the cost of putting a card's two dates at opposite ends of one glance; **(iii)** both past the `Spacer()`, beside or instead of the book names. Same line as above: `LibraryCatalogView.noteCard`'s title `HStack`.

#104 — **WHILE A DAY IS ARMED, THE LIBRARY IS NOTES ONLY — IS THAT THE RIGHT NARROWING? (2026-09-12, the use-report round's 18h batch 2 — R-Q8's build; (a) BUILT, PROVISIONAL, a few lines to change.)** Her R-Q8 rules the filter itself (*"a date field in the Library header ('on day…'), the union query — the last-edit stamp OR a savepoint that day"*). What it does not say is what happens to the OTHER two kinds the Library lists: a book and a block carry no edit stamp a day could be compared against (a book is a pointer list; a block hit's `updated_at` is not a note's edit day and is not what "notes edited on a day" asks about). So the arm had to decide.

**→ RATIFIED (a) 2026-09-13 (R-Q18): "Notes only", with the picker moved to posts on arming (the 18h ladder's R1-3).**

**(a) BUILT — a day names NOTES: the books and blocks arms stand down while one is armed, and the scope picker stands down with them** (`Queries.searchCatalog` skips both arms when `editedOn != nil`; the header's scope `Picker` is `.disabled` with a help line saying why). Reason: a listing that answered *"on Sep 9"* with every book in the shelf would be a lie by juxtaposition — the day would read as if it applied to them. It also makes the day search ONE statement instead of three (pinned: `LibraryDaySearchTests.theDaySearchIsOneStatementWhereTheOpenCatalogIsThree`). Cost: the scope picker greys out, which is a control changing state for a reason she has to read the tooltip to learn.

**(b) The books and blocks arms still list, unfiltered, beside the day's notes** — nothing greys out and the scope keeps working. Cost: the header says "on Sep 9" over a section of books that have nothing to do with Sep 9, and the answer to "what did I write that day" is buried under them.

**(c) Arming a day FORCES the scope to Notes** (and restores her previous scope when she clears it) — the same outcome as (a) but stated in the control she can see, rather than by disabling it. More state (the remembered previous scope) and a control that moves under her hand, which the app's own walk standard treats as a hazard.

**AMENDED at the 18h ladder's round 1 (R1-3(b), 2026-09-13) — the shipped arm (a) now also MOVES a scope already set:** arming a day with `Books` or `Blocks` selected sets the scope to `Notes` (`LibraryCatalogModel.setEditedOn`). The shipped arm said the picker stands down and never said what happens to a VALUE already pointing at a kind the day cannot answer — and that case was an EMPTY listing under a disabled picker with ✕ the only escape, plus (until the same fix) a confident sentence about the day under it. Option (c)'s "forces the scope" is therefore half-built: the value moves, and it does NOT move back when she clears the day (no remembered previous scope — a control that moves twice is worse than one that moves once). If she prefers (b) — books and blocks listed unfiltered beside the day's notes — that is still one line, and the scope move goes with it. Pin: `LibraryDayFilterTests.armingADayMovesABooksScopeToNotes`.

The line: `Sources/VariorumCore/Queries/ShelfQueries.swift` (`searchCatalog`'s two `editedOn == nil` guards) and the scope `Picker`'s `.disabled(model.editedOn != nil)` in `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Library/LibraryCatalogView.swift`. The pin that changes with the answer: `LibraryDaySearchTests.onlyNotesAnswerADay` and `LibraryDayFilterTests.theClearDoorReturnsTheWholeLibrary`.

#105 — **THE NOTES CARD'S SMALL ARRANGEMENT NEVER FIRES IN THE SHIPPED WINDOW: WHICH YIELDS FIRST, THE TITLE OR THE DATES? (2026-09-12, 17q — her R-Q17's second arm, measured on the walk; (a) BUILT, PROVISIONAL, one modifier to change.)** Her R-Q17 has two arms: *"in the library / pool there's enough room to show both. **if the area is small**, the same UI i mentioned earlier of clicking on the time should switch to show you the date created or the date associated with the note."* 17q built both — the pair where there is room, ONE click-flipping stamp where there is not, chosen by `ViewThatFits(in: .horizontal)` and pinned at both widths (`LibraryCardStampsTests`, the component at 520 pt and at 120 pt).

**What the walk measured (17q's captures `11` · `14`, and the window probe):** the SECOND arm never fires in the app. The card's title row is `title · stamps · Spacer · book names`, and the title is `lineLimit(1)` and compressible while the stamps are `fixedSize` — so as the room runs out **the TITLE yields and the stamps keep their ideal width**. With a deliberately long title at a 900 pt window (and again at 760) the card read *"A deliberately long note title for the narrow… dated sep 22 edited sep 8"* — the title truncated, both stamps whole. And the window cannot go small enough to change that: below ~700 pt the shell's own minimum content width takes over and the catalog is CLIPPED rather than reflowed, so no reachable window width ever hands the stamps a proposal small enough for the flip arm to win.

**→ RULED (a) 2026-09-12 (R-Q22), her pick: "Keep as built" — the title truncates, both stamps always show on the Mac; the flip arm stays pinned for narrower hosts.**

**(a) BUILT — leave the ranking as it is.** The flip arm ships, pinned, and is what a narrower host renders (the 13u2 restyle, a future panel, the phone's own version); the card she sees today always shows both facts and truncates a long title. Reason: this is the ranking the card has had since 18h put a stamp on it, 17q changed nothing about it, and reversing it is a taste call her ruling does not make. Cost: her *"if the area is small"* arm is invisible in the Mac app as shipped.

**(b) The STAMPS yield first** — `.layoutPriority(-1)` on `CatalogCardStamps` in `noteCard`'s `HStack`, so the title keeps its words and the pair collapses to the one flipping stamp when the room runs out. This is the reading that makes both of her arms visible, and it treats the title as the note's identity (which the Apple-Notes card she pointed at does). Cost: with a very long title the stamps get almost nothing and `ViewThatFits` draws its smallest arrangement clipped rather than not at all; and there is no mounted pin road for the card's own composition (the rig cannot mount the catalog — KnownIssues), so the evidence would be a walk capture, not a pin.

**(c) A WIDTH THRESHOLD instead of `ViewThatFits`** — the card measures its own width once and picks the arrangement from a named number. Deterministic and pinnable, and it costs a second layout pass plus a stored width (the first frame draws the wide arrangement, then collapses), which is the flicker `ViewThatFits` exists to avoid.

The line: `LibraryCatalogView.noteCard`'s title `HStack` (the one modifier) and `CatalogCardStamps` in `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Library/LibraryTimeFace.swift` (the two arrangements, unchanged under every option).

#106 — **WHERE DOES A RECEIPT GO ON A SURFACE WITH NO BOTTOM BAR? (2026-09-13, the use-report round's 18i — R-Q5's build; (a) BUILT, PROVISIONAL, and the road to (c) is composing one existing view in four more places.)** Her R-Q5 settles the setting and its default (*"Quiet should be the default, but toast should still be an option"*) and names where a quiet receipt goes: *"more subtle in the bottom bar in more muted text"*. 18i built exactly that — ONE `QuietReceiptLine` at the trailing end of **Write's bar**, the **takes/lenses bar**, and the **book's sidebar note box footer** (which is Write's own bar in its box axis).

**What the ruling does not say, and the build had to decide:** several surfaces have no bottom bar at all — **Canvas**, **Compare**, **Read**, **Blocks**, and every shell surface (**Library · Shelf · Pool · Archived**, the Quick Note pad). A receipt spoken there under `.quiet` — *"✓ chosen — the note shows this take everywhere now"* on the Canvas, *"Undid: Divide"* from the Library, *"Filed in Quicknotes"* as the pad closes — has no slot to land in, and today it is simply not shown. Under `.toast` and `.off` every surface behaves uniformly; this is a `.quiet`-only gap, and `.quiet` is the default.

**→ RULED (a) 2026-09-13 (R-Q23), her pick: "Quiet means quiet" — not shown on a bar-less surface; no new chrome; refusals toast everywhere.**

**(a) BUILT — quiet means quiet; a receipt with no slot on screen is not shown.** Quiet is a weaker `Off`, and `Off` is a state she deliberately allowed. The surfaces that produce the receipts she complained about — Write's joins and divides, the takes board's structural acts, the newlines chip — all carry a bar, so the common case is covered exactly as she asked. Cost: an act run from a bar-less surface confirms nothing under the default. Refusals are untouched everywhere (they always toast), so nothing that needs an answer is ever lost.

**(b) Fall back to the toast when no quiet slot is on screen.** Nothing is ever unsaid; the toast returns only where it has nowhere quieter to go. Cost: it needs a mounted-slot count on the status centre, and that count is **app-global while the windows are not** — a note window's bar would suppress the main window's fallback, so a receipt could vanish because a *different window* had a bar. That is a defect class, not a detail, which is why it was not built.

**(c) Give the bar-less surfaces a quiet slot of their own** — the same one view composed into a footer on Canvas, Compare, Read and Blocks, and into the Library's own caption row. The complete answer, and no new mechanism: `QuietReceiptLine` already takes only a status centre and tokens. Cost: it invents a strip of chrome on four surfaces that deliberately have none today (where on the Canvas?), which is a design call rather than a build one — yours, not a batch's.

The line: `App/Variorum/Variorum/Core/Views/QuietReceiptLine.swift` (the view, unchanged under every option) and its three composition sites — `Features/Editor/CommitBar.swift` (`quietReceipt`, the three tiers and `boxFooter`) and `Features/Takes/TakesBottomBar.swift`. The pins that change with the answer: none — `ConfirmationsSettingTests` pins the routing and the slot's geometry, neither of which moves.

#107 — **DOES A PALETTE REACH THE CALM READING PAGE? (2026-09-13, the use-report round's 18g — R-Q6/R-Q11/R-Q13's build; (a) BUILT.) — RULED (a) by the author 2026-09-14 (her R-Q26, verbatim: *"Keep the page cream (Recommended)"*): the page stays cream under every palette; nothing to build.** Her R-Q6 names what a palette recolours: *"the room (the tan panels), the wash, the accent (the coloured chrome words), the take tint/line and the hairline"*, and the sitting's mockup shows exactly that — every card's writing page stays the voice's own paper while the panels around it take the hue. 18g built it that way, and in light the note's paper is untouched by every one of the sixteen palettes.

**What the ruling does not say, and the build had to decide:** the **calm Read page** (the note's Read ▸ `read` sub-mode, the book's Read, the paged/spread book) does not draw from `VoiceTokens` at all. It has its own token type — `ReadingPageTokens` (cream paper, its own ink, its own serif), chosen by the colour SCHEME alone (`ReadModeView.swift:118`: `scheme == .dark ? .dark : .light`) and never routed through the theme store. That is deliberate and old: R2 made the reading page's identity **voice-independent** — *"the calm page for reading or quiet editing, reachable from any book in either voice"* — and only the ROOM around it follows the voice. A palette therefore stops at the page's edge: the room around the page takes the hue, the page itself stays cream. *(Determined by READING the type and its two call sites, not by a walk: the `edit ▸ read` pill did not take a synthetic click in the walk rig, so the capture `18g/t-light-teal-rust-read-page.png` shows the `edit` half under Teal & rust — palette-aware — and not the calm page.)*

**(a) BUILT — the calm page stays cream under every palette.** It is R2's letter: the reading page is a *place*, not a theme, and it reads the same in either voice; a palette is colour under a voice, so it inherits that boundary. It is also what the mockup shows for the writing page — paper is paper. Cost: a note read under Rose & moss has a strongly pink room around a cream page, which can read as two themes meeting at the page's edge rather than one; and the one surface she is most likely to sit inside for a long time is the one the palette never touches.

**(b) The palette tints the reading page's paper too** — `ReadingPageTokens` gains the same `(palette, tint)` parameter the voice resolver took, and its paper/chapter-ink derive from the room seed at the page's own (much lighter) lightness. One parameter, one call site, and the contrast floor already exists to keep the ink honest. Cost: it bends R2's voice-independence — not by much (the hue would be the palette's, not the voice's), but it is a ratified line and bending it is yours, not a batch's.

**(c) A palette-aware page only for the BOLD builds**, where the mismatch is loudest, and cream elsewhere. Rejected on sight as a per-preset special case — exactly the shape F-7 forbids in the derivation layer — but recorded because it is the instinct the walk provokes.

The line: `App/Variorum/Shared/Theme/VoiceTokens.swift`'s `ReadingPageTokens` (the type, unchanged under (a)) and `Features/ReadMode/ReadModeView.swift:118` (the one place the appearance picks it). The pins that change with the answer: none today — `PaletteDerivationTests` measures the families, and no pin asserts the reading page's paper.

*(2026-09-14 refinement, her 13u1 kickoff message — #108: the page stays cream (her R-Q26 stands) AND the BACKDROP behind it stays white/neutral, never the palette room — "leave that white so that the cream read page isn't on a colorful background." Owner: 13u2 item 19.)*

#109 — **A SELECTION WITH AN END SITTING ON A BLOCK BOUNDARY (2026-09-14, 13u1 UNIT 2 — item 5's build; (a) SHIPPED, PROVISIONAL. Title and scope CORRECTED at the 13u1 ladder, round 1: this entry first claimed the residual was "the ONE cross-block shape … touching the characters of only ONE block" — it is a FAMILY, and part (d) below names the wider member the first wording missed.)** Her item 5 ruling ended the cross-block refusal for *"selecting a bunch of visible text and deleting"*, and the compound now takes every span that **opens and closes inside block text** and reaches into two or more blocks. **What is left is the span with an end sitting ON the boundary separator**, in two sizes: **(i)** touching the characters of only ONE block — dragging from the middle of a paragraph and releasing exactly where the NEXT one begins (so the span is "the tail of A, plus the separator"), or the mirror at the other end; **(ii)** reaching into TWO OR MORE blocks' characters while opening (or closing) ON the separator — anchor the selection at a paragraph's END (the offset before the newline, an everyday anchor) and ⇧-extend into the paragraph after next: the span is "the separator, plus B whole, plus part of C", and the compound's contiguity guard refuses it exactly as the retired planner always did. Both keep the pre-item-5 refusal, worded since the ladder for what every member shares (*"One end of this selection sits on the boundary between two blocks. Nudge that end one character either way, or select the whole paragraphs."* — the ladder's round 1 found the first re-narrowed sentence claiming shape (ii) "does not reach into the next block", false there, with a road that changed nothing).

**Why it is a question and not a bug — and this paragraph speaks of shape (i)** *(scoped at the ladder's sanity pass: shape (ii) has the cleaner road part (d) describes)*: for the span touching only one block's characters, the obvious fix — widen the run to include the neighbour the span merely touches — has no good answer under **R-13u-2**. The cut at that end sits exactly at a paragraph break, so her ruling says the ends must NOT merge; the gesture would then delete a separator and leave the two blocks apart, i.e. *the newline you selected did not go away*. That reads as a bug even though it follows the rule, which is why the shape wants a ruling rather than a patch.

**(a) SHIPPED — keep the refusal, and say precisely what it is.** It is the pre-existing behaviour (verified on the pre-fix tree: both `planCrossBlockEdit` and the retired `planVisibleTakeEdit` declined on the same two guards), so nothing regressed, and the sentence names the real geometry — since the ladder's correction, for BOTH sizes of the shape — and offers the two roads out: nudge that end one character either way, or select the whole paragraphs (which is J9's delete road, and works). Cost: a drag that happens to land on a boundary refuses where a drag one character off it works, and the difference is invisible to the eye.

**(b) Take the span, and DELETE THE SEPARATOR TOO — i.e. join.** "She selected the newline, so the newline goes" is the plain-editor reading and it makes the gesture feel like every other editor. Cost: it CONTRADICTS R-13u-2 as written (the cut at that end is at a paragraph break), so it is a change to your ruling, not an implementation of it.

**(c) Take the span, honour R-13u-2, and leave the blocks apart** — the end loses its tail, the neighbour is untouched, the separator stays. Rule-faithful and cheap (the run widens by one entry). Cost: the separator she selected visibly survives the delete, which is the *"why didn't that go?"* class; and the widened run would apply the lock/gap fences to a block the gesture never edits, so a locked neighbour would refuse a gesture that does not touch it.

**(d) THE WIDER MEMBER — shape (ii) alone: TRIM the boundary separator into the run.** *(Named at the 13u1 ladder, round 1; the implementer's own precision at the round: the two sizes are NOT one call.)* For a span that reaches into two or more blocks while opening (or closing) ON a separator, the compound could absorb that edge separator: the block beside it is then covered WHOLE and rides `deleteBlock` to the tray, the far end keeps its ordinary partial edit, and **R-13u-2 answers the join with no awkwardness** — the cut at the trimmed end sits at a paragraph break, so the ends simply keep their own blocks, which is her rule speaking rather than a compromise of it. This is the shape where her *"selecting a bunch of visible text and deleting"* mandate reads most naturally (a whole paragraph IS deleted); the residual cost is (c)'s smaller cousin — one separator's worth of "why didn't that go?", softened because a paragraph visibly left. Shape (i) — the span touching only ONE block's characters — stays exactly the (a)/(b)/(c) question above: there, trimming leaves NOTHING for the gesture to do but delete a separator, and that is where (b) contradicts R-13u-2 and (c) leaves the selected newline standing. **One fence rides any trim ruling** *(the ladder's sanity pass)*: the trim speaks only of a separator BETWEEN TWO BLOCKS. At the trailing end of the LAST block, the next separator is the open TAIL's own — the tail is not a block yet, so it is never a neighbour the run can absorb: a trim there means CLAMPING the span back to the last block's end, and a span that reaches the tail's separator today refuses as the tail's own cause (the plan's condition 2 — `.openTail` for a tail with text, where the idle mint makes it an ordinary boundary case moments later; `.emptyTailAtSpanEnd` for the EMPTY tail since the ladder's round 2, whose road names the nudge and the empty line's own delete, because the mint road is false there). **Ruling (d) for shape (ii) does not decide shape (i), and neither is built — (a)'s refusal stands for both until your word.**

**→ RULED, BOTH SHAPES, 2026-09-15 (the 13u1 checkpoint, in-session — the coordinator's clarification first: selecting to the end of a paragraph's TEXT never refuses today; the refusal fires only when the span additionally swallows the invisible paragraph-break character):**
**Shape (ii) — TAKE IT, the (d) trim road, build at 13u2** (her pick: "Take it — build at 13u2"). **Shape (i) — CLAMP AND EDIT** (her lean, confirmed after the clarification; her words: *"I leant towards taking it as an in-block edit… if it's on the boundary, why not treat it as just part of that one block?"*): the selection clamps to the block's text and edits; the break she selected STANDS — consistent with R-13u-2 (a cut at a paragraph break never merges) and with (d)'s trim. **Together, ONE uniform law: a selection edge sitting on a boundary clamps to block text — separators are structure, never deletable content — and NO boundary-anchored selection refuses.** Both build at 13u2 with red-on-old pins (the pins named in "the line" above flip; `.partialBlockAcrossSeam` retires to the shapes that remain, if any — the empty-tail and with-text-tail causes stand untouched, and the tail fence binds the trim exactly as written). Recorded here and in the 13u2 plan's roster; the handoff's ratify item is DONE.

The line: `App/Variorum/Shared/EditorBrain/CrossBlockCompound.swift`'s run collection (the `overlaps || emptyInside` predicate and the contiguity guard) and `CrossBlockRefusal.Cause.partialBlockAcrossSeam`'s two clauses. The pins that change with the answer: `WholeBlockDeleteTests`' two TRACE tests (they record this exact decline), `CrossBlockCompoundTests.aSpanOpeningOnTheBoundaryRefusesWithTheTrueGeometry` (the ladder's pin on shape (ii)'s refusal and its sentence), and `CrossBlockCompoundTests` would gain the taken shape's own pin.

#110 — **THE INDEX SURFACES' HIGHLIGHT GRAMMAR: three calls inside your item 19 (2026-09-15, 13u2 UNIT B; (a)–(c) BUILT, PROVISIONAL → RULED the same night, see the block below the options).** Your ruling R-13u-10 settles what must be true; these are the three places where making it true asked a question it does not answer. All three are one-line reversals.

**(a) THE SELECTED NOTE'S MARK IN THE BOOK INDEX — its title line, not its body.** Your words: *"in index, even when I have a color mode on I don't want the whole note i'm editing to be that color, it's too much."* The wash was on the note's whole rectangle; it is now on the note's TITLE LINE — the same wash the index column's own row wears, so the two sides agree — and the body is the surface's paper. Options considered: **(i) BUILT** — the title line takes the wash (a mark, 25 pt of a 152-pt note, measured); **(ii)** no mark on the right at all, the column's row alone says which note is selected; **(iii)** the whole body at a much lower opacity. (i) keeps the affordance you liked (*"it will also highlight the block on the right, which I do think is helpful for when you're clicking around"*) without the slab; (ii) is quieter still if the title mark also reads as too much.

**(b) A COLUMN CLICK IS NAVIGATION — it releases the editing ring.** Your two clauses can only both be true if the index highlight and the editing cursor never name different blocks: a row click *"will also highlight the block on the right"*, and *"the index highlight should follow where your editing cursor is."* Today a row click selected WITHOUT moving the ring, so clicking `▸ 04` while your caret sat in block 02 left the column marking 04 — exactly the mismatch you reported. **BUILT:** a row click naming a DIFFERENT block than the live one blurs the live pane (its text lands through the one coordinator), the clicked block wears the selection wash, and nothing is being edited until you click into it. Options: **(i) BUILT** — the click releases the ring; **(ii)** the click MAKES the clicked block live (the ring follows the index, so every navigational click is an editing act — it mounts a live editor on each row click, which fights the calm read-cheap default this surface is built on); **(iii)** leave the ring where it is and let the column keep marking the cursor's block instead of the clicked one (which would make a row click do nothing visible, and lose the affordance you named). The same rule rides the BOOK Index's column, where the rows are notes.

**(c) THE EDITOR-SIDE EDITING HIGHLIGHT IS THE EXISTING SETTING.** Your words: *"if you want the highlight \*on\* on the block you're editing should be a setting - it shouldn't stop the item from being highlighted on the left and highlighted when you first click on it from the index column."* Read as: the R14/R22 editing-indicator family (Settings ▸ Appearance — *off · outline ring · background*) IS that setting, now honoured on these surfaces, with the left-column highlight and the first-click wash deliberately OUTSIDE it — neither is governed by the indicator, exactly as your sentence asks. Options: **(i) BUILT** — the existing family, no new key (your own ruling says *compose with the R14/R22 family, do not mint a rival*); **(ii)** a new enum setting of its own beside it (a rival mechanism, which the ruling forbids); **(iii)** a fourth case on the existing enum (it would change a synced setting's value domain to say something the three cases already say). One consequence worth your eye: under **off**, a block you are EDITING wears no mark at all in the pane — the caret is the mark — while the left column still highlights it and a block you merely selected from the column still washes.

**One thing that came off with this, recorded rather than shipped:** the double editing RING. Both these surfaces drew their own ring around the live pane AND the pane drew its own, in the same token and almost the same rectangle — two concentric rings, visible in the walk's before-captures. The hosts' rings are gone; the pane's stands alone.

**→ RULED 2026-09-15 (her replies at the 13u2 overnight run's mid-run check-in, verbatim):**
**(a) RATIFIED for the book, with a rider:** *"110a - this is fine for the book, though within one
note i do think you should be able to turn it on / off during editing as mentioned (though maybe
that's covered by the editing setting, not sure how it works on books + notes)."* Her rider IS
covered as built: the R14/R22 editing-indicator setting governs the block-you-are-editing mark on
every editing surface (the note's Read ▸ edit and the book's Index panes alike), so within a note
the during-editing highlight turns on/off with that setting; the title-line mark is the BOOK
Index's note-level "which note" mark, a separate register the setting deliberately does not govern.
The composition is on the orchestrator's end-of-phase walk list to verify behaving exactly as her
sentence describes.
**(b) RATIFIED, with the refinement recorded:** *"110b - this is good, it should highlight and
depending on your setting clicking into it makes the highlight go away as you start editing or
doesn't. the editing indicator setting probably does handle it though, as long as it initially
highlights still."* As built this is exactly the behaviour: the first-click highlight always shows
(outside the setting), and once she clicks in and editing starts, the indicator setting decides
whether an editing mark persists (`off` = the caret is the mark). The initial-highlight-always
condition is the walk-verification's second clause.
**(c) effectively CONFIRMED by both replies** (each leans on "the editing setting" handling the
during-editing mark, which is (c)'s build — the existing R14/R22 family, no new key); left formally
in the morning bundle only so she sees (c)'s one recorded consequence (under `off`, an edited
block wears no in-pane mark — the caret is the mark).

#111 — **THREE THINGS INSIDE YOUR #109 BOUNDARY-CLAMP LAW (2026-09-15, 13u2 UNIT C; (a) BUILT PROVISIONAL, (b) BUILT — your instruction executed, (c) a FINDING for your eye).** Your ruling settles what must be true — *a selection edge sitting on a block boundary clamps to block text, separators are structure, and NO boundary-anchored selection refuses.* These are the three places where making it true asked something the ruling does not answer.

**(a) A SELECTION OF EXACTLY THE LINE BREAK, TYPED OVER — where does the typed text land?** The smallest member of the family: she drags across exactly the newline between two paragraphs (no characters of either) and types. There is no block text in the selection at all, so the clamp's two edges cross. Options: **(i) BUILT** — the typed text lands at the END of the block the selection OPENED in, which is this brain's own standing rule (*"a gesture that TYPES keeps the first block… the typed text belongs where the selection opened"*); **(ii)** at the START of the block below (the clamp's leading direction, which would move her caret past a break she did not delete); **(iii)** keep refusing this one shape, which contradicts *"no boundary-anchored selection refuses"* for the smallest member of the family it names. The DELETE of the same selection is untouched and stays A7's carve / #34's merge with their own rulings — this is only about typing over it.

**(b) THE RETIRED CAUSE'S NEW WORDS.** Your ruling said `.partialBlockAcrossSeam` *"retires to the shapes that remain, if any"* and, if any remain, to *"name its true geometry and a road that actually opens"*. Two shapes remained, with DIFFERENT true roads, so the one case became two (the standing doctrine in that file: one declining guard, split into the causes whose roads differ). New words, both new: **"This keystroke can't join these two blocks."** + the deliberate-merge road (a seam keystroke no carve could take — the Mac's stale-projection case, the phone's permanent one), and **"This part of the note is still settling. Try that again in a moment."** (the surface disagreeing with its own index). The retired line — *"One end of this selection sits on the boundary between two blocks. Nudge that end one character either way, or select the whole paragraphs."* — is gone, because the shape it described can no longer refuse. Nothing else changed; flagged because it is copy you will read.

**(c) A FINDING, NOT A QUESTION — the iPhone has no seam carves at all.** Found while enumerating what the retired cause retires to, and grep-verified: neither A7's empty-block backspace nor #34's texted-seam merge is built on the phone, so an ordinary backspace at the seam between two paragraphs refuses there where it works on your Mac. It is why (b) had to split rather than collapse into one sentence. Recorded in `KnownIssuesAndTechDebt.md` with the mobile catch-up as its owner; both geometries are already in the shared brain, so it is the two roads and their authorizations that are missing, never a new rule. Nothing about it is a product call unless you want the phone's seam to behave differently from the Mac's.

#112 — **THREE CALLS FROM 13u2 UNIT D — THE SINGLES (2026-09-15; (a) and (c) BUILT PROVISIONAL, (b) RULED 2026-09-18 — see below).**

**(a) THE SPACE-EATER'S SCOPE — how much of your typed whitespace the durability mint keeps.** Item 2 is fixed (the trace and the numbers are in the 13u2 plan's TRACE D-1): the 2 s durability mint ran every piece of the open tail through the segmentation edge-trim, so a trailing space you had just typed was rewritten away under the caret — and the mint cleared the text-undo stack in the same breath, so ⌘Z could not bring it back. **Built, narrow:** the mint now keeps the trailing horizontal whitespace of the piece your CARET IS ON, and nothing else. The alternatives, both one condition away: **(ii)** every piece of a durability mint keeps its typed edges (leading indent included) — the fullest reading of "the durability mint must never alter what she typed", at the cost of storing trailing spaces on completed paragraphs; **(iii)** the shipped narrow rule, which is what you have. Bulk entry (a paste, an import, a pool drop) keeps its edge trim under every option — a paste is not your caret, and the K8 list-lead trim law is untouched either way.

**(b) ~~THE STAR CAP~~ — RULED 2026-09-18, option (ii) at FIFTEEN** (her words: *"i agree to change starLimit - scroll should be allowed, maybe 15 items max"*). **Built the same day:** `Operations.starLimit` 5 → 15; the four copy strings re-worded ("up to fifteen" — the Mac helper's two, live immediately; the two tutorials' one each, which reach an EXISTING store only at their next deliberate re-seed via the Open-the-Tutorial replace dialog — the phone's sentence also rides row 64's mobile-catch-up tutorial rewrite); the stale cap comments in `StarsModel`/`MobileStars`/`StarredScrollTests` updated, the geometry pin untouched (it was built cap-independent). **Owed to her next walk (the ladder's note): the scroll's ONE real-app try** — star eight or more things and watch the Starred section scroll inside its window of 7 instead of growing the column. The original finding, for the record:

**THE STAR CAP — item 10 is BUILT and CANNOT FIRE until you rule here.** Your 13u item 10, verbatim: *"starred items should only show up to like 7 items and then it should start scrolling."* The Starred section is now a bounded, scrolling section at 7, on the Open strip's exact shape (and the fit test no longer lets a long starred list compress the whole column). **But `Operations.starLimit` is FIVE — your own 13f W4 ruling, "up to 5 notes or books" — so the sixth star refuses honestly and five rows always fit inside seven.** The window can never engage in the shipped app. Raising the cap is not an implementer's call: it overturns a recorded ruling of yours AND rewrites product copy in three places on two platforms (the Mac tutorial's star lesson, the Mac helper's star paragraph, the phone's tutorial lesson — each says *"up to five"*). **Options: (i)** keep the cap at 5 — the bound is then insurance, costing nothing and firing never; **(ii)** raise it to a bounded number (say 20) so your sentence becomes true, with the three copy sites re-worded and the refusal (which already names the number) adapting for free; **(iii)** remove the cap — closest to the Open strip, which has none, but the star list is a synced `setting` blob whose boundedness is what keeps its observation cheap. Nothing else changes whichever you pick.

**(c) PASTE WITHOUT LINE BREAKS — the chord, and the rule.** Your item 11, verbatim: *"i want a way to paste without new lines (some kind of command shift v or command option v or whatever isn't taken, but it's a quality of life issue)."* **The chord BUILT is ⇧⌘V**, chosen by measurement rather than convention: a grep of the whole app finds no `keyboardShortcut` on "v" at all, and macOS's own Paste and Match Style is ⌥⇧⌘V — so ⇧⌘V is genuinely free here. Your named alternative ⌥⌘V is one modifier away. **The RULE built** (one function in `Shared/`, so the phone's own door will consume the same one): every line break — `\n`, `\r\n` or a bare `\r` — becomes a SINGLE SPACE; a blank line is a separator and adds no second space; the join trims only at the SEAM, so lines that already end in a space do not land a double one; **the first line's leading whitespace and the last line's trailing whitespace are kept, because what you copied at the edges is yours**; and a clipboard with no line break passes through byte-identical. The alternatives: joining with NOTHING (runs the last word of one line into the first of the next), or keeping a blank line as a paragraph break (which is not "without new lines" at all). **The PHONE has the rule and OWES the door** — its edit-menu item is the mobile catch-up's face work, recorded as a parity row; the rule is in shared code precisely so that build is a face and not a second decision.

#113 — **13u2 UNIT E — the Takes article's landing now ends when the NOTE'S CONTENT CHANGES, and that includes a metadata-only change (2026-09-15; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u2-feel.md`'s UNIT E RECORD, item 6).** PROVISIONAL, one line to change either way.

**The background, in one paragraph.** When you enter Takes, or click the fan panel's "go there" door, the article column is asked to REVEAL one row — a landing. It is kept alive for a few dozen frames, because a lazy column reports placeholder frames before it reports real ones, and a reveal that gave up on the first frame would aim at nothing. What must end it is anything that moves the page under it: a block minted or deleted above, a callout's padding, the selection banner appearing, a keystroke growing a row. Until tonight that was covered by a hand-written TABLE of a dozen gesture doors, and the table's completeness was argued three times across the 17p/17q ladders and falsified twice by measurement. It also could never cover the roads that are not gestures — a change arriving by SYNC from your phone, a structural undo in another window, an engine repair.

**What was built** (KnownIssuesAndTechDebt row 51's own recommended design, which is why it is built rather than asked first): ONE arm where the board adopts a projection. A landing is pending, a projection arrives that DIFFERS from the one the landing was judged against, the landing ends. A content change is exactly a projection that differs, whatever produced it — so the class is closed by construction. **The dozen gesture arms STAY**, because they end a landing AT the gesture (which is when your hand moved) and about fifteen pins assert that timing; the new arm is a net under them, not a replacement.

**(a) THE ONE THING WORTH YOUR EYE — a METADATA-only arrival ends a landing too.** `!=` on the takes projection fires on ANY field, so re-dating a note, renaming a lens, setting a word target or sealing it while a landing is in flight ends that landing. **What you would SEE if it ever happened: nothing moves** — the page simply stays where it is instead of completing a reveal. The window is a few dozen frames wide, and the states where a metadata write races an entry landing are rare. **Options: (i)** as built — any difference ends it, which is the safe direction and needs no second completeness argument; **(ii)** a field-by-field predicate ("does this change heights?") that ignores metadata — more precise, and a new list to keep complete, which is the exact kind of list this arm exists to retire; **(iii)** drop the arm and keep the hand-written table, which is what the last three rounds kept failing to make complete.

#114 — **TWO SCOPE EDGES OF 13u2b UNIT 3 — the mode-switch viewport-fraction carry (2026-09-18; both PROVISIONAL, built to the defaults below unless you say otherwise).** The rule you approved at the 13u2b kickoff ("do that piece now"): on a NOTE mode switch, the carried block lands at the same VIEWPORT FRACTION it occupied in the outgoing mode — a fraction, never a pixel (your pre-accepted ≈one-line tolerance: "if this is very difficult bc of the different visual elements of takes & stuff, I do think that's fine"). **(a) The book's word-tabs (Outline · Index · Book · Read) are NOT covered** — their landings are chapter-shaped (`LandingTarget` lands at the top, an Act 10 ruling) and your report's sentence was about the note's modes; one word extends the fraction rule there. **(b) Takes is deliberately OUTRANKED-and-untouched** — its entry-landing/memory policy is your own 17q ruling (R-Q20/R-Q21: the memory restores and holds; an entry landing outranks it only when the note's current block changed), so the fraction rule defers to it; Lenses/Compare/Canvas/Blocks likewise keep their standing policies. Reversing either is one condition.

#115 — **THE BOOK OUTLINE KEEPS YOUR PLACE WHEN THE WINDOW'S WIDTH CHANGES — and "your place" means the row that was at the top (2026-09-18, 13u2b UNIT 1; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u2b-landing-seam.md`'s UNIT 1 RECORD).** PROVISIONAL, one line to change either way.
