**The background.** The architectural trace measured this directly while staging your stutter report: *"after a settled landing, a WIDTH change alone silently relocated the page mid-story."* It has to move, and that is not a bug in itself — every row's height changes with the measure, so a scroll offset kept through a resize points at different prose. What was missing was any decision about what to keep. UNIT 1 makes the column's heights known, so keeping something is now possible; this is the choice of WHAT.

**What was built:** when the Outline's content width really changes (the window resized, the sidebar or the navigator dragged), the column RE-LANDS the row that was at the top of the page — the same landing road a chapter click takes, so it settles the same way and costs the same nothing. Walked: 696 → 518 pt mid-story, `settled at top=-0.11 after 0 hops`, the page still showing the story it was showing.

**(a) THE ROW AT THE TOP, or something else?** The candidates, all buildable:
- **(i) the row at the top** *(BUILT)* — what you were reading is what stays put; it is also the only thing the old offset unambiguously meant.
- **(ii) the note you are EDITING** — the caret's note, wherever it sat on the page. Kinder if you are typing near the bottom of a long story and drag the sidebar; stranger if you are reading somewhere else in the book while a note stays focused far away.
- **(iii) nothing** — today's behaviour before this unit, i.e. the page relocates silently. Recorded as an option only because it is the one you have actually lived with.

Nothing about the manifest depends on this; the re-land is one call at one `onChange`. If you want (ii) it is the same call with a different anchor.

**(b) THE VOICE-CHANGE ARM — RECORDED UNBUILT (the 13u2b ladder's round 1, MINOR 2; the reviewer's call at the fix pass, the UNIT 1 implementer agreeing on both the defect and the honesty).** A VOICE change (Terminal ↔ Study in Settings) drops every learned height and re-measures — the same mechanism as a width change, strictly stronger (re-wrap + line pitch + paragraph gap all move) — but does NOT yet re-land the row that was at the top: change voice mid-book and the page silently relocates, exactly the class (a) fixed for width. **Why it shipped recorded rather than fixed:** the fix is one guarded line (`.onChange(of: tokens)` re-lands `topRow` — guarded on `stand(at:)`'s "anything moved" return AND on no landing being in flight, because `tokens` flips ONCE at mount on a Study store as the theme lands, and a bare re-land there would override an in-flight reveal — the guards are recorded here verbatim so the builder inherits them), but the red-on-old pin cannot be built honestly today: the landing rig mounts `BookView` without a `ThemeStore` (reaching the real road means touching a helper seven tests share), and a live voice flip runs `EditorTypography.adopt` — a GLOBAL type change under Swift Testing's parallel suites, the load-flaky-red generator this repo already pays for. Under the hallucination guard's rule (no bug-class fix without red-on-old) the arm is recorded, not built blind. It is one line plus its pin whenever the rig gains a voice-flip road.

#116 — **WHAT "THE SAME VIEWPORT FRACTION" MEANS, AND WHAT IT OUTRANKS (2026-09-18, 13u2b UNIT 3; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u2b-landing-seam.md`'s UNIT 3 RECORD).** Three calls the plan's rule did not make, all PROVISIONAL, each one or two lines to change. Built and WALKED on the real Sherlock note: a block standing two thirds down Write came back from Read pixel-aligned (0.0 pt), and the calm page landed it at 0.666 where Write had 0.667.

**(a) The fraction is the ANCHOR, not the distance from the top.** Two honest readings of "the same viewport fraction", and they differ once the block renders at a different height in the arriving surface — which it always does a little (Read's rows carry their own padding and the calm page its serif leading). **(i) BUILT — the block's own f-point sits at the viewport's f-point**: 0 is flush with the top, 1 flush with the bottom, 0.5 centred. It is exactly SwiftUI's `scrollTo` anchor, which is why Read can honour it with no new landing machinery, and a block that renders δ points taller lands at most f·δ from where it was (measured: a third of a line on the walk). **(ii)** "the block's TOP sits the same fraction of the viewport down": simpler to say, but the arriving surface would have to know the block's height before it lands to place it, and the error when heights differ is f·(the whole block) — several lines on an ordinary paragraph rather than a fraction of one.

**(b) On a mode switch the carried height OUTRANKS Write's own session scroll anchor.** Write remembers where ITS page stood when you left it (17o batch b's RAISE-M1, which is why a mode round trip has returned you to your own view since then). When a mode switch now carries a height, the two disagree, and the carried one wins — it is the newer fact, and it is what "the same text stays at the same height" means. **What you would notice if it were the other way:** coming back to Write would put the page where Write last stood rather than where the block sat in the mode you just left, which is the behaviour the walk measured as `fraction 0.0` against Read's centre. The anchor is untouched everywhere else: a note re-opened from the Library, a relaunch, a width reflow all restore it exactly as before.

**(c) A block TALLER than the viewport keeps the caret, not the height.** If the carried block is longer than the screen, placing it at its carried fraction can put the caret you are about to type at off-screen. Built: the placement runs, and then, if the caret's line is outside the viewport, the ordinary reveal runs after it (the line at the top). So a very long block "loses" the carried height in exchange for the caret being visible. The alternative is to honour the height and let the caret sit off-screen until you type — which N12's "⌘1 then type" promise argues against.

*Scope note, not a question:* the calm page's **pages** form (real leaves, no scroll offset) is outside the rule by its geometry, as `#114(b)` already says of Lenses/Compare/Canvas/Blocks; and flipping Read's own `edit`/`read` switch is not a mode switch, so it keeps whatever the host's scroll had — pre-existing, untouched.

*(The next-free marker moved to the FILE'S END at 13u3 UNIT C — #117…#120 were appended after this line's old position, which is how a marker in the middle goes stale.)*

---

#117 — **THE WORKSPACE READ PANE AND THE NOTE WINDOW RUN PROSE AT THE PANE'S FULL WIDTH, WHERE EVERY OTHER SURFACE CAPS AT 680 (2026-09-18, 13u3 UNIT A1; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u3-faces.md`'s UNIT A1 RECORD, its host table).** PROVISIONAL — one line, and it is a FACE call, not a mechanism one.

Found while converting the five host cells to the gutter geometry. Write's editor, the book Outline and the spread book's page all put prose on `EditorTypography.maxColumnWidth` (680 — "≈68ch of body prose, the mockups' measure", R9). The workspace's READ-CHEAP pane (`RootView.readCheapProse`) and the note window's read face have never capped it at all: they hand the surface the whole pane and it runs prose edge to edge. On a 1200-pt pane that is a 1144-pt measure — and clicking into that pane mounts Write's own editor at 680, so **the note re-wraps completely the moment it goes live**. That inconsistency predates this unit by a long way; it became visible because the gutter rework has to state each surface's column out loud.

This unit did NOT change it (the brief's fence: a rule that narrows a measure no ruling capped is the architect's fork, never the implementer's). Both pages keep their exact measure and only their 20 pt padding became gutter — enough for a count badge that no longer stands on the words, never enough for a margin card.

- **(a) CAP THEM AT 680, like everything else** *(recommended by consistency)* — one line each (`ProseGutter.pane` gains `maxColumn: EditorTypography.maxColumnWidth`). The read face and the live editor would then wrap identically, so focusing a pane would move nothing, and a wide pane would have a real gutter — margin comment cards on both surfaces for the first time. Cost: a deliberate narrowing of two pages you have been reading at full width, and on a wide window the prose would sit centred with a lot of air either side.
- **(b) LEAVE THEM UNCAPPED** *(BUILT — the shipped behaviour, unchanged)* — the read pane fills its column. Cost: the re-wrap on focus stays, and those two pages can never show a margin comment card (a 28 pt gutter holds the badge and nothing more).
- **(c) CAP ONLY THE NOTE WINDOW** — its whole purpose is reading one note, so the measure matters most there, while a workspace pane is often narrow anyway. Cost: two rules where there were two, just differently drawn.

---

#118 — **THE COMMENTS PANEL IS A TEXT EDITOR NOW, AND FOUR SMALL RULES CAME WITH IT (2026-09-18, 13u3 UNIT A2; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u3-faces.md`'s UNIT A2 RECORD).** All four PROVISIONAL, each one or two lines to change, none of them touching what a comment IS.

**The background.** Your item 3: *"the comment add box is so small and it doesn't make sense given how big the overall comment box is that the whole thing isn't more of the text editor that you can write and click on."* So the panel is one writing surface now — every comment an editable area in place, the add area owning every point the comments leave, ⏎ a newline (a comment can hold paragraphs), ⌘⏎ committing what you are writing. That meant deciding four things the old bordered-field swap never had to answer.

- **(a) MOVING AWAY SAVES AN EDIT — but never FILES the add draft.** Click from a comment you have changed into anything else and the change is SAVED (closing the panel saves every changed comment too). Click away from a half-written NEW comment and it just sits there, unfiled, waiting. **Why the asymmetry:** saving keeps prose that already exists, while filing MINTS a comment, and a click somewhere else is not an act of minting. The other reading — a click away files it too — is one line; it would make the add area behave like the rest of the surface at the cost of minting things you had not finished.
- **(b) EMPTYING A COMMENT PUTS IT BACK, it does not delete it.** Select all, delete, move away: the comment returns to what is stored and the panel says *"A comment needs some text — Delete removes it."* **Why:** "empty it to remove it" is a gesture nobody ruled, and it would be the one road in this panel that destroys something. Delete is on the same row, one click, and the deleted fold below is the way back. The alternative — an emptied comment deletes itself — is a real option, and cheap; it is just not one I would choose for you without asking.
- **(c) ⎋ IS A LADDER, most local first:** with a draft in the add area, it clears the draft; in a comment you have changed, it puts that comment back; with nothing being written, it closes the panel. Two presses therefore always leave. The alternative is ⎋ = close, always (and no keyboard road to undo a draft).
- **(d) THE ENDS ARE TRIMMED, THE PARAGRAPHS ARE KEPT.** A comment saves without the whitespace hanging off either end, and keeps every newline inside it. This also FIXED a small inconsistency: the shipped panel trimmed on the add road and not on the edit road, so the same keystrokes stored different text depending on which box you typed them in, and a trailing newline then showed as a dangling blank line in the margin card (measured red-on-old in the record).

*Taste numbers, also PROVISIONAL and also one line each:* the panel's minimum is now 460 × 440 (it was 440 × 320 — a list with a one-line field), and the add area never shrinks below 132 pt however many comments there are, so there is always somewhere to write.

---

#119 — **THE NOTE-LIST CARD'S NAME: SIX SMALL CALLS INSIDE HER RULING (2026-09-19, 13u3 UNIT B; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u3-faces.md`'s UNIT B RECORD).** Her item 4 is RULED and built to its letter — a card leads with the explicit title where set, else a length-capped snippet of the note's first words (*"it can't be the whole first sentence if that sentence is super long"*), journal cards stay date-led, nothing is ever stored or renamed. These six are the details the ruling leaves to the build. All PROVISIONAL, each one line to change, none of them touching what the rule IS.

**(a) THE CAP IS 48 CHARACTERS**, cut at a word edge with an ellipsis (`NoteCardFace.leadCap`). Reason: 48 is the note title's own measured room on the Mac (item 13's cutoff was found HEALED at ~48 chars at 13u2, pinned at two widths), so a derived name reads like a name rather than like a paragraph; wider and the List row's tags and stamps start yielding to it (the title is that row's compressible member, #105); narrower and *"a little snippet of what the post starts with"* stops being a sentence. The alternatives are 56/64, or a WORD count instead of characters.

**(b) THE MARKDOWN IS RENDERED, NOT PRINTED.** A note that opens `# Garden plans` is called *Garden plans*, through the app's own display brain (`MarkdownRendering`) — never a second stripper — and a leading list glyph (`• `, `☐ `) is dropped as structure rather than words. Reason: the name is what the note LOOKS like, and `# Garden plans` is not what it looks like anywhere else in the app. Cost: a note that opens with a code fence or a blockquote (which the renderer does not transform) still shows its marker.

**(c) THE PREVIEW LINE CARRIES ON FROM THE LEAD.** Where the card leads with the note's own words, the line under it starts where the lead stopped — so one card never says the same words twice — and where nothing is left it shows NOTHING (not *"No text yet"*, which would be the card calling a note empty right after quoting it). A titled or date-led card previews the opening as before. The alternative is the simpler one: always show the opening words, repeated lead and all.

**(d) THE TITLE SHE ADOPTS DROPS THE LEAD'S ELLIPSIS.** The chip offers *Garden plans for the spring, and what to do* where the card's lead would read *…and what to do…* — same words, same word edge, no truncation mark. Reason: an ellipsis says *there is more of this sentence*, which is true of a card's first line and false of a name she has chosen, and the app must not put its own punctuation into her prose. The alternative is to store exactly what the lead shows.

**(e) THE CHIP IS A TOGGLE THAT TRACKS WHAT SHE HAS TYPED, AND IT RESETS WHEN THE BOX CLEARS.** Clicking it means *name this after its first words*, so if she keeps typing the offered name follows the words (and the chip always shows what will be stored); filing resets it, because the next capture is a fresh decision; a REFUSAL keeps both her text and her decision. The alternative is to freeze the words at the click.

**(f) THE LIBRARY LIST ROW'S NAME IS NOW ONE LINE.** It had no line limit, so a long name wrapped and broke the row's single baseline (the row is a title, a day, the tag chips and a stamp on ONE baseline); the Notes card's title has always been one line. Consequence beyond item 4: a long TITLE now truncates in the List row where it used to wrap.

*Two things this unit found and did NOT fix, recorded for their owners rather than carried:* the full-document editor SPUN on a `deleteBackward` during the walk (`EditorViewModel.refreshDecorations` → `NSTextLayoutManager.ensureLayoutForBounds` — TEXTKIT2 §2's class, forcing layout from inside a text-storage callback; a full `sample` stack is in the UNIT B RECORD, seen once, not reproduced), and some chip inside an expanded book story WRAPS today, which is a row height the book's manifest does not know about (found because clamping the shared chip to one line moved a settled Outline landing by 3,424 pt).

---

#120 — **THE MAC'S TWO CAPTURE BOXES NOW ANSWER ONE RULE, AND SIX SMALL CALLS CAME WITH IT (2026-09-19, 13u3 UNIT C; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u3-faces.md`'s UNIT C RECORD).** Your R-13u-4 is built to its letter on both Mac capture doors — **⏎ files what you have written · ⇧⏎ adds a paragraph**, a capture holds as many paragraphs as you type, and the hand-off goes through `importPost`'s bulk rule exactly as the ruling says. These six are the details the ruling leaves to the build. All PROVISIONAL, each one line to change, none of them touching what the rule IS.

**(a) ⏎ IN THE PAD FILES *AND CLOSES* IT.** The floating quick note has three doors onto one act — the key, ⌘⏎, and the "File It" button — and all three now file the note and put the pad away, which is what the button and the chord already did. **The alternative:** ⏎ files and the pad stays open, empty, ready for a second thought (one summon, several notes). I built the closing one because "one summon = one note" is the pad's shipped grammar (your round-14.2) and a window that stays open after it has filed is a window you then have to close. The shelf's box is the opposite by design — see (b).

**(b) THE SHELF'S BOX STILL FILES *AND OPENS* THE NOTE; THE PAD FILES AND CLOSES.** The two doors mean different things by "file", deliberately: the shelf bar is R3's type-and-go-there door (its own placeholder has always said *"⏎ opens it"*) and the pad is a sticky you throw a thought at without leaving what you were doing (your round-14.3: *"a sticky that yanks her out of what she was doing is the opposite of a quick note"*). **The alternative:** make them identical in that half too — which would mean either the shelf stops navigating or the pad starts.

**(c) THE SHELF BOX GROWS TO ABOUT EIGHT LINES AND THEN SCROLLS (160 pt).** It used to stop at four and could not be typed past in paragraphs at all. It lives in a busy window, so an unbounded box would push the shelf's own content down as you type. **The alternatives:** a taller bound, or no bound at all (the box takes the window). The note it becomes has no limit either way — this is only about how much of a long capture you can see while typing it.

**(d) ⏎ ON AN EMPTY PAD DOES NOTHING.** Not a file (there is nothing), not a close. The "File It" button is already grey in that state, so the key matches it. **The alternative:** treat it as "I'm done here" and close the pad — quick, but it makes ⏎ mean two different things depending on what is in the box, which is the shape of the phone defect this unit exists to make impossible.

**(e) A REFUSAL KEEPS THE PAD OPEN, AND SPEAKS ON THE PAD.** If the store refuses the note (it can: a failure mid-round, the store unreachable), the pad keeps your words, says so in its own line — *"…Your text is still here — try again in a moment."* — and does NOT close; the next ⏎ tries again. **This half is a real fix, not a taste call:** the pad used to empty itself BEFORE asking the store, so a refusal took the capture with it and spoke into the main window's status line, one window away (the red-on-old measurement is in the record). What is PROVISIONAL is only the wording and the place: the alternative is the shell's toast, which is where every other refusal in the app speaks. I put it on the pad because that is the window you are looking at when it happens.

**(f) TWO SMALL CONSEQUENCES OF THE BOX BEING THE APP'S OWN, worth your eye once.** The capture boxes are now the app's own text surface rather than SwiftUI's stock one (the only place ⏎ and ⇧⏎ can be told apart — AppKit gives shift-Return no binding of its own). Two visible effects: **(i)** spell checking in a capture box now follows **Settings ▸ spell check**, which ships OFF, exactly like every other writing surface in Variorum — if the old box underlined typos for you and this one does not, that setting is the door, and it is one line to make capture always check; **(ii)** the refusal line under the shelf's box is painted in the alarm register now instead of the accent/positive one it used to wear (a refusal that reads green is a refusal that reads like a receipt). Neither changes what a capture DOES.

---

#121 — **SAVEPOINTS CAN BE NAMED NOW, AND SIX SMALL CALLS CAME WITH IT (2026-09-19, 13u3 UNIT D; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u3-faces.md`'s UNIT D RECORD).** Your item 14 is built to its letter — an optional name at the manual-commit door, enter-to-skip, names in Block History — and your ruling R-13u3-1 with it: a savepoint's name is editable after the fact, from a rename door beside it. The snapshot's TEXT is untouched by every road here (I5). These six are the details the rulings leave to the build. All PROVISIONAL, each one line to change.

**(a) ⌘S NOW OPENS A FIELD; ⏎ FILES IT.** The savepoint lands when you press ⏎ — empty for no name, or after typing one — so the fast road is two keys where it used to be one. That is enter-to-skip as you described it, and it is worth your eye once because ⌘S is a reflex. **The alternative** is a second chord that skips the field entirely (⌥⌘S files unnamed, instantly) so the reflex keeps one key and naming becomes the deliberate one. I did not add it because two chords for one act is the thing the registry exists to avoid, and because the field is where the name becomes discoverable at all.

**(b) ⎋ CANCELS THE WHOLE COMMIT — no savepoint is written.** A cancelled field is not "a savepoint with no name"; it is no savepoint. Clicking outside the field and closing the window do the same thing, deliberately: one road, so a dismissal can never half-commit. **The alternative** reads ⎋ as *"no name"* and files it anyway — defensible (you did press ⌘S), but then there is no way to back out of a commit you started, and an accidental ⌘S becomes a permanent row.

**(c) A SEALED NOTE REFUSES A RENAME; A LOCKED TAKE ALLOWS ONE.** The seal refuses everything under the note (its ratified scope, and `setTakeLabel` one grain over reads the same way). The lock is the opposite call for the same reason it was for take labels: it freezes the BUFFER, and a savepoint's text is immutable anyway — so naming one cannot reach what the lock guards. An archived take's savepoints can be named too (that listing is exactly where a name helps). **The alternatives** are the two mirrors: let the seal through (a name is not authoring), or let the lock refuse (a lock means *nothing about this take changes*).

**(d) THE FIELD IS A SMALL POPOVER AT THE DOOR, NOT A SHEET.** It hangs off the bar that carries commit (Write, Takes/Lenses), off the prose in the book's editing modes where the doors live in the sidebar instead, and off the leading edge of the strip in Canvas and Compare — which render no commit door at all, so ⌘S is their whole commit road and the field needs a place to stand. **The alternative** is a sheet (unmissable, but it takes the window for an optional one-line name), or no field on the two board surfaces at all — which would make ⌘S mean two different things in one app.

**(e) THE NAME LEADS THE ROW.** In Block History a named savepoint shows its name first, in the accent register, with *when · how it was saved* muted underneath; an unnamed row reads exactly as it always did. **This is the one MOBILE-VISIBLE change in the unit**: the shared brain that words a savepoint row now puts the name first, so the phone's Block History listing shows names for free, in its single joined line. No phone chrome was built. **The alternative** is to leave the name where it was (third, after the date and the kind), which is right while names are rare and wrong the moment the door offers one at every ⌘S.

**(f) THE RENAME DOOR IS ON QUIET SAVEPOINTS TOO, AND THEY STAY QUIET.** Your "Editable" was unqualified, and a quiet moment — the one left behind when you closed the note — is exactly the one worth labelling *"the one before I left"*. Renaming one writes the name and nothing else: the row still says **quiet**, so the occasion is never overwritten by the name. **The alternative** is manual-only renaming, which would make the quiet rows the only ones you cannot annotate.

#122 — **THE RESTORE CONFIRM NOW NAMES WHAT IT TAKES — AND THE THING #80(e) SAID IT TAKES IS NOT WHAT IT TAKES (2026-09-19, 13u3 UNIT E; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u3-faces.md`'s UNIT E RECORD).** Your item 15 is built: the confirm counts, the tutorial teaches. One finding inside it is worth your eye before the rest, because it corrects a ratified analysis rather than a wording. All PROVISIONAL.

**(a) THE ANCESTORS DO NOT GO — MEASURED, NOT ARGUED. → RULED 2026-09-19, AS BUILT (the author, at the 13u3 morning check-in — asked directly, her lean stated first: "I think it sounds like it's fine." The confirm names the real cost in numbers and keeps computing the set-aside count as the tripwire; the recorded alternatives — drop the tripwire · re-open the Trash road — declined by the selection. R-13u3-7 in the plan's sitting record.)** #80(e) reads that restoring *"hard-deletes the outgoing blocks; preserved originals whose whole family that was go with them."* I drove that exact shape through the real operation and then ran the janitor's own physical sweep over the standing intents: **the preserved original survives, keeps listing in the note's tray with its takes, and the purge agrees it is not stranded.** The reason is one line of the copy loop: step 1's version of NOW copies EVERY outgoing block and mints a `derived_from` edge back to it, so each outgoing block ends the transaction with a live descendant OUTSIDE the purge family — which is precisely the condition the round-8b stranding rule requires to be ABSENT. So the loss your ratification accepted, in that shape, does not occur. **What the road DOES take for good** is the history of the paragraphs it replaces: every savepoint on their takes, every take deleted from them still waiting in Block History, every deleted comment — none of which any version holds, because a copy carries words, never the past. **The confirm therefore names THAT**, in numbers, and still names the set-aside originals whenever the count is real (the read computes them; it is one changed line in the copy loop away from being a number again, and the mechanism is pinned in core at both ends). **The alternatives:** drop the set-aside population from the read entirely (tidier, and blind on the day it matters), or re-open the road so the outgoing blocks go to the Trash instead — which is the *"Trash noise"* your eighth sitting declined, and which would make the history survivable.

**(b) THE CONFIRM'S PROMISE WAS TOO BIG, AND IS NOW EXACTLY TRUE.** It said *"so this is never a one-way door"*; it says *"so none of its words are lost."* The words really are all saved first — that is what the version of NOW is — but the savepoints, the deleted takes and the deleted comments on those paragraphs have no road back at all, so the old sentence promised something the app cannot do. The pane's standing explainer made the same promise two inches above the button and is corrected with it. **The alternative** is different wording for the same narrowing; the sentence is yours to retune.

**(c) PROMOTE IS POINTED AT IN WORDS, NOT OFFERED AS A BUTTON.** The confirm ends *"Promote instead keeps everything — it makes this moment a note of its own and deletes nothing"*, and Promote's own button is on the row behind the dialog. I did not put a **Promote instead** button INSIDE the confirm, because your #80(c) ratification kept Promote fast on the reasoning that *"one mis-click beside Restore"* is the risk it carries — and a second promote trigger beside a destructive default is exactly that mis-click, in the one place she is already reaching for a button. **The alternative** is the button (one press instead of two, and the zero-loss road made unmissable).

**(d) THE SENTENCE APPEARS ONLY WHEN THERE IS SOMETHING TO SAY.** A note nobody has worked in since its moment gets the confirm it has always had, word for word; the cost sentence arrives only when the count is real. **The alternative** is a line that always shows and sometimes says "nothing else goes with them" — reassuring, but it makes every ordinary restore read like a warning.

**(e) DELETED COMMENTS ARE COUNTED BESIDE DELETED TAKES.** They are the same class — something you deleted that is still restorable where it waits — and they go the same way. It does make the sentence occasionally list three things. **The alternative** is to count only the two big ones and leave comments out.

**(f) THE LESSON IS A NEW ONE, NOT A PARAGRAPH.** *"what a copy carries"* joins **where things go** as its fourth lesson, right after the two saves, and it teaches both halves of your instruction at once (a copy carries the placed paragraphs and their takes; no savepoint has ever ridden a copy). **This re-seeds your field guide:** `Help ▸ Restore Tutorial…` in the refreshed build is what puts the new lesson in your own copy, and until you press it your book is the old one. **The alternative** was four paragraphs bolted onto *"saving a moment"*, which would have made the chord's own lesson twice as long and buried the rule inside it.

#123 — **THE PDF EXPORT RENDERS YOUR FORMATTING NOW — AND FIVE CALLS ABOUT WHAT A PAGE OF PAPER IS (2026-09-19, 13u3 UNIT F; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u3-faces.md`'s UNIT F RECORD).** Your item 16 is built: *"the export as pdf is not rendering the markdown formatting you just see the markdown in the text."* It does now — headings are sizes, bold is bold, lists are bullets and boxes, code is monospaced — and it does it through the app's OWN display brain rather than a second parser, so the page can never learn a rule the app does not have. Everything the `.md` export writes is byte-unchanged (pinned). All PROVISIONAL.

**(a) THE PAGE HIDES THE MARKERS, THE WAY READ DOES — NOT DIMS THEM, THE WAY WRITE DOES.** The app has two faces for one vocabulary, and which one a surface wears is your own F-5 rule: an EDITING surface keeps every marker on the glass (a click has to land on the right character), a READ-ONLY one may hide them. A PDF has no gestures at all, so it takes the reading face — `# ` goes and the line gets big, `**` goes and the words get bold. **The alternative** is the editing face: your asterisks printed in grey beside bold words, which is what the app shows you while you type and is not, I think, what you meant by "rendering the formatting."

**(b) A CALLOUT STILL PRINTS ITS `> ` LEADS — THE ONE PLACE SYNTAX SURVIVES.** Exporting a callout block has mapped it to a Markdown blockquote since Phase 13 (`> ` on every line, your emoji leading the first), and a blockquote is NOT in the v1 markdown vocabulary F-3 names — so the display brain has never known what one is, and the page prints the characters. I left it, because the whole shape of this fix is *whatever the display brain does not style, the page does not style*: one truth, no medium-only rules. **The alternatives:** (i) teach the vocabulary `>` as a real block form — a canon addition, and every surface would gain it at once, including the editing face you type into; (ii) have the PDF read the note WITHOUT the blockquote mapping and indent callouts the way the reading page does — which is a second body projection built for one medium, the drift class. Say the word on (i) and it is a small, well-fenced piece of work.

**(c) THE PAGE WRITES NO COLOUR AT ALL — SO THE INK IS PAPER'S OWN BLACK.** Three small decisions, one reason, and the reason is stronger than I first wrote it. A PDF exported while the app is wearing dark must not arrive white on white paper, so nothing on the page may be a colour that resolves against an appearance. The first fix was a fixed black — and the app's own grep pin refused it (a literal colour is exactly what that pin exists to catch), rightly, because a literal is not the answer either. **The answer is no colour:** the exported document carries no ink, no link colour, no chip and no rule colour anywhere, which is byte-for-byte what the page has drawn since Phase 11b (a PDF's own drawing ink is black) and a guarantee nothing can erode — a page with no colour cannot resolve one. So a link prints as that ink under an UNDERLINE, which is print's own affordance and the only one a PDF has (it carries no clickable annotations), and an inline-code run prints monospaced with no tinted chip: the chip is a screen's way of hugging a span, and a grey box on paper is a typographic call rather than a formatting one. **The alternatives:** follow the voice and the appearance (a genuinely dark PDF, which some people do want); print the link in blue; print the chip.

**(d) NOTHING WAS RE-FONTED, AND NO SPACING WAS ADDED.** The page is the same 12-point system body it has been since Phase 11b, with the face written on top; a heading is that size scaled by the app's own heading rule, and the space between paragraphs is still the blank lines your note actually contains. The typographic pass — the serif, the measure, the margins, the running heads — is 13m's and I did not start it. **The alternative** is to give the PDF the reading page's typography now; I would rather it arrive whole in its own phase than half here.

**(e) A NOTE'S TITLE LINE IS NOT READ AS MARKDOWN.** The title at the top of an exported note is a stored name, printed the way the masthead prints it — so a note you actually named `**Draft**` prints those asterisks. A BOOK's title is different and needs no exception: it travels inside the body as `# <name>`, which is a heading and renders as one. **The alternative** is to run the title through the face too, which would make the one name you typed literally impossible to print.

#124 — **THE MERGE SHEETS NOW REFUSE TO JOIN A TAKE THEY DIDN'T SHOW YOU — AND ONE CALL INSIDE THAT (2026-09-19, 13u3 UNIT G; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u3-faces.md`'s UNIT G RECORD).** Background, one line: "Merge choosing takes" over a PAIR already checked, before it merged, that the takes it had shown you were still the takes the blocks show — and over a RUN of three or more it did not, so a take re-pointed under the open sheet (a sync round landing from your phone) could be joined although the list never showed it. It checks on every road now, and what the list marks is what joins. **The one call below is PROVISIONAL.**

**(a) A BLOCK WHOSE ONLY SHOWABLE TAKE IS AN ARCHIVED ONE NOW MERGES INSTEAD OF REFUSING.** A block can end up rendering a take that is archived — archive hides a take from the fan, not from a slot that has nothing else to show (that is the standing read rule, AppSpec §7). The run's list has always shown that take's text; what it could not do before was name it, so the merge resolved the block from the surface instead and the chain refused the whole run with *"these takes changed"*. Now the list carries the id of exactly the take it draws, and the merge joins that — so the run goes through, and what you saw is what you get. **The alternatives:** (i) keep the refusal and say why, i.e. name the block and tell you to un-archive or choose a take first — honest, but a dead end for a merge that was never illegal; (ii) have the list refuse to show an archived take at all and draw the block as empty, which would make the sheet lie about what the block reads. I took the joining one because the sheet's whole promise this unit is *what the list marks is what joins*, and refusing a shape the list can draw breaks that promise from the other side.

#125 — **THE TAKES BOARD'S TAKE CARDS NOW CARRY THE TAKE'S OWN ACTS — AND TWO CALLS INSIDE THAT (2026-09-19, 13u3 UNIT H; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u3-faces.md`'s UNIT H RECORD).** Background, one line: your F1 rule was that the take card menus MATCH — *"Write's panel ⋯ carries Archive · Label · Lock · Delete — Takes mode's card grammar must match"* — and that pass reached the Takes board's ARTICLE cell (its right-click) but not its take CARDS, so a spare take you were LOOKING at on the board could not be deleted, archived, labelled or locked where you were looking at it. It can now: every live take card wears the same ⋯ as Write's panel, in all four fan styles, from one component. **Both calls below are PROVISIONAL.**

**(a) THE DOOR IS THE ⋯ MENU WITH ALL FOUR ACTS, NOT A BARE DELETE BUTTON.** The brief owed only Delete. I gave the card the whole four-row menu because your F1 ruling is about the GRAMMAR matching, a one-row ⋯ would be odd, and a bare destructive button sitting permanently on every take card is louder than the app's habit. The alternatives: (i) a plain `Delete` button beside the ◀ ▶ arrows, the archived card's own precedent — smaller, but it breaks the card-grammar rule the other way and puts a destructive word on screen at all times; (ii) Delete alone inside the ⋯ — the least change, and the one that would have to grow the other three the first time you wanted them. If you want the card quieter, (i) is one line away.

**(b) DELETING A TAKE FROM A CARD STILL SPEAKS NO RECEIPT — ON EITHER SURFACE.** I found this rather than changed it: `deleteTake` has never said anything on success (Write's panel has behaved this way since Phase 7), so the only sign the act landed is the card leaving the fan. That is coherent with 13j's *takes do not confirm* and with the act being self-evidencing, and it sits oddly beside 18i's receipt grammar, where an act that LANDED reports. The alternatives: (i) leave it — the card vanishing is the report, and a quiet line for every take you delete is noise; (ii) a quiet receipt naming the road back (*"Take deleted — ⌥⌘Z undoes, or Put back from the block's history"*), which under your `quiet` default is one muted line at the bottom bar's trailing end and under `off` is nothing; (iii) a receipt only for the FIRST one in a session. I took (i) because it is the shipped behaviour of the surface this door was copied from and changing it would have invented product behaviour the brief did not ask for. The REFUSALS all speak, which is the half that was actually broken.

#126 — **THE ⌘⌫ CHECKLIST REPORT: THE CHORD YOU NAMED IS INNOCENT, AND THE ONE THAT DID IT IS ⌥⌫ — YOUR CALL ON WHETHER IT SHOULD STOP (2026-09-20, 13u4 UNIT E2; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u4-restyle.md`'s UNIT E2 RECORD).** Your report: on `- [ ] Create carousel format for blog posts (maybe just screenshots of personal blog to start?)` / `- [ ]`, *"Command delete at the end of the second line deletes this content: start?) ⏎ - [ ]"*. **I could not make ⌘⌫ do that, five ways, and then not on the running app either** — it dispatches `deleteToBeginningOfLine:`, which on macOS 26 means *to the beginning of the PARAGRAPH* (I measured it taking a whole wrapped paragraph, not the last visual line), and a paragraph stops at a newline by definition. Your block separator IS a newline, so that chord cannot reach through a line break or a block seam even in principle. **What DOES take exactly `start?)⏎- [ ] `, byte for byte, is ⌥⌫** — and it takes it in a bare macOS text view with none of Variorum wired to it, because that is what `deleteWordBackward:` has always meant on this system: from a caret after whitespace it scans back over the punctuation and the line break to the start of the previous word. I walked both chords on your two lines: ⌘⌫ left `start?)` untouched; ⌥⌫ took it. **I changed NOTHING** — the whole unit is one test file — because what follows is a product call and it is yours.

**(a) SHOULD ⌥⌫ STOP AT A LINE BREAK?** In the note you were actually in, both checklist items live in ONE block (⏎ inside a list continues the list, so the newline between them is content), and the app is doing byte-for-byte what TextEdit does with that keystroke. **The options:** (i) leave it — a system chord keeps its system meaning, which is the "if your text looks like basic text it should behave like basic text" principle you gave me for the seam backspace, pointed the other way; (ii) clamp ⌥⌫ (and its forward twin ⌥⌦) to the caret's own LINE, so a word-delete never crosses a line break anywhere in the app — kind to lists and to any note where a paragraph holds several lines, and a deliberate departure from every other Mac editor; (iii) clamp it only where the line is a LIST line, which is the narrow fix for the report and the one that would need explaining twice.

**(b) AND SHOULD IT STOP AT A BLOCK SEAM?** If the same two items are separate BLOCKS — which is what a pasted or imported checklist gives you, one block per line — then ⌥⌫ spans a seam, and the ruled cross-block road runs: the block the span covers whole goes to the Trash (recoverable, with a receipt naming where it went) and the block above keeps its cut text. That is the behaviour you ruled for a hand-made cross-block selection, arriving from a single keystroke. **The options:** (i) leave it — one rule for spans, however they were made; (ii) refuse a word-delete that would take a whole block and say so, keeping the Trash road for selections you made on purpose; (iii) clamp per (a)(ii), which answers this half for free, since a chord that cannot cross a line break cannot cross a seam either.

#127 — **THE CAPTURE MICROSYNTAX IS BUILT, AND SEVEN SMALL CALLS CAME WITH IT (2026-09-20, 13u4 UNIT C1; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u4-restyle.md`'s UNIT C1 RECORD).** Your item 2 is built to your ruling R-13u4-1's letter on both Mac capture doors — the shelf's quick-entry bar and the floating quick note. `{#dream}` applies the tag and a smart book keyed on it collects the note **by the standing tag rules and nothing else** (there is no routing machinery anywhere in this unit — the pin that proves it asserts the note joined the book with ZERO membership rows written); `{t:"my title"}` names it; only the braced forms parse, a bare #hashtag is never touched, malformed braces file as the text you typed, and the tokens disappear. Two things about WHEN and WHETHER, before the seven: the rule runs **at the filing act, never while you type** — the box holds exactly what you typed until ⏎ — and **filing never blocks on a token**: the note is minted first and the tags applied to it after, so nothing a token can do costs you the capture. All seven below are PROVISIONAL, each one line to change, none of them touching what the rule IS.

**(a) A SECOND `{t:"…"}` IN ONE CAPTURE IS IGNORED — BUT IT STILL DISAPPEARS.** The FIRST title wins, and a later one is parsed and removed like any other token rather than left standing as text. I took "first wins" because it reads as the decision you made first and then elaborated, and a well-formed token that stayed visible would look like a bug at the one moment the feature is supposed to feel invisible. **The alternatives:** the LAST one wins (you corrected yourself), or later ones survive as literal text so you can see they did nothing.

**(b) THE `t` IS READ CASE-INSENSITIVELY — `{T:"Ocean dream"}` WORKS.** Your own words put this on the phone first (*"this is more for quicknotes on the phone"*), and iOS capitalizes the first letter of a capture — so a title token typed FIRST, which is the natural place for it, arrives as `{T:`. Strict spelling would have made your commonest case silently file the token as text. Nothing else about the form is loose: the opener must be exactly `{#` or `{t:"`, so `{ #dream}` and `{t: "x"}` are still text. **The alternative** is byte-exact lowercase, which teaches you the rule by failing once.

**(c) A TOKEN TAKES THE SPACE BESIDE IT, AND A LINE OF NOTHING BUT TOKENS GOES ENTIRELY.** `Had a dream {#dream}` files as `Had a dream` with no trailing space; `{#dream} Had a dream` files the same; `Had {#dream} a dream` keeps exactly one space. Your own indentation is never a seam. And a token alone on its own line takes the line with it — otherwise a title token above your first paragraph would leave a blank line at the top of the note, which the paste-spacing rule stores as real content. **The alternative** is to remove only the token's own characters and leave whatever whitespace that produces, which is truer to "the app does not tidy your text" and leaves double spaces where a token stood mid-sentence.

**(d) A MALFORMED TOKEN FILES AS THE TEXT YOU TYPED, INCLUDING THE EMPTY ONES.** `{#}`, `{t:""}`, an unclosed `{#dream`, and a token with a line break in it are all ordinary text — they file, visibly, byte for byte. A token that cannot do its job stays where you can see it rather than vanishing silently. A near-miss also resumes the scan one character on, so `{#a{#b}` files `{#a` and still tags `b`. **The alternative** is to swallow anything brace-shaped, which would make a typo eat your words.

**(e) A `{t:"…"}` OUTRANKS THE FIRST-WORDS CHIP — AND THE CHIP STILL SHOWS THE RAW OPENING WHILE YOU TYPE.** If you both type a title token and click the offered name, the token wins. And when the chip is the only source, the name it stores is read from the CLEANED text, so a leading `{#dream}` can never end up inside the note's name. The visible seam: the chip offers what the box literally holds while you are typing, so with a token at the front it briefly offers `{#dream} Had a weird…` and then stores `Had a weird…`. That is the honest cost of parsing only at the hand-off — the alternative is to parse for the chip as you type, which makes tokens vanish from the offer mid-keystroke and quietly breaks "the box holds what you typed."

**(f) A CAPTURE OF NOTHING BUT TOKENS FILES NOTHING, AND THE BOX CLEARS.** `{#dream}` on its own has no note for a tag to go on, so each door takes its standing empty-capture branch — the same thing ⏎ on an empty box has always done. The cost is that your tokens clear with it and nothing is said. **The alternative** is to keep your text and speak a refusal (*"there's nothing to file — that capture is only tags"*), which never loses a keystroke but invents a sentence for a state the doors have never had one for.

**(g) A TAG THAT CANNOT APPLY SPEAKS A REFUSAL AND THE NOTE IS ALREADY SAFE.** The one reachable shape is a token that names no tag at all (`{#///}` — well-formed, all separators); a store failure mid-round is the other. Either way the note is filed first, and you get one line: *"The note was filed — the tag “///” didn't apply. …"* It is a REFUSAL by 18i's grammar, because the tag door did nothing, so it always shows whatever your receipt setting is. **The alternatives:** say nothing (the note landed, after all), or make it a receipt so `quiet` and `off` govern it — which would mean a tag you asked for could fail in silence.

#128 — **SELECTION → A TAKE IS BUILT, AND FIVE SMALL CALLS CAME WITH IT (2026-09-20, 13u4 UNIT C2; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u4-restyle.md`'s UNIT C2 RECORD).** Your item 9 is built to your ruling R-13u4-4's letter: highlight part of a block in Write, and **Move Selection into a New Take** takes those words out of the take you are in and gives them a take of their own on the same block — at the fan's end, branched from the take they left, and **not** chosen, so the page still reads the take you were writing minus the piece you moved. Your savepoint is there and it is the load-bearing part: the take it left is saved one keystroke before the move, so *"I wanted it to stay"* is a restore away in that block's History. The whole gesture is ONE ⌥⌘Z (and ⇧⌥⌘Z puts it back). Two doors, both where you write: the right-click menu on the selection, and the bar's `block ▾` row. **All five below are PROVISIONAL, each one line to change, and none of them touches what the act IS.**

**(a) THE SPAN MOVES EXACTLY AS YOU SELECTED IT — SO THE SEAM CAN BE LEFT WITH A DOUBLE SPACE.** Select `two` out of `one two three` and what is left is `one  three`, with the two spaces you did not delete still standing. That is U-19 (2)'s letter — never eat what she typed — and the same posture the capture boxes take with your prose. **The alternatives:** (i) collapse the seam's whitespace when the removal leaves two spaces or a space at an edge, which is a tidy-up nobody asked for and would make the act delete a character you did not select; (ii) trim the MOVED span instead, so `two ` becomes `two` in the new take and the original keeps its space — kinder to the seam, but then the new take is not byte-for-byte what you highlighted, which is the promise this way keeps.

**(b) THE NEW TAKE IS UNLABELLED.** It arrives as a plain take with no name, exactly like ⌘T's. **The alternatives:** (i) a fixed label (`moved`, the way a sync conflict arrives as `conflict` and a rescued take as `rescued`) — findable in a long fan, but it puts a word on a take you may be about to rename anyway; (ii) the span's first few words as a label, which is a name derived from text that is already the card's own preview.

**(c) SELECTING A WHOLE BLOCK AND MOVING IT IS ALLOWED, AND IT LEAVES THE BLOCK READING EMPTY.** The take you were in keeps its remainder, and when the remainder is nothing it is an empty take — legal, and the page shows an empty line with a fan badge on it; your words are one take away, not gone. **The alternatives:** (i) refuse a whole-block span and name the road (*"that already IS the block — ⌘T makes another take of it"*), which is what Make Block from Selection does with the same shape; (ii) allow it and CHOOSE the new take, so the page keeps reading the same words and the empty take is the one hiding — which quietly makes the act "swap what this block shows", a different gesture from the one you ruled.

**(d) IT HAS NO KEYBOARD SHORTCUT, DELIBERATELY.** The take family's keys are all spoken for (⌘T · ⌥⌘T · ⇧⌥⌘T · ⌘⇧T) and so are the span gestures' (⌘⇧E extract, ⌘⇧K make block); the free neighbours that remain read as *a variant of extract* rather than as this act, which is exactly the confusion the ⌥-means-the-deliberate-variant habit exists to avoid. So it ships as two menu doors you can see. **The alternative** is to pick a chord (⌥⌘E is genuinely free) — say the word and it comes with a Block-menu row and a line in the ⌘/ helper, which is what any declared key owes.

**(e) IT LIVES IN WRITE ONLY.** Takes mode has no door onto it, and that is a gap I am naming rather than hiding: the act's whole question is *"does this selection lie inside one block"*, which only the full-document page can answer — a takes pane holds one take already, so the same words would mean a different gesture there, with its own target rules. **The alternatives:** (i) leave it Write-only; (ii) rule a pane-side version (move a span out of the pane you are reading into a fresh take beside it) and I will build it; (iii) carry the selection into Write from a pane, the way Extract and Make Block do — which for this act would land you on a different surface to do something the pane could have done itself.

#129 — **WHEN A CHAPTER CLICK CANNOT REACH ITS CHAPTER, SHOULD THE APP SAY SO? (2026-09-20, 13u4 UNIT F1; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u4-restyle.md`'s UNIT F1 RECORD.)** Your item 10 is fixed for the ordinary case: clicking a chapter name in Index now lands that chapter at the top of the page and HOLDS it there — measured on Sherlock in both voices and on the black room, where before it missed 11 times in 20 and once left the page blank. **What is not fixed is the first seconds after a book opens**, while the notes' text is still arriving and the page's own height is climbing under the landing: there a deep chapter click can still stop short (measured: 2 of 5 cold first clicks). The app now KNOWS this — the landing files a numbers-only line in Console saying it gave up — but **it says nothing to you**, so the only difference you see is that the page stopped somewhere wrong. **The alternatives, and this is a product call rather than an engineering one:** (i) **stay silent** (shipped) — the page is only a scroll away from where you wanted, and a sentence about scrolling is noise in a room built for calm; (ii) speak a **refusal** on the status seam (*"the book is still measuring itself — try that chapter again in a moment"*), which is honest and is 18i's own grammar for a door that did not do what it said; (iii) speak nothing but RETRY once quietly a second later, which is the app fixing itself without a word and is also the one option that could move the page under your hand while you are reading. *(The underlying fix — the Index's rows standing at heights derived from their blocks' lengths, so the page stops moving under the landing — is named in the unit record and is an engineering unit, not one of these options.)*

#130 — **⌘[ NOW UNINDENTS, AND BACK/FORWARD MOVED TO ⌥⌘[ / ⌥⌘] (2026-09-20, 13u4 UNIT S1; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u4-restyle.md`'s UNIT S1 RECORD, audit 1). PROVISIONAL — one line each way.** Your item 5: *"command [ is moving me between my different open notes ? is that supposed to do that? I thought it unindented things. related - how are things unindented?"* Both halves were true. ⌘[ WAS Back — Phase 13f gave it that key on the stated reason that brackets are *"the platform's own back/forward idiom"* — and unindent had no key at all: ⇧Tab inside a list, or Format ▸ Outdent with nothing beside it, which is why the menu could not tell you it existed.

**What the measurement says, because that reason turns out to be half true.** ⌘[ / ⌘] are **outdent / indent** in **Apple Notes** and in **Pages** — Apple's own writing apps, in Apple's own published shortcut guides — and in Xcode and VS Code. They are **back / forward** in **Finder** and **Safari**. There is no Human Interface Guidelines rule naming them either way; the chord is app-defined territory, and which meaning is "standard" depends entirely on which kind of app you are in. Variorum is the writing kind. **And Apple Notes resolves this exact collision the exact way this now does:** plain brackets change the list level, and **⌥⌘[ / ⌥⌘]** are its own back-and-forward through linked notes.

**So, shipped:** ⌘[ outdents · ⌘] indents (the same act Tab and ⇧Tab already did inside a list — Tab is unchanged and is still the primary door); **⌥⌘[ / ⌥⌘] are Back / Forward**, and the context bar's ← → are untouched. Both pairs are now written in the ⌘/ helper, on their menu rows, and in the registry, so nothing in the app says the old keys any more. The collision sweep found ⌥ + brackets completely free, and a new pin checks the app's whole key table for duplicates so a swap like this can never quietly land on something.

**The three ways this could go instead, and it is your call:** (i) **leave it as shipped** — the writing-app convention wins in a writing app, and it is Apple Notes' own arrangement, which is the app you came from; (ii) **put back / forward on ⌘[ / ⌘] and leave indent/outdent keyless** (exactly as it shipped before today) — Finder's and Safari's arrangement, and the one your muscle memory has been using for weeks; the discoverability half is then answered by the Settings ▸ Shortcuts tab alone, which is real but is not a key; (iii) **back / forward on ⌘[ / ⌘] as before, and give indent/outdent some OTHER chord** — there is no obvious third pair (⇧⌘[ / ⇧⌘] is previous/next tab almost everywhere), so this one costs an invented key nobody else uses.

#131 — **SETTINGS IS NINE PANES AND A SEARCH FIELD NOW — SIX SMALL CALLS CAME WITH IT (2026-09-20, 13u4 UNIT S1; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u4-restyle.md`'s UNIT S1 RECORD).** Your items 4 and 6 are built together, because read beside item 5 they are one ask: *"an overall one, then maybe tabs on text editing shortcuts, window / reshaping shortcuts"* · *"simple text search within the settings menu - it's too hard to find how to make a callout"* · *"that's further proof that I need a better formatting shortcut menu."* What you are looking for in Settings is **what the app can do**, so the search reaches the settings, the keyboard map and the app's own dictionary at once — type "callout" and you get the word (*"A block wearing a colored frame (Block ▸ Callout)"*) and the chord (⌥⌘C), which is the actual answer to the sentence you wrote. **All six below are PROVISIONAL and none of them changes a single setting's behaviour — not one picker, toggle, help sentence or stored value moved.**

**(a) THE NINE PANES, AND THEIR NAMES.** General (voice · colour palette · light/dark · confirmations) · Editor · Takes · Writing · Shortcuts · Tags · Protection · Data Safety · Acknowledgments. Your *"overall one"* is General, and it holds FOUR controls on purpose: the reason the light/dark control was impossible to find is that fifteen controls shared one pane, so the cure is a short pane that opens. **The alternative** is any other cut of the same controls — say the word and they move; the split I chose puts "how it looks everywhere" in General, "how the page behaves" in Editor, and "what typing does to the note" in Writing.

**(b) TAKES IS ITS OWN PANE, WITH TWO CONTROLS ON IT.** Thin, deliberately: "where do I change how takes open?" should land on a pane with the word on it rather than on row eleven of a long Editor pane, which is the complaint item 6 is about in miniature. **The alternative** is folding those two back under Editor, which is one line and makes the list eight.

**(c) A PANE LIST DOWN THE SIDE, NOT MORE TABS.** Nine tabs do not fit a tab bar, the search field needs a home and this is where macOS puts one, and the mockups you ruled *"an actual guide"* are solid geometric lines and calm cards — which is a list, a hairline and a card. **The alternative** is fewer panes and a tab bar, which means the long panes come back.

**(d) THE SEARCH REACHES BEYOND SETTINGS — ON PURPOSE.** It indexes the settings rows AND the ⌘/ helper's shortcut list AND its dictionary. A settings-only search could not have answered "callout" at all, because a callout is not a setting. **The alternative** is settings-only, which is a smaller promise and fails the one sentence that asked for the feature.

**(e) THERE IS NOW A SHORTCUTS PANE IN SETTINGS AS WELL AS THE ⌘/ WINDOW.** It renders the helper's OWN list rather than a copy, so the two can never disagree, and ⌘/ still opens the window with the dictionary beside it. **The alternative** is to send you from Settings to ⌘/ with a button — one door instead of two, but it makes "search Settings" stop at a signpost.

**(f) THE WINDOW GREW, 560×460 → 720×520.** A list beside a detail column needs the width, and the extra height is what keeps a pane from having a fold of its own. **The alternative** is a narrower list column; the panes' own content is the floor on the rest.

#132 — **THE STAMP AND THE COUNTER CHANGE BY CLICKING NOW — EIGHT SMALL CALLS CAME WITH IT (2026-09-21, 13u4 UNIT S2; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u4-restyle.md`'s UNIT S2 RECORD).** Your item 1, verbatim: *"clicking on edited date goes to created, then collapses to a little clock icon. remembers this on every note, so if you're normally in clock mode a new note would open with only a clock icon. Equally, it would be nice if the word counter thing at the bottom could condense as well - I like that it offers to set a word count target, so I want to keep that functionality, but maybe a little icon meant to represent count or something could be an optional condensed view. Or show only note, only block, or only icon. I don't necessarily want an appearance setting - I want these to be changed by clicking on them."* Both faces are built exactly that way, and **nothing about either one appears in Settings**. The eight calls below are all PROVISIONAL and **not one of them changes what a stamp SAYS** — every face speaks the same one brain's words.

**(a) THE REMEMBERED CHOICE IS ONE CHOICE FOR EVERY NOTE, NOT ONE PER NOTE.** *"Remembers this on every note, so if you're normally in clock mode a new note would open with only a clock icon"* reads most naturally as a posture you are IN rather than a fact about each note, so both cycles remember one value, local to this Mac, never synced (the same class as the light/dark choice and the Library's sort). **The alternative** is per-note memory (the class that remembers which MODE each note was last in): then one note left in clock mode says nothing about the next one, and your sentence's "a new note would open with only a clock icon" would be false. Say the word and it is one line either way.

**(b) THE CLOCK STANDS FOR THE *EDITED* STAMP, WHATEVER YOU CYCLED PAST.** Hovering the collapsed icon says *"Edited Sep 19, 2026, 4:02 PM — click to show the date again"*, never the created one, because the masthead's resting fact is the edit. **The alternative:** the clock collapses whichever face you were in, so cycling created → clock leaves a clock that means "created" — which reads the same on screen and means something different on hover.

**(c) THE COUNTER'S CLICK TOOK THE GESTURE THE WORD-TARGET SETTER HAD — SO THE SETTER MOVED TO A RIGHT-CLICK.** This is the one real design call in the unit. Clicking the counts used to open the target popover; your mandate makes a click condense them. Both halves are yours, so: **click condenses; right-click offers "Set a word target…" in EVERY face including the bare icon**, with the four faces listed under it and a ✓ on the one you are in (so the cycle is something you can see rather than folklore). **The alternatives:** (i) a click opens that MENU instead of cycling — one click to see everything, two to change the face, and "changed by clicking on them" stops being literal; (ii) a click keeps the target popover as it always did and the cycle rides ⌥-click — the keep is untouched but the condensing becomes a chord nobody finds.

**(d) THE FOUR FACES, AND WHAT "ONLY BLOCK" MEANS.** full (*"12w block · 3w selected · 1,240w in note"*) → only the note → only this block → just an icon → round again. The **selected** count rides only in the full face, and *"only this block"* is the block's own count alone. On the Takes and Lenses bars the same face reads *"12w take"* and its menu row says *"Only this take"* — the bar's own noun, unchanged. **The alternative** is keeping the selection count in the block face too, which is two facts in a face whose name says one.

**(e) THE COUNT ICON IS A WORD-SPACING GLYPH, NOT A `#`.** *"Maybe a little icon meant to represent count or something"* — it is SF Symbols' `text.word.spacing`, which reads as words. **`#` was rejected outright** because `#` is this app's TAG door (it sits in the masthead four inches above), and one glyph meaning two things is exactly the confusion the app works to avoid. **The alternative** is any other glyph you prefer — a `123`, a `Σ`, a `≡`; one line.

**(f) A NARROW BAR MAY SHORTEN A FULL FACE, BUT NEVER LENGTHENS ONE YOU CONDENSED.** The folded bar and the book's note box have always shown less than the wide one; now that rule only ever narrows. If you have chosen "only this block", a narrow bar keeps showing the block, not the note. **The alternative** is the old fixed behaviour (a narrow bar always shows the note's total), which would overrule a choice you made.

**(g) THE TWO CYCLES REMEMBER SEPARATELY.** The masthead stamp and the bottom-bar counter have their own memories, so you can live in clock mode with full counts, or the reverse. **The alternative** is ONE "condense the chrome" posture governing both — fewer things to set, and no way to have one without the other.

**(h) THE LIBRARY'S TIMELINE NOW SPEAKS THE SAME WORDS AS EVERYTHING ELSE.** Fixing its typeface (it was the last listing in the app using the system sans instead of your voice) turned up a second thing: its rows printed the RAW stored stamp — `2026-09-20T05:11:42.318Z` — where every other Library row says *"edited today 1:33 PM"*. Both are fixed, because a row that wears the voice while speaking a database column is half a fix. **The alternative** is the raw column back, which nobody would choose deliberately; the real question is whether you want the Timeline's rows to carry MORE than they do (they show the title, the day where the lens is not already about it, and the lens's own stamp).

#133 — **THE CHROME SINGLES: EIGHT SMALL CALLS FROM THE ROUND OF LITTLE FIXES (2026-09-21, 13u4 UNIT S3; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u4-restyle.md`'s UNIT S3 RECORD).** This unit closed seven parked rows and one taste fork — the line-limit sweep, the unvoiced tag sheet, a DEBUG-door hygiene item, the editing ring crossing the gutter, the tutorial's odd-one-out confirm, the pool-and-scraps lesson's stale words, Block History's seal-blind rename field, and the Trash's card grammar. Nothing below changes what anything in the app DOES; they are the calls I had to make to close them.

**(a) THE RING NOW STOPS 4 pt SHORT OF THE PROSE ON A BLOCK WITH A CALLOUT GLYPH OR A COMMENT BADGE — INSTEAD OF 8.** The editing ring has always breathed 8 pt either side of the words. Since the gutter started holding the callout glyph and the comment count, the ring drew straight THROUGH them on the block you were in. It now stops at the glyph's edge, which means a slightly tighter ring on exactly those blocks and the shipped 8 pt everywhere else (a margin comment CARD is far enough out that nothing changes at all). **The alternative** is keeping 8 pt and moving the glyph, which the gutter law forbids — the prose column and its margins never move for a decoration.

**(b) THE TAG SHEET'S CHECKBOXES BECAME ROWS WITH A ✓.** That sheet (the one the Snippets cards, the scraps panel and canvas cards all open) was the last surface in the app wearing system chrome — it looked identical in Terminal and Study while everything around it changed. A system checkbox is the one control in there that cannot wear a voice, so an applied tag is marked with this app's own ✓ and the whole row is the click target. **The alternative** is keeping the checkboxes and voicing only the text, which leaves the sheet half-foreign.

**(c) THAT SHEET SAYS "SNIPPET" ON THE SNIPPETS SURFACES — AND STILL SAYS "BLOCK" ON THE CANVAS.** Its title used to say "block" everywhere, including on the two surfaces that speak snippets. The canvas keeps "block" because a canvas is opened on a NOTE or on the scraps area and the sheet has no way to know which; a word chosen there would be right half the time. **The alternatives:** (i) teach the canvas which it is (a small read at the card's door), (ii) word it neutrally on every surface ("tags for this piece"), (iii) leave it.

**(d) THE TRASH'S ROWS ARE CARDS NOW, AND "PUT BACK" IS THE EMPHASISED BUTTON ON EACH ONE.** The listing kept every decision it had — the grouping, the "from ‹note›" naming, the ancestors' fold, the filter chip, the countdown — and took the calm card grammar the rest of the app wears. Rows are a little taller for the card's padding. Put back is styled as the one you probably want; Delete Now and Empty Trash are in the alarm ink. **The alternative** is a flatter list (closer to the old density) or a quieter Put back.

**(e) UNDER A SEAL, BLOCK HISTORY'S "NAME THIS SAVEPOINT" NOW REFUSES INSTEAD OF OPENING A FIELD.** It used to open the name field, let you type, and refuse at ⏎ — a field that could never keep what you put in it. The door still acts and still says why, which is what everything else in that pane does under a seal. **The alternative** is hiding the door entirely under a seal, which is quieter but makes the pane's grammar inconsistent (Restore stays and refuses).

**(f) RESTORE TUTORIAL'S CONFIRM IS AN ORDINARY ALERT NOW, AND ITS "REPLACE" BUTTON IS RED.** It was the one confirm in the app using the old AppKit style. Its words are unchanged to the letter; what changed is the chrome and that the confirming button is marked destructive, which is what a wholesale replace is. **The alternative** is an unmarked button, as before.

**(g) THE POOL-AND-SCRAPS LESSON WAS SWEPT — BUT THREE WORDS WERE LEFT, ON PURPOSE.** It taught "the Snippets area's pool menu switches between pools", a control the app does not have; it now names the groups column, calls the items snippets, and quotes the doors it sends you to. Left alone: the note's side-panel button still says **"Scraps"** (renaming a door you use moves its help, its tests and the phone's twin with it — it is on the remainder list); the CHAPTER's title is still "pool & scraps" (the phone's tutorial holds the same string, so the two should move together); and the seeded group is still named "tutorial scraps" because Restore Tutorial FINDS it by that name. **What you might want:** the door and the chapter swept to "snippets" in a later pass, together with the phone's.

**(h) "RESTORE TUTORIAL" WILL FRESHEN TWO LESSONS AT ONCE.** Your seeded field guide keeps the OLD merge sentence (from the newline ruling) and the OLD pool-menu sentence until you press `Help ▸ Restore Tutorial…` once. One press covers both. **The alternative** is leaving it — nothing breaks, two lessons just describe the app as it was.

#134 — **KEEPING YOUR PLACE WHEN THE TYPE CHANGES SIZE (2026-09-21, 13u4 UNIT F2; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u4-restyle.md`'s UNIT F2 RECORD).** The bug you saw was real and it is fixed: with a long note open at the end, turning inline comments off (or the paragraph gap back to Standard) left you staring at a blank page with only the last line at the top. What actually happened is that the note got about 5,800 points shorter, your scroll position was past the new bottom, and the scroll view snapped you to the end — where, because Write always keeps about a page of empty room below the last line so you can type high on the screen, "the end" looks like a blank page. The fix is the one the app already used for window resizes: it remembers the LINE at the top of your page before the type changes and puts that same line back at the top afterwards. Four calls came with it.

**(a) IT KEEPS THE LINE AT THE TOP OF YOUR PAGE — NOT THE LAST LINE, AND NOT THE CARET.** That is what the app already does when a window resize re-wraps your text, so the two agree. The visible consequence at the very END of a note: the page after the change holds a bit LESS prose than before (the note really is shorter, so the room below the last line starts higher). **The alternative** is a different rule at the tail — "stay pinned to the end" — which would keep the page full but means the words you were reading move. *(Dated carve-out — 18l U0b, 2026-09-26, the Fable ladder's MINOR-1: in the page's TOP AIR — the viewport's edge above the text container, i.e. inside Write's 24-pt top inset, where the line was never "at the top" in the first place — both keepers now keep the page's air exactly as it was instead of scrolling that line up to the top. Everywhere else (a) stands as written, and the resize and re-font keepers STILL AGREE — both carry the same carve (`ReadingAnchor.reflowLandingInset`). Her report, the mechanism and the pins (`WriteTopAirTests`) are KnownIssues row 87; hers to overturn with that row. The full re-render's exact landing (row 87's B2) is not this row's rule and lives there.)*

**(b) THE GROWING DIRECTION KEEPS YOUR PLACE TOO, WHICH IT NEVER DID BEFORE.** Turning inline comments ON was never the bug you reported (the page stayed drawn), but your place still slid — a fixed pixel position holds EARLIER text once every paragraph gets taller. Both directions now hold. **The alternative** is fixing only the shrink, which would leave the app keeping your place in one direction and not the other for no reason you could see.

**(c) IF YOU HAVE SCROLLED INTO THE EMPTY ROOM BELOW THE LAST LINE, THERE IS NO LINE TO KEEP.** Down there the app cannot name a top line at all, so a type change made from inside the room leaves you where the scroll view puts you — at the end, which is where you were. **The alternative** is falling back to the caret or to the last line, which would move you somewhere you did not ask to go.

**(d) ONLY WRITE'S FULL-PAGE EDITOR IS COVERED.** The book's inline editor, the Read page and the take panes are sized to their content and scroll inside the surface around them, so they have no scroll position of their own to keep; what happens to THEIR page when the type changes is the remembered-note-lengths machinery's business, not this fix's. **The alternative** is teaching those hosts the same trick, which is a bigger piece of work and nobody has reported it. *(The phone has the same shape of change and is NOT covered — a parity row.)*

#135 — **CLICKING A CHAPTER IN A BOOK, IN THE FIRST SECONDS (2026-09-21, 13u4 UNIT F3; the record is `notes/plans/phase-13u4-restyle.md`'s UNIT F3 RECORD).** The leftover from the chapter-click fix is closed. Before this, a chapter click made within a few seconds of opening a book landed short — on this machine, **six times out of six** — because the index page had no idea how tall its notes were: each one stood at a title and a blank line until its text arrived from the store and at thousands of points afterwards, so the page kept growing underneath the landing while it was still trying to arrive. Now the page works out how tall each note is going to be from the lengths it already has on file, before any text is drawn, and the landing holds still. **Thirteen cold clicks out of thirteen now land on the chapter, and sixteen warm ones out of sixteen** — and the app never needed a single correction to do it. Four calls came with it.

**(a) A NOTE'S HEIGHT IS WORKED OUT FROM ITS LENGTH, AND REPLACED BY THE TRUTH THE MOMENT IT IS DRAWN.** The guess is close — within a couple of per cent on the real Sherlock book — but it is a guess, so a note you scroll to may settle by a few points as it draws. **The alternative** is showing nothing until every note's real height is known, which would mean staring at an empty page for the first moment of every book.

**(b) THE INDEX PAGE NOW BUILDS EVERY ROW, NOT JUST THE ONES ON SCREEN.** A row you are nowhere near is an empty box of the right height — it draws nothing and reads nothing — and only notes near your viewport are actually rendered. Measured: a 24-note book renders **6** of them, where before it rendered the whole book **52 times over** as you scrolled. So this is cheaper than it was, not dearer. **The alternative** is the old lazy page, which is what caused the problem.

**(c) WHEN A LANDING STILL CANNOT ARRIVE, THE APP STILL SAYS NOTHING** — that is **#129**, still yours, and it is now a much rarer question: nothing this unit measured ever failed to arrive. **The alternative** is a quiet word on the surface when a chapter click cannot reach its chapter.

**(d) THE FIRST HEARTBEAT OF A BOOK IS STILL A GUESS.** The app cannot know its notes' lengths before it has read them — one small query, a few tens of milliseconds — so in that instant every note stands at a stand-in height. You will not see it (the landing waits out that instant by itself), but it is why the fix is described as "the page holds still while a landing is running" rather than "from the very first frame". **The alternative** is reading every book's lengths when the app starts, which costs something on every book you never open.

#136 — **SIX DOORS THAT COULD LOSE OR MIS-COPY YOUR WORDS, CLOSED (2026-09-21, RUN A batch A1 — 17b; the record is `notes/plans/run-a-custody-text-loss.md`'s BATCH A1 RECORD).** Six independent doors, one consequence class — the one this app refuses by construction. Each was traced, pinned red on the old code, then fixed. These are the calls that came with them. All PROVISIONAL, each a line or two to change.

**(a) A TAKE YOU ARCHIVED IS NO LONGER A PICK.** "New note, only what this column shows" could copy an ARCHIVED take — the one you had picked into a scratch column before archiving it from the same board — and under the chosen-only option that archived take became the new note's ONLY take: a block that rendered but refused every keystroke, and whose words were not the ones the column was showing you. A stale pick now resolves exactly as the board itself resolves it: it falls through to what the column actually displays. **The alternative** is refusing the whole promote when a pick has gone stale, which tells you about a decision you made minutes ago instead of doing the obvious thing.

**(b) REPLACING OR DELETING A NOTE NOW ENDS THE REDO FUTURE — BUT NOT YOUR UNDO HISTORY.** Restoring a version (or deleting a note) left an abandoned timeline re-appliable: ⇧⌥⌘Z could put a block from the version you had just replaced back into the restored note, silently — or refuse and sit on top of the stack forever, burying every entry beneath it for the session. Both are closed. **What I deliberately did NOT do** is clear your UNDO history at those acts: that stack is session-wide and holds work on every note, so a restore of one note would take your undo for all the others. One residue survives and is pinned as known behaviour: an undo entry made BEFORE such a restore can still revive its block into the restored note. **The alternative** is note-scoped undo entries, which is a design rather than a line — recorded with an owner (17e). **RULED 2026-09-25 (the 18k kickoff sitting, R-18k-4): LEAVE IT FOR NOW** — her pick; the residue stays pinned as documented behaviour (KnownIssues row, owner re-set to 18o as record-only); note-scoped undo is a future design at her word.

**(c) A NEW TAKE ON THE BOARDS NOW CARRIES THE SENTENCE YOU JUST TYPED.** ⌘T / ＋ new take / diverge-here on the Takes and Lenses boards copied the take as the STORE had it, and the store lags your typing by up to half a second — so a new take made mid-sentence was a copy of the text without that sentence, and the ring then moved onto it, so the two takes diverged on words you never chose to drop. Write's ⌘T always flushed first; the boards now do too, as do the three "write new takes on every block" doors. **Blank takes deliberately do not flush** — they copy nothing, so there is nothing for a stale row to spoil. **The alternative** is flushing before every board gesture, which makes every act pay for a hazard only the copying ones carry.

**(d) ON THE PHONE, A REFUSED COMMENT NO LONGER EATS THE COMMENT.** The comments sheet emptied its field before it even tried to write, so commenting on a SEALED note — an ordinary thing to try, since the Comments row is offered on every block — left you reading *"This note is locked — unlock it to change comments."* with the paragraph you had just typed gone. The field now empties only when a comment row exists to hold the words, and a refused EDIT keeps the comment you were editing as the target. **The alternative** is clearing the field and putting the text back on a refusal, which is the same promise made a riskier way.

**(e) THE PHONE'S QUICK NOTE HAS A NEW SENTENCE, AND IT REPLACES A WRONG ONE.** If the box saved your note and then could not read it back (the store closing under it), it told you *"There's nothing to save yet."* about a note already written to disk, forgot the note existed — so a second one could be minted from the same words — and silently dropped everything you typed afterwards. It now says **"this note is saved — tap open ↵ to keep writing in it"** and holds, the way it already does when a capture's paragraph boundary cannot be named. **The wording is PROVISIONAL** (13s owns your voice); the behaviour is not a call — saying nothing was saved about a saved note is simply false.

#137 — **A QUICK NOTE THE STORE REFUSED IS NO LONGER ONE WINDOW AWAY FROM GONE (2026-09-21, RUN A batch A1 — 17b, KnownIssues row 65; the record is `notes/plans/run-a-custody-text-loss.md`'s BATCH A1 RECORD).** 13u3 fixed the pad's ⏎ road — a refusal keeps your words and says so on the pad (#120(e)). What it could not fix was the two roads with no pad left afterwards: **closing** the pad after a refusal, and **quitting**.
