Your words lived in that window and nowhere else, so both took the capture with them — and on the close road the app told you *"Your text is still here"* about a window it had just torn down. Both are closed now. All PROVISIONAL.

**(a) A REFUSED CAPTURE WAITS FOR YOUR NEXT QUICK NOTE.** Close the pad after a refusal and the next ⌘⇧N opens with your words in it, saying *"the capture that couldn't be filed — ⏎ tries again"*. It holds **refused** captures only — closing a pad the store accepted still files it, and an empty pad still keeps nothing, exactly as before. **The alternative** is a pad that remembers every abandoned draft, which is a different feature and not the one you ruled.

**(b) IT LIVES IN MEMORY, SO A CRASH OR A FORCED QUIT STILL LOSES IT.** This is the deliberate trade, and it is worth your eye. The obvious alternative — writing the capture to disk — would put your prose in a second place outside the encrypted store, and it would fail exactly when it is needed: the commonest reason the store refuses is that it is locked or unreachable, which is the same state in which no key exists to seal a file. So the loss window shrank from *"any close or quit after a refusal"* to *"a crash, a second ⌘Q while a refusal is standing, or a ⌘Q racing the close's own in-flight refusal"* — that last is the close road's alone (closing files asynchronously, so a quit pressed inside that split second is decided before the park is armed and proceeds undeclined; the quit road's own filing runs before the decision by construction). *(This sentence was extended at the RUN A ladder's round 2 — a record-truth edit: the window existed as shipped and the original wording didn't name it.)* **The alternative** is an encrypted parked file with a key that has to be available for it to work at all.

**(c) THE FIRST ⌘Q AFTER A REFUSAL DOES NOT QUIT — THE SECOND ALWAYS DOES.** If a refused capture is standing when you press ⌘Q, the app declines once and stays up with the pad on screen saying why (macOS's ordinary unsaved-work behaviour). Press ⌘Q again and it quits, whatever the store is doing — you can never be trapped by a store that will not take it. A refusal you resolve and then meet again gets its own single decline. **The alternative** is quitting straight through, which is what it used to do and is how the words were lost.

**(d) THE PAD BECOMES THE ONLY HOME AGAIN THE MOMENT IT OPENS.** Taking the capture back empties the park in the same breath, so there is no second copy anywhere and no way to restore it twice; if the next attempt is refused again it parks again. After the restore, closing that pad is your ordinary close. **The alternative** is keeping the parked copy until something files it, which means two homes for one capture and a rule about which wins.

**(e) TWO NEW SENTENCES, BOTH YOURS TO RETUNE.** The restored pad's line is in (a). The close road's status line now reads *"The quick note wasn't filed. Your words are kept — the next quick note opens with them."* — it deliberately carries no REASON, unlike the pad's own refusal, because on that road you are not looking at the pad and the reason is only useful where the retry is: the next summon brings the words back, and the next ⏎ says the reason if it still applies. **The alternative** is repeating the reason into a status line one window away from where you can act on it. (13s re-voices both.)

#138 — **CUSTODY & THE WORDS: WHAT RUN A's SECOND BATCH LANDED FROM YOUR #90 RULINGS, AND THE SIX CALLS THAT ARE STILL YOURS (2026-09-21, RUN A batch A2 — 17l items 1–4/6–7 + 17n + KnownIssues 32; the record is `notes/plans/run-a-custody-text-loss.md`'s BATCH A2 RECORD).** Your four #90 rulings are built and pinned on both platforms; everything below is either a sentence you can retune or a behaviour worth your eye. All PROVISIONAL, each one line to change.

**(a) A CEREMONY THAT FAILS AFTER "GENERATE NEW ONES" NOW SAYS THE WORDS DON'T WORK.** You press Replace All Keys, choose *Generate New Ones*, write twelve words on paper, confirm them — and then the act fails (the outbox isn't empty, the pull stalled, the vault refused). Those words never landed anywhere, and the failure sentence used to say only why the act failed. It now ends with *"The twelve words you just wrote down are NOT in use: nothing changed, and your existing twelve words still open your account."* This is #90's item 11, in the sentence family you ruled for the conflict-follow's Done. **The alternative** is a separate screen for it, which is a whole face for a rare failure.

**(b) THE FOLLOW DOOR SAYS "PICK UP NEW KEYS" ONLY WHEN IT WILL ASK NOTHING.** Your ruling on #90's item 2, built as a rule rather than a rename: the door reads *Pick Up New Keys* when the passphrase you just typed to unlock will be reused, and *Enter Passphrase…* when a field is coming. Every door that renders it — the notice bar, both sheets, the Encryption tab — asks the same question first, so the two can never disagree. **The alternative** is renaming it everywhere, which would promise "nothing to type" at the doors that do ask.

**(c) THE CONFLICT DONE AND THE CASTLE-BORN CONSENT, IN YOUR WORDS.** The Done now says your account's words are the ones *the other device showed when IT entered Castle* — "those are the set to keep" — which is your *"like look at what you just put into Castle"*. The consent now reads: *"If you'd rather not leave Castle: Cancel here, put the ACCOUNT in Castle from a device already signed into it (Settings ▸ Protection ▸ Use Castle…), then join again."* — cancel · Castle · rejoin, in your order. **13s re-voices both**; the order is pinned, so a re-voicing cannot quietly drop a step.

**(d) THE PASSPHRASE FLOOR IS TWELVE WHEREVER YOU SET IT — AND NEVER WHEN YOU OPEN.** Your 17n ruling, built at every setting door: turning sync on, changing it, replacing all your keys, and the Settings set sheet (which had no length rule at all and is four doors' worth of screen between the two apps). **A passphrase you already have keeps opening your library whatever its length**, and the migration's confirm re-wraps a shorter existing word rather than refusing it — both pinned, because that is the half that is easy to break. The tutorial's *keeping it private* and the book's Chapter 2 each say it once. **The alternative** is a floor at the unlock road too, which would lock out a word you have been using.

**(e) A QUICK NOTE'S WORTH OF BOOK-KEEPING: A DELETED "ENCRYPTION EXPLAINED" NOW STAYS DELETED AFTER THE TRASH FORGETS IT.** The tutorial door declined to re-mint a book you deleted by reading its tombstone — but Delete Now / Empty Trash eventually removes the tombstone itself, and 13j's purged-ids ledger holds ids, never names, so nothing that survives a purge can answer *"was a book called this deleted?"*. So the doors now RECORD your decision on this device while the evidence still stands. **What that cannot cover:** a device that ran neither door between the deletion and the purge re-mints the book. **The alternative** is a marker that travels with the library rather than the device — a real fix, and a bigger one.

**(f) AND THE ONE THAT IS STILL YOURS: SHOULD A LAUNCH RE-MINT A BOOK ANOTHER DEVICE DELETED?** Found while doing (e), not changed. The launch seeder skips a seeded book only on THIS device's own mark — so on a second device (the book arrived by sync; no mark here) a deletion you made anywhere is undone at that device's next launch. It is the same family as (e) but a different road, and it turns on what "deletion is final" should mean across devices, which is a call about the product rather than a defect. **Options:** (i) leave it — a device that never seeded it treats the book as missing furniture; (ii) honour the tombstone at launch as the tutorial door does; (iii) the travelling marker in (e)'s alternative, which answers both at once. **RULED 2026-09-25 (the 18k kickoff sitting, R-18k-5): (ii) HONOUR THE TOMBSTONE AT LAUNCH** — her pick; built at 18o (KnownIssues row, owner 18o), pin before fix.

**(g) A REFUSAL THAT DID NOT EXIST: TAKING THE TWELVE WORDS WHEN YOUR ACCOUNT'S VAULT CANNOT BE READ.** If your account's vault was written by a newer Variorum than the one you are running, the app cannot tell whether your account already has twelve words — and the nudge's *take the recovery phrase* offer used to mint a second set anyway, which the account's own words would later retire silently. It now refuses with *"Couldn't check your account for recovery words (…) — one set of words serves both, so making them waits until it can check."* This is the twin of what the passphrase side already did. **The alternative** is minting them and warning afterwards, which is the loss this refusal prevents.

#139 — **THE RE-LINK YOUR APPLE ACCOUNT DOOR, BUILT — AND THE SEVEN CALLS INSIDE IT (2026-09-21, RUN A batch A3 — 17m; the record is `notes/plans/run-a-custody-text-loss.md`'s BATCH A3 RECORD).** You ruled at #90's item 10 that we need this door (*"I think we do need a re-link your apple account door… I'll have to run that before release"*). It is built, Mac-side, and this is what it does: **Settings ▸ Protection ▸ Re-link Your Apple Account…** shows one screen naming which Apple account will hold your account's key, takes your passphrase, and writes that ONE copy of the key on our server under the iCloud key this Mac's Apple account holds now. No key is replaced, nothing is re-encrypted, your passphrase and twelve words are untouched — and a new Apple device can join with nothing typed again, which is exactly what a reset iCloud Keychain takes away. Everything below is PROVISIONAL and each is one line (or one move) to change.

**(a) WHERE IT LIVES.** The 17m entry said *Settings ▸ Account & Encryption*, which was written before Settings became nine panes. It is on the **Protection** pane, under a *Your Apple account* section beside the Standard/Castle cards — the pane that already holds everything about who can open your work. **The alternative** is the Account sheet's Encryption tab, where Replace All Keys lives; that tab is about the ACCOUNT's keys, and this is about your Apple account's copy, which is why Protection won. One move if you disagree.

**(b) HOW THE SENTENCE NAMES THE APPLE ACCOUNT.** The app is never told your Apple ID — it can see the key iCloud Keychain hands it, and nothing else — so the sentence says *"the Apple account signed into this Mac"* and shows the first eight characters of the key it holds, with the key on file named beside it as what it replaces. That is the only honest naming available, and it is what makes a Mac on a DIFFERENT Apple account tell you so. **The alternatives:** name no key at all (prettier, and then you cannot check what changed), or show the two ids as a small facts row above the sentence instead of inside it.

**(c) THE REFUSAL WHEN iCLOUD KEYCHAIN HANDS BACK NOTHING.** The door then refuses before asking anything, in the words the waiting face already uses: *"Getting your key from iCloud Keychain… This usually takes a moment and can take longer. You can also enter your passphrase or twelve words instead."* Reused whole rather than written again — and its second half is true here in its own right, since that is how a device joins while the copy is orphaned. **The alternative:** the first sentence alone plus a line of this door's own saying nothing was written.

**(d) THE DOOR IS ALWAYS THERE IN STANDARD, not only when something is wrong.** It renders whenever you are in Standard on a device that holds your keys; pressing it when the key on file is already this Apple account's writes that same key again and says so on the screen first. **The alternative** — hide it unless the two disagree — would need a Keychain read on every render and would hide the door in the one state where the answer is unknown, which is the state it exists for.

**(e) A REFUSAL FOR A RARE CORNER.** If the key your Apple account holds CHANGES while the door's screen is open, it refuses: *"The key your Apple account holds changed while this was open, so nothing was written."* You consented to an account, and writing a different key is not what you agreed to. **The alternative** is to write whatever it reads at the moment you press the button.

**(f) THE PHONE HAS NO SUCH DOOR.** 17m names Settings and the run built the Mac. The brain is shared already, so the phone's version is a face and its own pins rather than new machinery. **Options:** (i) leave it — the Mac is where this repair is done, and one device doing it fixes the account for every device; (ii) give the phone the same door at the mobile catch-up; (iii) give the phone a line that names the Mac's door when it finds itself waiting for a key that never arrives.

**(g) ONE SENTENCE FOR SEVERAL FAILURES.** If the write does not land — you are offline, this Mac's keys are not in hand, or a key replacement is still finishing somewhere — the door says *"Your account's key couldn't be updated just now, and nothing was changed."* The road it uses answers landed-or-not without a reason, so the sentence names what is true in every case rather than guessing which one you hit. **The alternative** is widening that road to carry its reason, which touches the automatic heal that shares it.

#140 — **A COMMENT DRAFT THE STORE REFUSED NOW SURVIVES ITS SURFACE'S CLOSE, ON BOTH PLATFORMS (2026-09-21, RUN A ladder round 2 — the round's MAJOR + its binding-5 sweep; the ruling is the run plan's "ROUND 2's MAJOR — THE ORCHESTRATOR'S FORK RULING", the record `notes/plans/run-a-ladder-round2.md`).** 17b P4 taught the phone's comment field to KEEP a refused draft — and the sheet's close then destroyed it anyway, on the very gesture the refusal invites (*"unlock it to change comments"* — the unlock lives outside the sheet). The Mac panel lost its refused drafts the same way (its model dies with the sheet or popover, and the add draft is deliberately never filed on leaving). Both got the quick-note park's shape (#137), one shared brain: refused drafts only, parked from the SUBMITTED text at refusal time, one held draft keyed to its block (a refused EDIT keeps its target comment too), restored — with one line saying why the box is not empty — at EVERY open of the same block's comments until it lands or a newer refusal replaces it. *(Record-truth re-base at the ladder's round 3, flagged: the round-2 build cleared the park AT the restore — the pad's rule — but these surfaces' closes never submit, so a reopen you closed without filing SPENT the insurance and the second close lost the words on the very unlock trip the refusal names. The restore is now a PEEK: the park lets go only when the SAME draft lands — keyed (block, comment), so a landed edit never spends a parked add draft's insurance or vice versa — or when a newer refusal replaces it. The pad's own park (#137) is untouched; its clear-at-restore is safe there because closing the pad FILES.)* All PROVISIONAL.

**(a) IT HOLDS ONE DRAFT, AND THE NEWEST REFUSAL WINS.** A refusal on another block (or a second refusal on the Mac's panel — several rows can be dirty at once there) REPLACES what was held. Bounded and deliberate: a queue of refused drafts is a different feature. **The alternative** is a per-block dictionary, which grows without a story for when it empties. *(Precision from the round-2 sanity pass, a record-truth line: when several dirty Mac rows refuse at once at a panel close, each commit runs in its own task, so "newest" means WHICHEVER LANDS LAST — not a deterministic pick. Strictly better than shipped, where all of them died; inside this item's one-draft trade.)*

**(b) WHAT IT RESTORES IS WHAT WAS REFUSED — never the field's later state.** Type on after the refusal (or empty the field by hand, or ⎋-clear the Mac's add area) and close: the next open brings back the SUBMITTED text the store refused. Over-preservation, never loss — and the same edge the quick-note park carries. *(Round 3, record-truth: under the peek this is now true in EVERY generation — however many times you reopen and close without filing, the refused text comes back, and the one line saying why reappears with it, until it lands or a newer refusal replaces it.)* **The alternative** is tracking the field's every later state, which is the draft-keeper the guard pin exists to forbid.

**(c) HER ORDINARY ABANDON IS UNTOUCHED.** A draft typed and never submitted still dies with the close, exactly as before — the park can never widen into a general draft-keeper (the guard is pinned on both platforms).

**(d) ONE RECORDED CORNER ON THE MAC — AND ITS PHONE MIRROR.** A parked in-place EDIT whose comment is deleted from ANOTHER device while parked is pruned by the panel's first snapshot (the standing `survivingDrafts` rule — a draft for a dead comment has nowhere to live). Narrow (a cross-device deletion racing a parked draft), and the stored comment's own text was never at risk. *(The phone's mirror, recorded at round 3: a draft parked on a block that then LEAVES the surface — deleted, or divided away — has no comments door to restore through while the block is gone; under the peek it simply holds for the session, harmless, and comes back if the block does (Put back). Until a newer refusal replaces it, nothing is spent.)*

**(e) THE RESTORED LINE, YOURS TO RETUNE (13s):** *"the comment that couldn't be saved — here to try again"* — one wording on the shared brain for both platforms; the Mac speaks it in the panel's muted receipt register, the phone above its draft field.

**(f) SCOPE:** the park is memory-only and dies with its home — the phone's with the note's editor, the Mac's with the app session — for #137(b)'s own reason: the refusal's commonest cause is a locked store, which is the state in which no key could seal a persisted park. *(Round 3, record-truth: within that home the park now clears on exactly three occasions — the same draft LANDS, a newer refusal REPLACES it, or the home ends. A restore never spends it.)*

needed): the 30-day Trash window runs on the DELETION's clock, not each device's discovery clock

and purge moments later, the horizon having guaranteed only that it SEES the deletion first); and

#142 — **THE RECREATED-TAG CATCH (RUN B batch B2, row 7 — the tag.path repair's arrival-order
leg; the implementer's draft verbatim, filed by the orchestrator).** The arrival-order leg keys

and the alternative discriminators are worse: batch-scoped state misses cross-batch arrivals, and
a keeper-id-ordering test made the outcome depend on random UUID order. Options: **(a)** keep the
rescue (shipped by this fix); **(b)** narrow the leg to same-batch-retired duplicates only
(re-opens the cross-batch loss). **RATIFIED (a) — 2026-09-22, the RUN B morning session's ratify
bundle, her word: "question 1: ratify." The rescue stands as RULED behavior; the shipped pins are

#143 — **THE F-1 FIX'S TWO PROVISIONAL EDGES (RUN B batch B2, row 15 — the dedupe
double-retirement fix; the orchestrator's rulings section has the full arbitration; AMENDED at
the round-1 ladder, 2026-09-22 — the fixes F-R1/F-R2 narrowed both edges, so this row now states
SHIPPED behavior).** **(a) The §6 clarification, ride-the-row (amended):** the merge matrix is
silent on a tombstoned row's TEXT when the whole block is dead; the fix makes an UNPUSHED losing
TEXT EDIT ride the row into the Trash (staying enqueued, converging with the bytes intact) — the
same end-state B1's P1 probe measured for the PUSHED direction, warranted by §6's "deleting an
edit nobody chose to lose is the one forbidden outcome." **The ladder's F-R1 narrowed the
discriminator: the ride fires ONLY when `working_text` is the sole divergence between the local
and server rows — a metadata-only-dirty row (a label, a lock, a fan move) keeps server-wins, so
stale ancestor text can never overwrite the peer's final edit.** Two recorded corners, hers if
she ever wants them ruled: MIXED dirt (a text edit + a metadata divergence on one row) falls
back to pre-run server-wins (never worse than old; a durable text-intent record would be schema);
byte-identical metadata still rides — and the ladder's round 2 sharpened that corner's on-ramp: it needs NO two-device coincidence (a net-zero metadata round-trip on one device — a label set then cleared before a round — leaves the row dirty with intent columns equal to the server's, so a stale ancestor text can still ride there; recorded, not fixable client-side without a text pre-image, which is schema). **RATIFIED —
2026-09-22, her word at the morning sitting ("yeah ratify both"): ride-the-row-as-narrowed IS
the §6 clarification (recorded at SyncSpec §6's tombstone row, dated); the recorded corners
stand as documented behavior.** **(b) The deliberate-delete-revival corner (amended — the original "once-per-store-
life window" premise was FALSIFIED by the ladder's F-R2 and the fix BOUNDED it):** the defense
now fires only while the meeting genuinely STANDS — the other copy live, or this device's own
retirement still unpushed, or the loser's tombstone within a ONE-HOUR settle window of the
arriving kill — so a deliberate cross-device delete of a kept, engaged copy a day (or month)
later is NEVER second-guessed. Inside that bounded window, a deliberate delete crossing the
race is revived visibly, never silently — err-toward-EDITED's letter. **The one-hour width is
the orchestrator's arbitrated call (the crossing's stamps are bounded by delayed pushes + the
~2 s band + round cadence — realistically minutes; an hour is generous headroom; the
unpushed-outbox arm covers longer offline gaps on its own):** hers to widen or narrow.
**RATIFIED — 2026-09-22, the same word ("yeah ratify both"): the bounded revival stands as
RULED behavior, the ONE-HOUR width ratified as set (still hers to move later if use argues);
recorded at SyncSpec §7's dedupe bullet, dated.** *(Process note: she asked for plainer
explanations of both halves at the sitting — given — then ratified both.)* *(Adjacent, ruled the same day, no action: S1's Q10 refinement — 17n's 12-character floor
is a length floor, not an entropy floor; whether to pair it with a strength check is on the
checkpoint bundle, propose-only — **DECLINED by her at the 2026-09-22 morning sitting ("decline");
17n stays closed, nothing further owed**.)*

#146 — **THE SIGNED-OUT-FIRST PHONE: A DELETED SAMPLE BOOK COMING BACK AT SIGN-IN (2026-09-25, the 18k kickoff sitting's follow-up — her question on #138(f), then her instruction: *"decide it provisionally and bundle for my review"*). PROVISIONAL — for the 18o build; yours to overturn.**
**The case:** you delete Sherlock on the Mac. Later someone installs the phone and uses it SIGNED OUT first. A signed-out store has no account, so the phone seeds its own fresh Sherlock (correctly — at that moment it is a standalone library). At sign-in, that copy syncs up. Your #138(f) ruling ("honour the tombstone at launch") does not reach this road: the seeder already ran. And the pristine-copy dedupe (SyncSpec §7) merges two LIVE copies only — the Mac's copy is deleted, so nothing retires the phone's. Result today: Sherlock reappears on BOTH devices.
**The provisional decision:** **the account's deletion wins over an UNTOUCHED local copy, and never over a touched one.** At the device's first sync after sign-in, a locally seeded copy that is still PRISTINE (the dedupe's own test: every story's text matches the shipped book, no manual savepoint, comment, tag, reference or extra take) is retired quietly when the account already holds that book DELETED. It goes into this device's Trash (Put back and Help ▸ Restore the Sample Library both still bring it back). An EDITED local copy is never retired. It survives and syncs, because "an edited copy is never silently retired" is the standing rule, and your own words outrank a sample book.
**Why this one:** it is the same meaning of "deleted" your #138(f) ruling chose, applied to the one road that ruling cannot see. It reuses the dedupe's existing pristine/edited line instead of inventing a new one. And it errs toward keeping anything you touched.
**The alternatives:** (i) leave it — a phone that started signed out keeps its Sherlock and the Mac gets it back (simplest; the book is sample furniture); (ii) retire the local copy even if edited (rejected: it could take your words); (iii) ask at sign-in ("Your account deleted the sample library — remove it here too?").
**Build note:** the retirement is decided where the arriving tombstone meets the local copy, which is likely `Sources/VariorumCore/Sync/` (the dedupe's home). If so, the standing rider applies: Fable implements and reviews it, and 18o's orchestrator casts accordingly or re-owns it to 18a with this ruling attached.

#147 — **WHICH LINE IS "THE ONE SHE WAS READING" WHEN THE PAGE'S TOP EDGE CUTS A LINE BELOW ITS LETTERS (2026-09-25, 18k C6b — the held walks' W-4 fix, found in its own walk). PROVISIONAL — built; yours to overturn.**
**The case:** your re-font rule (13u4 F2) is that the line you were reading stays at the top when inline comments, the paragraph gap or the voice changes the type. C6b fixed the gap case: with the page's top edge in the blank space between two paragraphs, the app now keeps the FOLLOWING paragraph's first line at the top (it used to put the previous paragraph's last line there). Its walk then found a neighbour of that case, at the end of *Copper Beeches* in Study after ⌘↓: the top edge sat 3 points inside the previous paragraph's last line — below its letters, so only the tips of its descenders (g, y, p) showed. To your eye that is a gap; to the old rule it was "a line cut by the edge", so the toggle brought that whole line back to the top above yours.
**The provisional decision:** **a line counts as the one you are reading while its LETTERS are on screen — while its baseline is at or below the top edge.** Once the baseline has scrolled off (only descenders showing), the next line with text is yours: the next line of the same paragraph, or the next paragraph's first line. A line cut anywhere ABOVE its baseline still stays yours and comes back whole (the walk's straddle rows keep passing, including the one cut through its middle).
**Why this one:** it is the gap rule's own reason applied one step further — you cannot be reading a line whose words have gone. The boundary is TextKit's own (the line's `glyphOrigin`, measured: 14 of 17 pt in Terminal, 17 of 22 in Study), not a tuned threshold.
**The alternatives:** (a) the line's whole box decides (the 13c/13u4 rule as it stood: a line counts while any of it, even a 3-pt sliver of descenders, is on screen); (c) half the line decides (a line counts while at least half its height is on screen — a line cut through its middle would flip).
**Where it lives:** one read, `EditorViewModel.topVisibleOffset()` — the capture every reading-position restore shares (a re-font, a width reflow, leaving and returning to a note, a full re-render). Reversing it is one condition.

#148 — **ONE SENTENCE IN THE BACKUP LOG: WHAT THE SECOND FOLDER'S ROW SAYS WHEN A RUN'S OWN BACKUP FAILED (2026-09-25, the 18k ladder round 1, NIT 1). PROVISIONAL — built; copy is yours.**
**The case:** since C7 the second folder is handed the run's OWN snapshot, never one re-discovered by listing. When the beside-the-library backup FAILS and the far folder is due, the far leg copies nothing and said *"There is no snapshot to copy yet."* — false whenever older copies exist beside the library (only THIS run made none). **The provisional wording:** *"This run made no snapshot to copy."* One string in `StoreBackup.performSecondLeg`; pinned (`BackupRunTests.aFailedPrimaryLegLeavesTheFarFolderAnHonestSentence`, red-on-old by the old string); reversing or rewording is one line.

#149 — **MAY A FORMATTED READ TIER TAKE A CARET CLICK? The 13g surface rule's carve-out (2026-09-26, 18l U0's ladder round 1, MINOR-1 — ruled R-18l-5, PROVISIONAL under your overnight mandate R-18l-4; yours to overturn at the morning bundle).**
**The case:** your Index-click fix works by mapping a click on the fully-formatted read tier back to the stored text (a pinned display→source translation), and the caret lands where you clicked. But 13g F-5's design law says a surface whose click SEEDS A CARET must show the dimmed-marker face so display == stored — a letter the fixed tiers now cross (they show the reading face AND place carets). The rule's own rationale says formatted faces are safe *"exactly where no gesture maps clicks to text offsets"* — and U0 built exactly that mapping, pinned for fidelity in both voices.
**The provisional ruling (b), as built and as you see it today:** the read tiers KEEP the formatted face; a click may place a caret ONLY through the pinned translation. The 13g F-5 entry carries the dated pointer; the `MarkdownRendering` header states the carve-out.
**The alternative (a):** switch the book Index and Read ▸ edit read tiers to the dimmed-marker face (display == stored, the rule's letter kept whole) — a visible look change on two surfaces you read daily: markers reappear, dimmed, on text you currently see clean.
**Reversing is contained either way:** (a) is a face flip per tier; (b) is what stands.
**RATIFIED (b) — 2026-09-27, the 18l-c standing-bundle sitting (the record: `notes/plans/phase-18l-c-navigation-feel.md`, THE SITTING RECORD): the carve-out stands; R-18l-5 graduates from PROVISIONAL to ratified; the 13g F-5 entry's dated pointer is the rule's settled state. CLOSED.**

#150 — **OPENING A NOTE: THE START ON THE SESSION'S FIRST OPEN, WHERE YOU WERE ON A RE-OPEN — FIVE CALLS INSIDE YOUR RULING (2026-09-26, 18l U0c; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18l-navigation-feel.md`'s U0c RECORD). PROVISIONAL under your overnight mandate R-18l-4 — each one line to change.**
Your R-18l-1 is built: a note's first open this session shows its START in every mode (Write's page at its top air, Takes' article at the top, Read's page and index at the top), and a re-open this session puts back the page you left — exactly, top air and all. What caused it is traced and recorded: the app opened a note at the caret you left there (sessions ago), and the reveal put that caret's line at the top of the screen — the END of a note you last pressed ⏎ at shows its empty last line and nothing else; your Sherlock middle was the same rule. Nothing wrote a caret you never placed.
**(a) Where the CARET goes on a first open — BUILT: at the start, with the page;** the note then remembers wherever you go from there. **Alternative:** keep the caret where you left it last session and only open the PAGE at the start (the sitting record's "the remembered caret survives"; your earlier Library-peek wish, "the place where your cursor was saved"). It was built that way first and walked: the note opened at its start, and ONE ⌘1 later Write showed the blank END again — a mode switch enters on the remembered block and reveals it — which is your report one keystroke later. So the start won.
**(b) A re-open whose caret is OFF the page you left — BUILT: the page wins**; the caret stays where it was until you type or click. **Alternative:** jump to the caret, which is what put the END back on your screen after you had scrolled up to read (measured in the app, same session).
**(c) ← → back/forward counts as a RE-open** (built) — so does the Open strip, ⌘⇧1–9, a split pane taking focus and a second window: any door into a note already opened this session.
**(d) A page left resting in the empty room below a note's last line** re-opens at the note's END (its last line at the top) — the nearest page there is to a blank screen. **Alternative:** the note's top.
**(e) A re-open after a visit that marked no place** (you only glanced, in a mode with no caret) opens at the start — never at an older session's place. Read, which keeps no page of its own, scrolls on a re-open to the block you were in this session.
**RULED 2026-09-27, the 18l-c #150 sitting (the record: `notes/plans/phase-18l-c-navigation-feel.md`, THE SITTING RECORD): (a), (c), (d), (e) RATIFIED as built. (b) RATIFIED FOR RE-OPENS ("simply clicking away and clicking back shouldn't move you back to your cursor") — and her answer opened R-18l-13, THE MODE-SWITCH CARET RULE (her words verbatim in the sitting record): switching modes anchors the EDITING modes to the caret — Takes joins Write; a mode's scroll memory never outranks a mode switch (re-opens only); Read's page-continuity carry stands. Built at 18l-c U8; the entry CLOSES when U8 lands (the note-open halves are settled now; only R-18l-13's build is outstanding).**
**CLOSED 2026-09-28 — R-18l-13 BUILT at 18l-c U8** (the stand-down retired at the one resolver site, the eleven outranking pins re-armed old → new, the ride-through deferral answering the 17q race; the record is the U8 RECORD + `notes/plans/18lc-u8-ladder-round1.md`). The one open edge inside the build — ride-through (A) vs never-consult (B) — is **#158 (1)**, PROVISIONAL, her bundle.

#151 — **NOTE READ'S LANDINGS: THE WATCH WITHOUT THE PLACEHOLDER ROWS (2026-09-26, 18l U1; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18l-navigation-feel.md`'s U1 RECORD). PROVISIONAL under your overnight mandate R-18l-4 — built as (a); nothing you see changed.**
**The case:** the round's charter (KICKOFF 2, the U1 bullet) asked note Read to adopt the book Index's whole landing shape — the one authority WATCHING each landing, AND every block row standing at a height computed from its stored length until it is drawn. Measured on your *A Scandal in Bohemia* before the change: every deep click already landed exactly where its face puts a block (12 of 12, both voices, both faces), in one step, with no stutter — the lazy column note Read uses draws a block's real height as it lands. The one landing that went wrong was a made-up note whose first screen is short lines and whose body is long paragraphs: the edit face stopped 11–14 blocks past the target.
**The provisional decision (a), as built:** the authority now watches every note Read landing (both faces) — it corrects the made-up note's miss in one step, ends a landing the moment you scroll, gives up honestly if a landing cannot arrive, and writes the `landing surface=ReadNote…` line into your Console that note Read never wrote. The rows stay in the lazy column. Measured after: 72 of 72 deep clicks on your story land at their place with zero corrections.
**Why not the placeholder rows (b):** built and measured the same evening — on your story they made a cold landing arrive **59 points off** and then **jump home a second later** (the rows around the target trading their computed heights for their real ones), on a surface that currently lands in one step; and a plain column asks every one of the note's rows where it is on every scroll frame, where the lazy column asks only the ones on screen.
**The alternatives:** (b) the charter's letter — placeholder rows (accepting the correction jump on a first visit, until a note's rows have been seen once); (c) (b) plus the warming you ruled for §W (rows pre-drawn off screen at idle) — that is §W's decision, never this round's.
**Where it lives:** `Features/ReadMode/ReadNoteLanding.swift`, `ReadEditPane.land`, `ReadPageColumn.land` (`ReadModeView.swift`); `LandingPolicy.readNote`/`.readNoteEdit`.
**Rides this ruling (the ladder's round-2 NIT-4):** the block manifest's `LandingFacts.offset` semantics were fixed as the REAL top padding, PROVISIONAL until you rule here — if you pick (b), the manifest is already ready (that was half the reason for the choice); if you keep (a), the padding fix simply stands.
**RATIFIED (a) — 2026-09-27, the 18l-c #150/#151 sitting (the record: `notes/plans/phase-18l-c-navigation-feel.md`, THE SITTING RECORD): the watch alone stands; the placeholder rows stay rejected on their measured 59-pt correction jump; (c)'s warming stays §W's own decision; the NIT-4 manifest-offset fix simply stands. CLOSED.**

#152 — **THE WARNING FAMILY'S FORK: YOUR RATIFIED PanePublisher SHAPE WAS BUILT, MEASURED, AND REVERTED AT YOUR OWN GATE (2026-09-27, 18l-b U3; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18l-b-navigation-feel.md`'s U3 RECORD). PROVISIONAL — the revert stands; nothing you see changed; the fork is yours, no rush.**
**What happened:** your ratified sentence ("attempt the frame-synchronous / state-deferred shape, walk the boards under load, and revert on any felt regression") was honored to the letter. Built, the shape removes the ENTIRE `Modifying state during view update` warning family (measured: 1,106 → 0 on the bounded set, 2,393 → 0 on the 191-suite set — 18k C3's attribution confirmed). But it also makes the Takes board's mount show a COLLAPSED article for 50–83 ms (every row empty, the ring at its placeholder) in 5 of 5 runs, 0 of 5 without it — the lever sizes the pane's AppKit frame, but the slot you SEE follows the deferred SwiftUI state. The gate fired; the shape came out whole; your app today is byte-identical in feel to before U3.
**The fork (one line to pick, whenever you like):** **(a)** accept and document — the warnings are hygiene, nothing user-visible stands, the family gets a documented-cost row (the KnownIssues row exists either way); **(b)** defer ONLY the writes made inside `makeNSView`/`updateNSView` (the one flagged shape 18k C3 measured) and keep the layout-pass and event-road writes synchronous — UNBUILT and UNMEASURED, but the collapse's first real measure lands in `layout()`, which is never flagged, so this variant plausibly keeps the warnings off AND the flash out; **(c)** re-attempt the full shape with the SLOT answered too (e.g. the representable answering its measured height via `sizeThatFits`) — a design of its own.
