If you rule the 50–83 ms not felt, say so and the shape returns exactly as built — one `git revert b60e5d6d` (verified clean on the round-1 tree, `04a30237`: `publishHeight` back on the lever, `PanePublishShapeTests` + `PaneSurfaceTests` byte-identical to `e113dd56`, the rider's tripwire seam kept).
**RULED (a) — 2026-09-27, the 18l-c standing-bundle sitting: ACCEPT AND DOCUMENT. The revert stands; the warning family is a documented cost (KnownIssues row 95 flips to documented-cost, owner 12); the shape stays recoverable at the hashes above if hardening ever re-opens it. CLOSED.**

#153 — **WHERE THE CARET GOES WHEN A CLICK IN READ LANDS YOU IN A BOOK'S INDEX (2026-09-27, 18l-b U4; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18l-b-navigation-feel.md`'s U4 RECORD). PROVISIONAL — built as (a); one line to change.**
**The case:** your U0 report — *"I click on top of a line of text, my cursor moves to the empty paragraph"* — was fixed for a click INSIDE the Index. U0's sweep found the same artifact on the Index's other road in: clicking a sentence in Read ▸ Scroll (or on a page in Read ▸ Pages, or "edit here" in the sidebar's note box while in Index) takes you to the Index with that block live — and the caret parked at the block's END, which in your notes is the empty line under the text (measured: 329 of 329 by U0's probe; 224 of 224 and 247 of 247 in this unit's pin). The field meant to carry "where inside the block" (`LandingTarget.utf16Offset`) was declared and never set by anything.
**The provisional decision (a), as built:** the field is DELETED and a landing puts the caret at the START of the block it lands on — the block's first line, at the top of the page where the landing puts your eyes. It is the rule that door was designed with (*"the landing then puts the caret at the block's start"*), what the same click in a NOTE's Read already does on any block other than the one you were last in (on that one, the note's Read lands at your remembered caret — which is (a′) below), and #97's built answer. A click inside the Index still lands exactly where you clicked (U0).
**(a′) — the START, except the block you were last in, which lands at your remembered caret. NOT built; one line to pick it.** This is note Read's rule exactly: its click-through lands the block you were last in at the place you left it, and any other block at its start. It also makes "edit here" in the sidebar's note box, while in Index, land where the same door already lands in Outline — at your remembered caret. It needs no wiring in Read's pages and does not touch #149: the app already keeps where you were in each note, on the road its other doors read. (Surfaced by U4's cold review; the unit built (a) because (a′) goes past removing the blank-line artifact into "where you were" behaviour, which is yours to choose.) *The one line:* the Index's landing takes the note's entry rule — `PaneCaretLanding.entering(currentBlock(for:), block:) ?? .start` in `BookIndexEditModel.takePendingLiveBlock`.
**Why not wire the click's own place (b):** traced, not guessed. Read ▸ Scroll draws a paragraph as plain text that only knows it was tapped — wiring it means building U0's click-place machinery a THIRD time, for the reading page's own type. Read ▸ Pages is cheaper: its page layout already names the character under the click, and U0's translation back to the stored text fits it in a few lines. But either one lets Read's own pages place a caret at a character — exactly your open #149 question (today's rule: Read maps a click to a block, never to a place in the text) — and wiring Pages alone would make your two Read faces disagree (Pages at the character, Scroll at the block's start). If you want (b), say which faces and it is its own small unit.
**The other alternative (c):** delete the field and keep the END — the charter's suggestion taken alone; your blank-line artifact would stay on this road.
**Where it lives:** `Shared/LandingTarget.swift` (the comment names the road the caret's place really travels on), `BookIndexEditModel.takePendingLiveBlock` (the `.start`); pins `IndexLandingCaretTests`.
**RULED (a′) — 2026-09-27, the 18l-c standing-bundle sitting: START, except the block she was last in, which lands at her remembered caret (note Read's own rule; "edit here" in Index lands like Outline's). BUILT 2026-09-28 (`4861947c` — the one line over `PaneCaretLanding.entering`, the pin's (a′) arm, behavioural revert red in the predicted shape; the diff rides the next 18l-c ladder window). CLOSED.**

#154 — **THE ONE THING THAT STILL MOVES WHEN YOU CLICK INTO INDEX TEXT: A STORED BLANK LINE UNDER THE PARAGRAPH (2026-09-27, 18l-c U5; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18l-c-navigation-feel.md`'s U5 RECORD). PROVISIONAL — built as (a), which is today's behaviour for this one piece; nothing else about your report is waiting on it.**
**The case:** your report — *"when you click onto text to edit, the whitespace around it moves around… in the book mode a larger space seems to be added"* — had FOUR causes, and U5 fixed three: the live block's lines were 4 pt tighter than the resting text (its *"compressed"*), its line length 11 pt shorter (the words that jumped lines), and its foot 6 pt deeper. Those are gone on both Index surfaces, in both voices — measured to 0.0 pt, line for line. The fourth is not a spacing number at all: the sample library stores the blank line between two paragraphs as TEXT at the end of each paragraph's block (211 of *Copper Beeches*' 212 blocks end that way — your own typed notes mostly do not). At rest, the Index draws only the words and folds that blank line into its ordinary paragraph gap. The moment a block goes live, you are in an editor, and an editor has to show you what is really stored — so the blank line appears as a real empty line under the text (the caret can go there; it is where U0's "refusal" landed you). That pushes the blocks below down by exactly one blank line: **30 pt in Study, 25 pt in Terminal** — the part of "a larger space" that remains on Sherlock's paragraphs.
**The provisional decision (a), as built (no change):** the live editor shows the stored blank line. It is AppSpec's I4 (a newline inside a block is content) and the same principle as the markdown marks (a live line shows its `**` — the held 18e's): an editor that hid stored text would be editing something you cannot see.
**(b) — the Index shows stored blank lines AT REST too.** Nothing would move on a click, because the blank line would already be there. The cost is the look: the resting Index gets airier — on Sherlock, one extra blank line (≈30 pt) under every paragraph, the way Write shows it. *One place:* the index read tiers stop dropping blank lines (`FlowParagraph`'s blank rule, for those two hosts only).
**(c) — the blank line lives inside the gap that is already there.** The live block's last empty line is drawn over the whitespace between it and the next block rather than below it. It does not fit (the gap is 20 pt in the book Index and 14 pt in a note's Read ▸ edit; the line needs 30), so something still moves unless the gaps grow, and the live frame would overlap the next block's top — not recommended; listed so the space is honest.
**(d) — change the sample library's shape** (seed Sherlock's paragraphs without the stored blank line, as your own notes are). Nothing would move on those stories; Write would then show them with the paragraph gap instead of a blank line; existing libraries keep their copy until restored.
**Where it lives:** the boundary is measured and pinned — `IndexTierMetricsTests` asserts the block below moves by exactly that one blank line and nothing more (so a regression in the other three causes still reds).
**RULED (b) — 2026-09-27, in-session (the 18l-c U5 acceptance sitting): SHOW STORED BLANK LINES AT REST on the two index faces** — display == stored, nothing ever moves on a click, for any note; consistent with Write's rendering of the same note and with the 2026-07-12 paste-spacing ruling (that whitespace is deliberate content). Built as the U5 RIDER (the 18l-c plan's ledger row 1b): the two hosts stop folding blank lines; the read PAGE (Read ▸ read), the book Read and the PHONE's read page are untouched (the shared transform keeps its default — a host parameter, never a new global). The moves-by-exactly-the-blank-line pin re-arms to 0.0; the harness gains the block-below row the U5 record suggested. **This entry CLOSES when the rider lands.**
**BUILT — 2026-09-28 (the U5 rider, commits `774c7cb7…cc3c577c`; double-walked, 16/20 pin cases at 0.0 both voices, the no-op proof for blank-free notes at 0 differing pixels). One named residual is its OWN KnownIssues row (owner 12): a block stored over ≥2 blank lines still moves the block below UP −10/−20 pt on going live — TK2 draws the live last blank line short (measured; pinned loud; absent from the sample library). CLOSED — the ladder gate PASSED 2026-09-28 (one round 0/0/1/3, zero disputes, sanity PASS).**

#155 — **THE TAKEMANIA BOOK AND THE SAMPLE-LIBRARY DEDUPE: SEEDED EVERYWHERE, JUDGED NOWHERE — AND WHERE ITS RESTORE DOOR LIVES (2026-09-28, 18l-c U6; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18l-c-navigation-feel.md`'s U6 RECORD, design call 1). PROVISIONAL — built as (a) and (i); nothing you see depends on it until you use two devices.**
**The case:** your R-18l-12 ruling made the two-devices question a disclaimer, not machinery, and the book's front note says so: *"this book can appear twice … Delete whichever copy you don't want … Restore brings a fresh one back."* The plan expected the sample-library dedupe to read the book as EDITED (it is born with takes and named savepoints) and leave both copies alone — true, but only half the story. The dedupe also has a DEFENSE leg (your #143(b) ruling): while two same-name copies stand, a copy deleted on one device is REVIVED by the other device if it reads as edited. Every takemania copy reads as edited, so if the book were in the dedupe's list, deleting the spare copy on your Mac would be undone by your phone — the exact opposite of what the disclaimer tells you to do.
**The provisional decision (a), as built:** the book stays OUT of the dedupe's list. The engine never looks at it: two copies are two ordinary books, and deleting one is final (to the Trash, like anything else). The list the sync engine receives is byte-identical to before (Sherlock + *Encryption Explained*); zero sync code changed — your ruling's letter.
**(b) — put it in the list.** Both copies survive (as the plan assumed), but a deleted spare comes back from the other device while its twin stands; the disclaimer would have to say "delete it on every device" instead.
**(c) — teach the dedupe to recognise an untouched history-born copy and fold the two into one.** The machinery R-18l-12 ruled out.
**The second half — the Restore door on the phone.** The front note tells you to delete takemania and choose Help ▸ Restore on the Mac for a fresh one. The phone seeds the book too, but has no restore row for it — Sherlock has none there either (only *Encryption Explained* does, because the tutorial names it). **(i), as built:** Mac-only, and the note says "on the Mac". **(ii)** a phone Settings row beside the encryption book's — the shared restore road already serves it; one row of face code.
**Where it lives:** `Book.joinsPristineDedupe` / `Book.dedupeCovered` / `allDedupeFacts()` in `Shared/SampleLibrary/SampleLibrarySeeder.swift` (one flag to flip for (b)); pinned by `JekyllTakemaniaSeedTests.theDedupeFactsLeaveTheJekyllBookOut` (red when the book joins the list — revert R-U6-2). The disclaimer text: `SampleLibraryJekyllBook.aboutThisBook` (its WORDING is yours to polish per R-18l-12 — not part of this question).
**DATED EVIDENCE for the second half (2026-09-29, 18m U1's finals — the first FULL mobile run since 18l-c):** the phone's no-redirect sweep (`MobileStructuralTests.nothingUserFacingNamesTheMac`) is RED on the shipped (i): the disclaimer's "on the Mac, choose Help ▸ Restore" is a phone-seeded string naming a Mac door (`SampleLibraryJekyllBook.swift:91`; pre-existing — born at 18l-c U6, unseen because 18l-c never ran the full mobile suite). So (i) and MobileSoul §4.4's no-redirect pin cannot both stand as-is: (ii)'s one phone Settings row would clear it (the sentence stops naming a platform), or keeping (i) means the sweep records a dated exception. Until you rule, the full-mobile baseline carries this one attributed red beside the 4 known (the 18m record's verify pass, F-1).

#156 — **TAKEMANIA SITS AT THE TOP OF YOUR LIBRARY ON FIRST LAUNCH — BECAUSE ITS TRAY HAS TO BE REAL; AND TWO NAMING/CONTENTS CALLS (2026-09-28, 18l-c U6 — its ladder round 1, F4; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18l-c-navigation-feel.md`'s U6 RECORD, design call 2, and `notes/plans/18lc-u6-ladder-round1.md`). PROVISIONAL — built as (a); yours to choose.**
**The case:** you asked for takemania with *"things released that are on the deletion tray"*. A note's tray lists only what was deleted in the last 30 days (the Trash clock), and the app's clean-up physically removes older deletions. So takemania's history is stamped RELATIVE TO THE DAY IT IS SEEDED — five weeks of sittings ending the evening before — which puts its deleted blocks inside the tray's window (about 11 to 30 days left). The price: takemania reads *"edited yesterday"*, so it sits at or near the TOP of every "edited" sort in your Library and Timeline on the first launch after the install refresh (and on any device that seeds it). Sherlock avoids exactly this by being stamped 1892 — but Sherlock ships nothing deleted.
**Why the whole history cannot simply carry 1886:** stamping takemania's history on the book's 1886 date instead was tried as a deliberate revert (R-U6-4 in the record) — the tray shipped EMPTY (0 of 5), and the deleted blocks would be removed at the first clean-up.
**(a) — as built:** a real Leaving tray of five blocks you can put back or play with, and takemania at the top of the Library on first launch (it drops as you write; the rest of the book — the front note and the two plain chapters — carries 1886 and sorts at the bottom like Sherlock).
**(b) — the 1886 clock for all of it:** no displacement in your Library — and the tray ships empty; the history keeps its takes, merges, divides, lineage and savepoints, only the deleted blocks are gone.
**(c) — the recent history, but its LAST edit stamped 1886:** the history runs as built (so the tray is real), and one final edit is written on the book's 1886 date, which is the stamp the "edited" sorts read — so takemania sinks to the bottom with Sherlock AND keeps a real tray. The price is dates that contradict each other: an 1886 edit and savepoint that come AFTER deletions dated this month, and a note whose history visibly runs through the last five weeks reading "edited 1886" (its created date still places it five weeks back). A small script change; not built.
**Also PROVISIONAL, riding this row (the U6 record's items 4–5):** **(d) the book's name** is written in Title Case — *The Strange Case of Dr Jekyll and Mr Hyde*, your ruling's own words — where Sherlock's shelf name is lowercase (*the adventures of sherlock holmes*); *Encryption Explained* is the Title-Case precedent. One constant to change. **(e) the contents:** the novella's FIRST and LAST chapters stand as the two plain notes, takemania is the reworked MIDDLE (the will → the last night), and every note uses your own typed-note shape (no blank line stored under each paragraph, unlike Sherlock's). One line of the extract script each.
**Where it lives:** takemania's clock — `Takemania.swift` (`Script.sitting`, the seed's own "now" read in `SampleLibrarySeeder.seed`); the book's name — `SampleLibraryJekyllBook.name`; the contents — `notes/source/jekyll-extract.py`'s `QUIET` table. Pinned: `JekyllTakemaniaSeedTests.takemaniaCarriesARealHistory` (the tray, its clocks, the dates in the past at a person's hours).

#157 — **SEARCH IN TAKES: WHERE THE KEYBOARD GOES WHEN YOU STEP, AND SIX SMALLER CALLS THE DESIGN BRIEF LEFT OPEN (2026-09-28, 18l-c U7; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18l-c-navigation-feel.md`'s U7 RECORD; the design is its U7 DESIGN BRIEF). PROVISIONAL — each built as (a); each one line or one constant to change.**
**The main one — (1) the keyboard while you step.** The brief's decision 6 makes the found take go LIVE with the match selected (so you can type at once), and its decision 1 says Esc closes the bar and "focus returns to the board". Both hold, split by WHERE you step from — the Write bar's own convention: **(a) as built:** ⏎ / ⇧⏎ in the find field step and the field KEEPS the keyboard (the found take is live, ringed and its match selected, but a second ⏎ steps again instead of typing a new line into your prose); Esc closes the bar and hands the keyboard to the take with the match still selected — type and it is replaced; ⌘G / ⇧⌘G pressed while you are in the board (not in the field) step AND hand the keyboard straight to the found take. *(Seen at the orchestrator's walk: ⌘G pressed while the field holds the keyboard also steps and LEAVES the keyboard in the field — so a stray keystroke lands in the query, never in your prose; the veto covers every stepping road.)* **(b)** every step hands the keyboard to the take — you can type immediately after ⏎, but ⏎ can never step twice (the second ⏎ would type a newline into the take). **(c)** no step ever moves the keyboard; only a click or Esc does.
**(2) The count's and the bar's words:** "N matches" before the first step, "3 of 23" after, "1 of 23 · wrapped" at the wrap, "No matches"; the toggle reads `only chosen takes: on / off` (the wash chip's grammar); the margin marker reads "1 in another take" / "2 in other takes"; the bar's close door says "done". All copy — yours to polish.
**(3) The colours:** the matches are painted in your palette's accent at a light wash and the current one stronger (VoiceTokens only). Seen in the walk: while the field holds the keyboard, the live take's selected match draws in AppKit's grey "inactive selection", which reads close to the painted matches — the ring is what marks the current one. **(a) as built;** (b) paint the current match in the live take too while the field holds the keyboard (the brief kept the live editor unpainted — decision 7).
**(4) Remembered for the session:** the query rides in the same one session slot as the toggle (decision 3 names the toggle; the query joining it is the find pasteboard's own habit — ⌘G after closing the bar steps with the last word). (b) forget the query when the bar closes.
**(5) ⌘G with nothing typed yet** opens the bar (a door that cannot step gives you the field, rather than doing nothing). (b) say "Type what to find first" instead.
**(6) The marker's reach:** it shows whenever "only chosen takes" is OFF and a block's other takes hold matches — even while that block's fan is open — and stands down beside an open margin-grid rail (the fan badge's own rule). (b) show it only while the fan is closed.
**(7) Esc when the take you're in is scrolled far out of view** *(joined at the U7 ladder's round 1 — the reviewer's R1-M1, ruled to the recorded boundary)*: **(a) as built** — the bar closes and the page stays put ("her place kept"); the keyboard has no pane to go to until you click a take or scroll back to yours, and the newly built live pane then claims it (shipped behaviour). The same state arrives via a mode round trip whose restored page doesn't hold your block (ledger row 7's road A — U8's R-18l-13 removes that way in). **(b)** Esc brings the take you're in back into view and hands it the keyboard — the page moves on Esc. Pinned as a named boundary (`TakesFindBoardTests`, `withKnownIssue` — it says so loudly the day (b) or U8 changes the behaviour).
**A measured correction, not a choice:** the brief said the Takes find would be "case/diacritic-insensitive exactly as the Write bar compares". Measured against the real Write bar: it ignores CASE but NOT accents (`cafe` never finds `café`) and treats the two ways of writing an accented letter as the same. The Takes find is built to match the Write bar exactly (the brief's governing words: one rule of sameness across the two bars), so it is accent-sensitive too — pinned against the real finder, so the two can never drift.
**Where it lives:** `Features/Takes/TakesFindController.swift` (the step, the keyboard rule, the slot), `TakesFindBar.swift` (the words, the chip), `TakesFindPlan.swift` (`compareOptions`, `TakesFindCopy`); the pane's side is `Surface/PaneSurface.swift`'s `PaneFindFace`. Pinned: `TakesFindBoardTests`, `PaneFindFaceTests`, `TakesFindPlanTests`, and the VisualWalk scenario `takes-find`.

#158 — **SWITCHING MODES: YOUR CARET RULE IS BUILT, WITH A COMFORT BAND AND THE FLASH — ONE OPEN EDGE OF THE RULE AND FIVE SMALL CALLS (2026-09-28, 18l-c U8; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18l-c-navigation-feel.md`'s U8 RECORD). PROVISIONAL under your overnight authorization — each one line (or one constant) to change. R-18l-13 itself is SETTLED (yours: "caret anchors the editing modes, read carries the page, no button"); these are the edges it did not decide.**
**(1) The one open edge of the caret rule — when you switch back into Takes and your block IS on the page you left there.** **(A) as built:** the page you left in Takes comes back first, exactly as before, and your block is revealed only if that page does not show it (it rests inside the comfort band). This is Write's own shape — Write also puts back the page it had and then reveals your caret only if it is off that page — so "Takes joins Write" literally, and the page moves the least. **(B)** a mode switch never uses Takes' remembered page at all: the article always opens at its top and reveals your block (every Write → Takes → Write round trip moves the page, even when your block was already in view). *(Why (A) needed one more piece: with the old "stand down" gone, the first look at your block could read its position before the restored page had settled and pull the page away — the exact race that rule was written against in 17q. Measured and answered: that first look now waits for a quiet moment — a quarter to half a second — before deciding.)*
**(2) The band's size:** the room kept above a landed line is each surface's own resting top air (Write 24 pt, Takes 18, Read's edit face 26, Read's page 28); below it, TWO lines of the surface's text (`ComfortBand.bottomLines`). A line already inside the band never moves. A block taller than the band keeps where the switch put it (the carry's tall-block rule and Takes' tall-row rule stand).
**(3) The flash's look:** the block highlight's own face — the wash fill with the accent border (what a ⌘-picked block wears) — because Read's edit face already washes the block you're in (your "index highlight"), and a wash over a wash shows nothing; the border is what reads there. Hold 0.3 s, fade 0.8 s, opacity only (`ArrivalFlash.holdSeconds` / `fadeSeconds`). **Alternative:** the wash alone (no border) — fainter, and invisible on Read's edit face.
**(4) Reduce Motion:** the highlight shows without fading for the same second and then goes. **Alternative:** no flash at all under Reduce Motion.
**(5) The setting:** "Flash your place after switching modes", in Settings ▸ Editor under a new "Switching modes" heading; synced (like the confirmations setting); on by default (yours). **Alternative:** this Mac only.
**(6) What flashes and what doesn't:** the flash marks a MODE SWITCH's landing only — Write's caret line, Takes' landed block, Read's landed row. A click, a find step, a bloom from the takes panel, and opening a note never flash. *(One recorded edge, from the ladder: on Write the trigger is literally #98(a)'s own non-opening reveal set plus the mode-switch carry — so if some other road ever earns that caret reveal, it would wear the band and the flash with it. Neither session found such a road; noted so "what flashes" stays honest rather than as a bug.)*
**Where it lives:** `Surface/ComfortBand.swift`, `Surface/ArrivalFlash.swift`, `Features/Takes/TakesArticleScroll.swift` (the entry landing), `Features/Editor/EditorViewModel.swift` (Write's settle hop), `Features/ReadMode/ReadNoteLanding.swift` + the two faces; pinned `ComfortBandAndFlashTests`, `TakesEntryBandTests`, `ModeSwitchFeelTests`, and the re-armed Takes pins (the U8 RECORD's table).

#159 — **ONE WAY TO SAY "THIS NOTE IS SEALED" — AND THE FEW OTHER REFUSALS THAT WERE WORDED TWO WAYS (2026-09-29, 18m U3 — one refusal table; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18m-editor-conductor-hoist.md`'s U3 RECORD + its CLASSIFICATION RULING). PROVISIONAL under your overnight authorization — copy is yours; each is one line (or one constant) to change.**
**Why this came up:** the Mac's Write editor kept its own private list of refusal sentences beside the app's main one, and the two had drifted; the phone had a third set, door by door. 18m U3 made ONE table both apps speak (each app's own buttons and shortcuts kept to itself), which meant choosing one wording wherever two existed.
**(1) The sealed-note refusal — built as (a).** It read four ways at the doors this unit covered: *"This note is sealed — unlock it to edit."* (the Mac, when typing) · *"This note is sealed — unlock it first."* (the Mac's Write editor, e.g. ⌘S) · *"This note is sealed — unseal it to change takes or choices."* (every other Mac door — and false at most of them: ⌘S on a sealed note in Takes said it about a savepoint) · *"This note is locked — unlock it to edit."* (the phone). **(a) as built — one sentence shape, each app's own door words:** the Mac says *"This note is sealed — unseal it first."*, and a door that knows what was refused says it: *"…unseal it to edit."* / *"…to add to it."* / *"…to restructure."* (the Mac's button is Seal / Unseal, so "unlock" named nothing there). The phone keeps its own words, unchanged: *"This note is locked — unlock it first."* / *"…unlock it to ⟨what you tried⟩."* (its button is Lock Note / Unlock Note). **(b)** the old main-table sentence everywhere on the Mac: *"…unseal it to change takes or choices."* **(c)** one noun on both apps — "sealed" on the phone too (its ~25 pinned phone sentences change) or "locked" on the Mac. **Not yet touched, listed so one word from you can bring them in:** Canvas's *"This note is sealed — order stays frozen until it's unsealed."*; the recovery doors' *"This note is marked complete — unseal it first."* / *"…unseal it to put this back."* (shared by both apps — so the phone's Trash says "unseal" while its button says Unlock); and the phone's quick note's *"This note is sealed, so it can't take new text. Unseal it in the note to keep writing."* (the Mac's words on the phone); and the versions door's *"This note is marked complete — unseal it to restore a version."* (a shared home both apps compile — only the Mac shows a Versions face today).
**(2) "This block has only one take" — built as the combined sentence.** One refusal, two old Mac wordings, each wrong at the other's door: *"A block keeps at least one take — delete the block instead."* (Write — it also answered Take to Block Below, where she wanted to move text out, not delete) and *"This block has only one take — a take moves out when there's another one left behind. Divide the block instead (⌘⇧D)."* (the rest — it also answered Archive on a one-take block). **As built:** *"This block has only one take — a block keeps at least one take, so delete the block instead, or divide it (⌘⇧D) to split it into blocks."* (the phone: the same with no chord). **Alternatives:** either old sentence everywhere.
**(3) Four small Write rewordings that came with the merge (the main table's words won):** *"This take is locked — branch or unlock it."* → *"That take is locked — unlock it or branch a new take."* · *"This take is shown in the note — choose another take first."* → *"The note shows this take — choose another take first, and then this one can go. (If it's the block's only take, it's the block you want to delete.)"* · *"This take is archived — …"* → *"That take is archived — unarchive it first."* · the rare catch-alls *"The operation was refused: …"* / *"Operation failed: …"* → *"Refused: …"* / the bare description. And the other way round: five sentences only one door had are now everyone's (the text-changed-while-landing line, the three Divide/Merge-choosing-takes sheet lines on the Mac, "No legal cut spot…", "A comment needs some text.") — doors that used to show a raw code name now show words, on both apps.
**(4) The phone's "not synced yet" line — built as (a).** Deleting a run of whole paragraphs that includes one whose text hasn't arrived from another device said *"This block hasn't fully synced; it is read-only for now."* on the phone, but *"A block in this selection hasn't arrived yet — nothing was changed. It stays read-only until it syncs."* on the Mac — and the phone ITSELF said the second sentence when the selection started mid-paragraph. **(a) as built:** the phone says the second sentence for both. **(b)** keep the phone's shorter line there. **A related edge, recorded but not changed:** when such a selection also ends on the empty line after the note's last block, the Mac names the unfinished last line first and the phone names the unsynced block first — both true. Making the phone match the Mac means removing one step of the phone's delete routing (a routing change, not a wording one) — yours if you want it.
**Where it lives:** `App/Variorum/Shared/OpsRefusalTable.swift` (the one table, `SealedNoteCopy`), `Variorum/Registry/OpsRefusalCopy.swift` (the Mac's half), `VariorumMobile/Features/Write/MobileRefusalRoads.swift` (the phone's half). Pinned: `RefusalTableConvergenceTests`, `MobileRefusalTableTests`, and `OpsRefusalTableIntegrityTests` (no Mac shortcut or phone menu word can slip into the shared table).


**STATUS ADDENDUM (the VM-5 whole-round fix wave, 2026-10-07 — PROVISIONAL, your word ratifies):** two of this entry's "one word from you brings them in" sentences were BROUGHT IN by the wave without that word, because the phone's ruled-vocabulary fix (the drifted quick-note/VersionActs "unseal" arms) forced the shared sentence to take a platform-words parameter and the Mac arm had to pass SOME words — the wave passed the entry's own ruled direction, (a)'s seal noun: the Mac's versions refusal now reads "This note is sealed — unseal it to restore a version." (was "…marked complete…"). One revert returns them if you'd rather they waited for your word; the record is `notes/plans/fixes/vm-5-whole-round-pass.md`.
#160 — **A SCRATCH COLUMN'S PICK THAT WAS ARCHIVED SWITCHES BACK TO THE CHOSEN TAKE WITHOUT A WORD (2026-09-29, 18o U1 — 17e P32; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18o-correctness-singles.md`'s UNIT U1 RECORD). OPEN — named, nothing built; today's behaviour stands until you pick.**
**The case:** on the Lenses board, an unnamed column ("Lens 1") shows the takes you pick in it, block by block. Those picks are remembered per note on this Mac, across quits. If a take you picked there is later archived (or deleted), the column quietly goes back to showing that block's chosen take — and nothing says so, not that day and not after a relaunch. A SAVED lens in the same situation says it out loud with a one-click Restore (your 13j J5 ruling: *"say loudly if that take was archived and this is a fallback WITH a button for quick restore"*). As recorded, J5 names saved lenses only, so the unnamed column's silence is not a broken rule — but the code's reason for leaving it out was wrong (it said those picks are never stored; they are), and that comment is now corrected. The damage the stale pick used to do downstream (a "Make this a note" copy carrying the archived take — 17b P5) was fixed at RUN A; what is left is only the silence.
**The options:** **(i) RECOMMENDED — give the unnamed column the same notice and Restore button the saved lens has.** Your R83 made an unnamed column visibly an unnamed lens, and J5's reason (a fallback shown as if it were your pick) applies to it word for word; the notice and its Restore road already exist and would be read from the column's own remembered picks. **(ii) Forget the pick when its take is archived or deleted** — the column then shows the chosen take because it is no longer picking anything; honest, silent, and un-archiving the take would not bring the pick back. **(iii) Leave it as it is** — recorded, accepted.
**If you pick (i) or (ii):** a small Mac build with a pin (the Lenses board and `TakesViewModel`), for a future 17 entry or the next phase that touches Takes; zero schema, zero sync (the picks are local view memory).

#161 — **A CUT YOU PLACE RIGHT AFTER "I" OR "A" AT A SENTENCE'S START SLIDES BACK TO THE SENTENCE'S START (2026-09-29, 18o U1 — 17e P34; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18o-correctness-singles.md`'s UNIT U1 RECORD). PROVISIONAL — today's behaviour stands and is now pinned; the alternative is yours.**
**The case:** your S4-22 cut rule lets a cut fall between any two words, with a gentle pull toward sentence ends and line breaks. The pull is two characters wide. In one spot it outreaches a cut you placed exactly: with "Yes. I went home." and the cut put right before "went", the pull sees the sentence start two characters back (before "I"), counts it as just as close, and — ties going to the earlier spot — the divide gives you "Yes." + "I went home." instead of "Yes. I" + "went home.". It only happens after a one-letter word that opens a sentence or a paragraph ("I", "A", "O"). The code's own notes used to say an exact placement always wins; they now say what really happens.
**Kept as built (PROVISIONAL):** the review that found it read this as the pull doing its job — a cut that lonely next to a sentence start is almost always meant for the sentence start. It is now pinned, so it cannot change without someone saying so.
**The alternative:** **exact wins** — a cut placed exactly on a word edge always stays there; the pull only helps a cut that is NOT already on an edge. One line in `BoundarySnapping.settle`; the same rule reaches plain divide, the takes ceremony's cuts and extract's span edges on both the Mac and the phone.

#162 — **A BACKUP COPY DATED IN THE FUTURE: BACKUPS NO LONGER STOP, AND WHAT THE DATA SAFETY ROW SHOWS ABOUT IT (2026-09-29, 18o U1 — KnownIssues row 84; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18o-correctness-singles.md`'s UNIT U1 RECORD). PROVISIONAL — built as (a); one line to change.**
**The case:** if this Mac's clock ever ran fast while a backup was taken, that copy's name carries a date in the future. Until now that one file made every automatic check think a fresh copy already existed, so automatic backups (beside the library, in your second folder, the Castle copy at lock, and the weekly plain-text export) silently stopped until the clock caught up with that date — and the Settings ▸ Data Safety row never turned orange to tell you. That is fixed: a date later than now is no longer taken as proof of anything, so the backups carry on daily. The future-dated file itself is kept (it is a real copy; only its date is wrong).
**What the row shows — (a) as built:** the row's "Newest" and "back to" are read only from copies dated no later than now; the future-dated copy is counted in the number of copies but never named as the newest; the row turns orange only when the copies it can trust are old. **(b)** show the future date as "Newest" and colour it orange until the clock passes it — honest about the odd file, but after the fix the backups are running, so the row would stay orange for as long as the date is ahead (months, for a clock that jumped a year), which is exactly how a colour teaches you to ignore it. **(c)** (a), plus one quiet line naming the odd file ("One copy here is dated in the future — this Mac's clock may once have run fast.").
**Where it lives:** `StoreBackup.stampIsTrusted` (the one rule), `DataSafetyModel.summarize` / `summarizeExports` (the row). Pinned: `BackupFutureStampTests`.

#163 — **A NOTE YOU EDITED INSIDE A BOOK NOW GETS ITS QUIET SAVEPOINT WHEN YOU MOVE TO ANOTHER NOTE — WHAT COUNTS AS "MOVING", AND COMPARE'S PEERS (2026-09-30, 18o U4 — your R-18k-6; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18o-correctness-singles.md`'s UNIT U4 RECORD). Your ruling itself is SETTLED; two readings of it are PROVISIONAL — each one line to change.**
**The case:** your words at the 18k sitting — *"a note edited inside a book should get a savepoint when you open it"*, then at once **"When I move to another note"** — and the ruling's own last clause, *"Compare peers follow the same rule, PROVISIONAL."* Until now a note you wrote in inside a book (or in a Compare peer column) got its quiet savepoint only when you quit, locked the screen, or after two idle hours. It now gets it the moment you move on, through the same one road leaving a note on its own always used — the same rules decide what is saved (one quiet savepoint per take you changed; nothing for a take that matches its last savepoint; nothing at all under "Only when I commit" or "Idle only").
**(1) What counts as moving to another note in a book — built as (a).** The book already has one answer to "which note are you in": the note box in the sidebar (no note · a note selected · your caret in a note). **(a) as built:** the note your CARET was last in is left when your caret goes into a different note, when the book points you at a different note without a caret (a click on another note's row in Index, which lets go of the block you were in; turning the page in Book mode), or when you leave the book (the pane shows something else, the split closes, the window closes). Clicking out of a note, switching to Read, switching modes inside the same note, clicking into the other pane, and scrolling are NOT moving — and a note you only looked at (the note Index opens on, a page you turned past) is never saved on your behalf, because your caret was never in it. **(b)** only a caret moving into another note counts — a row click in Index or a page turn waits until your caret lands somewhere (or you leave the book). **(c)** also treat clicking out of a note as leaving it.
**(2) Compare's peers — built as (a), the ruling's PROVISIONAL clause.** **(a) as built:** a peer note is left when you swap its column to another note, close its column, or leave Compare (switching the note's mode away from Compare, going to another note, the window closing); the note Compare is open on is never left by Compare itself — it is the pane's own note, and leaving it works exactly as it always has. **The one-line alternative:** peers keep today's timing — their savepoint waits for quit, the screen lock, or the idle backstop.
**One consequence, the same one every leaving has always had:** a note whose takes have never been savepointed at all counts as unsaved, so the first time you move on from it, it gets one quiet savepoint per take (exactly what quitting or locking did for it before — only earlier). The "N quiet savepoints · show" line in Block History folds them away.
**Where it lives:** `Features/BookView/BookNoteTrail.swift` (the rule), `BookViewModel.notePlaceChanged` / `leaveBook`, `BookView` (reports the note box's state), `CompareViewModel` (`peerSlots`, `teardown`), `Workspace/SavepointOnLeave.swift` (roads 5 and 6). Pinned: `BookLeavingSavepointTests`, `CompareLeavingSavepointTests`, `BookLeavingRegistryTests`, `BookNoteTrailTests`.

#164 — **YOUR TYPED CONFIRMATION FOR A NEAR-EMPTY "REPLACE ALL KEYS" (#86(c)) — THE LOSS IT GUARDED CAN NO LONGER HAPPEN ON TODAY'S DOOR; DO YOU STILL WANT THE GATE? (2026-09-30, 18o U5 — 17t item 1 / KnownIssues row 54; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18o-correctness-singles.md`'s UNIT U5 RECORD). OPEN — nothing built; the door is unchanged until you pick.**
**The case:** your ruling on #86(c) — *"I would add a typed confirmation phrase for the near empty case"* — answered your own question, *"someone trying to enroll their phone could somehow overwrite their whole store with nothing - that won't happen, right?"*. When you ruled it (2026-09-01), Replace Encryption Keys DELETED the server's copy and re-uploaded THIS device's copy, so a nearly empty device really could have flattened a full account; a warning with counts and dates stood in front of it. **What changed since:** your postures design (settled 2026-09-04) rebuilt the door as Replace All Keys, and the rebuilt door works the other way round — it first downloads everything the server has onto this device, then re-encrypts the SERVER's own copy in place. Nothing on this device ever overwrites the server, so an almost-empty device cannot lose anything the account holds. The design removed the old warning for exactly that reason; the typed confirmation was then recorded as "never wired" by a later check that only looked for the code. 18o U5 proved it rather than assumed it: a device holding 3 of an account's 18 notes runs Replace All Keys — all 18 are still on the server under the new keys afterwards, and all 18 are on that device too. (Nor is there any other door that could overwrite the account with this device's copy — every road was checked.) **So your question's answer is now simply "no — by construction".**
**(a) Close it as answered — no gate. RECOMMENDED.** The typed phrase would stand in front of a loss that cannot happen, and the old warning's words ("those would be lost") would be untrue. The unused comparison code (`RekeyRecencyProbe`, which still counts what the server holds that a device lacks) is retired at the next session that works inside sync — or kept as a tested tool; your word either way. **(b) Keep a typed confirmation anyway, as a heads-up** when this device is far behind the account ("the server has 15 notes this Mac hasn't downloaded yet — they'll be downloaded first"), with a phrase to type before it proceeds. Friction without a loss behind it; the sentence and the phrase are yours (13s). **(c) Something else you have in mind.**
**Where it lives:** `App/Variorum/Shared/PostureSwitch.swift` (the rebuilt door — step 1 downloads, step 3 re-encrypts in place), `Sources/VariorumCore/Sync/RekeyRecencyProbe.swift` (the unused counter). Pinned: `PostureSwitchTests.aNearEmptyCopysReplaceAllKeysLosesNothingTheFullerServerHolds`.

#165 — **"COPY ALL" ON YOUR TWELVE WORDS MAY ALSO REACH YOUR OTHER APPLE DEVICES' CLIPBOARDS (UNIVERSAL CLIPBOARD), WHERE THE MAC'S 60-SECOND CLEAR CAN'T FOLLOW (2026-09-30, 18o U5 — found while putting Copy All on every Mac words screen, 17t item 2; the record is `notes/plans/phase-18o-correctness-singles.md`'s UNIT U5 RECORD). OPEN — nothing changed; today's behaviour stands until you pick.**
**The case:** your decision 23 — *"The recovery phrase's COPY ALL clears the clipboard after 60 s"* — is built exactly: the Mac puts the twelve words on its clipboard and wipes them 60 seconds later if you haven't copied anything else since. But the Mac's clipboard is the ordinary system one, and with Handoff on, macOS shares it with your nearby iPhone and iPad (Universal Clipboard). So for a short while the twelve words can also sit on those devices' clipboards, and the Mac's 60-second wipe only ever clears the Mac's own. Nothing has leaked anywhere you don't own — it is your devices on your Apple account — but it is a copy of the words in a place the 60-second promise doesn't cover. (18o U5 put Copy All on four more Mac screens that show the words — sync setup, Regenerate Twelve Words, the set-a-passphrase sheet and the nudge's phrase sheet — all through the SAME one rule, so this reaches every one of them equally; nothing about it is new today.)
**(a) Keep the words on this Mac only — RECOMMENDED.** One line in the one rule: macOS can mark a copy "this device only" (`currentHostOnly`), so Universal Clipboard never carries it; the 60-second wipe then covers every copy there is. Pasting the words into something on your iPhone would no longer work (you would type or dictate them there, which the words screens are designed for anyway). **(b) Keep it as built** — a Universal Clipboard copy is yours and short-lived. **(c) Something else.** Whichever you pick also sets the phone's door when the mobile app builds it (the phone's clipboard has the same "this device only" switch, plus an expiry date).
**Where it lives:** `App/Variorum/Shared/WordsCopyDoor.swift` (`WordsClipboard.copy` — the one rule). Pinned: `WordsCopyDoorTests`.

#166 — **THE 13i DESIGN SITTING'S PROVISIONALS (2026-09-30; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-13i-sitting-record-2026-09-30.md` — §16 posed these, §17 carries her same-day rulings). FOUR OF SIX RULED THE SAME DAY; only (c) and (d) carry anything open.**
**(a) RULED — the starter shelf is "Welcome"** (her words: *"I thought we decided that the starter shelf would be named 'Welcome' earlier in this chat. It will be."*): one shelf at birth, named Welcome, holding the seeded content; ordinary and renamable; new books land on it until she makes shelves. (Record §17's design note: a birth-time "Your books" second shelf is a one-line tweak if ever wanted — kept, not ruled.)
**(b) RULED IN — the classics seeded lesson ships:** Sherlock OWNED · Jekyll & Hyde plain · both `#classics` · the seeded classics gathering on Welcome · the seeded quicknote. Dependency pinned: the hide-owned global default stays SHOW-badged.
**(c) RULED (third pass, same day):** click to collapse, click to expand, hover to reveal while collapsed; the state remembered as a small local memory, never a Settings-pane row.
**(d) RATIFIED (third pass, same day) — tags + star with and/or/not; no churn-shaped rules in books** (her words: *"we'll make sure that search is good and maybe add a very capable search within a book if needed"* — 13o's within-a-book scope carries it). Three mechanism defaults stand with it, PROPOSED in the record's §19: subtree matching; rules reference tag IDs never paths; unstar/untag are non-events for intake.
**(e) RULED — minimal naming, nothing deeper** (her words: *"it can say it was in a different wing but I don't think we should have much more detailed linking than that… I don't want to encourage too much cross wing behavior"*): the lineage door under a cross-wing copy names the wing and at most offers a plain go-to-that-wing road; no preview, no content across, no deep links.
**(f) RULED — the `grouped by` control lives on the Index masthead** as proposed (chapters ▾ · a tag family… · none; view-local; a gathering's Index the same minus chapters).

#167 — **THE OWNED-EXCLUSIVITY SYNC REPAIR'S SHAPE — RATIFIED (a) AS BUILT, 2026-10-02 at the 13i-c close (the question tool, the three shapes explained: earliest act stands and the later act becomes what its door would have done · the owned side always wins · both survive for her hand; her pick: the first).** *(The original entry, 2026-09-30, 13i-a U2 — the record `notes/plans/phase-13i-a-schema-event.md` §5, the pins `Phase13iRepairTests`.)*
**The case:** two devices race acts the OWNED flag forbids from co-existing — device A's note sits membered in an owned book while device B (not yet seeing that) adds the same note somewhere else, or claims it into a second owned book. The repair must be deterministic on every device and the sitting ruled no destruction and no silence (§6's intake rule: an owned book's intake auto-claims FREE notes and BELLS membered ones).
**(a) as built — intake's own rule applied at convergence:** the EARLIEST-ACCEPTED membership stands (a never-pushed row carries no authority — SyncSpec §3's seq-0 rule; ties break by id). Each LATER conflicting act becomes what its door would have done knowing the truth: a later OWNED claim over an already-membered note DEMOTES to an ARRIVAL (the bell — claim / copy in / leave, visible at the book); a later PLAIN add beside a standing owned claim is TOMBSTONED (an ordinary recoverable tombstone — the ownership promise defended at the door). Competing owned books: first claim wins, the second book's row waits at ITS Arrivals (§18.4's own sentence).
**(b)** the owned side always wins regardless of order (strong ownership — but a later claim would then silently sever memberships that were legally made first). **(c)** both survive and the conflict surfaces as a card/notice for your hand (nothing automatic — but the store then carries a standing exclusivity violation every surface must tolerate).
**Where it lives:** `Sources/VariorumCore/Sync/Repairs.swift` (`repairOwnedExclusivity`); SyncSpec §7's paragraph.

#169 — **THE 13i-c RATIFY BUNDLE (2026-10-02; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-13i-c-owned-finished-sealed.md` — its §1 carries YOUR four kickoff rulings verbatim, §9 the kickoff PROVISIONALs, the U2/U3/U4 RECORDs each a numbered PROVISIONALs list with a ready paragraph per item, the ladder files `13ic-ladder-round1.md` / `-round2.md` two more). EVERYTHING BELOW IS BUILT AS STATED; each is one line or one constant to overturn.**
**The kickoff's twelve (§9, P-13ic-1…12):** the Own conversion registers no structural undo (the copies are real notes; Stop Owning is the symmetric door) · owning a FINISHED or sealed book refuses (reopen first) · a copy-in that answers an arrival decides the pair permanently · the bind's owned-notes limb defaults to LEAVE OUT (copies are ceremonies) · tags RIDE every ownership-ceremony copy, while the older Duplicate as Version / Diverge for book doors still carry no tags — **(open) should those two carry tags now too?** · deleting a sealed book (book only) leaves its members' seals in place · Duplicate copies the book's CONFIGURATION never its state (unshelved, unstarred, open, non-owned), no backfill, no undo · `auto_tag_ids` travels PLAIN (ids only) · the revival pass ranks the earliest-created membership as the standing one · the badge family's one switch is a synced setting · `tagAllMembers(nil)` = every outflow tag, its receipt counting notes · Move in registers no undo.
**U2's nine (P-13ic-U2-1…9):** a ceremony copy's riding tags are a row copy, not a tagging act (they pull the copy into no living book) · an EMPTY note forks as a bare copy with no lineage edge · Copy in / Move in from Arrivals land at the END and Copy in applies the book's outflow · Move in REFUSES while a membership it would sever sits in a FINISHED book (Copy in offered instead) · duplicating the daily Journal makes a journal-trait book, never a second daily Journal · a note in the Trash when its book was SEALED comes back OUTSIDE the sealed book (unseal and add it by hand) — the soak's find · hide-owned also hides the gathering from the note's "appears in" chip · Stop Owning is allowed on a finished or sealed book · ceremony copies are born unarchived (an archived shared member's copy shows).
**U3's seven (P-13ic-U3-1…7):** a note's own Unseal inside a sealed book stays VISIBLE and explains the road (the alternative hides it) · **gatherings keep the shipped Finish and Word target / Note cap faces — RULED at the adjudication to the record's letter: they go at 13i-d (KnownIssues)** · Own Its Notes… stays on a finished/sealed book's menu and names the road · Edit… CLOSES the settings sheet and opens the rule editor over the book (the nested editor's offer was unreachable — the walk's find) · a dead outflow tag drops on any save · Reopen and Stop Owning act without asking · all new wording is 13s-revoicable.
**U4's eleven (P-13ic-U4-1…11):** the daily Journal's own "+ new entry" BELLS on an ownership conflict exactly as ⌘J does · Move in from the Arrivals line acts at once · Manage Notes… carries the remove/release door too · the two ownership settings live in Settings ▸ Editor ▸ Books · the marks' look (a small muted two-squares glyph inside a book; "owned by “X”" outside) · **a plain book's Delete now ASKS** (it acted at once before) · Duplicate's defaults (the name "X copy"; "with copies" off on a plain book) · the gathering's honest door offers only the rule's POSITIVE tags the note wears · no owned-by mark in the Pool (structurally silent — Pool notes are bookless) · a plain remove that leaves the note bookless says so · the wording.
**The ladder's two (genuinely open — product edges the ladder found):** **(a)** FINISHING or SEALING dismisses EVERY waiting arrival, archived-note arrivals included — the confirm's N now counts exactly that set (the fix). The alternative: finishing SPARES the arrivals of ARCHIVED notes (they would return on unarchive, P-13ib-U2-2's old promise, which now carries the exception "unless the book was finished meanwhile"). Which? **(b) RULED 2026-10-02 — HOME-ONLY** (her lean, the question tool: *"my lean is to have it home-only … why do I hit the same keyboard shortcut but now they're not in the same journal"*): the daily Journal lives in Home and stays there (a cross-wing move refuses; the designation is Home's); ⌘J and Today's Entry file into it from ANY wing — the entry born in Home beside its Journal; one entry per day for the whole store. 13i-d builds the guard (its item 6). **(a) RULED 2026-10-02 — "the three together, at 13i-d"** (her reasoning, verbatim-faithful: *"archived hides from a book's index and outline but it still is a part of that book … should we build being able to see the archived members into books? … a per book 'archive', since it's the soft trash, and then you see what didn't make it in … archived notes don't lose their books, it makes me think that the admission door should still show them … someone might want to add an archived note to their book"*). The shape: (1) a PER-BOOK ARCHIVED LINE (the Arrivals line's idiom — invisible when empty; "N archived" under the masthead, expanding inline to the archived members with Unarchive and Remove/Release) and HONEST COUNTS ("5 notes · 2 archived" on the shelf row and the masthead; hidden with details off) — the facts behind it: today every count and view of a book EXCLUDES archived members, so there is no hidden bump but also no door to a book's set-aside notes; (2) ARCHIVED WAITERS STAY VISIBLE at the door, marked "archived", with the same doors — Add seats one as an archived member (it lands in the archived line) — REVERSING 13i-b's P-13ib-U2-2 (hide while archived, return on unarchive); (3) FINISHING dismisses every waiter, all visible and counted (the 13i-c fix stands; nothing hidden). The Index itself never shows archived members (archive keeps its one meaning: kept, out of the working view). Faces + one query; no schema, no `Sync/` — 13i-d's item 9.
**Also yours, recorded:** the offer toast's 6 s clock is tight for "Also tag the 2 current members with #a #b now?" (U3's F-U3-8) — lengthen, or make offers stay until dismissed?
**THE TAILS RULED 2026-10-03 (the family-close sitting, the question tool):** Duplicate as Version SHOULD CARRY TAGS — her two beats the same sitting ("it's meant to be a duplication", then mid-walk: *"let's just build the \"duplicate as version\" copies tags now — I think it's a bit sloppy to not have it"*) — BUILDS AT **17y** (the 13i family fix pass, item c); **Diverge STAYS TAG-LESS** (her lean: "you want it to be… different than what's already there"); the offer toast KEEPS 6 s as built. Her follow-on idea — an automatic "Duplicate"/version-of tag — is PARKED at **17z** (an exploration, deliberately undecided: no tag and no chip FOR NOW, her word mid-walk). Nothing in this bundle remains open.

#174 — **THE 13i-d U5 PROVISIONALS — wings: the switcher, the per-wing surfaces, the note doors across wings, the Home-only daily Journal (2026-10-02; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-13i-d-bookcase.md` — its U5 RECORD carries each with options). Each built as stated; each one line or one constant to overturn.**
**Held for the architect first, not you:** the BOOK-MOVE half — moving a whole book to another wing and the bulk "Move everything to another wing…" (your R-13id-4 backdoor) — is STOPPED with a written proposal (what happens to finished/sealed books, archived members, waiting arrivals, a rule's tags that a wing lacks, the wing's Trash rows, colliding book names). Until it is built, a wing is emptied by hand (move or delete its notes, delete its books and tags), and Delete Wing then succeeds.
**Wings and where you are:** a wing's name may not repeat another wing's or Home's (U5-1) · the wing you're in is app-wide and remembered on this Mac (U5-3) · the switcher sits on the Bookcase masthead, "Bookcase · Home ▾", always drawn; other surfaces don't name the wing (U5-6) · switching is a way of looking — silent (U5-13) · Delete Wing names what's left and only asks when the wing is empty; the wing you're in returns you Home (U5-14) · a note deleted inside a wing you later delete shows in Home's Trash and comes back into Home (U5-2).
**What scopes and what doesn't:** Snippets stay one shared area across wings (U5-4) · the quick-note pad files into Home; ⌘N, the toolbar, the Bookcase box and dropped imports land in the wing you're in (U5-5) · Starred shows the wing you're in; the 15-star cap is shared (U5-10) · pickers, Compare, a book's add list and the book chooser offer the note's own wing; a typed tag lands in the note's wing (U5-15) · the sidebar's Open list keeps notes from another wing after a switch (F-U5-6 — keep, or scope it?) · book names stay unique across ALL wings (F-U5-10 — or per wing?).
**Moving and copying a note:** a move leaves its books (listed first), carries its tags (same-name tags adopted, missing ones made), takes its saved versions along, and refuses while the note sits in a finished or sealed book; a bookless note just moves (U5-7, U5-12) · a copy is born in the other wing, in no book, its tags adopted, remembering where it came from — Compare says "a version of this note lives in the wing “X” · go to X" and shows nothing of it (U5-8).
**The daily Journal (your #169(b), built):** ⌘J from another wing opens Home's entry and leaves you where you are; a day's entry moved out of Home stops being the day's entry; "Use as Daily Journal" only on Home books (U5-9).
**Words** are first drafts for 13s (U5-11).
**BOTH OPEN CALLS RULED 2026-10-03 (the question tool): KEEP AS BUILT** — the Open list survives a wing switch (F-U5-6), and book names stay unique across ALL wings (F-U5-10). **F-U5-6 REFINED BY HER THE SAME SITTING, mid-walk:** the survival stands, but a cross-wing Open item needs a WING INDICATOR, and clicking it + starting to edit should NAVIGATE to that wing ("your left bar showing 'library' should reflect the wing you're editing in at the very least"; her lived confusion recorded at the entry) — **17y item (f)** carries it, and **the shape is RULED (her 2026-10-04 follow-up):** switch on OPEN ("I said edit but I meant on open") with the quiet receipt; split panes follow the FOCUSED pane's note on focus too ("…doesn't make much sense to not change on focus if we normally change on open") — the damping PROVISIONAL resolved; one app-wide wing stays the truth; editors never close.

**Moving a book (Move to Wing ▸ on its Bookcase row):** its notes come with it — archived ones stay archived — and any of them that also sat in a book staying behind leave that book, listed in the confirm ("3 notes called “2026-10-01” also leave “u3 spawn walk”" when several share a name, U5b-14); a note also in a FINISHED or sealed book that stays refuses the move at the door, naming it; notes waiting at its door are dismissed, counted in the confirm; it lands unshelved, star kept; a gathering moves alone and shows the other wing's matches at once; the daily Journal has no such door; it always asks first and there is no undo — moving back is the way back (U5b-2, U5b-4, U5b-12) · a note waiting at ANOTHER book's door is quietly dismissed when its book moves (U5b-2) · moving does not make the other wing's books pick up notes that already match them — only the next tagging does (U5b-1) · a rule's tags that were already deleted stay deleted (U5b-10) · Quicknotes can move — but quick notes keep filing into Home, so a moved Quicknotes stops showing new ones (U5b-8: allow, or keep Quicknotes Home-only?).
**Move Everything (the switcher, on any wing but Home):** a submenu of the other wings; the confirm counts what moves and says what becomes of the wing's tags — joining same-named ones, made new, or (named) let go because nothing uses them — and that the wing ends empty; you stay in the emptied wing so Delete Wing is right there; an already-empty wing just says so (U5b-3, U5b-6) · a snippet wearing one of the wing's tags is re-pointed too, so the wing really ends empty (U5b-7).
**After the wing is gone (the Trash):** its Trash shows in Home's; a note put back comes home but outside any book that moved to a third wing, and a book put back comes home without notes that moved elsewhere — nothing crosses a wing by itself (U5b-5); such a note also comes back without the wing's tags, since the move retired them (U5b-13 — keep, or carry them?).
**Words** are first drafts for 13s, the Delete Wing refusal's new "— or move everything to another wing" included (U5b-9).
**BOTH OPEN CALLS RULED 2026-10-03 (the question tool): KEEP AS BUILT** — Quicknotes may move wings (U5b-8; her reasoning: a quick note is just a note wearing the tag — "I don't think quicknotes are more unique than just the tag being applied to them"; only the Journal is 1-of-1), and a deleted wing's Trash restoree returns without the retired tags (U5b-13).

#176 — **THE 13i-d U6 PROVISIONALS — the vocabulary sweep, and undo after a wing move (2026-10-02; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-13i-d-bookcase.md` — its U6 RECORD carries each with options). Each built as stated; each one line or one constant to overturn.**
**Undo after a wing move (ACT 0, built to the architect's ruling on #175's held item):** ⌥⌘Z / ⇧⌥⌘Z never put a note back into a book that now lives in another wing — such an undo or redo does nothing at all · redoing a "bind into a book" after any of its notes moved away (or after the gathering itself moved) does nothing — it stays a gathering, and binding again by hand gives you today's notes (U6-1: all-or-nothing, as ruled — or re-bind everything that still can?) · for now the bottom bar still says "Undid: …" when such an undo did nothing (an architect item, F-U6-2).
**Words:** the membership chip counts books and gatherings apart — "in the door · 1 gathering", and "This note is in 1 book and appears in 1 gathering:" (U6-2) · a refusal that knows the book is sealed says Unseal; one that does not know says both ("…reopen it first; if it's sealed, unseal it") (U6-4) · the Library says Notes, and its day filter says "edited on…" (U6-8) · two "Notes" now sit side by side at the top of the Library — the kind of thing, and the cards view (her call: rename the cards view?, F-U6-5) · "Create Gathering…" on the Bookcase and "Make a Gathering…" in Settings ▸ Tags (U6-7) · a gathering's footer: "it shows every note its rule matches and holds none of them. A note comes and goes with its tags and its star." (U6-9) · a wing stays a "wing", and the doors that make or cross one say it is "a separate library" (U6-6) · search and ⌘K call a book "gathering", "sealed book", "finished book" or "book" (U6-13) · dismissing an arrival now says "it won't come back on its own; add it by hand any time" (item 10, ruled).
**Faces:** the Library's sort menu and day filter sit 1 pt lower so they line up with the segmented controls beside them (U6-5) · Move to Wing / Copy to Wing are in the note's own note ▾ menu too (U6-11) · the Library now shows the quiet receipts its doors speak, in its message row (U6-10).
**Untitled notes:** the book's own lists (and receipts) still call an untitled note by its date while the Library calls it by its first words — kept for now (U6-3: keep · the two lines only · every book list, at 13s).
**THE THREE RULED 2026-10-03 (the question tool):** U6-1 KEEPS the all-or-nothing no-op — ruled after the mechanism was laid out (the redo replays EXACT pre-image rows, so a partial replay would mint a state neither undo nor redo ever saw; binding by hand is never stopped and gathers today's matches); **F-U6-5 RULED: the cards view renames to "Cards"** — builds at **17y** (item d); U6-3 KEEPS the naming split for now, her reason recorded as evidence: the date "makes it clear which notes have a real name versus don't in a book" (13s may still revisit). #177 — **THE 13i-d U7 PROVISIONALS — the books chapter, the classics lesson, the Welcome shelf (2026-10-02; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-13i-d-bookcase.md` — its U7 RECORD carries each with options). Each built as stated; each one line or one constant to overturn.**

Your real store never ran that build.

**Platform:** the phone does not set up Welcome yet — the same brain is ready for its catch-up (U7-14).

#178 — **THE SAMPLE LIBRARY'S ASYMMETRIC DEDUPE MEETING — two optional remedies, both yours (13i-d ladder B round 1's MINOR 1, ruled record-as-residual on 2026-10-02; the record is `notes/plans/13id-ladder-round2B.md` + the KnownIssues row it added).** The fact: the Welcome arrangement (Sherlock owned, #classics on the stories, the Welcome shelf) runs on the MAC only, so if a second device seeds the sample library before its first clean sync (a phone used signed-out, or an old build) and its RAW copy holds the smaller id, the sync dedupe keeps the raw copy and your arranged one retires with its demo state — the classics gathering loses Sherlock, Sherlock reads plain and unshelved. Nothing of yours is lost (the stories are the shipped bytes; Help ▸ Restore sets Welcome up again), it is pinned as documented behaviour, and the phone's arrangement catch-up closes it for good. Two stronger remedies exist, each a call only you can make: **(a)** the dedupe prefers an ARRANGED copy over a raw one before falling to smaller-id — one more arm in the ruled keeper matrix (it would amend your R-pre13u-8 "both pristine → smaller id" rule); **(b)** the seeder itself arranges on EVERY platform, so a fresh phone store is born with Welcome, the owned Sherlock and #classics (this decides what a first-write phone store looks like — content you have only ruled for the Mac). Doing neither is also fine: the recorded residual + the phone catch-up already cover it. **RULED 2026-10-03 (the question tool): NEITHER** — the recorded residual + the phone's arrangement catch-up stand; remedies (a) and (b) declined.

#179 — **THE 13i-e BUNDLE — book archive + the daily Journal (2026-10-03; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-13i-e-daily-journal.md` — the U1/U2 RECORDs carry each PROVISIONAL with options, the two architect adjudications carry the rulings, THE ORCHESTRATOR'S WALK carries W-O-1).** **AT ITS HEAD, THE ONE GENUINELY OPEN CALL — W-O-1, the ⌘J canon conflict found by use:** `createOrOpenJournalEntry` still carries the Phase 8 notebook rule (a day with a live entry OPENS it), so the identity memo §0(a)'s RULED always-mint ("Today's Entry / ⌘J / + new entry always MINT") was never built, and the sitting record §10's "door discouragement already gone" is wrong as a fact; your own words cut both ways (your 2026-09-04 "We should remove the door discouragement" vs. the same message's "where Today's Entry and the shortcut TAKES YOU"). PRESSED LIVE BY HER on the real store, 2026-10-04 at the walk close — both roads, the same-day re-archive included: "journal unarchived with every command J press — even after archiving it on the same day, that's good."** — **The sustained PROVISIONALs, each one line to overturn:** *the op family (U1):* P-13ie-U1-1 double archive/unarchive REFUSE (the block-archive precedent) · U1-2 archive-with-notes refuses a gathering only at the op; the DOOR offers it on owned books alone · U1-3 the exact-stamp corners (a note archived on its own stays archived after the book's unarchive; a note that left the book stays) · U1-4 no undo entry, the redo future untouched · U1-5 the pause lives in the ONE intake table (every auto road; a rule edit while archived fires nothing until unarchive) · U1-6 explicit adds untouched, the OUTFLOW still rides an explicit add · U1-7 the Bookcase excludes archived on both arms; the pickers KEEP archived books (marked); the Archived feed newest-first · U1-8 MEMBERSHIP IS A FACT (an archived book still homes its notes — not in the Pool; an archived owned book still owns) · U1-9 the ⌘K/search catalog drops archived books · U1-10 an archived STARRED book stays in the Starred section · U1-11 the E13 name guard skips archived names (your "archived/deleted books don't count") · U1-12 the comes-back un-archives FIRST with the filing savepointed — a REFUSED filing still brings the Journal back; a decided pair brings nothing back (W-O-1 is this arm's lived edge) · U1-13 superseded by the receipt ruling (built at U2). *The faces (U2):* P-13ie-U2-1 the archive door acts AT ONCE on a plain book, a gathering and the daily Journal;
