#180 — **THE CALM-ASKS CADENCE'S NUMBERS + U3-A's PROVISIONAL readings (13r U3-A, 2026-10-04; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-13r-distribution.md` — the U3 section's CALM-ASKS CADENCE, ratified as written at your U3 kickoff, and the U3-A RECORD's piece 6). Each built as stated; each one constant to change.**
**THE NUMBERS (provisional by the cadence's own letter, tunable from beta use):** the update ask from the **3rd** day of use · the backup-folder ask from the **5th** day · no ask until the app has been open **2 minutes** · and nothing typed for **20 seconds** · an ignored backup ask returns once after **7** further days of use, then never. (`CalmAskNumbers.shipped` — one struct.)
**The readings I had to make where the cadence is silent (each the most literal I could find):** (a) "nothing is open" also means Variorum is the frontmost app — an ask never appears in a window you are not looking at; (b) the cadence looks for a calm moment every 15 seconds; (c) if the update ask's window is closed WITHOUT an answer (Variorum quit while it was up), it asks again on a later day of use — Sparkle's own window has no "later" button, so an unanswered window is not an answer; (d) "writing of your own" = a note in the library with words on its page that wears none of the seeded tags (the field guide's, each sample book's, #classics) and is not the seeded quick note — a tutorial note you practised in does not count; (e) the backup offer toast keeps the app's ONE 6-second toast clock, and a toast that times out or is closed with ✕ counts as ignored (it returns once, as the cadence says); (f) What's New is NOT one of the asks — it shows once after a version change, and while its sheet is open no ask can appear (a sheet is open).
**Also yours before the first release:** 0.9.0's release notes (`App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/WhatsNew/ReleaseNotes-0.9.0.md` — What's New and the appcast both read it) are a functional draft for you to rewrite; and Settings ▸ General's new "Check for updates automatically" row's two sentences are first drafts (13s re-voices).
**Status: ANSWERED (2026-10-04, the post-close sitting via the question tool): RATIFIED AS BUILT — the five numbers and the six readings (a)–(f) stand as shipped; the numbers stay one struct, tunable from beta evidence. The notes item was discharged in-phase (her 0.9.0 rewrite shipped with the release). The §12 calm-asks contract line LANDED at the same sitting (AppSpec §12 + §14's 13r head).**

#181 — **U3-B's PROVISIONAL readings — crash reporting, the Diagnostics surface, Help ▸ Send Feedback… (13r U3-B, 2026-10-04; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-13r-distribution.md` — the U3-B RECORD). Each built as stated; each one line to overturn. Nothing here gates the beta.**
**The mail draft (R-13r-3/5/9 rule the draft; these are the roads under it):** (a) **Send Feedback… opens in your DEFAULT mail app** (a `mailto:` draft — Apple Mail, Outlook, a browser's mail handler, whichever you chose), while a crash report's Send uses **Apple Mail's compose window**, because it is the only road that can attach a file (Apple's own note: a `mailto:` cannot carry an attachment). Alternative: both through Apple Mail (one road, but a friend who lives in Gmail gets Mail's setup screen for plain feedback). (b) **When no mail app can take the attachment**, Variorum shows the report in Finder and opens the plain draft with a line asking for it to be attached ("Please attach crash-report-….json — Variorum has shown it to you in Finder."). Alternatives: refuse with the address and a Show in Finder door, opening no draft; or paste the report into the draft's text (rejected — mail apps cap a `mailto:` body around 2,000 characters and a crash report is longer). (c) The draft's words — the two subjects ("Variorum feedback", "Variorum crash report"), the rule and the two version lines, the "Attached:" line — are first drafts for 13s. (d) **Help ▸ Send Feedback… sits at the END of the Help menu after a divider**, always enabled (it needs no window or library), and is undeclared in the capability registry on the What's New / Open the Tutorial precedent — the ladder reads that as a P-1 question, not yours unless you want it elsewhere.
**What is collected:** (e) only MetricKit's CRASH diagnostics are kept — a hang or slow-disk diagnostic that arrives in the same delivery is never stored, shown or sent (your ruling names crash reports). Alternative: keep hangs too, as their own kind of report. (f) **turning crash reporting OFF keeps the reports already received** — they are yours, on this Mac, until you delete them. Alternative: OFF also clears them. (g) no cap on how many reports are kept (a handful a year is the expected rate). (h) **Delete Report acts at once, with no confirm** — a crash report is a local diagnostic file, not your writing, and it cannot be sent anywhere by deleting it. Alternative: a confirm ("Delete this crash report? It can't be brought back."). **(i) MEASURED ON THE WALK — a Mac whose Mail has NO account (this M3's, as it happens): both doors land in Mail's own "Add Account" sheet, no draft appears, and Variorum cannot tell** (the compose service reports success; cancelling the sheet quits Mail and the draft is gone; nothing is ever sent). A friend who never set up Apple Mail (Gmail in the browser, say) meets exactly this. Options: **keep as built** (the draft appears wherever Mail is set up; the address is in the Diagnostics caption and the support page); **Send Report… ALSO shows the report file in Finder every time** (so it can be attached from any mail app, one extra window); **a small choice before the draft** — "Open in Mail" or "Show the file and copy the address". The plain feedback door has the same shape, minus the file.
**Status: ANSWERED (2026-10-04, the post-close sitting via the question tool): (a)–(h) RATIFIED AS BUILT; (i) RULED — THE SMALL CHOICE BEFORE THE DRAFT (her pick of the third shape): both support doors ask "Open in Mail" / "Show the file and copy the address" before any draft opens — the plain feedback door the same shape minus the file. The build is 19f's (the active ledger, opened the same sitting); the words stay 13s's to re-voice.**

#182 — **19f's PROVISIONAL readings — the support doors' pre-draft choice (19f, 2026-10-04; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-19-standing-fixes.md` — the 19f RECORD). Your #181 (i) ruling is built as ruled: before ANY draft opens, Send Report… asks "Open in Mail" / "Show the file and copy the address" and Help ▸ Send Feedback… asks "Open in Mail" / "Copy the address"; "Open in Mail" is the old road exactly; the second road puts support@stargazing.tech on the clipboard (and, for a report, shows its file in Finder) and opens no draft. These are the edges the ruling left open — each built as stated, each one line to overturn. Nothing here gates the beta.**
(a) **Where Help ▸ Send Feedback… asks.** In the main window, as an ordinary alert (the same kind every confirm in the app uses); if you are in Settings or a note window, the main window comes forward to ask. With NO window to ask in (the library not open — e.g. the lock screen — or every window closed) the door, which is always enabled, asks in a free-standing system alert, the way it already says "couldn't open a draft" there. Alternatives: always the free-standing alert (one look everywhere, but the only confirm of its kind in the app); or grey the door when no window exists (it stops being always available).
(b) **The second road says what it did.** Help ▸ Send Feedback… → "Copy the address" leaves a receipt — "Copied support@stargazing.tech — paste it into any mail app." — in the bottom bar (your Confirmations setting governs it, quiet by default). Send Report… → "Show the file and copy the address" says "Copied support@stargazing.tech and showed crash-report-….json in Finder — attach it to an email from any mail app." under the reports in Settings ▸ Diagnostics. With no library open, nothing more is said (the question's own words said what the button does). Alternatives: say nothing anywhere (the button's words are the receipt); or always a toast.
(c) **The order and the default button.** "Open in Mail" first (your order), then the second road, then Cancel; the first is the one Return presses. Alternative: make the second road the default (it can never dead-end), or have no default.
(d) **The copied address never expires** from the clipboard (it is a public address; the twelve words' 60-second clear exists because they are a secret).
(e) **One word of the Diagnostics promise follows the change:** "Send Report… opens an email draft…" now reads "Send Report… offers an email draft…" — it asks first now, so "opens" stopped being exactly true. The rest of the promise is untouched; all of its words stay 13s's to re-voice.
**Status: ANSWERED (2026-10-04, the same sitting via the question tool): (a)–(e) ALL RATIFIED AS BUILT — with two riders from the 19f cold review, recorded the same hour:** (1) **(a)'s description is CORRECTED (the review's MINOR 2):** the free-standing alert's real trigger is "no MAIN window" — Settings alone, a note-only window or the quick-note pad may still be open and the door asks free-standing in front of them (the code branches on the window registry's `.full` kind; the as-built text's "every window closed" understated). Her ratification was taken on the pre-correction wording; the corrected clause is FLAGGED for her one-word confirm (the behavior itself is unchanged — only the description moved). (2) **(e) now names BOTH sites:** the pane's promise AND the Settings catalog blurb (the search result's detail line) — the blurb's "opens" was the review's MINOR 1, fixed at adjudication with a red-first pin (`theCatalogBlurbSpeaksThePromisesVerb`); one overturn reverts both. **And one 13s note (the review's NIT 6):** the choice's button says "Mail" while ratified (a) sends plain feedback to her DEFAULT mail app (possibly not Mail) — the detail line already says "in your mail app"; 13s re-voices with that nuance in view.

#183 — **17y B-SEED's PROVISIONAL readings — the field guide is now born with a fresh store (17y item (q), 2026-10-05; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-17-standing-fixes.md` — the 17y B-SEED RECORD). What changed: on a brand-new library the field guide now arrives at first launch, on the Welcome shelf beside the books (your §19 "Welcome = the field guide + the classics trio"); before, nothing at launch ever made the guide — it only ever came from Help ▸ Open the Tutorial / Restore Tutorial…, which is exactly what you hit. These are the edges the fix had to decide. Each built as stated; each one line to overturn. Nothing here gates the release.**
(a) **When it appears.** Only when a library is BORN (the same moment Welcome is set up) — never on a library that already exists, including a library born on the 0.9.x builds without a guide (yours from the new-computer walk): those keep Help ▸ Restore Tutorial… as their road, the same no-silent-retrofit rule Welcome follows. Alternative: a one-time "add the guide if it's missing" pass on existing libraries.
(b) **If anything of the tutorial is already there, it does nothing.** A guide already present (say, arrived from your account) is left alone; a guide you deleted stays deleted; and leftover tutorial notes with no guide book (notes tagged exactly "tutorial", the scraps) are never replaced at launch — replacing them is the Restore door's job, because it ASKS first and a first launch never asks (R-13r-11). Alternative: replace the leftovers silently (it could delete a note of yours tagged "tutorial").
(c) **THE ONE VISIBLE CONSEQUENCE — two field guides after signing in on a NEW computer.** A fresh install is always signed out at its first launch (your session never travels between Macs), so it makes its own guide; when you then sign in, your account's guide arrives too, and BOTH stand — nothing is lost, delete either. The sync engine does not merge guides (that would be deep sync work); this is the same thing that already happens today, before this fix, to the Jekyll book, the "starred notes" and "classics" gatherings, Quicknotes and "a first quick note" on that same road. Your Monday install is exactly this road. Alternatives: (i) teach sync to fold the guide (and those others) like Sherlock — a sync-engine batch, its own entry; (ii) the 13s seed-set reshape decides what a new library is born with at all, which shrinks this list.
(d) **It is silent.** No message at first launch; the guide is simply on the shelf (no popup — R-13r-11).
(e) **If you press Open the Tutorial in the same instant the new library is making its guide,** the press does nothing (the guide appears a moment later) — the tutorial doors' existing double-press rule; and a door already running makes the launch step aside so two guides can never be made at once.

#184 — **17y B2's PROVISIONAL readings — the drags, the quick-entry cursor, the title, Canvas (17y items (a)(b)(e)(m)(n)(p), 2026-10-05; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-17-standing-fixes.md` — the 17y B2 RECORD). What changed: every drag on the Bookcase and in the Book Panel now works (the app had never told macOS about its own drag types, so every drop was ignored — the old "it's just the test harness" note was this bug); "my order" shows a ⠿ handle on every row; a book you move to another shelf no longer freezes its row; the quick-entry box blinks one cursor; clicking a date-named note's title no longer shifts or clips the date; Canvas is gone from the released app and kept in the Debug build. These are the edges the fixes had to decide. Each built as stated; each one line to overturn. Nothing here gates the release.**
(a) **The ⠿ handle in "my order"** sits in each row's left margin and is always visible while "my order" is on — never on hover, never in A–Z or last edited. Alternatives: "≡" drag bars; or the handle only on hover.
(b) **A book with only ONE chapter shows no chapter handle in the Book Panel** — it has nowhere to move, and you said a handle that can't drag shouldn't show. Alternative: keep the handle anyway, matching the book's own navigator.
(c) **In a book sorted by date, dragging a note to a new spot inside its own chapter now SAYS why it can't** — "“Journal” is sorted by Entry date ↓, so a note can't be placed by hand — set the book's order ▾ to Manual order and save it." Dragging it into a DIFFERENT chapter still just files it there (at its date's place), as the book view always has. Alternatives: refuse every note drag in a date-sorted book; or offer to switch the book to Manual order right there. (The book's own navigator still does the silent version — recorded for a later fix.)
(d) **One cursor in the quick-entry box:** the Terminal block cursor steps aside the moment your caret is in the box (and comes back when it leaves) — that is what the box was built to do and had stopped doing. Alternative: while the box is still empty, keep the block cursor and hide the ordinary caret until you type.
(e) **The date shown in an open title field** is drawn in the voice's own placeholder grey (it was the system's grey before). Alternative: draw it in the same colour as the resting title, so nothing visibly changes when the field opens.
(f) **A note you left in Canvas on the released app reopens in Write** (your real store has used Canvas). Alternative: reopen it in Blocks.
(g) **The Snippets area's Canvas face goes too** in the released app (Canvas is one feature on notes and on snippets), and its Scraps | Canvas switch disappears with it. Alternative: keep the Snippets canvas in the released app.
(h) **A book dragged across the starred line doesn't land exactly where you dropped it** (found by the B2 cold review; added at its fix round). In "my order", starred books always stay at the top — your §20 rule ("send to top would still be underneath starred") — so an UNstarred book dropped onto a starred one goes below every starred book, among the unstarred ones wherever its new place falls (often at their top); a starred book dropped onto an unstarred one stays up with the starred. The handle's hover text used to promise "it lands right above the row you drop it on", which isn't true there; it now says "Dropped on a book, it lands right above it — except across the starred line: starred books always stay first." The behaviour itself is your settled design and is unchanged. Alternatives: refuse a drop across the starred line with a short reason ("Starred books stay first — unstar it to place it here"); or leave it as built and let your walk decide.

#185 — **17y B3's PROVISIONAL readings — faces, words, and the behaviours you ruled (17y items (c)(d)(g)(o)(h)(i)(f)(l)(r)(s) + the book view's drop gate, 2026-10-05; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-17-standing-fixes.md` — the 17y B3 RECORD). What changed: Duplicate as Version now carries the note's tags (Diverge doesn't); the Library's cards view is called Cards; "unfiled" is the one word for a note in no chapter; dragging a note to a new spot inside its chapter in a date-sorted book now says why it can't (the book's own view, like the Book Panel); the № panel drops down over the board instead of pushing it; a "starred" shelf sits beside All; books have a right-click menu in the Library, the Starred list and Archived; Arrivals is a small tray icon beside a book's gear, and a waiting note opens when you click its name; the "+ tag" pickers and "Add to Book" are searchable and can make a new tag or a new book; opening a note from another wing takes the app to that wing ("Now in Studio."), and the Open list marks notes from another wing. These are the edges the builds had to decide. Each built as stated; each one line to overturn. Nothing here gates the release.**
(a) **Duplicated tags don't pull the copy into books.** A duplicate wears the same tags, but a book that collects by one of those tags does not take the copy in (or ring it at Arrivals) — copying is not tagging, the same as every other copy door. Alternative: treat the copy's tags as a fresh tagging, so collecting books take it in.
(b) **"unfiled" is lowercase in the move menus too**, matching the headers. Alternative: "Unfiled" in menus. (The menu still offers "unfiled" for a note that is already unfiled — moving it there does nothing; hiding that choice is yours if you want it.)
(c) **The book view's own "sorted by Entry date" refusal** shows on the book view's error line at the bottom (where its "finished — reopen it" refusals already show), naming the order you're looking through. Alternative: the toast, like the Book Panel's.
(d) **The № panel floats over the first few rows' right ends** while it's open. Alternative: a small popover pointing at the №.
(e) **The starred shelf:** right after All, always shown, the plain word "starred" (your three provisionals); you can't place books by hand there (a book's hand position belongs to its real shelf, so dragging inside "starred" could move a book to another shelf without saying so); a new book made while you're looking at starred lands unshelved and unstarred; dropping a book on "starred" stars it silently (already starred: nothing happens; at fifteen stars it says why); gatherings you've starred show there too.
(f) **The book menu outside the Bookcase** (Library, Starred, Archived) is: Open · Star/Unstar · Book Settings… · Export Markdown… · Export PDF… · Archive (or Unarchive) · Delete Book… — the same everywhere. Renaming, the rule, length and the state doors are inside Book Settings. Alternatives: the whole Bookcase menu minus the moving doors; or Star alone.
(g) **Arrivals:** a tray icon beside the gear — faint with no number when nothing waits, the accent colour and the number when something does; only on books (a gathering has no door); in every book mode, Read included. Finishing a book now says "2 waiting at Arrivals will be dismissed." The popover says "Arrivals · 3 waiting", or when empty: "Nothing is waiting. When this book's rule brings in a note and its dial says “wait at Arrivals,” the note waits here for you." Clicking a waiting note's name opens it where you are (it isn't added — only Add adds).
(h) **Making a tag from the rule editor's picker makes it right away** — if you then Cancel the editor, the new tag stays (like a tag typed on a note). Alternative: make it only when you Save. The picker only offers "New tag …" for a name that doesn't exist yet in that book's wing; "/" makes a hierarchy (life/goals). The spawn sheet's tag picker searches but can't make tags yet, and the "+ group" menus are still flat lists — both are on the list to adopt the new picker next time they're touched.
(i) **"Add to Book" is always there now**, even with no other book (you can make one from it). A new book is made in the note's own wing. If the note can't simply be added (another book owns it) the copy/move choice opens for the NEW book — and if you cancel that, the new empty book stays. Alternative: remove the new book on cancel. The receipt: "Made the book “X” and added this note." The Pool's Add to Book sheet gets the same "New book …" row.
(j) **Following wings:** the receipt reads "Now in “Studio”." (or "Now in Home."). Clicking a note's Open row again counts as opening it, so it takes you to its wing even if it was already showing. BOOKS follow too (if you reach a book from another wing — say with Back — the wing switches the same way). Alternative: notes only. The Open list marks a note from another wing with that wing's name, faint, after its title. A note opened in its own small window doesn't move the wing.

#186 — **17y B-DEDUPE's PROVISIONAL readings — the seed-duplicate fold (R-17y-5/R-17y-8, 2026-10-05; the binding records are the B-DEDUPE RECORD in `notes/plans/phase-17-standing-fixes.md` and `notes/plans/17y-bdedupe-design-memo.md`). What changed: after you sign a fresh install into your account, the seeded things no longer double — the field guide, the classics / starred notes / Quicknotes gatherings, the first quick note and the tutorial scraps all fold to one copy when both copies are untouched and identical; anything you've touched always keeps both. Jekyll no longer seeds into fresh libraries on EITHER platform (restorable any time from Help ▸ Restore), and the field guide now teaches only the modes the released app actually has. Each built as stated; each cheap to overturn. Nothing here gates the release.**
(a) **The fold only merges copies that are untouched AND identical to each other.** A copy you've edited, renamed, re-ruled, starred/un-starred, shelved, or tagged is never merged — both stay, visibly. Alternative: the Sherlock-style rule (your edited copy kept, the untouched one retired even when they differ) — it folds more cases but retires seed copies beside your edited one.
(b) **An old-content field guide meeting a new-content one keeps BOTH** (they aren't identical). In practice your real store's guide is re-seeded to current content at this pass's close, so your Monday install folds clean. Alternative: fold the stale one away automatically.
(c) **Un-starring Quicknotes, or moving a seeded gathering to your own shelf, counts as "yours" and keeps both.** Alternative: treat those as cosmetic and fold anyway.
(d) **The Quicknotes designation only ever re-points from a folded loser to the keeper** — it never creates or revives a gathering (your deletion stays your word, as ruled at 13i-b).
(e) **During a key-ceremony's drain, guide copies aren't judged** (the drain engine carries the shared facts only); they fold at the next ordinary sync that touches any book. A corner measured in minutes, recorded.
(f) **The phone's Jekyll seed was removed too** — your ruling named "people's libraries", and a phone-seeded Jekyll would re-arrive everywhere by sync; this crossed the batch's zero-mobile fence as a recorded exception (one surgical removal, the mobile pin flipped with it).
(g) **One narrow timing window is recorded, not defended** (the ladder's call, yours to overturn): if the fold runs on one device and — in the same few minutes, before its result syncs over — you engage the OTHER device's copy (rename it, edit it), your engaged copy can still be retired when the result lands; a text edit rides into the Trash rather than vanishing. The books' dedupe gained a full defense for this exact class at RUN B only after a real crossing was seen; building the same defense for these objects is the alternative you can order.
(h) **A kept twin note's version link can point at a lesson that was folded away with the other guide copy** — the link's door then lands on a Trash-recoverable note. One sentence of record; the grains fold independently by design.

*(#186 correction at the ladder's round 1, honest-record (attribution tidied at round 2: the quoted phrase lives in (b); the HEAD's own pre-fix falsehood was its unqualified fold-to-one claim): the overclaim was asserted BEFORE the real-seed proof existed — the cold review found the shipped guide (its word target, its margin-comment lesson, its sealed lesson) could never fold at all. FIXED in the same round: the seed's own state now rides the identity instead of reading as yours, and the claim is now PINNED against the real seeder (`TutorialFurnitureFoldTests` — two real mints must classify foldable and equal). The stale-content caveat in (b) stands unchanged.)*

#187 — **17y B1's PROVISIONAL readings — the note index's scroll and the Library's whole-corpus scroll (17y items (j)(k) + your books-region rider, 2026-10-05; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-17-standing-fixes.md` — the 17y B1 RECORD). What changed: the index column beside a long note now scrolls at the display's pace (it was building a whole-note lookup and three live buttons for every row it drew — ~5 frames a second on a 2,000-block note, which is what made a quick flick leap); the Library now scrolls through EVERY note in both List and Cards, in every sort, reading a little at a time as you go (it stopped at 40); under All, once the books would fill more than half the height they scroll in their own half so the notes always show. These are the edges the builds had to decide — each built as stated, each one line to overturn — and one real question, (i). Nothing here gates the release except your walk.**
(a) **The index column's resting row buttons are a still picture.** The ↑ ↓ ⋯ on rows you aren't pointing at are drawn once as an image, exactly where the real buttons sit, a shade lighter than before; the real buttons appear on the selected row, and on the row under the pointer as soon as the scroll stops. Alternatives: no hint at all on resting rows; or the real buttons on every row (that was the lag). *(The ladder's rider, 2026-10-05: they're a still picture to VoiceOver too — the real buttons are reachable on the row you've selected, and selecting is one VoiceOver act; the alternative, yours to order, is custom accessibility actions on every row, reachable without selecting and with no lag cost.)*
(b) **The Library reads notes in growing batches** — 80 first, then as many again as are listed (160, 320, 640…), never more than 1,280 at a time — asked for well before you reach the end, so you shouldn't meet a wait. Alternatives: a fixed batch size; or everything at once (the cost would then grow with your whole library).
(c) **A batch that arrives while you're scrolling waits until the list goes still** (a fifth of a second), so its small hitch lands in a pause — unless you're within 40 notes of the end, where it lands at once. Alternative: always land at once.
(d) **Books are always listed in full; only notes are read in batches.** Your rider: under All, once the books would take more than half the height, they get their own half and scroll inside it; with fewer books it looks exactly as before; the Books scope keeps one list. Alternatives: a fixed number of book rows; a divider you can drag.
(e) **A typed search still names at most 40 blocks/scraps**, with its "showing the first…" line; notes and books are never cut. Alternative: read the blocks in batches too.
(f) **The bottom of the Library has no closing line** — "loading more notes…" shows only while a batch is on its way; when you're at the oldest note, the list simply ends. Alternative: an "end of library — N notes" line.
(g) **Sorting by created, date or title has no dedicated database index**: each batch is one pass over the wing's notes, measured at worst ~13 ms for 20,000 notes in the slow test build, off the main thread. Adding indexes for them is a sync-lockstep schema event — yours if you ever want it.
(h) **Acting on a row (rename, tag, archive, delete…) keeps your place** — the Library re-reads in place at the depth you'd scrolled to instead of jumping back to the top; the day filter's answer stays whole (a day's notes are few). Alternatives: back to the top after an act; batches for the day filter too.
(i) **THE QUESTION — the List view through notes it hasn't drawn yet.** Each NEW row the List draws costs ~3–4 ms in the test build (macOS's own table measuring each row's height) — the same per row as before; before, there were only 40. Over rows already drawn it moves at the display's pace, and Cards keeps that pace even through new rows; but a brisk flick through unseen rows in List runs at ~48 frames a second with a few short hitches. Options: **(1) keep the List as it is** (built — PROVISIONAL); **(2) put the List's notes on the same lazy column Cards uses** — smooth like Cards, but the List's rows would look and select a little differently, which is your eye's call; **(3) decide at your walk**, in the build you walk (the release build can't be measured from here).
    **ANSWERED AT HER WALK (2026-10-05 ~19:55): FIX — option (2)'s road, wearing the List's exact face.** Her words: "the scroll itself is not fine. it's noticeable how laggy it is and it's obviously worse than the index scroll I just tested. so we actually are going to have to fix it. the hit is too big - there's glitching and lags on the way down. ew!! not in our app." Runs as 17y B-WALK item W-1; LOOK PARITY with the shipped List rows is the acceptance's first leg, her eye the judge at the re-walk.

#188 — **librarywalkpolish batch 1 (the 17y B-WALK round) — the edges its builds had to decide (2026-10-05; the binding record is `notes/plans/phase-17-standing-fixes.md` — the 17y B-WALK RECORD; the pass is `notes/plans/librarywalkpolish.md`). What changed, in one breath: the Library's List now scrolls on the same engine as Cards, looking as it did; picking Cards moves the scope off "All" and picking "All" moves you off Cards; № and order ▾ open one quiet popover style of the app's own, and opening any masthead menu closes the № float; the shelf names sit on light-wash squares, the one you're on filled; "my order" and Send to Top/Bottom step back for 0.9.2; Terminal's Cards read larger; a dragged book shows just its title at your cursor; and a shelf heading no longer shows a line that does nothing. Each is built as stated and is one line to overturn; nothing here gates the release except your re-walk.**
(a) **Only "All" moves when you pick Cards.** Cards over Books or Blocks keeps that scope (Cards then says no notes match); leaving Cards never moves the scope back to All; the Timeline is untouched. Alternatives: Cards always moves the scope to Notes; leaving Cards restores All. **Round-1 product note: the same dishonesty survives through the Books/Blocks scopes in Cards ("No notes match" over a full library, reachable two ways); the GENERALIZED rule — Cards ⇒ Notes from any scope; a non-Notes scope picked inside Cards ⇒ List — builds PROVISIONAL in the fix round, yours to overturn.** **RULED at her next word ('yes, generalized change is fine to work with') — the provisional closes; the generalized rule is hers.**
(b) **Opening a masthead menu closes the № float for good** — it stays closed until you press № again (the remembered state follows). Alternative: it comes back when the menu closes.
(c) **Clicking the highlighted census row clears its solo** (the row is the act, both ways). Alternative: a separate clear row at the bottom.
(d) **The order popover behaves like a menu**: a click anywhere else just closes it (that click does nothing else, as a menu's doesn't); there is no Escape key for it yet. Alternative: the outside click also does what it was aimed at. **Round-1 correction (the ladder's MINOR): this claimed more than the build did — the outside-click catcher covered the board alone, and navigating away (⌘K/⌘N/⌘J, the sidebar) left the popover open for the return; the fix round closes it on the board's disappearance, window resign-key and app deactivation.**
(e) **Both popovers hang under their own control** with their words lined up under the control's words; the № float still covers the right end of the first rows while it's open (its earlier shape). Alternative: right-aligned to the board's edge as before.
(f) **A saved "my order" shows as A–Z while my order is hidden** — never rewritten, so when it returns your order is where you left it. Picking A–Z or last edited in the popover replaces it (that's your choice). Alternative: show last edited instead.
(g) **The strip's squares**: every shelf word is now in the full ink (the reference draws them so; they were muted), "+ shelf" rests on a square too, the resting fill is the voice's wash lightened and the filled one is the accent tone (Study's blue, Terminal's green). Alternatives: keep the inactive words muted; a warmer fill like the reference's beige.
(h) **The List's floating section heading** blurs the row under it a little less than the old one did; and the List now SHOWS your ⌘/⇧-click pick (a soft wash with a border) — the old List, in the test windows, showed none. Alternative: none needed unless your eye disagrees.
(i) **Terminal's Cards**: the preview lines moved to the voice's 13-pt mono (from 11.5) with a little space between the two lines, and a touch more air above and below each card; Study's Cards are pixel-for-pixel unchanged. Alternatives: a larger title too; a narrower preview measure.
(j) **The dragged book is a small card — ★ and the name — centred under where you grabbed.** Verified by rendering it; a real drag could not be driven with you at the machine, so your re-walk is the check. Alternative: the name alone with no card behind it.
(k) **Only a shelf HEADING's line was changed**: it now lights only for a book from another shelf. A book dropped on its own shelf's NAME in the strip still outlines that name and does nothing (the brief kept the strip's targets as they were). Alternative: give the strip names the same rule. **Round 1 folded the strip's "starred" word into this class: it outlines for an ALREADY-STARRED book and releases into starBook's silent guard — the same false offer; fixed with the class in the fix round.** (l) A book dragged from ANOTHER WINDOW's Bookcase meets a per-window drag record: a cross-shelf header drop there shows no hairline (or a stale one) though the drop acts — the W-5 fix's reverse, needing two Bookcase windows. ACCEPTED as a corner this round; the alternative (one app-wide drag record) is yours to order. **(The fix round completed (k)'s class — the strip's names, the ⋯ panel's rows and the "starred" word now outline only where the drop acts — so (l)'s corner now covers those targets too.)** (m) **In DARK, the List's floating section heading is a grey band** (found by the round-1 fix round's dark pass, nothing built): the new List draws its pinned "books"/"notes" heading as a warm grey bar (#2F2D29) across the black room in both voices, where the old List's own heading (still visible over the books region when there are many) sits almost on the room (#191B19 in Terminal, #25221E in Study); in light the two are identical. Options: keep it (a visible heading bar in dark); draw the heading from the voice's own colours (the room plus its hairline — black in dark, near-identical in light); or chase the old List's exact look further (the system material didn't reproduce it in three tries).

## Mobile Round 2 — RENUMBERED at the VM-5 merge (2026-10-07)

*(These eleven entries were minted as #84–#94 on the `mobile` branch (2026-08-30/31) while `main`
independently minted a DIFFERENT #84–#94 — so they are renumbered here into main's sequence. THE
KEY, for every mobile-round record that cites the old numbers: 84→189 · 85→190 · 86→191 · 87→192 ·
88→193 · 89→194 · 90→195 · 91→196 · 92→197 · 93→198 · 94→199. The mobile-era records are frozen
and keep the old citations; this table is how they resolve.)*


*The round's FIRST entries here. `notes/plans/mobile-round-2-handoff-2026-08-30.md` §7 is the whole-round status table; the closeout still owes a fuller pass over items held earlier in the round.*

#189 — **RULED 2026-08-30 (author, on reading the four-option proposal): "I want this option: Two rooms."** The phone's archive/trash IA — Archive becomes a room you browse, the Trash a utility you visit; two doors, two destinations, nothing shared. **This discharges PL-14(b)/§69.5's design-session gate and unblocks batch 6c.** She overruled the proposal's own recommendation (option 3, the Library-filter road) and named the reason, which became #190. Record: `notes/plans/mobile-archive-trash-design/`.

#190 — **RULED 2026-08-30, in the same breath — a NEW work row: the Library's filter vocabulary, and it OUTRANKS the archive work.** Her words: *"Archived is a comparatively rare option, and it needs the same filters that the regular library has. the mockup only had bookless and scraps but those are less useful than 'books' 'posts', etc. so I'd want to see that kind of sorting on the library, **which is more important because it's where you see everything**."* Today's phone filters are provenance-only (`bookless`, `scraps`); she wants **kind** filters, and **Archive inherits the same set** — the cost she knowingly accepted by choosing Two rooms over the filter-chip option. **Two open sub-questions for whoever builds it:** "books" and "posts" are different *entity kinds*, so the row may be expressing two things at once; and the filter row is **already at its width budget**. Owner: mobile.

#191 — **RULED 2026-08-30 — the shelf's two refinements.** *"the box should be one line until you click into it"* (settled batch 5's own PROVISIONAL about the large empty box on a near-empty shelf) and *"the shelf should be bounded so it always has a decent amount of room to write"* (the writing floor raised from a floor to a **guarantee**, now pinned across 2,408 shelf states rather than pinning the arithmetic). **Both BUILT.**

#192 — **PROVISIONAL, held to the 13s copy pass at her word — Block History's one-take case.** A block with exactly one take and no savepoints shows a **calm one-line sentence**; the Mac in the same situation shows a "Savepoints" header plus a collapsed "this take · 0" row. Her ruling: *"we can keep the sentence for now, I just have to see it later when I'm using the app and doing the copy pass anyway."* **No correctness content either way** — one line and one pin in either direction. *(The related BUG is fixed: a fanned block with 2+ takes and no commits used to report "one take" and draw no take sections at all.)*

#193 — **HERS, and she has taken it — B1's residual.** The starter-Journal seed can no longer fire before sync is settled (the seed moved after `await start()`, plus a `SyncGate` and a bounded wait that declines without consuming the mark). **But a case remains that no gate can close:** a fresh install where nobody is signed in seeds correctly — and if she *then* signs in from Settings, **her real Journal arrives beside the starter one**. It needs a product answer: sign-in offered *before* the shell, or a post-sign-in reconciliation that retires the starter. Her words: *"B1 is a real item though - that's good to note and I'll address it in my upcoming mac session."* **Do not build a guess for it.**

#194 — **OPEN — batch 5's remaining PROVISIONALs** (`notes/plans/mobile-batch-5-record.md` §3), all taste, none blocking: the starter-journal copy · the badge's short word · dropping "daily" from the badged row's meta · the starter note sorting last in the book · starred staying inside the bounded region · the box's 11.5 pt type now that it is the main writing surface · **blur-with-text** (the box currently collapses on blur even holding text; "collapse only when empty" is equally defensible and is hers).

#195 — **OPEN — the architecture review's findings to slot** (`notes/plans/mobile-architecture-review.md`). Its two MAJORs are **fixed**; what remains is hers to slot: **MINOR 3** (`MobileEditorStyle.voice`, an unsynchronised mutable global a non-serialized suite mutates while 81 of 86 suites run parallel) · **MINOR 4** (refusal presentation has ten homes and **71 raw `String(describing:)` dumps**, against the project's own "refusals speak" doctrine) · 2 NITs. **MINOR 1 and MINOR 2 are NOT hers to slot — they belong to 13u.** *(Precision, added 2026-08-30 at the Fable sweep's finding 8 — this clause used to read "MINOR 1 **is** 13u item 8's O(N²) row", which over-equates and could cost the fix. MINOR 1 is `applyDifferential`, BOTH platforms — **a DIFFERENT, unreported site** from the row's named `planVisibleTakeEdit` twin, and the review was careful to establish exactly that. It is covered only by the row's **sweep-the-class-to-completion** clause, never by fixing the row's named example: a 13u session that fixes `planVisibleTakeEdit`, declares the row done and stops leaves MINOR 1 standing.)*

#196 — **FOR THE 13u/13p-fix SESSION, not mobile's — the `main` iOS build break, found and fixed 2026-08-30.** The 13p-fix ladder's R3-2 moved `ProtectionCopy.wroteButUnreadable` into `Shared/StoreProtection.swift` so that, in its own comment's words, *"BOTH surfaces can say it"* — but placed it **inside the `#else` (macOS-only) arm**. Three `Shared/` sites reference it unconditionally, and `Shared/` is compiled by the phone, so **the iOS target did not build at all**. Proven pre-existing on a clean `origin/main` worktree; fixed by moving the declaration outside the conditional. **The Mac suite gave no signal — it compiled fine there**, which is the MobileSoul §3 class exactly. **Separately and NOT fixed:** `CustodyHandoverTests.theE2EESurfaceNamesTheSymptomWhenAWriteLandsUnreadable` fails on clean `main` too — a *behaviour* bug in the same R3-2 work, already routed to the phase that will cold-review those changes.

#197 — **RULED 2026-08-31 (author, via the orchestrator's question bundle — both answers were the recommended options): the Library's filter vocabulary (#190's two open sub-questions).** **(a) "Books" lists BOOKS AS ROWS** — the Library becomes the true everything-surface: filtering to "books" lists her books themselves as openable rows; "posts" lists notes; scraps stays. (The alternatives — note-kind partitioning, or membership-based "notes in books" — were offered and not chosen.) **(b) The width budget gives at the SORT: it shrinks to a glyph**, freeing the row for the kind doors; everything stays on one visible row (scrolling the row, moving sort to the search row, and measure-first were offered and not chosen). These two rulings unblock the filter batch; the Archive room inherits the same set per #190's letter.

#198 — **RULED 2026-08-31 (author, on the filter batch's report — two rulings, her words verbatim):** *"i think the question of book archiving is going to be handled in 13i, for the record. All should be a book + notes union - otherwise it just doesn't make sense."* **(a) BOOK ARCHIVING IS OWNED BY 13i** — the filter batch's logged candidate (an archived-books state would be an additive migration + a SyncSpec §12 lockstep event) goes to the 13i design sitting, not to a mobile batch; the Archive room's two-door set stands until 13i rules. **(b) The Library's `All` filter IS THE BOOKS + NOTES UNION** — this OVERTURNS the filter batch's PROVISIONAL 3 (which built All = notes-only to change nothing at rest); the union builds in the batch's fix wave. The interleaving detail (how a book's stamp orders against notes under the active sort) was not specified and lands PROVISIONAL with the build.

#199 — **RULED 2026-08-31 (author, verbatim): *"i agree that those four hoists should just be deferred to the post 13u merge - seems cleaner that way."*** The four Shared-hoist parity items — `FindReplaceGate` (rides PR21 replace, which never builds without it) · `TakeClipboard`'s composition (copy-all-takes) · `applyTypedPath` (tag-chip grammar) · `ThemeStore` (PR23 + arch MINOR 3's structural half) — are DEFERRED to the post-13u `main`→`mobile` merge. Batches 7/8 build phone-local halves only; each batch record carries the deferral so the post-merge session inherits a decision.

## VM-10 — the Write page's scroll authority (2026-10-07)

#200 — **PROVISIONAL, built most-spec-defensible — three calls her ruled shape does not make** (record: `notes/plans/fixes/vm-10-the-write-scroll-authority.md`). **(a) How long the page stays hers after a pan.** Built: **0.6 s** after her pan's last movement or release (plus the whole of a fling's deceleration), during which no programmatic reveal moves the page; a TAP is not a pan, so tap-to-type still reveals the caret at once. Options: (i) 0.6 s, built — covers the keyboard's spring-back after an `.interactive` drag (the traced fight) and never swallows a deliberate tap a moment later; (ii) longer (1–2 s) — safer against slow spring-backs, but a quick "scroll, then type" would find its first keystroke's reveal suppressed; (iii) no window, live-pan only — the spring-back after release would still throw her (the fight survives). ****CONSEQUENCE ADDENDUM (the cold review's MINOR 2 — so this ratification is on true consequences):** the 0.6 s window is ORIGIN-BLIND — for ≤0.6 s after a pan it also swallows reveals you asked for through a non-pan door: (i) typing with the caret off-screen inside the window types blind until the first keystroke after it (self-healing); (ii) a find-navigator "next match" tap inside the window highlights without scrolling (the second tap works). The reviewer's optional tightening — void the window from the edit door, since typing is your newest intent — is red-on-old pinnable and one small arm; choosing it is part of this (a). (b) The memory remembers the CARET with the page.** Built: the page's place (content-anchored) AND her caret are kept per note; a focus arrival then reveals HER line minimally (usually it is already on screen, so nothing moves). Options: (i) page + caret, built; (ii) page only — the caret lands at the start of the note as today, and a focus arrival would reveal line 1, undoing the restored place on every Today-door re-entry; (iii) page only and no focus reveal — the keyboard rises with the caret off screen. **(c) Where the memory lives.** Built: on `MobileShellRouter` (one stored-property line outside the three files the brief named — still phone-local; nothing in `Shared/`), session-only, never persisted, keyed by note so either tab stack re-opens where the note was last left — the twin of the Mac's `EditorRouter.scrollPlaces`. Options: (i) the router, built; (ii) a process-global store in the Write feature — no file outside the brief's three, but a global singleton (CLAUDE.md's rule against them); (iii) `AppSession` — fenced (`Shared/`) for this unit.

*(Next free number: **#213** — #212 is 13n-c's ratify bundle, 2026-10-12 at its merge; before it the marker read #210 and was STALE AGAIN — #210 (VX-143's bundle) and #211 (the lens pass's) already existed below it; corrected at the 13n-c merge. The earlier history: #209 is 13n-b's ratify bundle, 2026-10-10 at its merge; before it the marker here read #207 and was STALE AGAIN — #207 (the 13n sitting) and #208 (13n-a) already existed below it; corrected at the 13n-b merge. ## VX-106 — the follow's wrong-word sentence in a changed-elsewhere world (2026-10-08)

#205 — **A copy question for her and 13s (VX-106's round-1 MINOR; record `notes/plans/fixes/vx-106-posture-harness-standard-world.md`).** When a Castle account's Mac owes the Apple-openable follow AND the passphrase was changed on another device, the ruled precedence (the follow outranks the change notice — locked by VX-106's pin) means the "your passphrase was changed on [device] on [day]" notice never shows; if she then types her OLD passphrase into the follow, the refusal says **"That isn't your library's passphrase"** — not quite true: it IS the library's old word; the ACCOUNT's word moved elsewhere. The same sentence already shows in two sibling changed-elsewhere worlds. Options: (i) keep the sentence (rare corner; the right word works); (ii) in a changed-elsewhere world, the follow's refusal names the change — e.g. "Your passphrase was changed on [device] on [day] — enter the new one" (the model already knows the descriptor differs); (iii) let the change notice speak alongside the follow. Lean: (ii). Words 13s's. **13s status line (2026-10-08):** her ruling is (ii), twice over — first as the question-tool SELECTION “Name the change (Recommended)” relayed through the oct7coordinator session (~10:00 MDT), then FIRST-HAND in the 13s session, verbatim: “I think your passphrase was changed on device on day would be good.” The owning ticket is VX-113 (13s-a, Pre-launch); final wording lands there. **Status: ruled — (ii); the wording is VX-113’s.**

## VX-282 — Run: Editor & notes fixes (2026-10-13)

#220 — **VX-296 (U2), the caret-aware markdown face — three readings built PROVISIONAL** (the binding record is
`notes/plans/fixes/vx-282-editor-notes-fixes.md` § U2; your ruling on the ticket, fix (a), "your fix is good": while
your caret sits right after the space that disqualifies a closer, the closer still counts, in the live editor only).
*(Numbering: the next free number on main when written; a sibling run merging first may take it — the merging
session renumbers.)* Each is built as stated, with a line to reverse it:
**P-U2-1 — the OPENER side is NOT caret-aware.** Built: (a) closer only — your ruling's letter, and what typing words
forward inside a pair produces (the space always lands before the closer). The opener still flips when you backspace
the FIRST word away (`*| world*`) or type a space right after the opening star. Options: (b) the mirror too — a caret
right after an opening star whose next character is a space also counts; arithmetic stays plain at every caret
position either way (one caret can never rescue both halves), but a star typed in the middle of a line that has a
later closer (`5 *| 3 and *wow*`) would show a long wrong italic for as long as the caret sits there.
**P-U2-2 — the rule covers every closer the flanking rule governs, not only `*`.** Built: (a) `*` italic, `**` bold,
`~~` strikethrough, and `_` italic (which shares the rule) — ⌘B's pair flickered worse than ⌘I's (`**two |**` read as
an italic over `two *` with a stray dimmed star, then bold again at the next letter). Option: (b) the italic star
only, your report's literal case.
**P-U2-3 — ⌘I/⌘B still read the caret-blind grammar.** At `*hello |*` the face now shows italics, but ⌘I does what it
always did at that one spot: it inserts a new `**` pair (one letter later, at `*hello w|*`, ⌘I unwraps the span).
Built: (a) unchanged — the acts are among "every other reader" your ruling left untouched. Option: (b) the acts read
the caret-aware reading when nothing is selected, so ⌘I unwraps what the face shows.
**Said plainly, so it isn't a surprise (the run's round-1 review, R1-5):** the fix you approved was worded "the grammar
… untouched", but the caret rule was built INSIDE the grammar's module — `MarkdownSyntax.spans(in:caret:)` takes an
optional caret, and the closers ask one new question (`closerFlanks`: no space before me, or the caret sits exactly
here). With no caret — every reader except the live editors: the read pages, export, the phone's read page, search,
⌘B/⌘I — the reading is byte-for-byte what it was (the grammar's own suites run unchanged and green, and a pin walks
every caret position of nineteen texts to show a caret changes nothing except where it sits right after a space and
right before a closing mark — `*`, `~` or `_`). **One more thing it does there, so it isn't a surprise either (the run's round-2 review, F-1):**
when that star is an INNER one, the rule can reshape a span that already read fine — `*foo *bar*` reads as one italic
over `foo *bar`, but with your caret just before the inner star it reads as an italic `foo ` and a plain `bar*`, and
`*2 * 3 = 6*` does the same with the caret before the middle star; it changes only WHILE the caret sits there, and goes
back the moment it moves. Option (b), if you'd rather it never did that: the caret rescues a star only where no span
already covers it, so a stable span is never shortened. It lives there so
there is ONE grammar with one extra input, rather than a second matcher in the face that could drift from it. If you'd
rather the grammar file itself stayed untouched, the rule can move out into the face at the cost of that second copy.
Status: open — your eye in daily use.

#221 — **VX-154 (U3), Write's take panel answers every routed command about a block or a take on ITS block — seven
readings built PROVISIONAL** (the binding record is `notes/plans/fixes/vx-282-editor-notes-fixes.md` § U3 — the command
table, the traced mechanism, the pins). *(Numbering: the next free number when written; the merging session renumbers if
a sibling run took it.)* Each is built as stated, with a line to reverse it:
**P-U3-1 — ⌘S in the panel with no take clicked into commits the take the block shows**, and the ring moves onto it first
so you see which take you are committing. Built (a): P-VX150-1's rule (#210, ratified for ⌘T), and what ⌘S already did
when the panel was the caret block's. Options: (b) refuse, as the Takes board does ("Click into a take first — ⌘S commits
the take you're in."); (c) commit the shown take without moving the ring.
**P-U3-2 — which block acts the panel does and which go grey.** Built (a): the panel acts on its own block for Delete
Block, Archive Block, Move Take into a Block Below and Turn All Takes into Blocks (you see the result in the panel: its
"no longer in the note" face, takes leaving its list); the nine verbs that cut, join or add blocks where you write
(Divide, Divide choosing takes, Extract, Make Block, Merge, Merge choosing takes, Delete in Every Take, New Block Below,
New Block at End) are grey while the panel is up. Options: (b) those nine close the panel and run in Write at the panel's
block (needs new editor machinery, not built); (c) grey the four block acts too, so the panel never changes the note's
blocks; (d) act on all of them as the Takes board does (their results land on blocks the panel doesn't show).
**P-U3-3 — Move Take into a Block Below (⌥⌘⏎) needs a take clicked into**; with none it refuses as the Takes board does.
Option: (b) move the take the block shows (that is the chosen take — the riskier default).
**P-U3-4 — ⌘⇧T over the panel closes it** (the toggle's close half — ⌘⇧T opens it). Before, it swapped the panel to the
block your Write caret is in when the panel came from another block's ⑂ badge. Options: (b) keep the swap; (c) nothing.
**P-U3-5 — a callout set from the panel is silent** (as everywhere — the frame is the feedback), and the panel draws no
frame, so you see it on the page when the panel closes. Options: (b) a receipt in the panel; (c) the panel wears the frame.
**P-U3-6 — copy (your copy pass):** the panel's archive receipt "Take archived — never gone: “archived takes” below brings
it back." (the ticket's rider — it named the board's "archived · n") and the grey verbs' line "That works in the note
itself — close the takes panel first."
**P-U3-7 — the note's own commands still reach Write while the panel is up** (seal, ⌘⇧S, Versions, the tray, Find,
⌘⇧L). The ones that show something show it beneath or after the panel. Options: (b) grey them while the panel is up;
(c) the panel shows them itself.
**P-U3-8 — where ⌘S's name field sits in the panel** (the walk's finding): under the panel's header, so it covers the
first card — when nothing was clicked into, exactly the take being committed. Option: (b) hang it from the take's own card.
Status: open — your eye in daily use.

#223 — **VX-257 (U5), your half-typed comment survives a click away — four readings built PROVISIONAL** (the binding
record is `notes/plans/fixes/vx-282-editor-notes-fixes.md` § U5; your ruling on P-VX179-R2-1, #215: *"yes, clicking away
should save your half typed comment on a draft."*). When the comments panel closes, whatever is in its box is kept for
that block and comes back when you open the block's comments again; ⎋ still clears it deliberately (its first press
empties the box, its second closes, so nothing is kept); keeping never files a comment (#118(a) stands).
*(Numbering: the next free number when written; the merging session renumbers if a sibling run took it.)* Each is built
as stated, with a line to reverse it:
**P-U5-1 — the panel keeps ONE draft at a time, the newest.** Leaving block B's comments with words in the box replaces
a draft kept for block A — including one the store refused (a sealed note's). That is the park's existing one-draft rule
(#140(a)), now reached by a click away too. Options: (b) one kept draft PER BLOCK (the park becomes a small per-block
table — on the phone's park too); (c) a refused draft is never displaced by a merely unfinished one.
**P-U5-2 — Close keeps the draft too**, not only a click away: every way out except ⎋⎋ keeps what is in the box (one
leaving road for all of them). Option: (b) the Close button abandons the draft, as ⎋⎋ does.
**P-U5-3 — a kept draft comes back silently, with the caret after it**, ready to go on typing. A refused draft still
comes back with its line (*"the comment that couldn't be saved — here to try again"*). Options: (b) a line of its own for
a kept draft (say, "your unfinished comment — still here"); (c) the caret at the start, as a refused draft's restore had
it.
**P-U5-4 — emptying the box and leaving lets a kept draft go — a refused one included.** So ⎋⎋ after a refused draft
comes back abandons it; before, the refused words came back on every open until they landed (#140(b)). Option: (b) an
emptied box leaves a REFUSED draft kept; only an unfinished one goes.
Recorded, not a question: your ruling supersedes #140(c) on the Mac ("a draft typed and never submitted still dies with
the close"). The phone is unchanged — its comments sheet still drops an unsubmitted draft on close (your ruling named the
Mac popover); a VM ticket is the orchestrator's to mint.
Status: open — your eye in daily use.

#224 — **VX-326 (U6), the Bookcase's quick entry takes the caret when the app opens — two readings built PROVISIONAL**
(the binding record is `notes/plans/fixes/vx-282-editor-notes-fixes.md` § U6 — the trace, the pins, the walk; your
word: *"yeah you can fix this ticket if you think it's an annoying issue"*). What was wrong: the Bookcase asked for the
caret before its box was in the window, the ask was spent, and nothing tried again — so the app opened with the
keyboard nowhere. Now the ask waits until the box is in its window and takes the caret only if nothing else in that
window has the keyboard (never from a field, never under a sheet); it is decided once. *(Numbering: the next free
number when written; the merging session renumbers if a sibling run took it.)* Each is built as stated, with a line to
reverse it:
**P-U6-1 — the ask is decided when the box reaches its window, on that window alone.** Built (a): if nothing in the
Bookcase's window holds the keyboard and no sheet is on it, the caret goes into the quick entry; other windows are not
consulted. So if a note opened in its own window comes back at launch in front, that note keeps its keyboard, and when
you click over to the main window the caret is already in the quick entry. Options: (b) wait until the main window
itself becomes the front window and decide then, dropping the ask if another window came to the front first; (c) as
(a), but drop the ask whenever another window of the app is in front at that moment (then the quick entry is not ready
when you click over).
**P-U6-2 — only a window that OPENS on the Bookcase asks.** Built (a): launching, unlocking, and a new window each put
the caret in the quick entry; coming BACK to the Bookcase from a note or the Library does not (it never did — measured:
that road drops the ask the same way, and it is left exactly as it was). Options: (b) every arrival at the Bookcase puts
the caret there — "the shelf opens with the cursor already in the bar" (R102) read for every visit — which needs the
quick entry to stay out of the way while the tour runs (a tour started from a note walks you to the Bookcase); (c) only
the app's very first window at launch (not after an unlock, not a new window).
Status: open — your eye in daily use.


## VX-266 — Castle & backups before launch: the design (2026-10-14)

#225 — **THE VX-266 OVERNIGHT CALLS (the design merged 2026-10-14, PR #161 2f5ac7a7; the binding record is
`notes/plans/vx-266-castle-backups-prelaunch.md` §9, the reasons and options in `notes/handoffs/vx-266-design-handoff.md`).**
Six calls were made provisionally overnight at her word (*"ruling provisionally where needed"*). She answered them the
next morning, before the merge:
1. **A follower's anchor is also locked under the secret typed at its follow** (design §3.4, rule 5 in §3.1). Copies a
   device made before the leave open with the secret typed there to follow, as well as with whatever opened that
   device that day. CONFIRMED: *"i think that's fine, i'm good to confirm it either way."*
2. **"Off in Release" means the new code ships dormant in Release builds** (design §5). The core compiles and is
   tested in both configurations from phase 1, and the switch flips the wiring. CONFIRMED: *"dormant in release is what
   i meant."*
3. **How the migration's real-scope branch is rehearsed before her live stores migrate** (design §5, §9). She asked to
   decide it now (*"call 3 - we can decide this now. what are my options?"*). The options, as laid out to her: (a) a
   spare macOS user on the same Apple ID; (b) the Air first, as a canary; (c) the rehearsal taken as the first leg of
   the real migration. Whatever runs logs every Apple key id it mints. **DECIDED the same morning:** *"I'll go with
   your recommendation on 1, then air, then pro, then phone … i REALLY want this phase to be thoroughly tested before
   release."* A spare macOS login on her Apple ID first, then the Air as canary, then the Pro, then the phone, with
   her extra legs (a new Mac from a Castle library; a test account Standard → Castle → set aside → her real Castle
   account brought in). The rehearsal answers "not yet" to retiring the old shared key, which is retired once,
   after the phone. The details are on VX-329, for its plan. The case surfaced **VX-340** (two logins on one Mac would
   share one device identity), to be settled before the rehearsal.
4. **Today's bugs in Release until the switch.** RULED: *"yeah we can do the small bug fix."* Today-road fixes for
   VX-302 → **VX-338** and VX-317 → **VX-339**, as one small RUN at her word. VX-209, VX-107, VX-108, VX-109, VX-113 and
   VX-213 wait for the rebuild.
5. **The Touch ID re-arm question asks only after a press fails on an item that exists** (design §3.10). CONFIRMED:
   *"that's fine on calls 5 and 6."*
6. **A new device identity starts on Always ask** (design §3.10). CONFIRMED, as for 5; the exact words stay hers at
   phase 4's walk.
*(Numbering: the next free number on main when written; a sibling run merging first may take it — the merging session
renumbers.)*
Status: answered, all six.


## VX-246 — Run: MCP door fixes (2026-10-14)

#226 — **THE VX-246 RATIFY BUNDLE (merged 2026-10-14, PR #162, merge commit 712bbb4f; the binding record is
`notes/plans/fixes/vx-246-mcp-door-fixes-ai-docs.md` — her rulings during the run, ORCHESTRATOR'S PROVISIONAL
RULINGS, each UNIT RECORD's PROVISIONALs block with its options, THE LADDER (Fable round 1 → Opus round 2, closed);
your handoff `notes/handoffs/vx-246-handoff.md`).** Built overnight under your mandate (*"Rule provisionally in my
absence and leave me a handoff with any needed information to note down."*). Each was built as stated, with a line
to reverse it.
- **Setup keys (VX-258, U1 and U1b):**
  - **P-VX258-1** — a key from one client's tab presented under ANOTHER approved name waits for your OK, as an
    Any-client key does (a tag only ever adds the zero-click right for its own name). Option: (b) refuse it with the
    one sentence — stricter, but a client whose real name differs from its tab's then has only the Any tab.
  - **P-VX258-2 — ANSWERED.** The bind arm (an approved name with no key tied to it — after opening a library from
    another Mac, a Deny then an approve, or a client approved before VX-168) takes a key copied from that client's own
    tab with no clicks, and any other new key waits for your OK. Your word, 2026-10-13: *"i ratify what you built"*.
  - **P-VX258-3** — a waiting connection sees no tools, every known tool answers the pending sentence, and an
    unknown name hears "Unknown tool" as anyone would. Residual: a MALFORMED call to a known tool still tells a waiting
    connection from a first-contact one. Options: (b) route the waiting session through every tool's standing read
    (closes the residual; touches every tool); (c) refuse the waiting connection at the handshake with its own
    sentence (simplest, but tells its holder the name is approved, and needs a reconnect after your Allow).
  - **P-VX258-4** — one waiting setup per name: a newer one replaces the older, whose key is forgotten. Option: (b)
    one row per key — several asks for one name that you cannot tell apart.
  - **P-VX258-5** — the wait is logged once in Activity as not allowed; a re-presentation logs nothing, and a waiting
    connection's calls log nothing. Option: (b) log it as connected, and/or log its calls one by one.
  - **P-VX258-6** — the key's tag is the client's name in plain text (`vmk2_<name>_…`; 1–64 of letters, digits, `.`
    `_` `-`; a name outside that is refused at the copy). Option: (b) an encoded tag that carries any name and that
    nobody can read.
  - **P-VX258-7** — the "New setup for ‹name›" row leads Waiting, above the connection requests, with Allow and
    Don't allow equal and quiet. Options: (b) after the requests; (c) Allow styled as the primary button (set aside:
    the press hands a stranger the name's grants).
  - **P-VX258-8** — Activity words the wait as an ask: "‹name› asked to use a setup that wasn't copied for it".
    Option: (b) the refusal form, "‹name› was refused connecting with a setup that wasn't copied for it".
- **Comments (VX-260, U1 and U1b):**
  - **P-VX260-1** — your edit on ANOTHER device. As built: (a) your edit on this Mac forgets the AI's authorship
    record, AND (b) the record keeps a digest of the words the client wrote, so any change to them from any device or
    client makes deleting it need EDIT. Options: (a) alone (comments you edit on the phone or the Air stay deletable at
    suggest); (c) compare `updated_at` (set aside: an undo, a delete and Put back, and every sync apply bump it with
    no word changed). Accepted residual: an edit-level client's own rewrite also ends its suggest-level ownership.
  - **P-VX260-2** — a record from before the digest existed counts as UNPROVEN, so deleting that comment needs EDIT.
    Options: (b) backfill the digest from today's words (would bless a comment you already rewrote on another device
    or under the earlier build); (c) trust the old record (the hole stays open for every comment written before this
    build).
- **Her editor vs the wire (VX-275, U3):**
  - **P-VX275-1** — before every listed act the door flushes EVERY open note's pending typing. Options: (b) only the
    act's own note, resolved from its ids before the act (a read outside the act's transaction, and a timing oracle
    on notes the client can't reach); (c) (b) after the grant decides, by splitting the act into turns (re-opens the
    gap the decide-and-act guard closed).
  - **P-VX275-2** — the remaining window (a keystroke typed in the milliseconds between the flush and the act meets
    the old behaviour, narrowed to those characters) is accepted and recorded. Options: (b) a keyboard fence over
    every open editor for each wire act's flight (an editor-seam change; your typing pauses for each AI write);
    (c) refuse a wire delete or freeze while the app holds unsaved typing for that take.
- **The undo class (VX-276 · VX-277 · VX-119, U2):**
  - **P-VX276-1** — a quiet half retires its own entry and leaves the other stack as it was. Options: (b) it also ends
    the redo future (throws away redos your earlier presses earned); (c) the entry moves on marked quiet and its
    partner does nothing (the Edit menu then offers a "Redo …" that does nothing).
  - **P-VX276-2** — a quiet press speaks a refusal (a toast) naming the entry: "Nothing to undo — ‹label› was already
    undone or changed since." / "Nothing to redo — it changed again after ‹label› was undone." Option: (b) a quiet
    receipt. (When nothing is left to redo, the Redo item is simply disabled and no sentence is spoken.)
  - **P-VX276-3** — an entry with several parts (slots, columns, blocks, rows, a merge's mint and its sources) moves
    back and forth only the parts its partner moved, while your change to another part stands. Option: (b) any
    divergence makes the whole entry quiet (simpler; the parts you never touched become un-undoable).
  - **P-VX276-4** — every revive is keyed on its own undo's stamp: a row deleted on any other stamp (your Trash after a
    Put back, another device's delete) is never brought back by a redo. Option: (b) revive any dead row the entry names
    (would let a redo undo another device's delete).
  - **P-VX277-1** — your own adopt doors (✓ Make this the note) undo per block: a block is restored only while it still
    shows the adopted take. Option: (b) restore every block unconditionally (your later ✓ on an adopted block is
    overwritten by your own ⌥⌘Z).
- **The orchestrator's provisional rulings (ORCHESTRATOR'S PROVISIONAL RULINGS in the record):**
  - **Ruling 1** — on U3's timing leg a scripted client stood in for "a real client": Claude Code answers in seconds
    and can't land a call inside your 500 ms autosave pause, so a small driver on the real Debug shim proved the
    timing (answered 53 ms and 51 ms after the last keystroke), and a real Claude Code turn proved the rest. Option:
    (b) require a real client for the timing leg (not reachable with a model-driven client).
  - **Ruling 2** — a navigate-away never takes your focus: when a note is deleted while it shows in a pane you are NOT
    typing in, that pane goes to the Library in place and your caret stays where it is — on every road, your own
    Delete door included. Option: (b) as before: the re-routed pane takes the focus. **ANSWERED 2026-10-14 — RATIFIED,
    her words: *"i think the focus sounds fine - it would be annoying for claude to move where you're working, you
    should be able to work fine in the app while the AI is working (though the undo stack is shared and i'm aware of

  - **Ruling 3** — the night's merge carried the four FIX units (U1–U4 and their follow-ups); U5–U8 (the wire tools'

## VX-336 — Run: MCP wire tools & AI docs (2026-10-15)

#227 — **THE VX-336 RATIFY BUNDLE (merged 2026-10-15, PR #166, merge commit 4f47720d; the binding record is
`notes/plans/fixes/vx-336-mcp-wire-tools-ai-docs.md` — her rulings during the run, the ORCHESTRATOR'S PROVISIONAL
RULINGS, each UNIT RECORD's PROVISIONALs with their options and the orchestrator's rulings on its forks, THE LADDER;
your handoff `notes/handoffs/vx-336-handoff.md`).** Built through the evening and the night under your away-mode answer
(*"Rule provisionally, continue"*) and your overnight word (*"rule provisionally overnight in my absence and leave me a
handoff in the morning"*). Each was built as stated below, with a line to reverse it.
- **The ones most worth your eye:**
  - **Two pre-launch calls, yours to make:** **VX-363** — an AI could learn the name of a book it can't read by
    importing a book with the same name. Fixed in this run; filed High. Is it Pre-launch? · **VX-357** — text holding
    the invisible character U+0000 is stored cut off at it, on every door (yours and the AI's), and the AI's new
    fingerprint then vouches for the whole text. Not fixed (Todo, Medium). Is it Pre-launch?
  - **P-VX311-2 — where an AI's duplicate lands, and who may make one.** As built: like your Duplicate as Version, the
    copy lands in no book, in the note's own wing's Pool, so the AI needs Read on the note and Write new on the whole
    wing; an AI whose Write new reaches only a book can't duplicate (it asks you for the wing). Options: (b) an
    optional `book_id` that also files the copy into that book at Write new (no door of yours does that today);
    (c) the copy joins every book the original is in (not your door; an owned book would refuse or fork).
  - **P-VX255-1 — "at the end" for `insert_blocks`.** As built: with Write new an AI adds paragraphs only at the very
    end of a note, by giving no anchor at all; naming any block, even the last one, needs Edit. Your ruling names a
    place ("add blocks at the end"), the rule is decided before the note is read, and it never flips while you type
    new paragraphs at the end. Option: (b) naming the note's current last block also counts as the end at Write new.
  - **P-VX362-1 — the new refusal sentences you may see, on the Mac and the phone** (they replace raw dumps like
    "Refused: alreadyInReading(…)"): "That note is already in this book." · "This book is at its note limit." · "That
    comment is already back." · "A chapter needs a name." · "That note isn't there any more — it may have just been
    deleted." · "That isn't a book — a gathering shows the notes its rule matches and holds none of its own." · "That's
    already a book — only a gathering binds into one." · "That tag isn't there any more — nothing changed." · "That
    block is already in a note — nothing changed." (and a block not in its note speaks the existing "not placed"
    sentence). The first two and the bind sentence are your own doors' words already. And `apply_tag` on a tag the
    note already wears now answers "done, nothing changed" (`changed: false`): no undo entry, no confirmation line, the
    Activity line as for any landed act, and a once grant spent as by any landed call (your rulings don't say; this
    follows the door's rule for every other act that changes nothing). Options: (b) your VX-234 pass rewords any of
    these; (c) a worded refusal for the second `apply_tag` instead ("That note already wears the tag … — nothing
    changed.") — set aside, since your seven tag doors treat it as done; (d) the not-shared sentence for the "isn't
    there any more" class — set aside, since every such refusal follows a decision that allowed the target.
  - **P-VX256-1 — how big a page of a long note is.** Your first real-client round showed a page ended by 32,000
    characters of TEXT came to about 65,000 characters of answer, which Claude Code saves to a file instead of
    reading. Re-ruled and rebuilt: a page ends at `limit` blocks or before the WHOLE ANSWER would pass 32,000
    characters (all three re-probes then arrived whole: 71 blocks · 71 · 115 with `ids_only`). Options: (b) a higher
    bound nearer the measured limit (e.g. 40,000: fewer pages, `ids_only` pages close to the client's default);
    (c) one bound for prose and another for `ids_only`. Its four edges, each built as (i): **e1** the first page
    always carries its first block, even when the note-wide lists alone fill it ((ii) a first page with no block;
    (iii) the note-wide lists on a page of their own) · **e2** characters are counted as UTF-16 units, what the
    client counts ((ii) Unicode characters, looser on emoji; (iii) UTF-8 bytes) · **e3** old commits' text rides
    newest first until the first that doesn't fit ((ii) keep scanning for smaller older ones) · **e4** an asked-for
    commit too large for its page's room refuses with "…isn't a commit of a take on this page", and rides where its
    block starts a page ((ii) always keep it, the page then past the bound; (iii) as (i), with words naming the cursor
    to ask from). A finding, no change: no page can hold 200 blocks under this bound (about 150 at the smallest), so a
    `limit` above about 150 never ends a page.
- **Labels and lenses (VX-267, VX-253):**
  - **P-VX253-1** — the per-block picks your ruling names keep the wire's existing name, `take_ids` (now optional).
    Options: (b) rename them `picks`; (c) accept both names.
  - **P-VX253-2** — a lens label that no take of the note wears refuses ("No take in this note is labelled … — nothing
    was saved."), so a typo never leaves you a lens a Suggest client can't delete. Options: (b) save it anyway, every

    also refuse an EMPTY `take_ids`, which still saves an empty lens today, unchanged.
